### Question 1
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is assessing a 62-year-old client who just received a dose of sublingual nitroglycerin for an episode of stable angina. Which assessment finding requires the nurse's immediate intervention?

A) The client reports a mild, throbbing headache.
B) The client's blood pressure drops to 86/52 mmHg.
C) The client states they feel a tingling sensation under their tongue.
D) The client's heart rate is 78 beats per minute.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B) The client's blood pressure drops to 86/52 mmHg.

<strong>Rationale:</strong> Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator used to relieve angina. While it commonly causes mild hypotension, a significant drop in blood pressure (such as 86/52 mmHg) indicates severe hypotension, which compromises perfusion and requires immediate intervention. A throbbing headache and a tingling sensation under the tongue are expected, normal side effects of sublingual nitroglycerin. A heart rate of 78 bpm is within normal limits.
</details>

---

### Question 2
The nurse is evaluating a 78-year-old client with a history of right-sided heart failure in a long-term care facility. When performing the morning assessment, which finding should the nurse most likely observe?

A) Jugular venous distention
B) Crackles in the lung bases
C) A dry, hacking cough
D) Shortness of breath while lying flat

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A) Jugular venous distention

<strong>Rationale:</strong> Right-sided heart failure causes blood to back up into the systemic venous system. The nurse should expect to observe systemic signs of congestion, such as jugular venous distention (JVD), peripheral edema, and hepatomegaly. Crackles in the lungs, a dry cough, and orthopnea (shortness of breath while lying flat) are classic signs of left-sided heart failure due to pulmonary congestion.
</details>

---

### Question 3
A 65-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with peripheral arterial disease (PAD) reports severe cramping pain in their calves while resting in bed at night. Which action should the nurse instruct the client to take to relieve the pain?

A) Elevate the legs on two pillows above the level of the heart.
B) Firmly massage the calf muscles for ten minutes.
C) Dangle the legs over the side of the bed.
D) Apply tightly fitting compression stockings before sleeping.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C) Dangle the legs over the side of the bed.

<strong>Rationale:</strong> In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), there is a lack of adequate arterial blood flow to the lower extremities. Dangling the legs over the side of the bed allows gravity to assist arterial blood flow down to the legs, thereby relieving ischemic rest pain. Elevating the legs or wearing tight compression stockings would further restrict arterial blood flow and worsen the pain. Massaging the calves is contraindicated as it does not improve arterial supply and could dislodge a potential undetected thrombus.
</details>

---

### Question 4
A nurse at a community health fair checks the blood pressure of a 45-year-old client. The reading is 162/98 mmHg. The client seems surprised and says, "I feel completely fine, so I must not have high blood pressure." Which is the best response by the nurse?

A) "You probably have a severe headache that you just haven't noticed yet."
B) "We need to call an ambulance immediately because you are at risk for a stroke."
C) "Let me check your temperature, as a fever is the most common cause of high blood pressure."
D) "High blood pressure often causes damage without showing any outward symptoms."

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D) "High blood pressure often causes damage without showing any outward symptoms."

<strong>Rationale:</strong> Essential hypertension is frequently referred to as the "silent killer" because individuals usually remain completely asymptomatic until significant target-organ damage has occurred. The nurse's best action is to educate the client that a lack of symptoms does not mean the blood pressure is normal. The client does not necessarily need an immediate ambulance for this reading without other acute symptoms, and fever is not the primary cause of hypertension.
</details>

---

### Question 5
A nurse in the emergency department is applying continuous cardiac monitoring electrodes to a 50-year-old client reporting sudden heart palpitations. The client appears anxious and asks, "Will these wires shock me if my heart stops?" What is the most appropriate response by the nurse?

A) "No, this equipment only reads and records your heart's electrical activity."
B) "Yes, the monitor will automatically deliver a small shock to save your life."
C) "The machine will only shock you if the doctor presses a button at the desk."
D) "It will only deliver a shock if your heart rate goes above 150 beats per minute."

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A) "No, this equipment only reads and records your heart's electrical activity."

<strong>Rationale:</strong> Standard continuous cardiac monitors (telemetry or ECG monitors) are purely diagnostic tools. They observe, transmit, and record the electrical activity of the heart so the healthcare team can assess the rhythm. They do not have the capability to deliver therapeutic shocks (defibrillation or cardioversion) to the client. Explaining this simple fact alleviates the client's anxiety.
</details>
### Question 6
A home health nurse is assessing a 72-year-old client who has a history of right-sided heart failure. During the physical examination, which clinical finding should the nurse expect to observe?

A) Crackles auscultated in the lung bases
B) Distention of the jugular veins
C) A sudden onset of severe chest pain
D) Bright red flushing of the face

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B) Distention of the jugular veins<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Right-sided heart failure causes systemic venous congestion because the right ventricle cannot effectively pump blood forward into the lungs. This back-up of fluid leads to clinical signs such as jugular venous distention (JVD), peripheral edema, and hepatomegaly. Crackles in the lungs are indicative of left-sided heart failure due to pulmonary congestion. Chest pain and facial flushing are not expected findings of right-sided heart failure.
</details>

---

### Question 7
A clinic nurse is providing discharge instructions to a 45-year-old client who has just been prescribed a new antihypertensive medication. Which instruction should the nurse emphasize to promote client safety?

A) "Change positions slowly when moving from lying down to standing up."
B) "Take this medication with a full glass of milk to prevent stomach upset."
C) "Check your radial pulse for one full minute before taking the pill."
D) "Stop taking the medication immediately if you feel slightly dizzy."

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A) "Change positions slowly when moving from lying down to standing up."<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Antihypertensive medications often cause orthostatic hypotension, a sudden drop in blood pressure when changing positions, which can lead to dizziness and falls. Changing positions slowly allows the cardiovascular system time to adjust. Taking the medication with milk is not universally required for blood pressure drugs, pulse checking is specific to certain medications like digoxin or beta-blockers rather than all antihypertensives, and clients should never abruptly stop these medications without consulting their healthcare provider.
</details>

---

### Question 8
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is evaluating an 80-year-old client admitted with severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD) of the lower extremities. When assessing the client's legs, which finding should the nurse anticipate?

A) Warm, reddened skin on the calves
B) Significant pitting edema around the ankles
C) Diminished or absent pedal pulses
D) Bounding pulses in the feet

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C) Diminished or absent pedal pulses<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Peripheral arterial disease (PAD) is characterized by reduced arterial blood flow to the extremities due to narrowed or blocked arteries. As a result of this decreased perfusion, the nurse should expect to find diminished or absent pedal pulses, cool skin temperature, and pallor or cyanosis. Warm, reddened skin and bounding pulses indicate increased blood flow, while pitting edema is more commonly associated with venous insufficiency or heart failure.
</details>

---

### Question 9
A triage nurse in the emergency department is caring for a 60-year-old client experiencing an episode of stable angina. The nurse administers a prescribed dose of sublingual nitroglycerin. Which assessment is the priority for the nurse to perform immediately after administration?

A) Monitoring the client's oral temperature
B) Checking the client's pupil reactivity to light
C) Assessing the client's capillary blood glucose
D) Measuring the client's blood pressure

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D) Measuring the client's blood pressure<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Nitroglycerin is a potent systemic vasodilator used to relieve angina by reducing cardiac workload and improving coronary blood flow. Because it dilates blood vessels systemically, it commonly causes a rapid and significant drop in blood pressure. The nurse's priority is to monitor the client's blood pressure closely to ensure they do not become severely hypotensive. Temperature, pupil reactivity, and blood glucose are not primarily affected by nitroglycerin administration.
</details>

---

### Question 10
A post-anesthesia nurse is caring for a 28-year-old client who recently underwent an exploratory laparotomy. To reduce the client's risk of developing a deep vein thrombosis (DVT) in the postoperative period, which action should the nurse take?

A) Place several pillows directly under the client's knees.
B) Encourage early and frequent ambulation in the hallway.
C) Vigorously massage the client's calf muscles every 2 hours.
D) Restrict the client's oral fluid intake to prevent fluid overload.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B) Encourage early and frequent ambulation in the hallway.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Venous stasis is a major risk factor for the development of deep vein thrombosis (DVT) in postoperative clients. Early and frequent ambulation promotes venous return from the lower extremities, effectively reducing this risk. Placing pillows directly under the knees can compress the popliteal vein and actually promote venous stasis. Massaging the calves is contraindicated because it could dislodge an existing, undetected clot, causing a pulmonary embolism. Adequate hydration is encouraged to prevent blood hypercoagulability, not restricted.
</details>
### Question 11
A nurse is measuring the blood pressure of a 45-year-old client during a routine visit at an outpatient clinic. The reading is 158/96 mm Hg. The client seems surprised and says, "I feel totally fine, so my heart must be doing okay." Which of the following is the best response by the nurse?

A) "You are likely feeling well right now because your body has fully compensated for the high blood pressure."
B) "We should recheck it immediately on the other arm; the machine must be malfunctioning."
C) "High blood pressure often has no symptoms but can still cause long-term damage to your body."
D) "Since you feel fine today, we will just monitor your blood pressure again at your next annual visit."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br><br>
Hypertension is frequently referred to as the "silent killer" because clients often remain completely asymptomatic even while the elevated pressure causes progressive damage to organs such as the heart, kidneys, and eyes. Answering with an explanation of this concept provides necessary client education. The nurse should not ignore the reading (Option D) or assume the machine is broken without reason (Option B). 
</details>

### Question 12
A nurse in a long-term care facility is assessing an 82-year-old resident with a history of severe right-sided heart failure. Based on this specific diagnosis, which clinical finding should the nurse expect to observe during the physical assessment?

A) Swelling and pitting edema in the feet and ankles
B) Production of pink, frothy sputum
C) Fine crackles auscultated in the bases of the lungs
D) A dry, hacking cough that worsens at night

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<br><br>
Right-sided heart failure causes systemic venous congestion. Because the right ventricle cannot pump blood forward effectively into the lungs, fluid backs up into the systemic circulation, leading to peripheral edema (such as swelling in the feet and ankles), ascites, and jugular venous distention. Options B, C, and D are classic signs of left-sided heart failure, which results in pulmonary congestion.
</details>

---

### Question 13
A 68-year-old client arrives at the emergency department complaining of sudden chest tightness. The healthcare provider prescribes sublingual nitroglycerin for suspected angina. What instruction must the nurse provide to the client when administering this medication?

A) "Chew the tablet thoroughly before swallowing to make it work faster."
B) "Take a large sip of water to help wash the pill down into your stomach."
C) "Place the tablet on top of your tongue and let it sit there for 15 minutes."
D) "Allow the tablet to dissolve completely under your tongue without swallowing it."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<br><br>
Sublingual medications, like nitroglycerin, are designed to be absorbed directly through the highly vascular mucous membranes under the tongue. Swallowing or chewing the tablet (Options A and B) directs it to the gastrointestinal tract, where it will be destroyed by the liver (first-pass effect) and become ineffective. Placing it on top of the tongue (Option C) does not allow for optimal absorption.
</details>

---

### Question 14
A nurse is caring for a 29-year-old client who is two days postoperative from abdominal surgery. The client reports sudden, intense pain and cramping in the right calf. Upon inspection, the nurse notes the right calf is significantly red, warm, and swollen compared to the left. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Vigorously massage the client's calf to relieve the muscle cramping.
B) Instruct the client to remain in bed and notify the healthcare provider.
C) Apply a warm, moist compress to the leg and elevate it on two pillows.
D) Assist the client to ambulate in the hallway to improve blood circulation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<br><br>
The client is exhibiting classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The immediate priority is to prevent the clot from dislodging and traveling to the lungs (pulmonary embolism). The nurse should keep the client on strict bed rest and notify the provider immediately. Massaging the leg (Option A) or ambulating (Option D) can dislodge the clot and are strongly contraindicated. 
</details>

---

### Question 15
A home health nurse is evaluating a 74-year-old client who takes daily oral digoxin for atrial fibrillation. The client complains of recent extreme nausea, a total loss of appetite, and states, "The lights in the living room look like they have yellow halos around them." What is the priority action by the nurse?

A) Document the findings as expected, mild side effects of the medication.
B) Advise the client to take the medication with a full glass of milk to prevent nausea.
C) Contact the healthcare provider immediately to report suspected medication toxicity.
D) Instruct the client to take an extra dose of digoxin to better regulate the heart rate.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br><br>
Anorexia, nausea, vomiting, and visual disturbances (specifically seeing yellow or green halos around lights) are hallmark signs of digoxin toxicity. Digoxin has a narrow therapeutic index, meaning the difference between a safe dose and a toxic dose is very small. The nurse must immediately report these findings to the provider so a serum digoxin level can be drawn and the dose adjusted. Giving an extra dose (Option D) could be fatal.
</details>
### Question 16
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is evaluating a 55-year-old client recently diagnosed with primary hypertension. The client has been prescribed a daily thiazide diuretic and given lifestyle modification advice. Which statement by the client indicates a need for further teaching by the nurse?

A. "I will take my medication every morning with breakfast."
B. "I should stand up slowly when getting out of bed."
C. "I will start walking for 30 minutes most days of the week."
D. "I can stop taking this pill once my blood pressure returns to a normal level."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Hypertension is a chronic condition that typically requires lifelong medical management. The nurse must teach the client that medications should not be stopped abruptly even if blood pressure readings normalize, as doing so can cause rebound hypertension. Taking the diuretic in the morning prevents nocturia (waking up to urinate), standing slowly prevents orthostatic hypotension, and regular exercise is a recommended lifestyle modification. 
</details>

---

### Question 17
A nurse on a post-operative unit is caring for a 72-year-old client who had a total hip replacement yesterday. To prevent the development of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT), which nursing intervention is the most appropriate to implement?

A. Assisting the client to ambulate in the hallway as prescribed.
B. Placing pillows directly under the client's knees to provide comfort.
C. Massaging the client's calves gently with warm lotion to improve circulation.
D. Encouraging the client to limit oral fluid intake to prevent fluid overload.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Early ambulation is one of the most effective nursing interventions to prevent venous stasis and the formation of a DVT in post-operative clients. Placing pillows under the knees is contraindicated because it restricts venous return. Massaging the calves is dangerous because it can dislodge an existing clot, causing a pulmonary embolism. Limiting fluids can lead to dehydration, which increases blood viscosity and the risk of clot formation.
</details>

---

### Question 18
A home health nurse is conducting an initial assessment on a 68-year-old client with a history of right-sided heart failure. Based on this specific diagnosis, which clinical finding should the nurse expect to observe during the physical examination?

A. Crackles auscultated in the lung bases
B. Noticeable swelling in the feet and ankles
C. A dry, persistent, hacking cough
D. Reports of waking up gasping for air at night

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Right-sided heart failure involves the right ventricle's inability to pump blood effectively into the lungs, causing a backup of blood into the systemic venous system. The nurse would observe dependent edema (swelling in the feet, ankles, and legs), jugular vein distention, and abdominal distention (ascites). Options A, C, and D are classic signs of left-sided heart failure, which involves fluid backing up into the pulmonary system.
</details>

---

### Question 19
A 60-year-old client arrives at the emergency department reporting chest pain that started while shoveling snow. The nurse administers a prescribed dose of sublingual nitroglycerin. Five minutes later, the client reports the pain has decreased from an 8 to a 4 on a 0-10 scale. What is the nurse's immediate next action?

A. Document the client's pain level and reassess in 15 minutes.
B. Instruct the client to chew an enteric-coated aspirin.
C. Administer a second dose of sublingual nitroglycerin.
D. Withhold further medication until an electrocardiogram (ECG) is completed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
When treating angina, if chest pain is not completely relieved 5 minutes after the first dose of sublingual nitroglycerin, the nurse should administer a second dose. The standard protocol allows for up to three doses spaced 5 minutes apart. Waiting 15 minutes delays critical treatment, and while an ECG and aspirin are part of chest pain protocols, the immediate nursing action for persistent pain at the 5-minute mark is the next dose of nitroglycerin.
</details>

---

### Question 20
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 78-year-old client admitted with severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client complains of aching, burning pain in the lower legs while resting in bed. Which action should the nurse take to help relieve the client's pain?

A. Apply bilateral compression stockings tightly over the calves.
B. Elevate the client's legs on two pillows above the level of the heart.
C. Apply a hot heating pad directly to the client's lower legs.
D. Have the client dangle their legs over the side of the bed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), blood flow to the lower extremities is compromised. Dangling the legs over the side of the bed allows gravity to assist arterial blood flow to the distal tissues, relieving ischemic rest pain. Elevating the legs or applying tight compression stockings would further decrease arterial blood flow and worsen the pain. Applying a heating pad directly to the skin is contraindicated because clients with PAD often have decreased sensation and are at high risk for burns.
</details>
### Question 21
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is preparing to measure the blood pressure of a 45-year-old client who just rushed in from the parking lot and appears slightly out of breath. Which action is the most appropriate for the nurse to take first?

A) Measure the blood pressure immediately while the client is still standing.
B) Have the client rest quietly in a chair for 5 minutes before taking the reading.
C) Use a blood pressure cuff that covers 100% of the client's upper arm length.
D) Ask the client to hold their breath while the blood pressure is being measured.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
To obtain an accurate resting blood pressure, the nurse should ensure the client has rested quietly in a seated position for at least 5 minutes before the measurement. Rushing or recent exertion can falsely elevate blood pressure readings. Standing immediately to take the blood pressure is incorrect, as seated is the standard for routine screening. A cuff covering 100% of the arm is too large and will give a falsely low reading (the bladder should encircle 80% of the arm). Holding the breath can alter intrathoracic pressure and affect the accuracy of the reading.
</details>

---

### Question 22
An 82-year-old client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an exacerbation of right-sided heart failure. When assessing the client, which clinical manifestation should the nurse anticipate observing?

A) Pink, frothy sputum
B) Crackles auscultated in the lung bases
C) Pitting edema in the lower extremities
D) Orthopnea when lying flat in bed

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Right-sided heart failure causes systemic venous congestion, leading to fluid accumulation in the dependent areas of the body, such as pitting edema in the lower extremities, jugular venous distention, and hepatomegaly. Pink, frothy sputum, crackles in the lungs, and orthopnea are all signs of pulmonary congestion, which is characteristic of left-sided heart failure. 
</details>

---

### Question 23
A nurse on a telemetry unit is caring for a 55-year-old client who has a prescription for sublingual nitroglycerin PRN for chest pain. The client reports a chest pain level of 6 out of 10 and takes one dose. Five minutes later, the client complains of a sudden, pounding headache. Which action should the nurse take?

A) Notify the healthcare provider immediately for a suspected hemorrhagic stroke.
B) Instruct the client to spit out any remaining medication immediately.
C) Administer an immediate dose of intravenous naloxone.
D) Reassure the client that a headache is an expected side effect of the medication.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator. While it dilates coronary arteries to relieve angina, it also dilates cerebral blood vessels, frequently causing a pounding headache. This is a common and expected side effect, so the nurse should reassure the client and may offer a mild analgesic like acetaminophen if prescribed. It does not indicate a stroke, and naloxone is an opioid antagonist, which is irrelevant to nitroglycerin administration. 
</details>

---

### Question 24
A 28-year-old client presents to the emergency department reporting acute right calf pain after a 12-hour international flight. Upon physical assessment, the nurse notes the right calf is red, warm, and swollen. Which nursing action is the safest and most appropriate intervention at this time?

A) Elevate the affected leg on a pillow.
B) Vigorously massage the right calf to improve circulation.
C) Apply an ice pack directly to the reddened area.
D) Assist the client to ambulate in the hallway to relieve the stiffness.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
The client is exhibiting classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). Elevating the affected extremity promotes venous return and helps decrease swelling and pain. Massaging the calf is strictly contraindicated because it can dislodge the clot, leading to a life-threatening pulmonary embolism. Warm, moist compresses are generally preferred over ice packs to soothe the area and promote circulation without causing severe vasoconstriction. Ambulation is typically delayed until the client is properly anticoagulated to prevent clot dislodgment.
</details>

---

### Question 25
A pediatric nurse is caring for a 2-year-old child diagnosed with Tetralogy of Fallot. While drawing blood for laboratory tests, the child becomes extremely agitated, turns deep blue around the lips, and begins to breathe rapidly. What should the nurse do first?

A) Administer a rapid intravenous fluid bolus.
B) Initiate cardiopulmonary resuscitation (CPR).
C) Place the child in a knee-to-chest position.
D) Prepare to administer a dose of epinephrine.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
The child is experiencing a hypercyanotic episode, commonly known as a "tet spell," which is triggered by stress or crying. Placing the child in a knee-to-chest position increases systemic vascular resistance, which decreases the right-to-left shunting of deoxygenated blood and improves oxygenation to the lungs. CPR is not indicated unless the child loses a pulse or stops breathing. IV fluids and medications may be ordered later, but positioning is always the immediate, non-invasive first nursing action.
</details>
### Question 26
A nurse on a surgical unit is caring for a 62-year-old client who is 2 days post-operative from a right knee replacement. The client suddenly reports a new, cramping pain in the right calf. Upon assessment, the nurse observes the area is swollen, red, and warm to the touch. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Massage the right calf gently to relieve the muscle cramping.
B) Assist the client to walk the hallway to improve blood circulation.
C) Keep the client on bed rest and immediately notify the healthcare provider.
D) Apply a hot, moist compress directly to the swollen area.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The client is exhibiting classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT), a common complication following orthopedic surgery. The priority nursing action is to keep the client on bed rest to prevent the potential clot from dislodging and causing a pulmonary embolism, and to notify the provider immediately for diagnostic testing and intervention. Massaging the leg, ambulating, or applying heat can dislodge the clot.
</details>
