### Question 27
A home health nurse is conducting a routine visit for a 78-year-old client with a history of left-sided heart failure. During the assessment, which observation by the nurse requires the most immediate intervention?

A) The client's resting blood pressure is 128/84 mmHg.
B) The client's daily weight log shows a 4-pound weight gain since yesterday.
C) The client reports urinating more frequently during the daytime.
D) The client sleeps with two pillows at night for comfort.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
A sudden weight gain of 2 to 3 pounds in one day, or 5 pounds in a week, is a primary indicator of fluid retention and worsening heart failure. This observation requires immediate intervention, such as notifying the provider for a possible diuretic adjustment. A BP of 128/84 is acceptable, daytime urination is normal (especially if taking morning diuretics), and sleeping with two pillows is a common, chronic compensation for mild orthopnea that does not necessarily indicate an acute crisis like rapid weight gain does.
</details>

---

### Question 28
An occupational health nurse is evaluating a 55-year-old client who recently started taking a newly prescribed antihypertensive medication. The client states, "Whenever I get up from my desk too quickly, the room spins and I feel dizzy." What is the most appropriate action for the nurse to take?

A) Instruct the client to change positions slowly and hold onto a stable surface when standing.
B) Advise the client to stop taking the blood pressure medication immediately.
C) Provide the client with a large cup of coffee to increase their heart rate.
D) Perform a rapid, vigorous massage of the client's lower extremities.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The client is experiencing symptoms of orthostatic hypotension, a very common side effect of antihypertensive medications. The most appropriate and safe nursing action is to teach the client to change positions slowly, such as sitting on the edge of the chair or bed before standing, to allow their blood pressure to adjust. Nurses should never advise a client to abruptly stop prescribed cardiac medications without a provider's order.
</details>

---

### Question 29
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is assessing a 70-year-old client admitted with chronic peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The nurse observes that the client's lower legs are shiny, hairless, and cool to the touch. What is the nurse's best action?

A) Elevate the client's legs on three pillows high above the level of the heart.
B) Apply a heating pad directly to the client's calves and toes to warm them.
C) Wrap the client's legs tightly with elastic compression bandages.
D) Document the expected findings and palpate the bilateral pedal pulses.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Shiny, hairless skin and cool extremities are expected, chronic findings in a client with peripheral arterial disease (PAD) due to decreased arterial blood flow. The nurse should document these baseline findings and assess the pedal pulses to ensure adequate circulation remains. Elevating the legs above the heart decreases arterial flow and worsens PAD pain. Heating pads are contraindicated due to the risk of burns in clients with decreased sensation, and tight compression stockings can further restrict arterial blood flow.
</details>

---

### Question 30
A triage nurse in the emergency department is caring for a 48-year-old client experiencing chest pain. Five minutes after the nurse administers a prescribed dose of sublingual nitroglycerin, the client states, "My chest pain is completely gone, but now I have a pounding headache." Which action should the nurse take?

A) Immediately administer a second dose of sublingual nitroglycerin.
B) Document a severe allergic reaction and update the client's allergy band.
C) Explain to the client that this is a common and expected effect of the medication.
D) Prepare to administer an emergency dose of intravenous epinephrine.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator used to relieve angina. Because it dilates cerebral blood vessels as well as coronary arteries, a headache is a very common and expected side effect. The nurse should reassure the client that this is normal and expected, and can offer a mild analgesic like acetaminophen if prescribed. It is not an allergic reaction, and since the chest pain is resolved, a second dose of nitroglycerin is not indicated.
</details>
### Question 31
A nurse is caring for a 68-year-old male patient on a medical-surgical unit who was recently admitted with a confirmed deep vein thrombosis (DVT) in his right calf. The patient complains of a dull ache and swelling in his lower leg. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate?

A) Massage the right calf gently to relieve the aching pain.
B) Apply a cold pack directly to the right calf for 20 minutes.
C) Elevate the patient's right leg on a pillow while he is resting in bed.
D) Encourage the patient to perform vigorous range-of-motion exercises with the right leg.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Elevating the affected extremity promotes venous return and helps decrease swelling and pain associated with a DVT. The nurse should never massage the affected leg (Option A), as this can dislodge the thrombus and cause a pulmonary embolism. Warm, moist compresses—not cold packs (Option B)—are sometimes prescribed to relieve symptoms. Vigorous range-of-motion exercises (Option D) are contraindicated during the acute phase of a DVT due to the risk of clot dislodgement.
</details>

---

### Question 32
A home health nurse is teaching a 75-year-old female client with a history of chronic heart failure about monitoring her fluid status at home. Which instruction should the nurse emphasize as the priority action for the client to perform?

A) "Weigh yourself every morning after using the bathroom and before eating."
B) "Check your blood pressure each time you feel dizzy or lightheaded."
C) "Drink at least three liters of water daily to help flush out your kidneys."
D) "Measure the circumference of your ankles with a tape measure before going to bed."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Daily weights are the most reliable and straightforward indicator of fluid volume status and fluid retention in clients with heart failure. The client should be taught to weigh themselves at the same time each day, on the same scale, with the same amount of clothing. While blood pressure monitoring (Option B) is important, daily weights directly track fluid accumulation. Heart failure clients are often on fluid restrictions, so drinking three liters of water (Option C) is contraindicated. Measuring ankle circumference (Option D) is not as accurate or reliable as daily body weight for assessing overall fluid status.
</details>

---

### Question 33
A nurse in a primary care clinic is preparing to measure the blood pressure of a 45-year-old male client who is being evaluated for new-onset hypertension. To ensure an accurate reading, which action should the nurse take?

A) Use a blood pressure cuff that covers at least 80% of the length of the client's upper arm.
B) Ensure the client has been resting in a comfortable, seated position for at least 5 minutes.
C) Place the blood pressure cuff directly over the client's thin, long-sleeved shirt.
D) Ask the client to let his arm hang loosely and unsupported by his side during the measurement.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
To obtain an accurate blood pressure reading, the client should rest quietly in a seated position for at least 5 minutes before the measurement is taken. The cuff bladder width should equal about 40% of the arm circumference, and the length should wrap around at least 80% of the arm, but stating it should cover 80% of the arm's *length* (Option A) is incorrect. Blood pressure should be measured on a bare arm, not over clothing (Option C). The arm should be supported at heart level, not hanging loosely at the side (Option D), which can artificially elevate the reading.
</details>

---

### Question 34
A 55-year-old female presents to the emergency department complaining of sudden, crushing chest pain. The provider prescribes sublingual nitroglycerin. What is the priority nursing action immediately after administering the first dose?

A) Assess the patient's blood pressure and current pain level.
B) Encourage the patient to chew the tablet completely to speed up absorption.
C) Administer a second dose if the chest pain is not relieved within 1 minute.
D) Instruct the patient to drink a full glass of water to wash the medication down.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator that can cause significant hypotension. The priority nursing action after administration is to monitor the patient's blood pressure and assess if the chest pain has decreased. Sublingual tablets must be placed under the tongue to dissolve; they should never be chewed (Option B) or swallowed with water (Option D), as this bypasses the rapid sublingual absorption route. If pain is not relieved, the nurse should wait 5 minutes—not 1 minute (Option C)—before administering a second dose, up to a maximum of three doses.
</details>

---

### Question 35
A nurse in a long-term care facility is conducting a morning assessment on an 82-year-old male resident with advanced peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The resident complains of severe pain in his toes that woke him up during the night. Which action should the nurse take to help relieve the client's pain?

A) Elevate the resident's legs above the level of his heart on two pillows.
B) Apply tight compression stockings to both lower extremities to improve flow.
C) Place a heating pad on the high setting directly over the resident's feet.
D) Assist the resident to dangle his legs over the side of the bed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Ischemic rest pain in peripheral arterial disease (PAD) occurs because of poor arterial blood flow to the extremities. Dangling the legs over the side of the bed places them in a dependent position, allowing gravity to assist arterial blood flow to the feet, thereby relieving the pain. Elevating the legs (Option A) is contraindicated in PAD because it further reduces arterial blood flow and worsens ischemia. Compression stockings (Option B) restrict arterial flow and are generally contraindicated in severe PAD. Applying a heating pad directly to the feet (Option C) is dangerous because patients with PAD often have decreased sensation, putting them at high risk for severe burns.
</details>
### Question 36
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 65-year-old client who reports sudden, severe calf pain and swelling in the right leg. Which action should the nurse take immediately?

A) Vigorously massage the calf to relieve the client's pain.
B) Keep the client in bed and notify the healthcare provider.
C) Apply a warm, moist compress and encourage ambulation.
D) Place sequential compression devices (SCDs) on both legs.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
The client is exhibiting classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The immediate nursing priority is to maintain bed rest and notify the healthcare provider to prevent the dislodgment of the clot. Massaging the leg (Option A), ambulating the client (Option C), or applying SCDs to the affected leg (Option D) are strictly contraindicated because these actions can dislodge the thrombus, potentially causing a life-threatening pulmonary embolism.
</details>

---

### Question 37
A 72-year-old client with a history of right-sided heart failure visits the outpatient clinic. The nurse is evaluating the client's understanding of home care management. Which statement by the client indicates that teaching has been effective?

A) "I will weigh myself once a week on Sunday mornings."
B) "I should drink at least three liters of water every day."
C) "I will stop taking my water pill if I need to use the bathroom too often."
D) "I will call the clinic if I gain more than 2 pounds in a single day."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Rapid weight gain is an early and highly reliable indicator of fluid volume overload in clients with heart failure. Clients should be taught to notify their provider if they gain 2 to 3 pounds in a day or 5 pounds in a week. Clients should weigh themselves daily, not weekly (Option A). Fluid restrictions, not increased fluid intake, are typically required for heart failure clients (Option B). A diuretic should never be stopped abruptly without consulting a healthcare provider, even if frequent urination is bothersome (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 38
A nurse at a community health center is preparing to measure the blood pressure of a 45-year-old client who has a very large arm circumference. How should the nurse proceed to ensure an accurate reading?

A) Select a blood pressure cuff where the bladder encircles at least 80% of the client's arm.
B) Use a standard adult cuff and inflate it 50 mm Hg above the expected systolic pressure.
C) Position the client's arm so it rests unsupported and comfortably below the level of the heart.
D) Have the client cross their legs to stabilize their posture before taking the reading.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
To obtain an accurate blood pressure measurement, the nurse must use an appropriately sized cuff. The bladder of the cuff should encircle about 80% of the arm. Using a standard cuff on a large arm will result in a falsely high reading (Option B). The client's arm should be supported at heart level; an unsupported arm below the heart will yield a falsely high reading (Option C). Crossing the legs can also artificially elevate blood pressure readings and should be avoided (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 39
A 58-year-old client with stable angina is being discharged from the emergency department with a new prescription for sublingual nitroglycerin. Which instruction should the nurse include when teaching the client how to use this medication during an episode of chest pain?

A) "Swallow the pill whole with a full glass of water."
B) "Take one pill every 10 minutes until your chest pain disappears."
C) "Place the tablet under your tongue and let it dissolve completely."
D) "Chew the medication thoroughly before swallowing it."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Sublingual nitroglycerin is designed to be absorbed rapidly through the mucous membranes under the tongue to provide quick relief for angina. It must be allowed to dissolve completely. Swallowing the pill whole (Option A) or chewing it (Option D) bypasses this rapid absorption route and renders the medication ineffective due to the first-pass effect in the liver. The standard protocol for nitroglycerin is to take one tablet every 5 minutes (not 10 minutes) for up to 3 doses if pain persists (Option B).
</details>

---

### Question 40
A nurse in a long-term care facility is assessing an 81-year-old client diagnosed with peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client complains of severe aching pain in the lower legs that awakens them at night while resting in bed. Which immediate intervention should the nurse implement to help relieve the client's pain?

A) Elevate the client's legs on two pillows.
B) Assist the client to dangle their legs over the side of the bed.
C) Apply tight, thigh-high compression stockings.
D) Apply a cold ice pack to the affected lower extremities.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Clients with peripheral arterial disease (PAD) experience inadequate arterial blood flow to the lower extremities. "Rest pain" often occurs at night when the legs are horizontal. Dangling the legs over the side of the bed allows gravity to assist arterial blood flow down to the distal tissues, relieving the ischemic pain. Elevating the legs (Option A) makes it harder for arterial blood to fight gravity, worsening the pain. Compression stockings (Option C) further restrict arterial circulation. Applying ice (Option D) causes vasoconstriction, which will severely exacerbate ischemia and pain.
</details>
### Question 41
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is preparing to measure the blood pressure of a 45-year-old client who has just been diagnosed with primary hypertension. Which action by the nurse is essential to ensure an accurate reading?

A) Ask the client to sit with both feet flat on the floor.
B) Position the client's arm comfortably below heart level.
C) Deflate the cuff rapidly to prevent patient discomfort.
D) Apply a cuff that overlaps the upper arm completely.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Ask the client to sit with both feet flat on the floor.

**Rationale:** To obtain an accurate blood pressure reading, the client should be seated comfortably with both feet flat on the floor and legs uncrossed. Crossing the legs can falsely elevate the blood pressure reading. The arm should be supported at heart level (not below it), the cuff size should be appropriate (not overlapping completely, which indicates it is too large), and the cuff should be deflated slowly and steadily, not rapidly. 
</details>

---

### Question 42
A 68-year-old client is recovering on the surgical unit on the second day following a total hip replacement. While assisting the client with a bed bath, the nurse notices the client's left calf is red, warm to the touch, and visibly larger than the right calf. What is the nurse's best initial action?

A) Elevate the left leg on two pillows and apply a warm compress.
B) Instruct the client to perform vigorous ankle pumps.
C) Notify the healthcare provider of the findings immediately.
D) Massage the left calf gently to promote venous return.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Notify the healthcare provider of the findings immediately.

**Rationale:** Unilateral redness, warmth, and swelling in the calf are classic clinical signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The nurse should immediately notify the healthcare provider so diagnostic testing (such as a venous Doppler) and treatment can be initiated. Massaging the leg is strictly contraindicated as it may dislodge the clot, leading to a pulmonary embolism. Elevating the leg and applying warm compresses require a provider's order, and vigorous movement should be avoided until a clot is ruled out.
</details>

---

### Question 43
A nurse on a telemetry unit is providing discharge instructions to a 72-year-old client who was admitted with an exacerbation of left-sided heart failure. The nurse is evaluating the client's understanding of home fluid management. Which statement by the client indicates a need for further teaching?

A) "I will weigh myself every morning after I urinate."
B) "I should drink at least 3 liters of water every day to flush my kidneys."
C) "I will call my doctor if I gain more than 2 pounds in one day."
D) "I need to check the sodium content on the nutrition labels of my food."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "I should drink at least 3 liters of water every day to flush my kidneys."

**Rationale:** This statement requires further teaching because clients with heart failure are typically placed on fluid restrictions to prevent fluid overload, which exacerbates the condition. Drinking 3 liters of water daily is contraindicated for this client. The other statements show a correct understanding of heart failure management: daily morning weights, monitoring for sudden weight gain (a key indicator of fluid retention), and limiting sodium intake.
</details>

---

### Question 44
A 55-year-old client arrives at an urgent care center reporting mild chest tightness. The provider prescribes sublingual nitroglycerin. As the nurse prepares to administer the medication, which instruction should be provided to the client?

A) "Chew the tablet thoroughly before swallowing."
B) "Rinse your mouth with water immediately after taking the pill."
C) "Stand up slowly right after taking the medication to avoid dizziness."
D) "Keep the tablet under your tongue until it completely dissolves."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) "Keep the tablet under your tongue until it completely dissolves."

**Rationale:** Sublingual nitroglycerin must be placed under the tongue and allowed to dissolve completely so it can be rapidly absorbed through the oral mucosa into the bloodstream. It should not be chewed or swallowed, and rinsing the mouth would wash the medication away before it can absorb. Nitroglycerin causes rapid vasodilation, which can lead to orthostatic hypotension; therefore, the client should remain seated or lying down rather than standing up.
</details>

---

### Question 45
A nurse at a community health fair is assessing an 80-year-old client who has a history of type 2 diabetes and reports a dull, cramping pain in the calves when walking to the mailbox. The nurse suspects peripheral arterial disease (PAD). Which assessment technique should the nurse perform first to evaluate the client's lower extremity circulation?

A) Measure the circumference of both calves.
B) Palpate the dorsalis pedis and posterior tibial pulses.
C) Ask the client to perform a deep knee bend.
D) Elevate the client's legs to observe for dependent edema.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Palpate the dorsalis pedis and posterior tibial pulses.

**Rationale:** The client's symptom of pain during exercise that is typical of peripheral arterial disease is known as intermittent claudication. Palpating the peripheral pulses (dorsalis pedis and posterior tibial) is a primary and essential nursing assessment to evaluate arterial blood flow to the lower extremities. Measuring calf circumference is a technique used when checking for DVT. Leg elevation in PAD causes pallor, while dependent positioning causes rubor; however, looking for edema is more associated with venous insufficiency or heart failure. Deep knee bends are not a standard cardiovascular assessment.
</details>
### Question 46
A 60-year-old patient on the medical-surgical unit is diagnosed with a deep vein thrombosis (DVT) in the right calf. The nurse enters the room and observes the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) providing care. Which action by the UAP requires immediate intervention by the nurse?

A. Elevating the patient's right leg on a pillow.  
B. Assisting the patient with putting on anti-embolism stockings.  
C. Massaging the patient's right calf to relieve muscle cramping.  
D. Encouraging the patient to drink plenty of oral fluids.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Massaging a calf with a known deep vein thrombosis (DVT) is strictly contraindicated because it can dislodge the clot, potentially causing a life-threatening pulmonary embolism. The nurse must intervene immediately to stop the UAP. Elevating the leg, applying prescribed anti-embolism stockings, and encouraging hydration are all appropriate interventions for a patient with a DVT.
</details>

---

### Question 47
A home health nurse is evaluating a 78-year-old patient with a history of chronic right-sided heart failure. The nurse is assessing the patient's understanding of how to monitor for fluid volume overload at home. Which statement by the patient indicates that the teaching was successful?

A. "I will weigh myself every morning after I urinate and before I eat breakfast."  
B. "I should weigh myself right before I go to bed each night."  
C. "I only need to check my weight on the days my shoes feel tight."  
D. "I will weigh myself each day wearing my heavy winter coat and boots to be safe."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The most reliable indicator of fluid gain or loss in a patient with heart failure is daily weights. To ensure accuracy, patients should weigh themselves at the same time every day, on the same scale, wearing the same amount of clothing. The best time is in the morning, after voiding and before eating or drinking. Waiting until symptoms appear (tight shoes) or weighing at night/with heavy clothes leads to inaccurate tracking of fluid retention.
</details>

---

### Question 48
A 65-year-old patient arrives at the emergency department experiencing chest pain and receives a dose of sublingual nitroglycerin. Five minutes later, the patient reports a sudden, throbbing headache. What is the most appropriate action by the nurse?

A. Immediately notify the healthcare provider of a severe allergic reaction.  
B. Prepare to administer a reversal agent for the medication.  
C. Withhold any further doses of nitroglycerin permanently, regardless of chest pain.  
D. Assess the patient's vital signs and explain that this is a common side effect.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator used to relieve angina. Because it dilates cerebral blood vessels as well as coronary arteries, a throbbing headache is a very common and expected side effect. It does not indicate an allergic reaction. The nurse should assess the patient's vital signs (specifically looking for hypotension, another effect of vasodilation) and reassure the patient, often offering a mild analgesic like acetaminophen if prescribed.
</details>

---

### Question 49
A 45-year-old client visits an outpatient clinic for a routine check-up. The nurse obtains an initial blood pressure reading of 158/94 mmHg. The client appears flustered and states, "I was running late to get here and drank a giant iced coffee on the drive over." Which action should the nurse take next?

A. Administer a PRN rapid-acting antihypertensive medication immediately.  
B. Have the client rest quietly for several minutes and retake the blood pressure.  
C. Document the finding as the client's new baseline blood pressure.  
D. Transfer the client to the emergency department for a hypertensive crisis.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Recent physical activity (rushing) and caffeine intake can temporarily elevate blood pressure readings. The nurse should allow the client to sit and rest quietly for at least 5 minutes before taking another reading to get an accurate baseline measurement. One elevated reading, especially with known temporary triggers, does not warrant immediate medication or a transfer to the emergency department.
</details>

---

### Question 50
A nurse is caring for an 82-year-old resident in a long-term care facility who has severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The resident rings the call bell at night and complains of a severe burning pain in the lower legs while lying flat in bed. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate to relieve the resident's pain?

A. Elevating the resident's legs on two pillows above the level of the heart.  
B. Placing a heating pad directly on the resident's bare calves.  
C. Assisting the resident to sit on the edge of the bed and dangle their legs.  
D. Applying tight, knee-high compression wraps to both lower extremities.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Peripheral arterial disease (PAD) causes decreased blood flow to the lower extremities. When the patient lies flat, arterial flow must fight gravity, leading to ischemic "rest pain." Dangling the legs in a dependent position allows gravity to assist arterial blood flow to the feet, thereby relieving the pain. Elevating the legs or applying tight compression wraps would further restrict arterial blood flow and worsen the pain. Applying a heating pad directly to the skin is contraindicated due to the patient's decreased sensation and risk for burns.
</details>
### Question 51
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is preparing to measure the blood pressure of a 72-year-old client who has a history of essential hypertension. Which action by the nurse ensures the most accurate reading?

A. Asking the client to cross their legs while sitting in the examination chair.
B. Positioning the client's arm so that the blood pressure cuff is at heart level.
C. Applying the blood pressure cuff over the client's thin long-sleeved shirt.
D. Releasing the pressure valve rapidly to prevent patient discomfort.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
To obtain an accurate blood pressure reading, the client's arm should be supported at the level of the heart. Crossing the legs can falsely elevate blood pressure. Applying the cuff over clothing can cause inaccurate readings, and the cuff should be applied to a bare arm. Releasing the valve too rapidly can lead to a false reading, specifically underestimating the systolic pressure or overestimating the diastolic pressure.
</details>

---

### Question 52
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is assessing a 55-year-old client who is two days postoperative following abdominal surgery. The client reports a new, dull ache in the right lower extremity. Which finding should the nurse identify as an early clinical indication of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT)?

A. Bilateral pitting edema of the ankles.
B. Diminished pedal pulses in both feet.
C. Pale, cool skin on the right lower leg.
D. Unilateral swelling and redness of the right calf.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Unilateral leg swelling, localized redness, warmth, and calf pain are classic early signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). Bilateral edema is typically associated with systemic issues, such as heart failure. Pale, cool skin and diminished pulses point to an arterial occlusion rather than a venous issue like a DVT.
</details>

---

### Question 53
A triage nurse in the emergency department is preparing to obtain a 12-lead electrocardiogram (ECG) for a 28-year-old client reporting sudden palpitations. Which action should the nurse take when placing the chest electrodes?

A. Ensure the client's skin is clean, dry, and free of excessive hair.
B. Instruct the client to take deep, rapid breaths during the procedure.
C. Place the electrodes directly over the client's clothing to protect privacy.
D. Apply lotion to the client's chest to help the electrodes adhere better.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
For a clear and accurate ECG tracing, the skin must be clean, dry, and relatively hair-free to ensure good contact with the electrodes. Applying lotion or placing electrodes over clothing prevents proper adhesion and electrical conduction. Instructing the client to breathe rapidly can cause patient movement and respiratory artifact on the tracing; the client should be instructed to lie still and breathe normally.
</details>

---

### Question 54
A home health nurse is teaching an 80-year-old client with chronic heart failure about monitoring fluid retention at home. Which instruction should the nurse include regarding daily weights?

A. "Weigh yourself once a week and record the number in a log."
B. "Weigh yourself at bedtime after consuming your evening meal."
C. "Weigh yourself every morning after voiding, using the same scale."
D. "Weigh yourself only when you notice your shoes are feeling tight."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Clients with heart failure should be instructed to weigh themselves daily at the same time, ideally in the morning after voiding and before eating. They should use the same scale and wear similar clothing to provide the most consistent assessment of fluid status. Weighing weekly or only when symptomatic delays the recognition of fluid volume overload.
</details>

---

### Question 55
A nurse on a telemetry unit is receiving a 45-year-old client who just returned from a diagnostic cardiac catheterization performed via the right femoral artery. Which action is the priority for the nurse to perform during the initial post-procedure assessment?

A. Offer the client a heavy meal since they were NPO for the procedure.
B. Palpate the right pedal pulse and inspect the groin insertion site for bleeding.
C. Assist the client out of bed to ambulate to the bathroom.
D. Perform passive range-of-motion exercises on the client's right leg.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Following a cardiac catheterization via the femoral artery, the nurse's priority is to monitor for potential immediate complications such as hemorrhage or arterial occlusion. Checking the insertion site for bleeding or hematoma and assessing distal pulses (like the pedal pulse) are critical actions. The client must be kept on bed rest with the affected leg kept straight for a prescribed amount of time, making ambulation and range-of-motion exercises contraindicated. 
</details>
### Question 56
A nurse is caring for a 72-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an exacerbation of heart failure. During the morning assessment, the nurse notes the client has gained 3 lb (1.4 kg) over the last 24 hours. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Encourage the client to drink a 16 oz glass of water to promote diuresis.
B) Notify the healthcare provider of the rapid weight gain.
C) Inform the client that this is an expected finding with their diagnosis.
D) Document the finding and plan to weigh the client again in the evening.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
A weight gain of 3 lbs in 24 hours in a patient with heart failure indicates significant fluid retention and worsening fluid volume overload. The healthcare provider must be notified so medications, such as diuretics, can be adjusted. Encouraging fluids (Option A) would worsen the overload. A rapid weight gain is an adverse complication, not an expected or benign finding (Option C). Delaying intervention by just documenting and re-weighing later (Option D) ignores the immediate clinical change.
</details>

---

### Question 57
An outpatient clinic nurse is providing discharge instructions to a 55-year-old client recently diagnosed with peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client asks how they should position their legs when sitting in a chair reading or watching television. Which recommendation should the nurse make?

A) Elevate the legs above the level of the heart using several pillows.
B) Cross the legs firmly at the knees to compress the superficial veins.
C) Keep the legs in a dependent, downward position to promote blood flow.
D) Apply tight, restrictive clothing around the thighs to increase pressure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), there is a lack of adequate arterial blood flow to the extremities. Keeping the legs in a dependent (downward) position utilizes gravity to help arterial blood flow reach the lower legs and feet. Elevating the legs above the heart (Option A) is appropriate for venous insufficiency, not arterial disease, as it makes it harder for arterial blood to reach the tissues. Crossing the legs (Option B) and wearing restrictive clothing (Option D) further impair already compromised circulation and should be avoided.
</details>

---

### Question 58
A nurse is assessing a 34-year-old client on postoperative day two following an abdominal surgery. The client complains of new onset, aching pain in their right lower leg. The nurse notes the right calf is visibly swollen, red, and warm to the touch. What is the nurse's priority action?

A) Instruct the client to remain in bed and notify the healthcare provider.
B) Vigorously massage the affected calf to relieve the muscle cramping.
C) Apply a sequential compression device (SCD) to the affected leg.
D) Assist the client to ambulate in the hallway to stretch the leg muscles.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Unilateral calf swelling, redness, warmth, and pain are classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The priority action is to keep the client on bedrest to prevent a potential blood clot from dislodging and traveling to the lungs (pulmonary embolism), and to notify the provider for further orders (such as an ultrasound or anticoagulants). Massaging the calf (Option B), applying SCDs to a leg with a suspected clot (Option C), or ambulating the patient (Option D) are all strictly contraindicated as they can cause the clot to dislodge.
</details>

---

### Question 59
A nurse in a long-term care facility has just administered a scheduled morning dose of an oral antihypertensive medication to an 81-year-old client. Ten minutes later, the client presses the call light and states they need to use the restroom. Which action should the nurse take to ensure the client's safety?

A) Tell the client to stand up quickly to establish steady footing.
B) Offer a bedpan because the client is on strict bedrest after this medication.
C) Withhold all oral fluids for two hours to prevent nausea from movement.
D) Have the client sit on the edge of the bed for a few minutes before standing.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Antihypertensive medications can cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, especially in older adults, leading to dizziness and a high risk of falls when changing positions. Having the client dangle their legs on the edge of the bed allows their blood pressure to stabilize before standing. Standing up quickly (Option A) drastically increases the risk of syncope and falls. The medication does not require strict bedrest (Option B) or withholding fluids (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 60
A 60-year-old client in the emergency department reports crushing chest pain that began during exercise. The nurse administers the first dose of prescribed sublingual nitroglycerin. Five minutes later, the nurse reassesses the client, who states the chest pain is still present at a 6 out of 10. Which action should the nurse take next?

A) Instruct the client to wait an additional 15 minutes to see if the pain subsides.
B) Administer a second dose of sublingual nitroglycerin as prescribed.
C) Have the client swallow the next nitroglycerin tablet with a full glass of water.
D) Tell the client to take deep breaths and begin walking around the room.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
The standard protocol for treating angina with sublingual nitroglycerin is to administer one tablet (or spray) every 5 minutes, up to a maximum of three doses, if chest pain persists. Because 5 minutes have passed and the pain is still present, administering the second dose is the correct action. Waiting 15 minutes (Option A) delays critical treatment. Sublingual nitroglycerin must be dissolved under the tongue, not swallowed (Option C). The client should be resting to decrease myocardial oxygen demand, not walking around (Option D).
</details>
### Question 61
A home health nurse is visiting a 72-year-old client diagnosed with right-sided heart failure. Which observation by the nurse indicates that the client understands the self-management plan for their condition?

A) The client drinks at least 3 liters of water daily.  
B) The client uses a heating pad on their swollen ankles.  
C) The client weighs themselves every morning after voiding.  
D) The client sleeps flat on their back without pillows.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Weighing daily at the same time, usually in the morning after voiding and before eating, is the most effective way to monitor for fluid retention and fluid overload in heart failure. Option A is incorrect because clients with heart failure are often on fluid restrictions, not encouraged to drink excessive amounts. Option B is incorrect because applying heat to edematous extremities can lead to burns due to poor tissue perfusion. Option D is incorrect as many heart failure clients experience orthopnea and need their head elevated to breathe comfortably.
</details>

---

### Question 62
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 55-year-old client who is two days postoperative following an abdominal hysterectomy. The client reports a sudden, aching pain in the right calf. What is the nurse's most appropriate initial action?

A) Instruct the client to remain in bed and notify the healthcare provider.  
B) Massage the client's right calf to relieve the muscle cramping.  
C) Apply a sequential compression device (SCD) to the affected leg.  
D) Assist the client to ambulate in the hallway to promote circulation.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Unilateral calf pain in a postoperative client is a classic sign of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The nurse should keep the client on bed rest to prevent a potential clot from dislodging and traveling to the lungs (pulmonary embolism), and immediately notify the healthcare provider for further evaluation (such as a venous Doppler ultrasound). Options B, C, and D are all contraindicated because massaging, applying an SCD, or walking can dislodge an existing clot. 
</details>

---

### Question 63
A clinic nurse is evaluating the diet diary of a 48-year-old client newly diagnosed with essential hypertension. Which meal choice indicates that the client needs further teaching about a heart-healthy diet?

A) Grilled chicken breast, steamed broccoli, and brown rice.  
B) Baked salmon, mixed green salad, and a fresh apple.  
C) Turkey sandwich on whole-wheat bread with a side of baby carrots.  
D) Canned soup, saltine crackers, and pickled cucumbers.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Clients with hypertension are encouraged to follow a low-sodium diet (such as the DASH diet). Canned soups, saltine crackers, and pickled foods are highly processed and contain excessive amounts of sodium, which exacerbates hypertension. This choice indicates a need for further teaching. Options A, B, and C represent balanced, lower-sodium meals that are appropriate for a heart-healthy diet.
</details>

---

### Question 64
A 60-year-old client in the emergency department reports sudden chest pain while walking to the bathroom. The nurse administers a prescribed sublingual nitroglycerin tablet. Which assessment finding should the nurse prioritize monitoring immediately after administration?

A) Respiratory rate  
B) Blood pressure  
C) Temperature  
D) Bowel sounds  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent systemic vasodilator used to relieve angina. Because it dilates blood vessels, it rapidly decreases preload and afterload, which can cause significant hypotension. The nurse must prioritize monitoring the client's blood pressure to ensure it does not drop to dangerously low levels. While other vital signs are important, changes in temperature, respiratory rate, and bowel sounds are not immediate or primary concerns following the administration of nitroglycerin.
</details>

---

### Question 65
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a 68-year-old client with severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD) of the lower extremities. Which statement by the client demonstrates a correct understanding of how to manage their pain at home?

A) "I will elevate my legs above the level of my heart when I am sitting."  
B) "I will wear tight compression stockings every day to improve blood flow."  
C) "I will dangle my legs over the side of the bed when they start to ache."  
D) "I will apply ice packs to my feet when I feel cramping."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), the delivery of oxygenated blood to the lower extremities is impaired. Dangling the legs in a dependent position allows gravity to assist arterial blood flow down to the feet, which helps relieve ischemic pain. Option A is incorrect because elevating the legs forces blood to travel against gravity, worsening arterial ischemia and pain. Option B is incorrect because tight compression stockings can further restrict arterial blood flow. Option D is incorrect because cold application causes vasoconstriction, which further decreases arterial perfusion to the extremities.
</details>
### Question 66
A 45-year-old patient with primary hypertension is at an outpatient clinic for a follow-up visit. The nurse asks the patient to select a meal from a sample cafeteria menu to evaluate their dietary understanding. Which selection indicates to the nurse that the patient understands the recommended dietary modifications?

A. Canned tomato soup with a grilled cheese sandwich
B. Scrambled eggs with bacon and buttered toast
C. A bowl of oatmeal topped with fresh sliced strawberries
D. Pancakes with maple syrup and a side of breakfast sausage

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Patients with primary hypertension are encouraged to follow a low-sodium, heart-healthy diet, such as the DASH (Dietary Approaches to Stop Hypertension) diet. Fresh fruits and whole grains like oatmeal are naturally low in sodium and promote cardiovascular health. Options A, B, and D contain highly processed foods, canned items, and cured meats (bacon, sausage) which are very high in sodium and saturated fats, making them poor choices for a patient managing hypertension. 
</details>

---

### Question 67
A nurse is caring for a 78-year-old patient admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an exacerbation of heart failure. The nurse is delegating morning care to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Which instruction provided by the nurse is the most important for accurately monitoring the patient's fluid status?

A. "Make sure to weigh the patient before breakfast using the same scale."
B. "Ask the patient how many glasses of water they drank overnight."
C. "Check the patient's ankles for swelling after they walk in the hall."
D. "Measure the patient's abdominal girth at the end of your shift."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Daily weights are the most sensitive and reliable indicator of fluid volume status in patients with heart failure. To ensure accuracy, the patient should be weighed at the same time every day (typically in the morning before breakfast, after voiding), using the same scale, and wearing similar clothing. While asking about intake, checking for edema, and measuring abdominal girth can provide supplemental information, they are not as objective or precise as taking a standardized daily weight.
</details>

---

### Question 68
A 62-year-old resident in a long-term care facility has a history of severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD). During an afternoon walk down the hallway, the resident suddenly stops and tells the nurse, "My calves are cramping and burning really badly." What is the best initial action by the nurse?

A. Elevate the resident's legs above the level of the heart immediately.
B. Apply a hot compress to the resident's calves to promote circulation.
C. Massage the resident's lower legs vigorously to relieve the cramping.
D. Have the resident sit down and rest until the leg pain goes away.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
The resident is experiencing intermittent claudication, a classic sign of peripheral arterial disease (PAD) where the oxygen demand of the leg muscles during exercise exceeds the impaired arterial supply, causing pain. The immediate and most effective intervention is to have the patient rest, which decreases the oxygen demand and relieves the pain. Elevating the legs (Option A) decreases arterial blood flow to the lower extremities and will worsen the pain in PAD. Applying heat (Option B) is contraindicated due to decreased sensation and the risk of burns. Massaging (Option C) is generally avoided and does not resolve the underlying ischemia.
</details>

---

### Question 69
A 30-year-old patient is recovering on the orthopedic surgical unit two days after an open reduction and internal fixation of a fractured femur. While assisting the patient with a bed bath, the nurse observes that the patient's right calf is swollen, red, and warm to the touch. What should the nurse do next?

A. Assist the patient to ambulate in the hallway to increase venous return.
B. Instruct the patient to remain in bed and notify the healthcare provider.
C. Place a sequential compression device (SCD) on the affected right leg.
D. Perform a deep massage of the patient's right calf to decrease the swelling.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Unilateral calf swelling, redness, and warmth are classic clinical signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT), a common complication following orthopedic surgeries. The nurse's immediate priority is to prevent a pulmonary embolism. The patient should be kept strictly on bed rest while the healthcare provider is notified to evaluate the patient and prescribe anticoagulation therapy. Ambulating (Option A), applying an SCD to the affected limb (Option C), and massaging the leg (Option D) are strictly contraindicated because these actions can dislodge the clot, causing a life-threatening pulmonary embolism. 
</details>

---

### Question 70
A 68-year-old patient is being discharged from the emergency department with a new prescription for sublingual nitroglycerin to treat stable angina. The nurse is observing the patient practice medication administration steps and discussing home safety. Which statement by the patient demonstrates that the nurse's teaching was effective?

A. "I will chew the tablet thoroughly so it enters my system faster."
B. "I will swallow the tablet with a full glass of water to prevent throat irritation."
C. "I will sit or lie down before placing the medication under my tongue."
D. "I will leave the pills in my clear weekly pill organizer so I remember to take them."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator that rapidly lowers blood pressure, frequently causing dizziness, lightheadedness, and orthostatic hypotension. Sitting or lying down prior to administration is a crucial safety measure to prevent falls. The tablet must be dissolved sublingually (under the tongue) to bypass the liver and work quickly; chewing (Option A) or swallowing it (Option B) destroys its effectiveness. Nitroglycerin is sensitive to light and moisture and must be kept in its original dark glass container, not transferred to a clear pill organizer (Option D).
</details>
### Question 71
A nurse is preparing to measure the blood pressure of a 45-year-old client in an outpatient clinic. The client enters the triage room breathing heavily and states, "I just ran up three flights of stairs because the elevator was broken." What is the best action by the nurse?

A) Take the blood pressure immediately while the client is still standing.  
B) Use a blood pressure cuff that is one size smaller than usual.  
C) Have the client rest quietly in a chair for 5 minutes before taking the measurement.  
D) Tell the client to return to the clinic tomorrow for a more accurate reading.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Physical exertion temporarily increases blood pressure and heart rate. To obtain an accurate baseline blood pressure reading, the nurse should have the client rest quietly in a seated position for at least 5 minutes before taking the measurement. Taking the pressure immediately (Option A) will result in a falsely elevated reading. Using a smaller cuff (Option B) will also cause a falsely high reading. Sending the client away (Option D) is unnecessary and delays care.
</details>

---

### Question 72
A nurse is caring for an 82-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an exacerbation of heart failure. The primary healthcare provider prescribes daily weights to monitor fluid volume status. Which action should the nurse implement to ensure the most accurate measurements?

A) Weigh the client at the same time every morning using the same scale.  
B) Weigh the client immediately after they eat a large breakfast and drink fluids.  
C) Ask the client to estimate their daily weight changes based on how their clothes fit.  
D) Weigh the client in the evening before bedtime while they are wearing heavy shoes.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Daily weights are the most reliable indicator of fluid volume gain or loss in clients with heart failure. To ensure accuracy, the client should be weighed at the same time each day (preferably in the morning after voiding), wearing the same amount of clothing, and using the same scale. Weighing after eating (Option B) or with shoes on in the evening (Option D) introduces variables that distort the baseline. Estimating weight (Option C) is not an accurate clinical measurement.
</details>

---

### Question 73
A 68-year-old client is recovering on the surgical unit 12 hours after an abdominal hysterectomy. The nurse is teaching the client about interventions to prevent deep vein thrombosis (DVT) during recovery. Which action by the client indicates that the teaching was effective?

A) The client places a pillow directly under her knees while resting in bed.  
B) The client crosses her legs at the ankles while sitting in the bedside chair.  
C) The client states she will remain on strict bed rest for the next two days.  
D) The client performs active ankle pumps and circles while lying in bed.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Performing active leg exercises, such as ankle pumps and circles, promotes venous return and helps prevent venous stasis, which is a major risk factor for DVT. Placing a pillow under the knees (Option A) and crossing the legs (Option B) are contraindicated because they compress the popliteal veins and impede blood flow. Strict bed rest (Option C) promotes venous stasis; early ambulation is instead encouraged to prevent DVT.
</details>

---

### Question 74
A nurse in the emergency department is caring for a 55-year-old client who reports sudden, crushing chest pain radiating to the left arm. The provider prescribes sublingual nitroglycerin. After administering the first dose, what is the most important clinical action for the nurse to perform?

A) Offer the client a sip of water to help them swallow the medication.  
B) Assess the client's blood pressure and current pain level.  
C) Immediately administer a second dose of nitroglycerin to ensure efficacy.  
D) Place the client in a flat, supine position to promote maximum circulation.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator used to relieve angina. Because it dilates blood vessels, it can cause a significant drop in blood pressure. The nurse must assess the client's blood pressure and pain level before determining if a subsequent dose is safe and necessary. Sublingual medication should dissolve under the tongue, not be swallowed (Option A). A second dose is only given 5 minutes later if pain persists and blood pressure is stable (Option C). A flat supine position (Option D) is not indicated and can worsen breathing if the client is experiencing cardiac ischemia; semi-Fowler's is preferred.
</details>

---

### Question 75
A nurse on a telemetry unit is assessing a 72-year-old client with a history of severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client complains of aching pain in his lower legs while resting in bed. Which action should the nurse take to help alleviate the client's pain?

A) Elevate the client's legs above the level of the heart on two pillows.  
B) Apply ice packs directly to the client's calves for 15 minutes.  
C) Assist the client to sit on the side of the bed and dangle his legs.  
D) Apply tightly fitted compression stockings to both of the client's legs.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), blood flow to the lower extremities is compromised. Dangling the legs in a dependent position allows gravity to assist arterial blood flow to the distal extremities, thereby relieving ischemic rest pain. Elevating the legs (Option A) reduces arterial flow and will worsen the pain. Applying ice (Option B) causes vasoconstriction, which further impedes blood flow. Tight compression stockings (Option D) can further restrict already compromised arterial circulation.
</details>
### Question 76
A nurse is assessing a 68-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an exacerbation of right-sided heart failure. Which clinical observation should the nurse anticipate finding during the physical examination?

A. Crackles auscultated in the bilateral lung bases  
B. Frequent, frothy, blood-tinged sputum  
C. Pronounced distention of the jugular veins  
D. A persistent, dry, hacking cough  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Right-sided heart failure causes blood to back up into the systemic venous system rather than the lungs. This systemic congestion manifests as jugular venous distention (JVD), peripheral edema, hepatomegaly, and ascites. Crackles, blood-tinged sputum, and a hacking cough are classic signs of left-sided heart failure due to pulmonary congestion.
</details>

---

### Question 77
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is evaluating the lifestyle modifications of a 45-year-old client who was recently diagnosed with essential hypertension. Which statement by the client indicates that the nurse's dietary teaching has been effective?

A. "I will limit my brisk walking to once a week so my heart doesn't work too hard."  
B. "I am replacing my daily canned soups with fresh vegetable salads."  
C. "I can continue to smoke cigars as long as I take my blood pressure pill daily."  
D. "I should completely stop taking my medication if I feel slightly dizzy when standing."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
A major lifestyle modification for managing hypertension is reducing sodium intake. Canned soups are notoriously high in sodium, so replacing them with fresh vegetables demonstrates a strong understanding of a heart-healthy diet. Aerobic exercise should be increased (not limited), smoking is a major risk factor that should be stopped entirely, and clients should never abruptly stop their antihypertensive medications without consulting a healthcare provider.
</details>

---

### Question 78
A nurse is caring for a 52-year-old client in the post-operative unit who suddenly reports severe pain, tenderness, and swelling in the right calf. Which immediate action should the nurse take?

A. Instruct the client to remain in bed and refrain from rubbing the leg.  
B. Vigorously massage the right calf to relieve the muscle cramping.  
C. Apply a sequential compression device (SCD) to the affected leg.  
D. Assist the client with ambulating in the hallway to promote circulation.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The client's symptoms strongly suggest a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The priority action is to keep the client on bed rest and prevent any manipulation of the leg to reduce the risk of dislodging the clot, which could cause a pulmonary embolism. Massaging the leg, applying an SCD to a leg with a suspected clot, or immediate ambulation are contraindicated as they increase the risk of embolization.
</details>

---

### Question 79
A 60-year-old client arrives at the emergency department triage desk reporting chest pain. The client states they just took a dose of their prescribed sublingual nitroglycerin while in the car. Which observation by the nurse is an expected, common outcome of this medication?

A. A sudden, dangerous spike in blood pressure  
B. The client shivering and reporting chills  
C. A significantly decreased heart rate  
D. The client reporting a throbbing headache  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator used to treat angina by widening blood vessels and increasing blood flow to the heart. Because it causes systemic vasodilation, it also dilates cerebral blood vessels, frequently resulting in a throbbing headache. This is an expected side effect. Nitroglycerin causes a decrease (not an increase) in blood pressure, and it does not commonly cause chills or a significantly decreased heart rate (it may actually cause reflex tachycardia).
</details>

---

### Question 80
A nurse in a long-term care facility is conducting a morning assessment on a 78-year-old resident who has a history of peripheral arterial disease (PAD). Which client observation should the nurse expect to document?

A. Warm, swollen, and reddened lower extremities  
B. A bounding pedal pulse present in both feet  
C. Reports of calf pain when walking to the bathroom that goes away with rest  
D. Brown pigmentation and scaling skin around the ankles  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Intermittent claudication—pain, cramping, or aching in the calves or thighs that occurs during exercise and is relieved by rest—is the hallmark symptom of peripheral arterial disease (PAD). It occurs due to inadequate blood flow to the muscles during exertion. Warm, swollen legs and brown pigmentation around the ankles are signs of venous insufficiency, not arterial disease. Clients with PAD typically have weak or absent pedal pulses, not bounding ones.
</details>
### Question 81
A home health nurse is visiting a 72-year-old client with a history of chronic left-sided heart failure. During the initial assessment, which observation by the nurse requires the most immediate intervention?

A. The client reports feeling slightly tired after walking to the mailbox.  
B. The client has 1+ bilateral pitting edema in the ankles.  
C. The client reports needing to sleep on three pillows instead of two.  
D. The client's weight has remained the same for the past week.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Needing to sleep on extra pillows to breathe comfortably (orthopnea) is a classic sign of worsening pulmonary congestion and fluid overload in a client with left-sided heart failure. This requires immediate intervention. Mild fatigue with exertion (Option A) and mild chronic ankle edema (Option B) are expected, stable findings in chronic heart failure. A stable weight (Option D) is a positive finding, as sudden weight gain would indicate fluid retention.
</details>

---

### Question 82
A 55-year-old client at an outpatient clinic is prescribed a new oral antihypertensive medication. Shortly after taking the first dose in the clinic, the client tells the nurse they need to get up to use the restroom. Which action should the nurse take first?

A. Instruct the client to sit on the edge of the examination table for a few minutes before standing.  
B. Provide the client with a bedside commode instead of allowing them to walk.  
C. Escort the client to the bathroom immediately without stopping to rest.  
D. Check the client's blood pressure while they are lying flat on the examination table.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
First-dose syncope and orthostatic hypotension are common side effects of newly prescribed antihypertensive medications. To ensure safety and prevent falls, the nurse should instruct the client to change positions slowly and dangle their legs on the edge of the table or bed before standing. A bedside commode (Option B) is unnecessary if the client can ambulate safely. Escorting them immediately (Option C) ignores the risk of orthostatic hypotension. Checking the blood pressure while lying flat (Option D) does not directly address the immediate safety risk of the client standing up to use the restroom.
</details>

---

### Question 83
A nurse on a surgical unit is caring for a 48-year-old client who is 2 days post-operative from an abdominal hysterectomy. The client reports a new onset of unilateral right calf pain and swelling. Which action is the priority for the nurse?

A. Massage the client's right calf to relieve the muscle discomfort.  
B. Assist the client out of bed to ambulate in the hallway to promote circulation.  
C. Apply an ice pack to the affected area to reduce the swelling.  
D. Instruct the client to remain in bed and notify the healthcare provider.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Unilateral calf pain and swelling in a post-operative client are highly suspicious for a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The priority is to keep the client on bed rest and notify the healthcare provider immediately to prevent the potential dislodgment of the clot, which could cause a pulmonary embolism. Massaging the calf (Option A) or ambulating (Option B) are strictly contraindicated as they can dislodge the clot. Applying an ice pack (Option C) is not the standard of care for a suspected DVT and delays necessary medical intervention.
</details>

---

### Question 84
A 61-year-old client arrives at the emergency department triage desk reporting sudden, crushing chest pain. The nurse anticipates administering sublingual nitroglycerin. Which assessment finding must the nurse report to the healthcare provider before giving the medication?

A. The client complains of a mild headache along with the chest pain.  
B. The client has taken sildenafil within the past 24 hours.  
C. The client reports a pain severity of 6 on a 0 to 10 scale.  
D. The client's heart rate is 88 beats per minute.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator. It is strictly contraindicated in clients who have taken phosphodiesterase-5 (PDE5) inhibitors, such as sildenafil (Viagra), within the last 24 to 48 hours because the combination can cause profound, life-threatening hypotension. A headache (Option A) is a common, expected side effect of nitroglycerin, not a contraindication. A pain level of 6 (Option C) and a heart rate of 88 (Option D) are expected findings in a client experiencing angina and do not contraindicate the medication.
</details>

---

### Question 85
A medical-surgical nurse is evaluating a 78-year-old client with a history of severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client complains of a dull, aching pain in the lower extremities that worsens at night while sleeping. Which intervention should the nurse implement?

A. Elevate the client's legs on two pillows above the level of the heart.  
B. Apply tight compression stockings to both legs before the client goes to sleep.  
C. Instruct the client to hang their legs in a dependent position over the side of the bed.  
D. Encourage the client to apply a heating pad directly to the feet to soothe the pain.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), blood flow to the lower extremities is compromised. Placing the legs in a dependent position (dangling them over the side of the bed) uses gravity to help increase arterial blood flow to the legs, thereby relieving ischemic rest pain. Elevating the legs (Option A) makes it harder for arterial blood to reach the feet and worsens the pain. Compression stockings (Option B) further restrict arterial blood flow. Heating pads (Option D) are dangerous because clients with PAD often have decreased sensation and are at a high risk for thermal burns.
</details>
### Question 86
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 72-year-old client admitted with an exacerbation of heart failure. Which action is the most accurate way for the nurse to evaluate the client's fluid status on a daily basis?

A) Measure the client's intake and output every shift.
B) Check the client's dependent edema at bedtime.
C) Weigh the client each morning using the same scale.
D) Assess the client's blood pressure prior to giving diuretics.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C) Weigh the client each morning using the same scale.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> While intake and output, edema assessment, and blood pressure monitoring are all important, daily weights are the most reliable and accurate indicator of fluid volume status in clients with heart failure. The nurse should weigh the client at the same time each day, using the same scale, and with the client wearing similar clothing.
</details>

---

### Question 87
A 45-year-old client who is two days postoperative from abdominal surgery complains of sudden pain and swelling in the right calf. The nurse observes that the right calf is warm and red. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Maintain the client on bed rest and notify the healthcare provider.
B) Gently massage the right calf to relieve the client's pain.
C) Apply a sequential compression device (SCD) to the right leg.
D) Assist the client to ambulate in the hallway to promote circulation.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A) Maintain the client on bed rest and notify the healthcare provider.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Unilateral calf swelling, pain, warmth, and redness are classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The nurse's priority is to keep the client on bed rest to prevent dislodging the clot (which could cause a pulmonary embolism) and to immediately notify the provider. Massaging the leg, ambulating, or applying SCDs to the affected leg are contraindicated as they can dislodge the thrombus.
</details>
