### Question 88
A 60-year-old client arrives at the emergency department reporting chest pain. The healthcare provider prescribes sublingual nitroglycerin. What assessment must the nurse prioritize before administering this medication?

A) Respiratory rate
B) Apical pulse rate
C) Capillary refill time
D) Blood pressure

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D) Blood pressure<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Nitroglycerin is a potent systemic vasodilator used to relieve angina. Because it dilates blood vessels, a common and potentially dangerous side effect is sudden hypotension. The nurse must check the client's blood pressure prior to administration to ensure it is safe to give the medication. 
</details>

---

### Question 89
A nurse is discharging a 38-year-old client from the outpatient clinic who was recently prescribed a new oral antihypertensive medication. During the discharge teaching, what instruction should the nurse emphasize to prevent client injury?

A) "Take the medication with a full glass of grapefruit juice."
B) "Change positions slowly when moving from sitting to standing."
C) "Stop taking the medication immediately if you get a headache."
D) "Check your heart rate every hour while you are awake."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B) "Change positions slowly when moving from sitting to standing."<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Antihypertensive medications commonly cause orthostatic (postural) hypotension, especially when initiating therapy. Instructing the client to change positions slowly allows the body to adjust and helps prevent dizziness, fainting, and fall-related injuries.
</details>

---

### Question 90
A nurse is assessing a 78-year-old client in a long-term care facility who has a history of severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The nurse is unable to palpate the dorsalis pedis pulse in the client's left foot. What is the nurse's best initial action?

A) Elevate the client's left leg on two pillows.
B) Apply a warm compress to the client's left foot.
C) Obtain a Doppler ultrasound device to locate the pulse.
D) Immediately activate the rapid response team.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C) Obtain a Doppler ultrasound device to locate the pulse.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> In clients with severe peripheral arterial disease, pedal pulses may be very weak, thready, or difficult to palpate manually. The best initial action is to use a Doppler ultrasound device to detect the presence or absence of blood flow. Elevating the leg worsens arterial flow, applying heat can cause burns due to decreased sensation, and activating the rapid response team is premature before verifying the pulse with a Doppler.
</details>
### Question 91
A nurse in a community health clinic is preparing to measure the blood pressure of a 45-year-old client who has just been diagnosed with primary hypertension. Which action by the nurse is most appropriate to ensure an accurate reading?

A) Position the client's arm unsupported below the level of the heart.
B) Ensure the client has been resting quietly in a chair for at least 5 minutes.
C) Use a blood pressure cuff that overlaps entirely around the client's bicep.
D) Instruct the client to take deep breaths and talk during the measurement to reduce anxiety.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Ensure the client has been resting quietly in a chair for at least 5 minutes.

**Rationale:** To obtain an accurate blood pressure reading, the client should be seated quietly in a chair with their feet flat on the floor for at least 5 minutes prior to the measurement. The arm should be supported at the level of the heart (not unsupported below it). The cuff bladder should encircle 80% of the arm, not overlap entirely, which indicates the cuff is too large. The client should not talk during the measurement, as this can artificially elevate the blood pressure reading.
</details>

---

### Question 92
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 78-year-old client admitted with an exacerbation of left-sided heart failure. The primary healthcare provider prescribes daily weights to monitor fluid retention. Which action should the nurse take when weighing this client?

A) Weigh the client right after their largest meal of the day.
B) Have the client wear their orthopedic walking shoes during the weigh-in.
C) Use a different scale each day to ensure calibration accuracy.
D) Weigh the client at the same time every morning after they void.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Weigh the client at the same time every morning after they void.

**Rationale:** Daily weights are the most reliable indicator of fluid volume status in heart failure clients. To ensure accuracy and consistency, the nurse should weigh the client at the same time every day (preferably in the morning), using the same scale, after the client has voided, and while the client is wearing the same amount of clothing. Weighing after a large meal, wearing heavy shoes, or using different scales introduces variables that make tracking fluid trends inaccurate.
</details>

---

### Question 93
A nurse at a long-term care facility is caring for an 82-year-old client with a history of severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client reports aching pain in their lower legs while trying to sleep at night. Which intervention should the nurse implement to help relieve the client's discomfort?

A) Allow the client to dangle their legs over the side of the bed.
B) Elevate the client's legs above the level of the heart on two pillows.
C) Apply a tight compression dressing to both calves.
D) Apply a direct heating pad to the client's feet on the highest setting.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Allow the client to dangle their legs over the side of the bed.

**Rationale:** In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), blood flow to the lower extremities is compromised. Dangling the legs over the side of the bed allows gravity to assist arterial blood flow to the distal extremities, which helps relieve ischemic rest pain. Elevating the legs or applying tight compression would further impede arterial blood flow. Applying direct heat is contraindicated due to decreased sensation in the extremities, which places the client at a high risk for thermal burns.
</details>

---

### Question 94
A triage nurse in the emergency department is caring for a 60-year-old client experiencing a mild episode of stable angina. The client has a prescription for sublingual nitroglycerin. How should the nurse instruct the client to take this medication?

A) Swallow the pill whole with a full glass of cold water.
B) Chew the tablet thoroughly before swallowing to speed up absorption.
C) Place the tablet under the tongue and let it dissolve completely.
D) Crush the tablet and mix it into a spoonful of applesauce.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Place the tablet under the tongue and let it dissolve completely.

**Rationale:** Sublingual nitroglycerin must be placed under the tongue and allowed to dissolve completely. The rich blood supply under the tongue absorbs the medication rapidly directly into the bloodstream, bypassing first-pass metabolism in the liver. Swallowing, chewing, or crushing the tablet renders the medication ineffective for rapid angina relief.
</details>

---

### Question 95
A nurse in a surgical step-down unit is caring for a 28-year-old client who had an abdominal appendectomy 12 hours ago. The nurse is planning care to prevent the development of deep vein thrombosis (DVT). Which action should the nurse take?

A) Instruct the client to place a pillow directly under their knees while in bed.
B) Encourage the client to ambulate in the hallway as early and frequently as tolerated.
C) Massage the client's calves gently every 4 hours to promote venous return.
D) Keep the client on strict bed rest for the first 48 hours post-operation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Encourage the client to ambulate in the hallway as early and frequently as tolerated.

**Rationale:** Early and frequent ambulation is one of the most effective, easy-to-implement interventions to prevent deep vein thrombosis (DVT) in post-operative clients, as it promotes venous return through muscle contraction. Placing a pillow under the knees restricts venous return and increases DVT risk. Massaging the calves is contraindicated because if a clot has already formed, massage could dislodge it, causing a pulmonary embolism. Prolonged bed rest increases the risk of venous stasis and DVT formation.
</details>
### Question 96
A nurse is assigned to care for a 55-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with a deep vein thrombosis (DVT) in the right lower extremity. Which action by the newly hired unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) requires immediate intervention by the nurse?

A. Elevating the client's right leg on two pillows while in bed.  
B. Offering the client a bedpan to minimize ambulation.  
C. Massaging the client's right calf to relieve muscle cramping.  
D. Providing a warm, moist compress to the client's right leg.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br><br>
Massaging the affected extremity in a client with a deep vein thrombosis (DVT) is strictly contraindicated. Massage can dislodge the blood clot, potentially leading to a life-threatening pulmonary embolism. The nurse must intervene immediately to stop the UAP. Elevating the leg, minimizing ambulation (if prescribed), and applying warm compresses are standard, appropriate interventions for a DVT.
</details>

---

### Question 97
A home health nurse is visiting a 72-year-old client with a history of left-sided heart failure. Which observation by the nurse is the most reliable indicator that the client's condition is worsening and requires immediate medical attention?

A. The client reports a weight gain of 4 pounds over the last 2 days.  
B. The client's resting heart rate increases from 76 to 84 beats per minute.  
C. The client states they have been urinating more frequently during the day.  
D. The client requests an extra blanket while sitting in the living room.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br><br>
Rapid weight gain (such as 3 or more pounds in 2 days, or 5 pounds in a week) is a classic, early sign of fluid retention and worsening heart failure. It indicates that the heart is failing to pump effectively, leading to systemic fluid overload. A slight increase in heart rate, daytime frequency of urination, or feeling cold are not the most reliable or urgent indicators of an acute exacerbation of heart failure.
</details>

---

### Question 98
A nurse on a telemetry unit is preparing to administer a scheduled morning dose of metoprolol to a 48-year-old client with hypertension. The client's morning vital signs are blood pressure 138/86 mmHg, heart rate 52 beats per minute, and respiratory rate 16 breaths per minute. What is the most appropriate action by the nurse?

A. Administer the medication as prescribed with a full glass of water.  
B. Administer half the dose of the medication to prevent severe bradycardia.  
C. Encourage the client to ambulate in the hallway before giving the medication.  
D. Withhold the medication and notify the healthcare provider.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br><br>
Metoprolol is a beta-blocker that lowers both blood pressure and heart rate. A standard nursing parameter is to withhold the medication and notify the provider if the apical heart rate is below 60 beats per minute, as administering it could cause profound, symptomatic bradycardia. The nurse does not have the authority to alter the dose, and ambulating to temporarily raise the heart rate is unsafe and does not address the underlying bradycardia.
</details>

---

### Question 99
A 65-year-old client arrives at the emergency department reporting acute chest pain. The physician prescribes a sublingual nitroglycerin tablet. Which action should the nurse take immediately *before* administering the medication?

A. Obtain an arterial blood gas (ABG) sample.  
B. Assess the client's current blood pressure.  
C. Auscultate the client's bowel sounds.  
D. Ask the client to drink a full glass of water.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent systemic vasodilator used to relieve angina. Because it dilates blood vessels, it can cause a rapid and significant drop in blood pressure. The nurse must assess the client's blood pressure prior to administration to ensure it is safe to give. Providing a full glass of water is unnecessary for a sublingual medication, and ABGs or bowel sounds are not priority assessments before giving nitroglycerin.
</details>

---

### Question 100
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is evaluating an 80-year-old client with peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client reports severe lower leg pain that frequently awakens them at night. Which instruction should the nurse provide to help the client manage this nighttime pain?

A. "Apply tightly fitting compression stockings before going to sleep."  
B. "Place a heating pad on the highest setting directly over your calves."  
C. "Dangle your legs over the side of the bed when the pain occurs."  
D. "Elevate your legs above the level of your heart on three pillows."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br><br>
Ischemic rest pain at night is a common complication of peripheral arterial disease (PAD). Dangling the legs over the side of the bed places them in a dependent position, allowing gravity to assist arterial blood flow to the ischemic lower extremities, thereby relieving pain. Elevating the legs (Option D) or wearing tight compression stockings (Option A) would further impede arterial blood flow and worsen the pain. Applying high heat (Option B) is dangerous due to the client's decreased sensation and the risk of burns.
</details>
### Question 101
A nurse is preparing to obtain a blood pressure reading for a 45-year-old client in an outpatient clinic. The client has just arrived after briskly walking several blocks from the parking garage. Which action should the nurse take first?

A. Apply the blood pressure cuff over the client's thick sweater.
B. Allow the client to rest quietly for 5 minutes before taking the measurement.
C. Instruct the client to stand up while the blood pressure is being measured.
D. Take the blood pressure immediately to obtain a baseline ambulatory reading.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> To obtain an accurate blood pressure reading, the nurse should allow the client to rest quietly for at least 5 minutes before measurement, especially after physical exertion like brisk walking. Taking the pressure immediately (Option D) will likely result in a falsely elevated reading. The cuff should be applied to a bare arm, not over thick clothing (Option A), and the client should ideally be seated with feet flat on the floor, not standing, unless orthostatic vitals are specifically ordered (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 102
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 72-year-old client admitted with a severe exacerbation of heart failure. The healthcare provider has ordered daily weights to monitor fluid retention. Which nursing action ensures the most accurate weight tracking for this client?

A. Weigh the client using a different scale each day to verify calibration.
B. Weigh the client immediately after they have eaten a full breakfast.
C. Weigh the client in the evening before bedtime with their shoes on.
D. Weigh the client at the same time each morning wearing similar clothing.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The most accurate way to track daily fluid balance in a client with heart failure is to weigh them at the same time each day (preferably in the morning after voiding), wearing similar clothing, and using the exact same scale. Using different scales (Option A) can introduce mechanical variations. Weighing after meals (Option B) or at night with shoes on (Option C) will inaccurately reflect fluid shifts due to food intake and variable clothing weight.
</details>

---

### Question 103
A 55-year-old client is 3 days post-operative from an abdominal surgery and suddenly reports unilateral pain, tenderness, and swelling in the right calf. Which action should the nurse take immediately?

A. Vigorously massage the right calf to help relieve the client's muscle cramp.
B. Apply sequential compression devices (SCDs) tightly to both lower legs.
C. Keep the client on bed rest and promptly notify the healthcare provider.
D. Encourage the client to ambulate in the hallway to promote blood flow.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Unilateral calf pain, tenderness, and swelling in a post-operative client are classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The nurse's immediate priority is to keep the client on bed rest to prevent the clot from dislodging and causing a pulmonary embolism, and to notify the provider for further diagnostic orders. Massaging the leg (Option A), applying SCDs to an extremity with a suspected clot (Option B), or ambulating (Option D) are strictly contraindicated as they can dislodge the thrombus.
</details>

---

### Question 104
A 60-year-old client arrives at the emergency department reporting sudden, squeezing substernal chest pain. The nurse prepares to administer prescribed sublingual nitroglycerin. What specific instruction should the nurse provide to the client regarding this medication?

A. "Place this tablet under your tongue and let it dissolve completely."
B. "Chew this tablet thoroughly before swallowing it with water."
C. "Swallow this pill whole with a large, warm cup of tea."
D. "Tuck this pill between your cheek and gums until it is gone."

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Sublingual medications, such as nitroglycerin used for angina, are designed to be absorbed directly into the highly vascular tissue under the tongue for rapid systemic effect. The client should be instructed to place the tablet under the tongue and allow it to dissolve completely. Chewing (Option B) or swallowing the pill whole (Option C) bypasses the intended mucosal absorption route and subjects the drug to the first-pass effect, delaying or neutralizing its action. Buccal administration (Option D) involves placing the pill between the cheek and gums, which is incorrect for a sublingual formulation.
</details>

---

### Question 105
A home health nurse is visiting an 80-year-old client with a history of peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client complains that their feet consistently feel cold and numb. Which assessment action should the nurse perform to evaluate the circulation to the client's lower extremities?

A. Measure the client's radial pulse for one full minute.
B. Palpate the dorsalis pedis and posterior tibial pulses bilaterally.
C. Assess the capillary refill time on the client's fingernails.
D. Ask the client to perform active range of motion exercises with their hips.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Peripheral arterial disease (PAD) leads to decreased blood flow to the lower extremities. The best way for the nurse to evaluate distal circulation in the legs and feet is to palpate the pedal pulses (dorsalis pedis and posterior tibial pulses). Measuring the radial pulse (Option A) or assessing fingernail capillary refill (Option C) checks upper extremity perfusion, not lower extremity circulation. Hip range of motion (Option D) evaluates musculoskeletal function, not peripheral tissue perfusion.
</details>
### Question 106
A home health nurse is visiting a 72-year-old client with a history of right-sided heart failure. Which action by the client indicates to the nurse that teaching about fluid volume management has been effective?

A) The client drinks at least 3 liters of water daily.
B) The client measures their blood pressure once a week.
C) The client weighs themselves every morning before eating.
D) The client sleeps flat in bed without any pillows.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The client weighs themselves every morning before eating.

**Rationale:** Daily weights are the most reliable indicator of fluid volume status in clients with heart failure. Weighing themselves every morning at the same time, in the same clothing, and before eating shows an understanding of how to monitor for fluid retention. Drinking 3 liters of water daily is contraindicated, as heart failure patients often require fluid restriction. Sleeping flat may exacerbate dyspnea (orthopnea) in heart failure patients.
</details>

---

### Question 107
A 45-year-old client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with a confirmed deep vein thrombosis (DVT) in the left calf. While assisting the client with morning care, the nurse notes the client vigorously rubbing their left calf. What is the nurse's priority action?

A) Instruct the client to immediately stop massaging the leg.
B) Apply a warm, moist compress to the area being rubbed.
C) Provide lotion to help the client massage the leg more smoothly.
D) Elevate the left leg above the level of the heart.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Instruct the client to immediately stop massaging the leg.

**Rationale:** Massaging a leg with a deep vein thrombosis is highly dangerous because it can dislodge the clot, leading to a life-threatening pulmonary embolism. The nurse must immediately intervene to stop the client from rubbing or massaging the affected calf. While elevation and warm compresses may be part of the treatment plan, the immediate priority is stopping the dangerous action. 
</details>

---

### Question 108
At a community health clinic, a nurse is following up with a 35-year-old client who was recently prescribed daily medication for primary hypertension. The client states, "I stopped taking the pills a few days ago because my headaches went away and I feel great." Which response by the nurse is the most appropriate?

A) "It is good that you are listening to your body's signals."
B) "You should only take the medication when your blood pressure is high."
C) "I will let the doctor know so we can lower your dosage."
D) "Blood pressure medication needs to be taken daily even if you feel well."

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) "Blood pressure medication needs to be taken daily even if you feel well."

**Rationale:** Hypertension is often referred to as a "silent killer" because it is frequently asymptomatic. Clients must understand that the absence of symptoms does not mean their blood pressure is cured or controlled. Abruptly stopping antihypertensive medications can lead to dangerous rebound hypertension. The nurse must educate the client on the necessity of lifelong adherence to their medication regimen.
</details>

---

### Question 109
A triage nurse in the emergency department is assessing a 68-year-old client experiencing stable angina. The client has a prescription for sublingual nitroglycerin tablets for chest pain. The nurse observes the client preparing to take a dose. Which action by the client requires correction by the nurse?

A) The client takes a sip of water to moisten their mouth before taking the pill.
B) The client swallows the tablet whole with a glass of water.
C) The client sits down on a chair before taking the medication.
D) The client places the tablet directly under their tongue.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) The client swallows the tablet whole with a glass of water.

**Rationale:** Sublingual nitroglycerin must be placed under the tongue and allowed to dissolve completely so it can be absorbed directly into the systemic circulation. Swallowing the tablet exposes it to the liver's first-pass effect, which destroys the medication and renders it ineffective for relieving angina. Sitting down is an appropriate action to prevent injury from a sudden drop in blood pressure.
</details>

---

### Question 110
A nurse in an outpatient vascular clinic is walking down the hall with a 55-year-old client diagnosed with peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client suddenly stops walking, grimaces, and complains of cramping pain in their right calf. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Assist the client to stand on their right leg to stretch the calf muscle.
B) Elevate the client's right leg above their heart on a nearby chair.
C) Have the client sit down and rest until the pain subsides.
D) Apply a cold pack directly to the client's right calf.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Have the client sit down and rest until the pain subsides.

**Rationale:** The client is experiencing intermittent claudication, which is ischemic muscle pain caused by exercise and relieved by rest. The priority action is to have the client sit and rest, which decreases the oxygen demand in the leg muscles. Elevating the leg can worsen arterial blood flow in PAD, and applying a cold pack causes vasoconstriction, which would further impair arterial circulation to the ischemic tissue.
</details>
### Question 111
A clinic nurse is evaluating an 82-year-old client with a history of left-sided heart failure. Which observation by the nurse best indicates that the client is managing their fluid volume appropriately at home?

A. The client reports sleeping with three pillows every night to breathe comfortably.  
B. The client's daily weight fluctuates by less than 1 pound per week.  
C. The client takes an extra dose of furosemide whenever their shoes feel tight.  
D. The client limits their daily oral fluid intake to 3.5 liters.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Maintaining a stable daily weight is the most reliable indicator of effective fluid management in a client with heart failure. A weight fluctuation of less than 1 pound per week shows stable fluid status. Sleeping with three pillows indicates orthopnea (worsening failure). Taking extra medication without a prescription is unsafe, and an intake of 3.5 liters is excessive for a client who likely requires fluid restriction.
</details>

---

### Question 112
A nurse on a surgical unit is caring for a 28-year-old client who is two days post-appendectomy. The client complains of new-onset aching pain in the right calf. What is the nurse's priority action?

A. Massage the client's right calf to relieve the muscle ache.  
B. Assist the client out of bed to ambulate in the hallway.  
C. Instruct the client to perform active range-of-motion exercises with the right leg.  
D. Keep the client in bed and assess the right calf for redness and swelling.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Unilateral calf pain in a postoperative client is a classic sign of deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The nurse should keep the client on bed rest to prevent a potential clot from dislodging and assess for other signs such as redness, swelling, and warmth. Massaging the leg, ambulating, and vigorous range-of-motion exercises are strictly contraindicated as they can dislodge a thrombus, leading to a pulmonary embolism.
</details>

---

### Question 113
A 65-year-old client arrives at the emergency department reporting chest pressure that started while mowing the lawn. The physician prescribes sublingual nitroglycerin. How should the nurse administer this medication?

A. Place the tablet underneath the client's tongue and instruct them to let it dissolve.  
B. Ask the client to chew the tablet thoroughly before swallowing it with water.  
C. Dissolve the tablet in a cup of water and have the client drink it quickly.  
D. Instruct the client to swallow the tablet whole with a full glass of milk.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Sublingual nitroglycerin must be placed under the tongue and allowed to dissolve completely. The sublingual mucosa is highly vascular, allowing the medication to be rapidly absorbed directly into the bloodstream to relieve angina. Chewing, swallowing whole, or dissolving the medication in a drink subjects it to the first-pass effect in the liver, rendering it ineffective.
</details>

---

### Question 114
A nurse at a long-term care facility is providing evening care for a 74-year-old client with severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client reports that their feet feel freezing cold. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate?

A. Fill a hot water bottle and place it directly against the client's bare feet.  
B. Elevate the client's legs above the level of the heart on a stack of pillows.  
C. Provide the client with a pair of thick, warm cotton socks to wear in bed.  
D. Apply a continuous electric heating pad to the client's lower extremities.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Clients with peripheral arterial disease (PAD) often experience cold extremities due to decreased arterial circulation. They also frequently have diminished sensation. Providing warm socks is a safe, effective way to promote warmth. Using hot water bottles or electric heating pads is contraindicated due to the high risk of thermal burns. Elevating the legs above the heart level decreases arterial blood flow to the feet, which will worsen PAD symptoms.
</details>

### Question 115
A public health nurse is reviewing the dietary choices of a 45-year-old client recently diagnosed with primary hypertension. Which lunch selection made by the client indicates to the nurse that dietary teaching has been effective?

A. A canned vegetable soup with a side of salted saltine crackers.  
B. A grilled chicken salad with fresh greens and a squeeze of lemon juice.  
C. A slice of pepperoni pizza and a large glass of tomato juice.  
D. A turkey and Swiss cheese sandwich on a heavily salted pretzel bun.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A grilled chicken salad with fresh greens and lemon juice is a nutrient-dense, low-sodium option that aligns with dietary recommendations for hypertension (such as the DASH diet). Canned soups, salted crackers, pretzel buns, Swiss cheese, pepperoni, and tomato juice are all highly processed and very high in sodium, which can exacerbate hypertension.
</details>
### Question 116
The nurse is caring for a 72-year-old male admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an exacerbation of right-sided heart failure. Which action by the nurse is the most accurate way to monitor the patient's daily fluid status?

A) Measure the patient's abdominal girth at the end of every shift.
B) Document the patient's oral fluid intake every 4 hours.
C) Weigh the patient every morning using the same scale and clothing.
D) Assess the patient's bilateral pedal edema before bedtime.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Daily weights are the most reliable and accurate indicator of fluid volume status in patients with heart failure. To ensure accuracy, the nurse should weigh the patient at the same time each day (usually in the morning after voiding), using the same scale, and with the patient wearing the same amount of clothing. While intake and output, edema assessment, and abdominal girth are helpful monitoring tools, they are not as universally accurate for tracking rapid fluid shifts as daily weight measurements.
</details>

---

### Question 117
A nurse is preparing to assess the blood pressure of a 45-year-old female patient arriving at the primary care clinic for a routine check-up. The patient mentions she just finished a brisk walk to the clinic from the parking garage. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Have the patient sit and rest for 5 minutes before taking the measurement.
B) Use a blood pressure cuff that covers 100% of the patient's upper arm.
C) Take the blood pressure immediately while the patient is standing.
D) Ask the patient to hold her breath while the cuff is inflating.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
To obtain an accurate resting blood pressure, the patient should sit quietly for at least 5 minutes before the measurement, especially after physical activity like brisk walking, which can temporarily elevate blood pressure readings. A cuff that covers 100% of the arm is too large and will yield a falsely low reading. Taking the blood pressure immediately while standing or having the patient hold their breath will also result in inaccurate readings.
</details>

---

### Question 118
A 28-year-old female patient on the postpartum unit complains of sudden, severe pain and swelling in her left calf two days after a Cesarean delivery. The nurse observes that the left calf is red and warm to the touch. Which action should the nurse perform next?

A) Vigorously massage the left calf to relieve the patient's muscle cramps.
B) Apply a hot pack to the left calf and encourage immediate ambulation.
C) Perform a forceful dorsiflexion of the patient's left foot to assess for pain.
D) Keep the patient on bed rest and notify the healthcare provider immediately.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
The patient is exhibiting classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The immediate nursing priority is to keep the patient on bed rest to prevent the clot from dislodging and causing a pulmonary embolism, and to notify the healthcare provider. Massaging the leg, encouraging ambulation at this specific acute moment, or performing forceful dorsiflexion (Homans' sign) are contraindicated because they can mechanically dislodge the thrombus.
</details>

---

### Question 119
A 60-year-old male patient presents to the emergency department with stable angina and is prescribed sublingual nitroglycerin for chest pain. The nurse is instructing the patient on how to take the medication at home. Which statement by the patient indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) "I should chew the tablet thoroughly before swallowing it."
B) "I should place the tablet under my tongue and let it dissolve."
C) "I will take the medication with a large glass of water."
D) "I can take up to five tablets every 15 minutes if my pain continues."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
Sublingual nitroglycerin must be placed under the tongue to dissolve. This allows the medication to be rapidly absorbed through the oral mucosa directly into the bloodstream to relieve angina. It should not be chewed or swallowed with water, as it will be destroyed by the liver (first-pass effect) and become ineffective. The standard dosing is one tablet every 5 minutes, up to a maximum of three tablets, and emergency services should be contacted if pain is not relieved after the first dose.
</details>

---

### Question 120
A 68-year-old male with a history of severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD) is resting in bed on the telemetry unit. He presses the call light and tells the nurse that his toes are aching and feel numb. Which initial nursing action is most appropriate to help relieve his symptoms?

A) Elevate the patient's legs on two pillows above the level of his heart.
B) Apply tight, thigh-high compression stockings to both legs.
C) Assist the patient to dangle his legs over the side of the bed.
D) Place a heating pad directly on the patient's feet on the highest setting.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), blood flow to the lower extremities is compromised due to narrowed arteries. Assisting the patient to dangle his legs over the side of the bed uses gravity to help improve arterial perfusion to the distal extremities, which relieves ischemic pain. Elevating the legs or using tight compression stockings would further decrease arterial flow and worsen the pain. Heating pads should not be applied directly to the extremities, as patients with PAD often have decreased sensation and are at a high risk for thermal burns.
</details>
### Question 121
A nurse in a primary care clinic is assessing a 68-year-old client with a history of heart failure. The client reports a weight gain of 4 pounds over the last two days and feeling more fatigued than usual. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Advise the client to restrict their fluid intake to 500 mL per day.
B) Auscultate the client's breath sounds. 
C) Administer an extra dose of their prescribed daily diuretic.
D) Document the weight and recheck it in 48 hours.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
A sudden weight gain of 3 or more pounds in two days is a classic sign of fluid retention and worsening heart failure. The nurse's priority action using the nursing process (assessment first) is to auscultate the client's breath sounds to check for crackles, which would indicate pulmonary edema (fluid backing up into the lungs). The nurse cannot prescribe or alter medication doses (Option C) without a provider's order. A 500 mL fluid restriction (Option A) is excessively strict and requires a provider's order. Simply documenting and waiting 48 hours (Option D) ignores a potentially acute exacerbation of heart failure.
</details>

---

### Question 122
A nurse in a long-term care facility is observing a 75-year-old client with peripheral arterial disease (PAD) resting in bed. The client complains of aching pain in both lower legs. Which immediate action by the nurse is most appropriate to help relieve the pain?

A) Elevate the client’s legs above heart level on a pillow.
B) Apply a hot water bottle directly to the client's calves.
C) Assist the client to dangle their legs over the side of the bed.
D) Massage the client’s calf muscles vigorously.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), arterial blood flow to the lower extremities is compromised. Dangling the legs over the side of the bed allows gravity to assist arterial blood flow down to the lower legs, which helps relieve ischemic pain. Elevating the legs (Option A) is appropriate for venous insufficiency, but it worsens PAD because it makes it harder for arterial blood to reach the extremities. Applying direct heat (Option B) is dangerous because clients with PAD often have decreased sensation and are at high risk for thermal burns. Vigorously massaging the calves (Option D) is contraindicated as it could dislodge an unknown thrombus or cause tissue damage to ischemic muscles.
</details>

---

### Question 123
A 42-year-old client who is two days postoperative from an abdominal hysterectomy complains of a sudden, deep, aching pain in the right calf. The nurse notes the right calf is warm to the touch and visibly swollen compared to the left. What is the priority nursing action?

A) Instruct the client to remain in bed and notify the healthcare provider.
B) Perform passive range-of-motion exercises on the right leg.
C) Place a sequential compression device (SCD) on the affected leg.
D) Gently massage the right calf to help relieve the client's pain.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
The client is exhibiting classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT), including unilateral swelling, warmth, and calf pain. The priority nursing action is to prevent the clot from dislodging and causing a pulmonary embolism. The nurse should keep the client strictly on bed rest and immediately notify the healthcare provider for further diagnostic testing and orders (such as an ultrasound and anticoagulants). Massaging the calf (Option D), applying an SCD to the affected leg (Option C), or performing range-of-motion exercises (Option B) are strictly contraindicated because these actions can mechanically dislodge the clot.
</details>

---

### Question 124
A home health nurse is visiting an 82-year-old client who was recently prescribed a new antihypertensive medication. The client reports feeling dizzy and lightheaded whenever getting out of bed in the morning to use the restroom. Which instruction should the nurse provide?

A) "Stop taking the medication immediately until you see your doctor."
B) "Drink a large glass of cold water right before standing up."
C) "Exercise your legs briskly while standing to pump the blood up."
D) "Sit on the edge of the bed for a few minutes before standing."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
The client is experiencing orthostatic (postural) hypotension, a common side effect of antihypertensive medications, especially in older adults. The best nursing action is to teach the client to change positions slowly. Sitting on the edge of the bed (dangling) allows the cardiovascular system time to adjust to the change in posture, preventing dizziness and falls. The nurse should never advise a client to abruptly stop a prescribed antihypertensive medication (Option A) without consulting the provider, as this could cause rebound hypertension. Drinking cold water (Option B) does not immediately resolve orthostatic changes, and brisk exercise while standing (Option C) increases the immediate risk of a fall while the client is already dizzy.
</details>

---

### Question 125
A 58-year-old client arrives at the emergency department reporting chest pain. The nurse administers the first prescribed dose of sublingual nitroglycerin. Five minutes later, the client reports that their chest pain is gone, but they now have a pounding headache. Which action should the nurse take next?

A) Administer a second dose of nitroglycerin immediately.
B) Recognize this as an expected side effect and reassure the client.
C) Prepare to administer a dose of intravenous naloxone.
D) Notify the healthcare provider of a severe allergic reaction.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator used to relieve angina by dilating the coronary arteries. However, it also dilates cerebral blood vessels, which commonly causes a transient, pounding headache. The nurse should recognize this as a normal and expected side effect of the medication, reassure the client, and offer a mild analgesic (like acetaminophen) if prescribed. Since the chest pain is resolved, there is no need for a second dose of nitroglycerin (Option A). Naloxone (Option C) is an opioid antagonist and is irrelevant here. A headache is an expected side effect, not a sign of an allergic reaction (Option D).
</details>
### Question 126

A 45-year-old postoperative patient complains of a new, aching pain in the right calf while resting in bed. When the nurse assesses the patient's lower extremities, which clinical finding requires immediate intervention?

A) Bilateral cool, pale extremities with weak pedal pulses.
B) Unilateral calf swelling, redness, and warmth.
C) Numbness and a tingling sensation in both feet.
D) Pitting edema extending up to the thighs bilaterally.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
Unilateral calf swelling, redness, and warmth are classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT), which is a common postoperative complication requiring immediate intervention to prevent a pulmonary embolism. Option A suggests peripheral arterial disease. Option C suggests neuropathy. Option D suggests fluid volume overload or heart failure, which would typically be bilateral, unlike the unilateral presentation of DVT.
</details>

---

### Question 127

A 62-year-old patient in an outpatient clinic is scheduled to receive a regular morning dose of metoprolol for hypertension. Which assessment must the nurse prioritize before administering this medication?

A) Respiratory rate and oxygen saturation.
B) Bowel sounds and abdominal tenderness.
C) Apical pulse and blood pressure.
D) Capillary refill and pedal pulses.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Metoprolol is a beta-blocker that lowers both blood pressure and heart rate. The nurse must assess the patient's apical pulse and blood pressure before administration to ensure they are within safe parameters (typically withholding if the heart rate is less than 60 bpm or systolic blood pressure is less than 90 mmHg). While the other assessments are important for general care, they are not the specific priority assessments required prior to administering a beta-blocker.
</details>

---

### Question 128

A 78-year-old resident in a long-term care facility has a documented history of right-sided heart failure. While performing the morning assessment, which finding should the nurse expect to observe?

A) Noticeable distension of the jugular veins.
B) Crackles auscultated in the lung bases.
C) A dry, hacking cough that worsens at night.
D) Blood-tinged, frothy sputum.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
Right-sided heart failure causes systemic venous congestion because the right ventricle cannot pump blood effectively forward into the lungs, leading to backward backup. Expected findings include jugular vein distension (JVD), peripheral edema, and hepatomegaly. Options B, C, and D are classic signs of left-sided heart failure, where fluid backs up into the pulmonary system.
</details>

---

### Question 129

A 55-year-old patient arrives at the emergency department experiencing chest pain and is prescribed sublingual nitroglycerin. What is the priority safety instruction the nurse should provide when giving this medication?

A) "Chew the tablet thoroughly before swallowing it."
B) "Take this medication with a full glass of water."
C) "Keep the medication in a clear plastic bag in your pocket."
D) "Sit or lie down before placing the tablet under your tongue."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator that rapidly lowers blood pressure, frequently causing orthostatic hypotension and dizziness. The nurse must instruct the patient to sit or lie down to prevent falls and syncope. Option A is incorrect because sublingual tablets must dissolve under the tongue, not be chewed. Option B is incorrect as it is not swallowed with water. Option C is incorrect because nitroglycerin must be kept in a dark, light-resistant container to prevent degradation.
</details>

---

### Question 130

A 70-year-old patient on a medical-surgical unit reports severe cramping in the legs when walking down the hallway that goes away after resting for a few minutes. Suspecting peripheral arterial disease (PAD), the nurse examines the lower extremities. Which observation supports this condition?

A) Dark brown pigmentation around the ankles.
B) Shiny, hairless skin on the lower legs.
C) Warm, reddened calves that are tender to touch.
D) Bounding pedal pulses in both feet.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
The patient's symptom of intermittent claudication (pain with exercise that is relieved by rest) is a hallmark of peripheral arterial disease (PAD). Physical assessment findings of PAD include shiny, hairless skin on the lower legs, diminished or absent pulses, and cool extremities due to decreased arterial blood flow. Option A describes venous stasis dermatitis (venous insufficiency). Option C describes signs of a deep vein thrombosis. Option D contradicts PAD, as pulses would be weak or absent, not bounding.
</details>
### Question 131
A 55-year-old patient is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with a newly diagnosed deep vein thrombosis (DVT) in the right calf. The patient reports aching pain and moderate swelling in the affected leg. Which action should the nurse take to safely manage the patient's discomfort?

A. Place a hot, damp heating pad directly over the right calf for 45 minutes.  
B. Encourage the patient to ambulate vigorously in the hallway to push blood through the clot.  
C. Elevate the patient's right leg on a pillow while they are resting in bed.  
D. Firmly massage the patient's right calf to soothe the aching muscles.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Elevating the affected extremity promotes venous return and helps decrease localized swelling and aching associated with a DVT. <b>Option A</b> is incorrect because applying localized high heat can cause tissue damage and excessive vasodilation. <b>Option B</b> is incorrect because while early, controlled ambulation is often encouraged once a patient is anticoagulated, "vigorous" ambulation is unsafe. <b>Option D</b> is incorrect and highly dangerous; massaging a calf with a DVT can dislodge the clot, causing a pulmonary embolism.
</details>

---

### Question 132
A home health nurse is visiting a 78-year-old client who has a history of left-sided heart failure. The client asks the nurse how to best monitor for fluid retention at home so they can notify the doctor before breathing becomes difficult. Which instruction should the nurse provide?

A. "Weigh yourself every morning at the same time, using the same scale, after going to the bathroom."  
B. "Measure your blood pressure every evening before going to sleep and write it down."  
C. "Check your ankles for swelling once a week after you finish your daily walk."  
D. "Keep a strict written log of every single ounce of liquid you drink throughout the week."  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
Daily weights are the most reliable and easiest at-home indicator of fluid status in patients with heart failure. A sudden weight gain (e.g., 2-3 lbs in a day or 5 lbs in a week) indicates fluid retention. <b>Option B</b> is helpful for general cardiovascular health but does not directly or quickly measure fluid volume overload. <b>Option C</b> is incorrect because checking only once a week is too infrequent to catch acute fluid shifts. <b>Option D</b> is incorrect because while fluid restriction is sometimes necessary, measuring intake does not measure the fluid the body is actually retaining; daily weights are the superior monitoring tool.
</details>

---

### Question 133
A 42-year-old client receives their first dose of a newly prescribed oral antihypertensive medication in the outpatient clinic. Thirty minutes later, the client presses the call light and states, "I need to go to the restroom." What is the priority nursing action?

A. Bring a bedside commode into the client's examination room immediately.  
B. Tell the client to walk quickly to the restroom before the medication takes full effect.  
C. Allow the client to walk to the restroom independently while the nurse waits outside.  
D. Assist the client to stand slowly and escort them safely to the restroom.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Patients receiving a first dose of an antihypertensive medication are at a high risk for orthostatic (postural) hypotension, which can cause dizziness and falls. Assisting them to stand slowly and escorting them ensures patient safety. <b>Option A</b> is unnecessary if the patient is capable of walking with assistance. <b>Option B</b> is unsafe, as moving quickly increases the risk of a fall. <b>Option C</b> is unsafe because the nurse must directly assist the patient to prevent injury from potential dizziness.
</details>

---

### Question 134
An 85-year-old resident in a long-term care facility complains of cramping pain in both lower legs after walking to the dining hall. The resident has a known history of severe peripheral arterial disease (PAD). Which action should the nurse perform first?

A. Elevate the resident's legs above the level of the heart on a stack of pillows.  
B. Palpate the resident's dorsalis pedis and posterior tibial pulses.  
C. Apply sequential compression devices (SCDs) tightly to both lower legs.  
D. Instruct the resident to continue walking immediately to build collateral circulation.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
The resident is experiencing intermittent claudication, a classic sign of PAD. The nurse's first action should be a basic vascular assessment, which includes checking the pedal pulses (dorsalis pedis and posterior tibial) to ensure adequate blood flow to the extremities. <b>Option A</b> is incorrect because elevating the legs in a patient with arterial disease makes it harder for oxygenated blood to reach the tissues, worsening the pain. <b>Option C</b> is incorrect because SCDs are used to prevent venous thrombosis, not to treat arterial disease, and could further compromise arterial flow. <b>Option D</b> is incorrect; while walking programs are good for PAD, the patient should rest until the current claudication pain subsides before resuming activity.
</details>

---

### Question 135
A 60-year-old patient is admitted to the emergency department for observation of stable angina. While resting in bed, the patient begins to experience chest pain. The nurse administers one sublingual nitroglycerin tablet as ordered. Which action should the nurse take next?

A. Prepare to administer an immediate dose of intravenous epinephrine.  
B. Instruct the patient to chew a second nitroglycerin tablet immediately.  
C. Reassess the patient's pain level and blood pressure in 5 minutes.  
D. Encourage the patient to take deep breaths and walk slowly around the room.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
The standard protocol for sublingual nitroglycerin is to administer one tablet, wait 5 minutes, and then reassess the patient's chest pain and blood pressure. Nitroglycerin is a vasodilator that can cause significant hypotension, so checking blood pressure is vital. <b>Option A</b> is incorrect because epinephrine increases heart rate and oxygen demand, which worsens angina. <b>Option B</b> is incorrect; sublingual nitroglycerin should be allowed to dissolve under the tongue, not chewed, and doses must be spaced 5 minutes apart. <b>Option D</b> is incorrect because a patient experiencing chest pain should remain on bed rest to decrease the heart's workload and oxygen demand.
</details>
### Question 136
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is preparing to measure the blood pressure of a 45-year-old male client who is being evaluated for primary hypertension. Which action should the nurse take to ensure an accurate baseline reading?

A) Ask the client to hold his breath continuously during the measurement.  
B) Instruct the client to sit back in the chair with both feet flat on the floor.  
C) Apply the blood pressure cuff tightly over the client's long-sleeved shirt.  
D) Position the client's arm comfortably resting below his heart level.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
To obtain an accurate blood pressure reading, the nurse should ensure the client is seated comfortably with their back supported and both feet flat on the floor. Crossing the legs or letting them dangle can artificially elevate the blood pressure reading. Option A is incorrect because holding the breath (Valsalva maneuver) can acutely alter blood pressure. Option C is incorrect because the cuff should be placed on a bare arm, as clothing can interfere with the reading. Option D is incorrect because the arm should be supported at heart level; positioning it below heart level can result in an artificially high reading.
</details>

---

### Question 137
A nurse at a long-term care facility is caring for a 78-year-old female client with a history of right-sided heart failure. The provider has ordered daily weights to monitor for fluid overload. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate when obtaining the client's weight?

A) Weigh the client immediately after she consumes her largest meal of the day.  
B) Use a different scale each day to verify the facility's equipment calibration.  
C) Have the client wear a heavy winter coat to maintain her privacy and warmth.  
D) Weigh the client at the same time every morning after she voids.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Daily weights are the most reliable indicator of fluid status in clients with heart failure. To ensure accuracy and consistency, the client should be weighed at the same time every day (preferably in the morning), using the same scale, in similar clothing, and after emptying her bladder. Options A, B, and C introduce variables that will cause inaccurate fluctuations in the client's daily weight trending. 
</details>

---

### Question 138
A nurse on a surgical unit is caring for a 62-year-old male client who is 12 hours post-operative following an open appendectomy. The nurse is implementing interventions to prevent the development of deep vein thrombosis (DVT). Which action should the nurse take?

A) Assist the client to ambulate in the hallway as tolerated.  
B) Place a firm pillow directly under the client's knees while he is in bed.  
C) Massage the client's calves vigorously to promote venous circulation.  
D) Encourage the client to sit in a bedside chair with his legs securely crossed.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Early ambulation is one of the most effective interventions to promote venous return and prevent DVT in post-operative clients. Option B is incorrect because placing a pillow under the knees puts pressure on the popliteal space, which can impede venous return and cause pooling of blood. Option C is incorrect because massaging the calves can dislodge a clot if one has already formed, leading to a pulmonary embolism. Option D is incorrect because crossing the legs compresses the veins and restricts blood flow, increasing the risk of clot formation.
</details>

---

### Question 139
A home health nurse is visiting a 68-year-old female client who was recently diagnosed with peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The client reports experiencing a deep aching pain in her lower legs that awakens her at night. Which action should the nurse suggest to help relieve the client's pain?

A) Apply ice packs to the lower legs thirty minutes before going to sleep.  
B) Elevate both legs on two pillows while resting flat in bed.  
C) Dangle the legs over the side of the bed when the pain occurs.  
D) Wear tightly fitted knee-high compression stockings while sleeping.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
In peripheral arterial disease (PAD), arterial blood flow to the lower extremities is restricted. Dangling the legs over the side of the bed places them in a dependent position, allowing gravity to assist arterial blood flow to the feet and relieving ischemic rest pain. Option A is incorrect because cold causes vasoconstriction, which further restricts blood flow. Option B is incorrect because elevating the legs works against gravity and makes it harder for arterial blood to reach the tissues (elevation is useful for venous insufficiency, not arterial). Option D is incorrect because compression stockings can further restrict arterial blood flow in PAD.
</details>

---

### Question 140
A nurse in the emergency department is discharging a 55-year-old male client who was treated for a minor episode of stable angina. The client has a new prescription for sublingual nitroglycerin tablets. Which observation during a teach-back session indicates the client correctly understands how to take the medication?

A) The client states he will chew the tablet quickly to speed up the medication's absorption.  
B) The client places the tablet directly under his tongue and lets it dissolve completely.  
C) The client swallows the tablet whole with a full, eight-ounce glass of tap water.  
D) The client places the tablet between his cheek and gum, then drinks warm tea.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Sublingual nitroglycerin is designed to be absorbed quickly through the highly vascular mucosa under the tongue. The client must place the tablet under the tongue and allow it to dissolve completely without swallowing it. Option A is incorrect because chewing the tablet prevents proper sublingual absorption. Option C is incorrect because swallowing the tablet subjects it to the first-pass effect in the liver, rendering it ineffective for acute angina. Option D describes the buccal route rather than the sublingual route, and drinking fluids while a sublingual tablet is dissolving can wash the medication down the throat.
</details>
### Question 141
A nurse is assessing a 72-year-old client in the cardiology clinic who has a history of right-sided heart failure. During the physical assessment, which clinical manifestation should the nurse expect to observe?

A) Crackles auscultated in the lung bases  
B) Jugular venous distention  
C) A persistent, dry, hacking cough  
D) Complaints of severe orthopnea  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Jugular venous distention  

**Rationale:** 
Right-sided heart failure prevents the heart from effectively pumping blood into the lungs, causing blood to back up into the systemic venous circulation. This results in peripheral symptoms such as jugular venous distention (JVD), lower extremity edema, and hepatomegaly. Crackles in the lungs (A), a persistent cough (C), and orthopnea (D) are classic signs of left-sided heart failure, which causes fluid to back up into the pulmonary system.
</details>

---

### Question 142
A nurse on a surgical unit is caring for a 45-year-old client who is 2 days post-operative following an abdominal hysterectomy. The client reports new, localized pain and swelling in the right calf. What is the nurse's priority action?

A) Vigorously massage the right calf to relieve the pain.  
B) Apply a warm, moist compress to the calf and reassess in an hour.  
C) Keep the client on bed rest and notify the healthcare provider immediately.  
D) Assist the client to ambulate in the hallway to promote circulation.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Keep the client on bed rest and notify the healthcare provider immediately.  

**Rationale:** 
The client is exhibiting classic signs of a deep vein thrombosis (DVT). The nurse's priority is to keep the client safe from complications, specifically a pulmonary embolism. The nurse should keep the client in bed to prevent dislodging the clot and notify the healthcare provider immediately to obtain orders for a diagnostic ultrasound and anticoagulation therapy. Massaging the calf (A) or ambulating (D) can cause the clot to break loose and travel to the lungs. A warm compress (B) delays definitive diagnosis and treatment.
</details>

---

### Question 143
A 68-year-old client arrives at the emergency department complaining of sudden chest pain. The physician prescribes a sublingual nitroglycerin tablet. After administering the first dose, what is the most important assessment for the nurse to perform?

A) Monitor the client's blood pressure.  
B) Check the client's pupil reactivity.  
C) Assess the client's deep tendon reflexes.  
D) Auscultate the client's bowel sounds.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Monitor the client's blood pressure.  

**Rationale:** 
Nitroglycerin is a potent vasodilator used to relieve angina by opening up the coronary arteries. Because it causes systemic vasodilation, a common and potentially dangerous side effect is a sudden drop in blood pressure (hypotension). The nurse must closely monitor the client's blood pressure before and after administration. Pupil reactivity (B), deep tendon reflexes (C), and bowel sounds (D) are not directly affected by this medication and are not the priority assessments in this cardiac scenario.
</details>

---

### Question 144
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is evaluating the lower extremities of a 78-year-old client diagnosed with peripheral arterial disease (PAD). The nurse palpates for the pedal pulses but is unable to feel them. What should the nurse do next?

A) Document the absence of pulses as an expected finding for a client with PAD.  
B) Elevate the client's legs above the level of the heart to promote venous return.  
C) Apply an ice pack to the client's feet to reduce localized tissue swelling.  
D) Obtain a Doppler ultrasound device to check for blood flow in the feet.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Obtain a Doppler ultrasound device to check for blood flow in the feet.  

**Rationale:** 
If a nurse is unable to palpate a peripheral pulse, the immediate next step is to use a Doppler ultrasound device to determine if blood flow is present but simply too weak to feel by hand. Documenting it as expected without verifying with a Doppler (A) is unsafe, as the client could be experiencing acute arterial occlusion. Elevating the legs (B) is contraindicated in PAD because it further decreases arterial blood flow to the extremities. Applying ice (C) causes vasoconstriction, which worsens arterial perfusion.
</details>

---

### Question 145
A nurse is reviewing a dietary log with a 30-year-old client newly diagnosed with essential hypertension. The nurse has just provided teaching on a low-sodium diet. Which lunch choice selected by the client indicates an understanding of the teaching?

A) Canned tomato soup with a sleeve of saltine crackers  
B) A grilled chicken breast with a fresh side salad and oil-based dressing  
C) A turkey, cheese, and salami deli meat sandwich with dill pickles  
D) Two slices of fast-food pepperoni pizza with extra cheese  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) A grilled chicken breast with a fresh side salad and oil-based dressing  

**Rationale:** 
A grilled chicken breast with a fresh salad is a whole-food meal that is naturally low in sodium, indicating the client understands the dietary teaching for hypertension. Canned soups and crackers (A), deli meats and pickles (C), and pepperoni pizza with cheese (D) are all highly processed foods containing excessive amounts of sodium, which can exacerbate hypertension.
</details>
### Question 146
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is assessing a 45-year-old client with type 1 diabetes. The client suddenly reports feeling shaky, and the nurse observes diaphoresis and mild hand tremors. The client's point-of-care capillary blood glucose is 58 mg/dL. Which of the following is the most appropriate initial nursing action?

A) Administer a rapid-acting insulin lispro injection.
B) Provide a high-protein, high-fat snack.
C) Give the client 4 ounces of regular fruit juice.
D) Recheck the blood glucose in 15 minutes before intervening.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The client is experiencing symptomatic hypoglycemia (blood glucose less than 70 mg/dL). According to the "rule of 15," the nurse should immediately provide 15 grams of fast-acting, easily absorbed carbohydrates, such as 4 ounces (120 mL) of regular fruit juice. Giving insulin (A) would lower the blood sugar further, causing severe harm. High-protein/high-fat snacks (B) delay carbohydrate absorption and are not suitable for initial correction. Waiting 15 minutes to recheck without treating (D) places the client at risk for severe hypoglycemia, seizures, or coma.
</details>

---

### Question 147
A nurse in a long-term care facility is preparing to administer morning medications to an 82-year-old resident who was recently prescribed oral levothyroxine for hypothyroidism. To ensure optimal absorption of this medication, which action should the nurse take?

A) Administer the medication 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast.
B) Give the medication along with an antacid to prevent stomach upset.
C) Crush the medication and mix it into a bowl of high-fiber oatmeal.
D) Administer the medication immediately after the resident finishes their morning meal.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Levothyroxine should be administered on an empty stomach, ideally 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast, to ensure optimal absorption. Antacids, calcium supplements, and iron (B) can bind to the medication and severely decrease its absorption. Food, especially high-fiber meals (C) or eating immediately before taking the pill (D), also impairs the intestinal absorption of levothyroxine, rendering the treatment less effective.
</details>

---

### Question 148
A 28-year-old client on a medical-surgical unit is being discharged with a prescription for oral prednisone after experiencing a severe systemic allergic reaction. During discharge teaching, the nurse observes the client packing their belongings and discussing their new medication. Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching was effective?

A) "I should stop taking this medication as soon as I feel completely better."
B) "I will take this medication right before I go to bed to sleep better."
C) "I can drink grapefruit juice while taking this medication without any issues."
D) "I need to gradually decrease my dose as instructed by my doctor instead of stopping suddenly."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Corticosteroids like prednisone suppress the body's natural adrenal function. They must be tapered gradually rather than stopped abruptly (A) to prevent a potentially life-threatening Addisonian (adrenal) crisis. Prednisone can cause insomnia, so it is best taken in the morning, not at bedtime (B). While grapefruit juice (C) interacts with many medications, the primary safety teaching for oral corticosteroids is the absolute necessity of a tapering schedule.
</details>
