### Question 217
A nurse is conducting a telehealth follow-up with a 68-year-old client who was recently prescribed levothyroxine for hypothyroidism. Which statement by the client indicates that the nurse's medication teaching was effective?

A) "I will take this medication right before I go to sleep."
B) "I should take this pill in the morning on an empty stomach."
C) "I can take this medication with my morning calcium supplement."
D) "I will stop taking the medication when my energy levels improve."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
Levothyroxine should be taken in the morning on an empty stomach, typically 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast, to maximize absorption. Taking it with other medications, particularly calcium or iron supplements, can significantly decrease its absorption. Levothyroxine is a lifelong medication for hypothyroidism and should not be stopped once symptoms improve. Taking it at bedtime can cause insomnia and reduces optimal absorption depending on the timing of the last meal.
</details>

---

### Question 218
While assessing a 35-year-old client with Graves' disease in the outpatient endocrine clinic, the nurse observes notable exophthalmos. The client reports that their eyes feel gritty and irritated. Which recommendation should the nurse make?

A) "Limit your daily fluid intake to reduce the swelling around your eyes."
B) "Apply warm, moist compresses to your eyes every morning."
C) "Use over-the-counter artificial tears regularly to keep your eyes moist."
D) "Rub your eyes gently to stimulate your natural tear production."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Exophthalmos (bulging of the eyes) is a common manifestation of Graves' disease (hyperthyroidism). Because the eyes bulge, the eyelids may not close completely, leading to dry, irritated, and exposed corneas. Using artificial tears helps keep the eyes lubricated and prevents corneal abrasions. Rubbing the eyes increases the risk of injury. Fluid restriction is not indicated. Cold compresses, not warm, are sometimes used to decrease swelling.
</details>

---

### Question 219
The nurse is admitting a 55-year-old client who has been taking high doses of oral corticosteroids for several months to treat severe asthma. During the physical assessment, which finding should the nurse identify as a complication of this medication?

A) Purple striae on the abdomen and a rounded face
B) Profound, unintentional weight loss
C) A severely slow and irregular heart rate
D) Dark, bronze hyperpigmentation of the skin

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
Long-term corticosteroid use can lead to iatrogenic Cushing's syndrome. Clinical manifestations of excess cortisol include a rounded "moon" face, fat deposition on the back of the neck ("buffalo hump"), weight gain, thin skin, and purple striae (stretch marks) on the abdomen. Weight loss and bronze hyperpigmentation are associated with Addison's disease (adrenal insufficiency), not corticosteroid excess. Bradycardia is not a typical side effect of corticosteroids.
</details>

---

### Question 220
A 40-year-old client has just returned to the neurosurgical unit following the removal of a pituitary tumor. The nurse observes that the client's urinary catheter has drained 1,500 mL of pale, clear urine over the last hour. Which additional finding should the nurse expect if the client is developing diabetes insipidus?

A) Significant generalized peripheral edema
B) High urine osmolarity
C) A dangerously elevated blood glucose level
D) A low urine specific gravity

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Diabetes insipidus (DI) is a potential complication of pituitary surgery due to a deficiency of antidiuretic hormone (ADH). A lack of ADH causes the kidneys to excrete massive amounts of dilute urine. Because the urine is highly dilute, the urine specific gravity will be remarkably low (typically less than 1.005). Urine osmolarity would be low, not high. Edema is seen with fluid retention (like SIADH), whereas DI causes severe dehydration. DI is related to water balance, not blood glucose levels.
</details>
### Question 221

A nurse in a long-term care facility is preparing to administer morning medications to a 78-year-old female client diagnosed with hypothyroidism. The client is prescribed oral levothyroxine. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate?

A) Administer the medication with a high-fiber breakfast to prevent constipation.
B) Give the medication on an empty stomach 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast.
C) Hold the medication if the client's heart rate is below 80 beats per minute.
D) Administer the medication at bedtime along with the client's calcium supplement.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Levothyroxine should be taken on an empty stomach, usually in the morning, 30 to 60 minutes before the first meal, beverage, or other medications. This ensures optimal absorption of the medication. Taking it with food (especially high-fiber foods) or supplements like calcium drastically decreases its absorption. A heart rate below 80 is normal and expected; holding the medication would be inappropriate.
</details>

### Question 222

A 10-year-old boy with type 1 diabetes mellitus is brought to the school nurse's office after feeling weak during gym class. The nurse observes that the child is pale, profusely sweating, and his hands are visibly shaking. Which action should the nurse take immediately?

A) Provide the child with 4 ounces of apple juice.
B) Administer the scheduled dose of regular insulin.
C) Encourage the child to rest in a dark, quiet room.
D) Offer the child a snack of celery and peanut butter.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The child is exhibiting classic signs of hypoglycemia (pallor, diaphoresis, tremors, and weakness). The nurse should immediately provide 15 grams of a fast-acting, simple carbohydrate, such as 4 ounces of juice, to rapidly raise the blood glucose level. Giving insulin would worsen the hypoglycemia. While peanut butter contains some carbohydrates, its high fat content slows down glucose absorption, making it inappropriate for rapid correction of hypoglycemia.
</details>

### Question 223

A nurse is completing an admission assessment at an outpatient clinic for a 45-year-old male client with suspected Cushing's syndrome. Which clinical manifestation should the nurse expect to observe during the physical examination?

A) Noticeable weight loss and increased muscle mass in the extremities
B) A significantly abnormally low resting blood pressure
C) A thick, swollen neck and reports of severe intolerance to cold
D) Increased adipose tissue around the trunk and a rounded, flushed face

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Cushing's syndrome is caused by an excess of corticosteroids (glucocorticoids). Common physical findings include truncal obesity, a rounded "moon face," and an accumulation of fat on the upper back ("buffalo hump"). Patients typically experience muscle wasting and weakness in the extremities, not increased muscle mass. Hypertension is common due to fluid retention, and cold intolerance with a swollen neck (goiter) is a sign of hypothyroidism, not Cushing's syndrome.
</details>

---

### Question 224

A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is evaluating the dietary choices of a 29-year-old female client recently diagnosed with hyperthyroidism (Graves' disease). Which lunch selection by the client indicates to the nurse that the dietary teaching was effective?

A) A mixed green salad with fat-free dressing and a diet iced tea
B) A small cup of clear chicken broth and a few plain saltine crackers
C) A large grilled chicken breast, baked potato, and a milk shake
D) A bowl of steamed spinach and a large glass of grapefruit juice

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Hyperthyroidism drastically increases the patient's metabolic rate, which can lead to severe weight loss and tissue wasting if caloric intake is inadequate. Patients require a high-calorie, high-protein diet with frequent meals and snacks to meet their increased metabolic demands. A grilled chicken breast, baked potato, and milk shake provide excellent sources of calories, protein, and carbohydrates. Options A, B, and D do not provide sufficient calories or macronutrients for a hypermetabolic state.
</details>

---

### Question 225

A nurse in a step-down unit is caring for a 55-year-old male client who recently suffered a traumatic brain injury. The nurse suspects the client is developing diabetes insipidus. Which observation by the nurse strongly supports this suspicion?

A) The client's routine blood glucose reading suddenly spikes to 350 mg/dL.
B) The client's urinary catheter bag fills rapidly with pale, dilute urine.
C) The client develops widespread peripheral edema in the lower legs.
D) The client begins exhibiting deep, rapid Kussmaul respirations.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Diabetes insipidus (DI) is an endocrine disorder caused by a deficiency of antidiuretic hormone (ADH), which frequently occurs following head trauma, neurosurgery, or brain tumors. Without ADH, the kidneys cannot concentrate urine, resulting in a hallmark presentation of extreme polyuria (massive amounts of pale, dilute urine) and polydipsia. DI is entirely unrelated to blood glucose levels (Option A), and because the patient is losing massive amounts of fluid, they are at risk for dehydration, not peripheral edema (Option C). Kussmaul respirations are associated with diabetic ketoacidosis, not DI (Option D).
</details>
### Question 226
A nurse in a long-term care facility is preparing to administer morning medications to a 72-year-old female resident newly diagnosed with hypothyroidism. The provider has prescribed oral levothyroxine. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate when administering this medication?

A) Administer the medication with a full glass of milk at bedtime.
B) Crush the medication and mix it into the resident's oatmeal at breakfast.
C) Give the medication on an empty stomach 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast.
D) Provide the medication immediately after the resident finishes a large meal.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Give the medication on an empty stomach 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast.

**Rationale:** Levothyroxine must be administered on an empty stomach, typically 30 to 60 minutes before the first meal of the day, to ensure optimal absorption. Administering it with food, especially calcium-rich items like milk or oatmeal, can significantly decrease its absorption in the gastrointestinal tract. 
</details>

### Question 227
A school nurse is called to the playground to assess a 10-year-old boy with a known history of type 1 diabetes mellitus. The child is sweating profusely, trembling, and complains of feeling dizzy. A quick capillary blood glucose check reveals a level of 52 mg/dL. Which immediate action should the nurse take?

A) Provide the child with 4 ounces of apple juice.
B) Administer a scheduled dose of regular insulin.
C) Encourage the child to run a lap to mobilize stored glucose.
D) Give the child a snack of string cheese and turkey slices.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Provide the child with 4 ounces of apple juice.

**Rationale:** The child is exhibiting classic signs of hypoglycemia (blood sugar < 70 mg/dL). The nurse's immediate action should be to provide a fast-acting, simple carbohydrate, such as 4 ounces (1/2 cup) of fruit juice, to quickly raise the blood glucose level. Giving insulin would worsen the hypoglycemia. Protein and fat (like cheese and turkey) delay glucose absorption and are not appropriate for immediate treatment, though they can be given *after* the blood sugar stabilizes. Exercise would further deplete glucose stores.
</details>

### Question 228
A nurse in an endocrine outpatient clinic is caring for a 35-year-old female client diagnosed with Graves' disease. During the assessment, the nurse notes that the client has prominent, bulging eyes and reports frequent eye dryness and irritation. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate to include in the client's care plan?

A) Apply warm, moist compresses to the eyes for 20 minutes daily.
B) Instruct the client to tape her eyes completely shut during the day.
C) Teach the client to perform rigorous eye-stretching exercises.
D) Provide the client with lubricating artificial tears to use as needed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Provide the client with lubricating artificial tears to use as needed.

**Rationale:** Exophthalmos (bulging of the eyes) is a common clinical manifestation of Graves' disease (hyperthyroidism). It prevents the eyelids from closing completely, leading to dry, irritated corneas. Administering artificial tears helps keep the eyes lubricated and prevents corneal ulceration. Taping eyes is generally reserved for sleep, not daytime. Warm compresses and rigorous stretching are not standard or safe treatments for this condition.
</details>

---

### Question 229
A 45-year-old male client is recovering in the neurological intensive care unit following severe head trauma. The nurse suspects the client may be developing diabetes insipidus. Which clinical observation would most strongly support this suspicion?

A) A sudden weight gain of 5 pounds over the past 24 hours.
B) A large volume of pale, dilute urine filling the catheter bag rapidly.
C) A bounding pulse with significantly elevated blood pressure.
D) A localized pitting edema in the client's lower extremities.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) A large volume of pale, dilute urine filling the catheter bag rapidly.

**Rationale:** Diabetes insipidus (DI) can occur after head trauma due to a deficiency of antidiuretic hormone (ADH). Without ADH, the kidneys cannot concentrate urine, resulting in the excretion of massive amounts of very dilute, pale urine (polyuria). Weight gain, bounding pulses, elevated blood pressure, and edema are signs of fluid volume overload, which is characteristic of Syndrome of Inappropriate Antidiuretic Hormone (SIADH), the opposite of DI.
</details>

---

### Question 230
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is performing an admission assessment on a 55-year-old female client who has been taking high doses of prescription corticosteroids for a severe autoimmune condition. The nurse is evaluating the client for signs of Cushing's syndrome. Which physical observation should the nurse expect to document?

A) Extreme weight loss and darkened pigmentation on the palms.
B) Cold intolerance and a visibly enlarged thyroid gland.
C) A rounded "moon" face and a fat pad at the base of the neck.
D) Persistent tachycardia and fine tremors in both resting hands.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) A rounded "moon" face and a fat pad at the base of the neck.

**Rationale:** Prolonged use of exogenous corticosteroids can lead to Cushing's syndrome. Classic physical findings observed by the nurse include a rounded "moon" face, a cervicothoracic fat pad ("buffalo hump"), central obesity, and purple striae on the abdomen. Weight loss and hyperpigmentation are signs of Addison's disease. Cold intolerance and goiter are related to thyroid issues, while tachycardia and tremors are signs of hyperthyroidism.
</details>
### Question 231
A 10-year-old student comes to the school nurse's office reporting shakiness, sweating, and feeling dizzy after participating in a physical education class. The student's health record indicates a history of type 1 diabetes mellitus. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Administer the student's prescribed rapid-acting insulin injection.
B) Provide the student with 4 ounces of regular apple juice.
C) Encourage the student to lie down and rest for 30 minutes.
D) Call the student's parents to come and take them home immediately.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Provide the student with 4 ounces of regular apple juice.

**Rationale:** The student is exhibiting classic signs of mild to moderate hypoglycemia (shakiness, diaphoresis, dizziness) brought on by physical exertion. The immediate nursing action is to treat the hypoglycemia by providing a fast-acting, 15-gram simple carbohydrate, such as 4 ounces of fruit juice, to raise the blood glucose levels safely and quickly. Insulin would worsen the condition, and having the student rest or wait for parents delays necessary treatment.
</details>

### Question 232
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is reviewing medication instructions with a 70-year-old client who was newly prescribed levothyroxine for hypothyroidism. Which statement by the client indicates that the nurse's teaching has been effective?

A) "I should take this medication right before I go to bed at night."
B) "I will take the pill with my breakfast, especially if I have milk."
C) "I can stop taking this medication once my energy levels return to normal."
D) "I will take this pill every morning on an empty stomach."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) "I will take this pill every morning on an empty stomach."

**Rationale:** Levothyroxine must be taken in the morning on an empty stomach, typically 30 to 60 minutes before eating breakfast, to ensure optimal absorption. Taking it with food or calcium-rich products (like milk) decreases its absorption. It is a lifelong medication for hypothyroidism and should never be stopped abruptly, even if symptoms improve.
</details>

### Question 233
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 28-year-old client who has Graves' disease and exhibits prominent exophthalmos. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate to include in this client's plan of care?

A) Instill artificial tears into the client's eyes regularly.
B) Keep the head of the client's bed completely flat at all times.
C) Tape the client's eyelids open while they are sleeping.
D) Apply warm, moist compresses to the eyes every hour.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Instill artificial tears into the client's eyes regularly.

**Rationale:** Exophthalmos (bulging of the eyes) in Graves' disease can prevent the eyelids from closing entirely, leading to dry, irritated corneas and an increased risk of corneal abrasions. Instilling artificial tears helps maintain eye moisture. The head of the bed should be elevated (not flat) to help reduce periorbital edema, and eyelids may be taped *closed* (never open) to protect the eyes during sleep. 
</details>

### Question 234
A 45-year-old client is admitted to the hospital for a diagnostic evaluation of suspected Cushing's syndrome. While completing the admission assessment, which physical finding should the nurse expect to observe?

A) Unexplained weight loss and severe heat intolerance.
B) Hyperpigmentation of the skin on the hands and face.
C) Purple striae on the abdomen and a rounded face.
D) A significantly enlarged thyroid gland in the neck.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Purple striae on the abdomen and a rounded face.

**Rationale:** Cushing's syndrome is caused by an excess of corticosteroids (glucocorticoids). Classic physical assessment findings include a rounded "moon" face, truncal obesity, thin extremities, fragile skin, and purple striae (stretch marks) on the abdomen. Weight loss and heat intolerance are associated with hyperthyroidism, while hyperpigmentation is a classic sign of Addison's disease.
</details>

### Question 235
A nurse in the neuro-intensive care unit is assessing a 35-year-old client who just returned from a transsphenoidal hypophysectomy (surgical removal of a pituitary tumor). The nurse notes that the client has voided 1,500 mL of pale, clear urine over the last hour. What is the priority action for the nurse to take?

A) Notify the healthcare provider immediately.
B) Administer a prescribed loop diuretic to reduce fluid retention.
C) Encourage the client to aggressively drink oral fluids.
D) Document this as a normal expected postoperative diuresis.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Notify the healthcare provider immediately.

**Rationale:** A massive output of very dilute urine (e.g., 1,500 mL in a single hour) following pituitary surgery is a classic sign of diabetes insipidus (DI). This is a potentially life-threatening complication caused by a deficiency in antidiuretic hormone (ADH) due to surgical trauma. The nurse must notify the provider immediately so that appropriate treatment (such as IV fluids and desmopressin) can be initiated to prevent severe dehydration and hypovolemic shock.
</details>
### Question 236
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is teaching a 45-year-old female patient newly prescribed levothyroxine for hypothyroidism. Which statement by the patient indicates to the nurse that the teaching was successful?

A) "I will take my medication every night right before I go to bed."
B) "I will take this pill in the morning on an empty stomach."
C) "I should take this medication with an antacid if it upsets my stomach."
D) "I can stop taking this medication once my energy levels return to normal."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Levothyroxine should be taken in the morning on an empty stomach, typically 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast, to maximize its absorption. Taking it at night or with food can decrease its efficacy. Antacids, iron, and calcium supplements can severely inhibit the absorption of levothyroxine and should be taken hours apart. Hypothyroidism is generally a lifelong condition, so the patient should not stop the medication just because their symptoms improve. 
</details>

---

### Question 237
A nurse on a pediatric unit observes a 9-year-old boy with type 1 diabetes mellitus who is suddenly sweating profusely, trembling, and appearing highly irritable. What is the most appropriate initial action for the nurse to take?

A) Administer a scheduled dose of regular insulin.
B) Have the child drink a large glass of plain water.
C) Instruct the child to rest quietly in a dark room.
D) Perform a capillary blood glucose check.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** The child is exhibiting classic signs of hypoglycemia (sweating, tremors, irritability). The nurse's first action must be to assess the patient's blood glucose level to confirm hypoglycemia before intervening with a fast-acting carbohydrate. Administering insulin would fatally exacerbate hypoglycemia. Giving plain water or having the child rest does not address the underlying physiological issue of low blood sugar.
</details>

---

### Question 238
A nurse is caring for a 28-year-old female patient admitted with Graves' disease who is experiencing severe exophthalmos. Which nursing action should be included in the patient's daily care plan?

A) Administer artificial tears as prescribed to keep the corneas moist.
B) Keep the patient's room brightly lit to stimulate visual acuity.
C) Apply warm, dry compresses to the eyes every hour.
D) Encourage the patient to look upwards frequently to stretch the eye muscles.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** Exophthalmos (bulging of the eyes) is a common manifestation of Graves' disease (hyperthyroidism). Because the eyelids may not close completely, the corneas are at high risk for dryness, irritation, and injury. Administering artificial tears helps maintain moisture and protect the cornea. The nurse should also encourage the use of dark glasses (not bright lights) to prevent photophobia, tape the eyelids shut at night if necessary, and elevate the head of the bed to reduce periorbital edema.
</details>

---

### Question 239
A nurse is completing an admission assessment on a 62-year-old male patient with suspected Cushing's syndrome on a medical-surgical floor. Which physical observation should the nurse expect to document for this patient?

A) Unexplained weight loss and severe muscle hypertrophy.
B) Frequent episodes of severe, unexpected hypoglycemia.
C) The presence of purple striae across the abdomen.
D) Pale, cool, and clammy skin upon palpation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Cushing's syndrome results from an excess of corticosteroids (glucocorticoids). Classic physical assessment findings include truncal obesity, a "moon face," a "buffalo hump," thin extremities due to muscle wasting (not hypertrophy), and purple striae (stretch marks) on the abdomen, breasts, or buttocks due to thinning of the skin. Patients typically experience hyperglycemia, not hypoglycemia, due to the excess cortisol.
</details>

---

### Question 240
A nurse in a trauma unit is monitoring a 16-year-old female patient who developed diabetes insipidus following a traumatic head injury. She is currently receiving desmopressin (DDAVP). Which clinical observation indicates to the nurse that the medication is effective?

A) The patient's blood glucose levels stabilize within normal limits.
B) The patient's hourly urine output decreases to a normal volume.
C) The patient exhibits a steady increase in core body temperature.
D) The patient reports a sudden, intense craving for salty snacks.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Diabetes insipidus (DI) is caused by a deficiency of antidiuretic hormone (ADH), leading to massive polyuria (excessive urination) and profound dehydration. Desmopressin (DDAVP) is a synthetic form of ADH used to replace the missing hormone. The nurse will know the medication is effective when the kidneys begin to reabsorb water, resulting in a decrease in urine output and an increase in urine specific gravity. DI is unrelated to blood glucose control.
</details>
### Question 241
A 68-year-old resident in a long-term care facility is prescribed daily levothyroxine for hypothyroidism. When planning the morning medication administration, which action by the nurse is correct?

A. Administer the medication immediately after the resident finishes breakfast.  
B. Give the medication on an empty stomach, 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast.  
C. Administer the pill at the same time as the patient's daily iron supplement.  
D. Provide the medication with a calcium-based antacid to prevent stomach upset.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Give the medication on an empty stomach, 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast.  

**Rationale:** Levothyroxine must be administered on an empty stomach, typically 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast, to ensure proper and consistent absorption. Giving the medication with food (Option A) or with specific supplements like iron or calcium antacids (Options C and D) significantly decreases the gastrointestinal absorption of the thyroid hormone, rendering the treatment less effective.
</details>

---

### Question 242
A 14-year-old student with type 1 diabetes arrives at the school nurse's clinic complaining of feeling shaky and dizzy. The nurse observes that the student's skin is pale and profusely sweating. Which action should the nurse take first?

A. Administer a scheduled dose of subcutaneous regular insulin.  
B. Instruct the student to rest quietly on a cot for 20 minutes.  
C. Offer the student a sugar-free diet soda to drink immediately.  
D. Perform a capillary blood glucose test to assess for hypoglycemia.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Perform a capillary blood glucose test to assess for hypoglycemia.  

**Rationale:** The student is exhibiting classic clinical signs of hypoglycemia (shakiness, dizziness, pallor, and diaphoresis). The nurse's first action should be to verify the blood glucose level to confirm hypoglycemia before treating. Giving insulin (Option A) would dangerously lower the blood sugar further. Resting (Option B) ignores a potentially life-threatening drop in blood sugar. Diet soda (Option C) does not contain carbohydrates and will not raise the student's blood sugar; if hypoglycemia is confirmed, a simple carbohydrate (like regular juice or regular soda) should be given.
</details>

### Question 243
A 42-year-old male patient is being evaluated in an outpatient endocrine clinic for suspected Cushing's syndrome. During the physical assessment, which observation by the nurse supports this potential diagnosis?

A. The presence of truncal obesity and a rounded "moon" face.  
B. A report of significant, unintended weight loss over the past month.  
C. Noticeable hyperpigmentation of the oral mucosa and gums.  
D. The patient wearing a heavy winter coat in a warm examination room.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. The presence of truncal obesity and a rounded "moon" face.  

**Rationale:** Cushing's syndrome is caused by an excess of corticosteroids (specifically glucocorticoids). Classic physical manifestations observed by the nurse include truncal (central) obesity, a rounded "moon" face, and a fat pad on the back of the neck ("buffalo hump"). Unintended weight loss and hyperpigmentation of the gums (Options B and C) are characteristic of Addison's disease (adrenocortical insufficiency), not Cushing's. Cold intolerance (Option D) is a classic sign of hypothyroidism.
</details>

---

### Question 244
A 29-year-old female patient is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an acute exacerbation of Graves' disease. While preparing the patient's room for her arrival, which environmental intervention should the nurse implement?

A. Place a portable space heater next to the patient's bed.  
B. Provide several extra thick wool blankets on the mattress.  
C. Ensure the room temperature is set to a cool, comfortable level.  
D. Turn off the air conditioning to prevent chilling the patient.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Ensure the room temperature is set to a cool, comfortable level.  

**Rationale:** Graves' disease is the most common cause of hyperthyroidism, which results in a hypermetabolic state. Patients with this condition commonly experience severe heat intolerance and diaphoresis. Therefore, the nurse should ensure the room environment is kept cool to promote patient comfort. Options A, B, and D would increase the patient's temperature and exacerbate their discomfort.
</details>

---

### Question 245
A 21-year-old male patient is recovering in the neuro-trauma unit following a severe traumatic brain injury. The nurse is monitoring the patient for the potential development of diabetes insipidus. Which finding requires the nurse to immediately notify the healthcare provider?

A. Development of deep, rapid respirations with a fruity breath odor.  
B. A sudden, massive increase in the output of pale, dilute urine.  
C. Pitting edema noted in the bilateral lower extremities.  
D. A capillary blood glucose reading consistently above 250 mg/dL.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. A sudden, massive increase in the output of pale, dilute urine.  

**Rationale:** Diabetes insipidus (DI) can occur after head trauma due to damage to the hypothalamus or pituitary gland, leading to a deficiency in antidiuretic hormone (ADH). The classic hallmark of DI is extreme polyuria (often outputting several liters of very dilute urine per day) and polydipsia, which can quickly lead to severe dehydration and hypernatremia. Options A and D are signs of diabetes mellitus (specifically diabetic ketoacidosis), which involves insulin and glucose metabolism, not ADH. Option C suggests fluid retention, which is characteristic of Syndrome of Inappropriate Antidiuretic Hormone (SIADH), the opposite of DI.
</details>
### Question 246
A nurse in a long-term care facility is conducting an admission assessment on a 68-year-old female client who has a history of unmanaged hypothyroidism. Based on this diagnosis, which finding should the nurse expect to observe?

A) Frequent reports of feeling too hot
B) A resting heart rate of 110 beats per minute
C) Recent, unexplained weight loss
D) Persistent fatigue and cold intolerance

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D) Persistent fatigue and cold intolerance</strong><br><br>
Hypothyroidism slows down the body's metabolic rate. Common clinical manifestations observed by the nurse include fatigue, cold intolerance, weight gain, bradycardia, and constipation. Heat intolerance, tachycardia, and weight loss are signs of a hyperactive metabolism, which is seen in hyperthyroidism, not hypothyroidism.
</details>

---

### Question 247
A nurse in a pediatric unit is caring for a 12-year-old boy with Type 1 diabetes mellitus. The client calls the nurse and reports feeling shaky, dizzy, and visibly diaphoretic. His capillary blood glucose reading is 54 mg/dL. Which action should the nurse take immediately?

A) Administer a scheduled dose of subcutaneous regular insulin.
B) Provide the client with 4 ounces of apple juice to drink.
C) Document the finding and recheck the blood glucose in 30 minutes.
D) Instruct the client to lie down and rest until the shakiness passes.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B) Provide the client with 4 ounces of apple juice to drink.</strong><br><br>
The client is experiencing symptomatic hypoglycemia (blood glucose < 70 mg/dL). The nurse's immediate action for a conscious client who can swallow is to provide 15 grams of a fast-acting, simple carbohydrate—such as 4 ounces of fruit juice or regular soda—to rapidly increase blood glucose levels. Giving insulin would dangerously lower the blood sugar further, and waiting or simply resting delays necessary, potentially life-saving treatment.
</details>

---

### Question 248
An outpatient clinic nurse is assessing a 32-year-old female client who was recently diagnosed with Graves' disease. During the physical examination, which specific clinical sign should the nurse anticipate observing?

A) Exophthalmos
B) Dry, flaky skin
C) Pitting edema in the lower legs
D) Sluggish deep tendon reflexes

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A) Exophthalmos</strong><br><br>
Graves' disease is an autoimmune condition that is the most common cause of hyperthyroidism. A classic clinical manifestation observed during assessment is exophthalmos, which is a bulging or protrusion of the eyes due to fluid accumulation and tissue enlargement behind the eyeball. Dry skin, edema, and sluggish reflexes are typically associated with hypothyroidism.
</details>

---

### Question 249
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is reviewing the care plan for a 45-year-old male client admitted with Cushing's syndrome. When assessing the client's physical appearance, which finding correlates directly with this condition?

A) A bronze pigmentation of the skin
B) Unexplained, severe weight loss
C) A rounded, "moon-like" face
D) Hypotension with positional dizziness

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C) A rounded, "moon-like" face</strong><br><br>
Cushing's syndrome results from an excess of corticosteroids (glucocorticoids) in the body. Classic physical manifestations the nurse will observe include a rounded "moon face," central obesity, a "buffalo hump" on the upper back, and thin extremities. Bronze skin pigmentation, weight loss, and hypotension are associated with a deficit of corticosteroids, known as Addison's disease.
</details>

---

### Question 250
A nurse in the neuro-intensive care unit is caring for a 28-year-old male client recovering from a traumatic brain injury. The nurse suspects the client has developed diabetes insipidus (DI). Which nursing observation strongly supports this complication?

A) The client exhibits generalized, severe peripheral edema.
B) The client complains of extreme thirst and excretes large volumes of dilute urine.
C) The client's capillary blood glucose reading is persistently above 350 mg/dL.
D) The client's urinary catheter output drops to 15 mL over a two-hour period.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B) The client complains of extreme thirst and excretes large volumes of dilute urine.</strong><br><br>
Diabetes insipidus (DI) occurs due to a deficiency of antidiuretic hormone (ADH), often following head trauma, pituitary tumors, or neurosurgery. Without ADH, the kidneys cannot conserve water. The nurse will observe massive diuresis (polyuria) of very pale, dilute urine and excessive thirst (polydipsia). DI is a disorder of water balance, not a disorder of glucose metabolism like diabetes mellitus. Oliguria (low urine output) and edema are not associated with DI.
</details>
### Question 251
A 45-year-old female client in the outpatient clinic has been taking levothyroxine for primary hypothyroidism for the past three months. Which clinical observation by the nurse indicates that the medication is effective?

A) The client reports needing extra blankets to stay warm at night.
B) The client states her daily energy levels have improved significantly.
C) The client has experienced a recent 5-pound weight gain.
D) The client's resting heart rate is consistently measured at 52 beats per minute.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) The client states her daily energy levels have improved significantly.

**Rationale:** Levothyroxine is a synthetic thyroid hormone used to treat hypothyroidism. Classic symptoms of hypothyroidism include fatigue, extreme lethargy, weight gain, cold intolerance, and bradycardia. An improvement in energy levels indicates a return to a euthyroid (normal thyroid) state, showing the medication is effective. Needing extra blankets, weight gain, and a low heart rate indicate that the client is still experiencing symptoms of a suppressed metabolism (hypothyroidism).
</details>

---

### Question 252
A 10-year-old child with type 1 diabetes mellitus arrives at the school nurse's office shortly after physical education class. Which assessment finding should prompt the nurse to immediately check the child's blood glucose level for hypoglycemia?

A) The child reports excessive thirst and frequent urination.
B) The child has a flushed, dry face and deep, rapid breathing.
C) The child complains of trembling hands and feels clammy to the touch.
D) The child states they have a mild stomach ache and loss of appetite.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The child complains of trembling hands and feels clammy to the touch.

**Rationale:** Physical activity can rapidly decrease blood glucose levels in children with type 1 diabetes. Tremors, diaphoresis (clammy skin), irritability, and tachycardia are classic early signs of hypoglycemia caused by the release of epinephrine. Excessive thirst, frequent urination, and flushed, dry skin with deep, rapid breathing (Kussmaul respirations) are signs of hyperglycemia and potential diabetic ketoacidosis (DKA). 
</details>

---

### Question 253
The nurse is admitting a 62-year-old client with suspected Cushing's syndrome to the medical-surgical unit. During the initial physical assessment, which characteristic finding does the nurse expect to observe?

A) Purple stretch marks on the abdomen and a rounded, flushed face.
B) Extreme cold intolerance and severe thinning of the hair.
C) Bulging of the eyes and a visible, palpable lump in the neck.
D) Unexplained weight loss and dark hyperpigmentation of the gums.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Purple stretch marks on the abdomen and a rounded, flushed face.

**Rationale:** Cushing's syndrome results from an excess of corticosteroids (specifically glucocorticoids). Classic physical observations include a rounded "moon" face, truncal obesity, buffalo hump, and purplish striae (stretch marks) on the abdomen due to thinning of the skin. Cold intolerance is seen in hypothyroidism, bulging eyes (exophthalmos) in hyperthyroidism, and weight loss with hyperpigmentation in Addison's disease.
</details>

---

### Question 254
A 38-year-old client is recovering in the neurological intensive care unit following the surgical removal of a pituitary tumor. Which observation should alert the nurse to the potential development of diabetes insipidus (DI)?

A) A sudden increase in blood pressure and a bounding pulse.
B) The client develops generalized edema and crackles in the lung bases.
C) The client exhibits muscle twitching and a positive facial nerve spasm.
D) A massive output of very pale, dilute urine over the last two hours.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) A massive output of very pale, dilute urine over the last two hours.

**Rationale:** Diabetes insipidus (DI) is a common complication after pituitary surgery due to a deficiency of antidiuretic hormone (ADH). Without ADH, the kidneys cannot conserve water, leading to polyuria (massive output of very dilute urine) and extreme thirst. Increased blood pressure, bounding pulses, and edema are signs of fluid volume overload (which can occur in SIADH, the opposite of DI). Muscle twitching is related to hypocalcemia.
</details>

---

### Question 255
A triage nurse in the emergency department is assessing a 28-year-old client who reports feeling "jittery and anxious all the time." The client's medical record indicates a history of Graves' disease. Which additional observation by the nurse supports a finding of hyperthyroidism?

A) The client demonstrates excessively slow, lethargic movements.
B) The client's resting heart rate is assessed at 112 beats per minute.
C) The client states they have been constantly constipated for a week.
D) The client is wearing a heavy winter sweater despite the warm summer weather.

<details>
<summary>Answer and Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) The client's resting heart rate is assessed at 112 beats per minute.

**Rationale:** Graves' disease is the most common cause of hyperthyroidism, a condition characterized by an excessive output of thyroid hormones. This creates a hypermetabolic state in the body. Tachycardia (a resting heart rate over 100 bpm), palpitations, heat intolerance, weight loss, and anxiety are hallmark manifestations. Lethargy, constipation, and wearing heavy clothing in warm weather (cold intolerance) are classic manifestations of a slowed metabolism, indicating hypothyroidism.
</details>
### Question 256
The nurse is providing discharge instructions in an outpatient clinic to a 45-year-old female client newly diagnosed with hypothyroidism who has been prescribed levothyroxine. Which statement by the client indicates to the nurse that the teaching was effective?

A) "I should take this medication with my evening meal to prevent nausea."
B) "I will take this pill first thing in the morning on an empty stomach."
C) "I can stop taking this drug once my energy levels return to normal."
D) "I will take it with an antacid if it causes an upset stomach."

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "I will take this pill first thing in the morning on an empty stomach."

**Rationale:** Levothyroxine, a synthetic thyroid hormone, must be taken exactly as prescribed to ensure consistent absorption. It is best absorbed on an empty stomach, so the nurse should teach the client to take it first thing in the morning, at least 30 to 60 minutes before eating breakfast or taking any other medications. Taking it with food or antacids decreases its absorption, and it is a lifelong medication that should not be stopped simply because symptoms improve.
</details>

---

### Question 257
A 10-year-old boy with type 1 diabetes mellitus arrives at the school nurse's office complaining of extreme hunger and dizziness. Upon observation, the nurse notes that the child is pale, sweating profusely, and his hands are visibly trembling. Which of the following is the most appropriate initial action by the nurse?

A) Administer a prescribed dose of short-acting insulin.
B) Encourage the child to drink a large glass of diet cola.
C) Perform a capillary blood glucose test immediately.
D) Instruct the child to lie down and rest for 15 minutes.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Perform a capillary blood glucose test immediately.

**Rationale:** The child is exhibiting classic signs of hypoglycemia (pallor, diaphoresis, tremors, hunger, and dizziness). The nurse's first action should be to quickly verify the blood glucose level to confirm hypoglycemia before treating it. Once confirmed, the nurse should provide a fast-acting, simple carbohydrate (like regular soda or fruit juice, not diet cola). Giving insulin would worsen the hypoglycemia, and simply having the child rest delays necessary, potentially life-saving treatment.
</details>

### Question 258
A 32-year-old female client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an acute exacerbation of Graves' disease. During the morning assessment, the nurse notes significant exophthalmos. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate to include in this client's daily plan of care?

A) Administer prescribed artificial tears to moisten the conjunctiva.
B) Provide a dietary tray that is high in iodine-rich foods.
C) Keep the overhead lights brightly lit to promote visual acuity.
D) Position the client flat in bed to reduce facial fluid accumulation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Administer prescribed artificial tears to moisten the conjunctiva.

**Rationale:** Exophthalmos (abnormal bulging of the eyes) is a common manifestation of Graves' disease. Because the protruding eyes can prevent the eyelids from closing completely, the eyes are at high risk for dryness, irritation, and corneal abrasions. The nurse should administer lubricating eyedrops or artificial tears to keep the eyes moist. The head of the bed should be elevated (not flat) to help reduce periorbital edema, and the room should be kept dim or the client should wear dark glasses to reduce photophobia. 
</details>

---

### Question 259
The nurse is assessing a 58-year-old male client in a primary care clinic who has been taking high-dose oral corticosteroids for six months to manage severe rheumatoid arthritis. Which physical observation should the nurse recognize as an expected finding related to this long-term medication use?

A) Unintentional weight loss and deeply sunken cheeks
B) Bronze, hyperpigmented patches of skin on the hands
C) Thickened, brittle hair and reports of cold intolerance
D) Fat accumulation in the trunk and a rounded face

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Fat accumulation in the trunk and a rounded face

**Rationale:** Long-term use of high-dose corticosteroids suppresses the normal immune response and mimics the effects of cortisol, leading to iatrogenic Cushing's syndrome. The nurse should expect to observe abnormal fat redistribution, commonly presenting as truncal obesity, a rounded "moon" face, and a "buffalo hump" on the upper back. Bronze pigmentation is associated with Addison's disease, and cold intolerance with brittle hair is associated with hypothyroidism.
</details>

---

### Question 260
A 24-year-old male client is recovering in the neurovascular step-down unit following a severe traumatic brain injury. The client develops diabetes insipidus and is prescribed intravenous desmopressin (DDAVP). Which assessment finding strongly indicates to the nurse that the medication is having the desired therapeutic effect?

A) The client's blood pressure drops from 140/90 to 110/70 mmHg.
B) The client's urine output decreases from 400 mL/hr to 100 mL/hr.
C) The client's fasting capillary blood glucose level stabilizes at 95 mg/dL.
D) The client reports a significant and persistent increase in thirst.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) The client's urine output decreases from 400 mL/hr to 100 mL/hr.

**Rationale:** Diabetes insipidus (DI) is a condition caused by a deficiency of antidiuretic hormone (ADH), which leads to the kidneys' inability to conserve water, resulting in massive polyuria (excessive urination) and polydipsia (excessive thirst). Desmopressin is a synthetic replacement for ADH. The nurse evaluates the medication's effectiveness by observing a decrease in urine output, meaning the kidneys are appropriately reabsorbing water. Despite its name, diabetes insipidus is not related to blood glucose levels.
</details>
### Question 261

A nurse in an outpatient clinic is providing discharge instructions to a 45-year-old female newly diagnosed with hypothyroidism who has been prescribed levothyroxine. Which statement by the patient indicates successful teaching by the nurse?

A) "I will take this medication with my breakfast every morning."
B) "I should take this pill on an empty stomach with a full glass of water."
C) "I can stop taking this medication once my fatigue goes away."
D) "I will take it right before bedtime with a small snack."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "I should take this pill on an empty stomach with a full glass of water."

**Rationale:** Levothyroxine is a thyroid hormone replacement medication that is best absorbed on an empty stomach. The nurse should instruct the patient to take it first thing in the morning, 30 to 60 minutes before eating breakfast, with a full glass of water. Taking it with food (Option A or D) impairs absorption. Hypothyroidism is typically a lifelong condition, and the medication should not be stopped simply because symptoms improve (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 262

A school nurse is evaluating a 10-year-old boy with type 1 diabetes who reports feeling shaky, dizzy, and sweaty during recess. The nurse checks his capillary blood glucose and notes a reading of 58 mg/dL. What is the nurse's immediate action?

A) Administer a rapid-acting insulin dose subcutaneously.
B) Instruct the child to lie down and rest for 20 minutes before rechecking.
C) Provide the child with 4 ounces of regular apple juice.
D) Notify the parents to come pick up the child immediately.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Provide the child with 4 ounces of regular apple juice.

**Rationale:** The child is experiencing symptomatic hypoglycemia (blood glucose < 70 mg/dL). The nurse's immediate action is to follow the "rule of 15" by providing 15 grams of fast-acting carbohydrates, such as 4 ounces (120 mL) of regular fruit juice, to quickly raise the blood sugar. Administering insulin (Option A) would dangerously worsen the hypoglycemia. Having the child rest without treating the low sugar (Option B) could lead to loss of consciousness. The parents should be notified, but treating the hypoglycemia takes clinical priority (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 263

A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is performing an admission assessment on a 28-year-old female with a history of untreated Graves' disease. Which clinical finding should the nurse expect to observe during the examination?

A) Bulging of the eyes and a resting heart rate of 110 bpm.
B) Extreme sensitivity to cold temperatures and dry, flaky skin.
C) A puffy, pale face and a sluggish, slowed speech pattern.
D) Sudden, unexplained weight gain over the last two months.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Bulging of the eyes and a resting heart rate of 110 bpm.

**Rationale:** Graves' disease is the most common cause of hyperthyroidism, resulting in a hypermetabolic state. The nurse should expect to observe signs such as tachycardia, weight loss, heat intolerance, and exophthalmos (bulging of the eyes). Cold intolerance, dry skin, puffy face, slowed speech, and weight gain (Options B, C, and D) are all classic clinical manifestations of *hypothyroidism*, not hyperthyroidism.
</details>

### Question 264

A 55-year-old male is recovering in the intensive care unit following a severe traumatic brain injury. The nurse observes that his urinary output over the last hour is 800 mL of pale, water-like urine. Suspecting diabetes insipidus, which additional assessment finding should the nurse anticipate observing in this patient?

A) Hypertension and bounding peripheral pulses.
B) Edema in the bilateral lower extremities.
C) Dark, amber-colored urine with a high specific gravity.
D) Extreme thirst and dry mucous membranes.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Extreme thirst and dry mucous membranes.

**Rationale:** Diabetes insipidus (DI) frequently occurs after neurotrauma or pituitary surgery due to a deficiency in antidiuretic hormone (ADH). This causes massive diuresis of dilute urine. As the patient loses large volumes of fluid, the nurse should expect to observe signs of profound dehydration, such as extreme thirst (polydipsia), dry mucous membranes, tachycardia, and hypotension. Hypertension, bounding pulses, and edema (Options A and B) indicate fluid overload. Dark urine with high specific gravity (Option C) indicates concentrated urine, whereas DI presents with very dilute, low-specific-gravity urine.
</details>

---

### Question 265

A nurse at a rehabilitation facility is caring for a 62-year-old female with Cushing's syndrome resulting from long-term corticosteroid use. During morning care, which specific nursing action is most important for protecting this patient?

A) Placing the patient in an isolation room to prevent radiation exposure.
B) Handling the patient's skin very gently during turns and transfers.
C) Offering a high-sodium snack between breakfast and lunch.
D) Keeping the room temperature cool to manage frequent hot flashes.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Handling the patient's skin very gently during turns and transfers.

**Rationale:** Patients with Cushing's syndrome have an excess of cortisol, which leads to thinning of the skin, loss of collagen, and increased fragility of blood vessels. The nurse must handle the patient with extreme care to prevent skin tearing, bruising, and subsequent infection. Cushing's syndrome does not require isolation for radiation (Option A). These patients often retain sodium and fluid, so a high-sodium diet is contraindicated (Option C). Hot flashes are associated with menopause, not typically Cushing's syndrome (Option D).
</details>
### Question 266
A school nurse is called to the playground for a 9-year-old student with type 1 diabetes who is pale, sweaty, and trembling after playing kickball. The student is conscious, alert, and able to swallow without difficulty. What is the nurse's *best* immediate action?

A) Administer a subcutaneous injection of regular insulin.
B) Provide 4 ounces of apple juice to the student.
C) Have the student rest in the shade for 15 minutes.
D) Encourage the student to drink two large glasses of water.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Sweating, pallor, and trembling are classic signs of hypoglycemia. Because the child is conscious and able to swallow, the nurse's immediate priority is to administer a fast-acting simple carbohydrate, such as 4 ounces of apple juice, to rapidly raise the blood glucose level. Giving insulin would worsen the hypoglycemia, and simply resting or drinking water does not address the underlying dangerously low blood sugar.
</details>

---

### Question 267
A clinic nurse is providing discharge instructions to a 68-year-old client who was recently prescribed levothyroxine for primary hypothyroidism. Which statement by the client indicates to the nurse that the teaching was effective?

A) "I will take this pill with my dinner every evening."
B) "I can stop taking this medication once my energy levels improve."
C) "I should take this medication with an antacid to prevent an upset stomach."
D) "I will take my pill first thing in the morning on an empty stomach."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Levothyroxine should be taken in the morning on an empty stomach, at least 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast. This ensures optimal absorption and helps prevent medication-induced insomnia that could occur if taken later in the day. Antacids interfere with the absorption of levothyroxine, and the medication is typically a lifelong requirement, even after symptoms improve.
</details>

---

### Question 268
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is admitting a 45-year-old client diagnosed with Cushing's syndrome. During the initial physical assessment, which clinical observation should the nurse expect to find?

A) Purple striae on the abdomen and a rounded "moon" face.
B) Unexplained weight loss and a persistent low-grade fever.
C) Deeply bronzed skin pigmentation and severe hypotension.
D) Intolerance to cold and thinning of the outer eyebrows.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Cushing's syndrome is caused by an excess of corticosteroids (glucocorticoids). Classic physical manifestations include weight gain, a rounded "moon" face, a fat pad on the back of the neck ("buffalo hump"), central adiposity, and purple striae (stretch marks) on the abdomen. Bronzed skin and hypotension are indicative of Addison's disease (adrenal insufficiency), while cold intolerance and thinning eyebrows are signs of hypothyroidism.
</details>

---

### Question 269
A 22-year-old client is admitted to the neurocritical care unit following a traumatic brain injury. The nurse observes that the client's urinary output has been over 1,000 mL per hour for the last two hours, and the urine is remarkably clear and pale. Which action should the nurse take *first*?

A) Restrict the client's oral and intravenous fluid intake immediately.
B) Administer a loop diuretic to help the kidneys clear excess fluid.
C) Notify the healthcare provider and check the urine specific gravity.
D) Prepare the client for an emergency hemodialysis session.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Massive polyuria of dilute urine following a head injury is a classic sign of diabetes insipidus (DI), which results from a lack of antidiuretic hormone (ADH). The nurse should suspect DI, check the urine specific gravity (which will be abnormally low due to the dilution), and notify the provider for further orders, such as fluid replacement and administration of desmopressin. Restricting fluids or giving diuretics would cause severe, life-threatening dehydration.
</details>

---

### Question 270
A triage nurse in the emergency department is assessing a 30-year-old client who reports a racing heart, excessive sweating, and a recent 10-pound weight loss despite having a massive appetite. The client appears highly anxious and is wearing a thin tank top despite the freezing weather outside. What nursing intervention is *most* appropriate for this client while waiting to see the provider?

A) Place the client in a cool, quiet room to reduce stimulation.
B) Provide extra warm blankets to prevent sudden hypothermia.
C) Offer a high-fiber snack to slow down the client's metabolism.
D) Encourage the client to walk around the waiting area to burn off energy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The client is exhibiting classic signs of hyperthyroidism (such as Graves' disease), which include tachycardia, weight loss, heat intolerance, diaphoresis, and anxiety. Providing a cool, quiet environment helps decrease sensory stimulation, reduces anxiety, and promotes physical comfort for a client suffering from severe heat intolerance and a hypermetabolic state.
</details>
### Question 271

An 8-year-old child with type 1 diabetes mellitus is brought to the school nurse's office directly after participating in a strenuous gym class. The nurse observes that the child is pale, profusely sweating, and trembling. When asked how they feel, the child reports feeling dizzy and confused. Which action should the nurse take immediately?

A) Administer a prescribed dose of short-acting insulin.
B) Encourage the child to rest quietly on a cot with the lights dimmed.
C) Provide 4 ounces of apple juice for the child to drink.
D) Contact the child's parents to come and pick them up from school.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: C) Provide 4 ounces of apple juice for the child to drink.**

**Rationale:** The child is exhibiting classic signs of hypoglycemia (pallor, diaphoresis, tremors, dizziness, and confusion), which was likely triggered by the increased energy expenditure during gym class. The nurse's immediate priority is to raise the child's blood glucose level by providing a fast-acting, simple carbohydrate like 4 ounces of juice (the "rule of 15"). Administering insulin (Option A) would further lower blood sugar and be life-threatening. Having the child rest (Option B) or waiting for parents (Option D) delays necessary, immediate medical intervention and could lead to a hypoglycemic seizure or coma.
</details>

---

### Question 272

A nurse in a long-term care facility is preparing to administer morning medications to a 72-year-old resident who has a history of hypothyroidism. The medication administration record includes a daily dose of levothyroxine. Which action by the nurse demonstrates the correct administration technique for this medication?

A) Give the medication with a glass of water on an empty stomach 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast.
B) Crush the medication and mix it thoroughly into a cup of warm oatmeal during breakfast.
C) Administer the medication at the same time as the resident's prescribed calcium supplement.
D) Withhold the medication until right before bedtime to prevent daytime drowsiness.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: A) Give the medication with a glass of water on an empty stomach 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast.**

**Rationale:** Levothyroxine is a thyroid hormone replacement that must be taken on an empty stomach, typically 30 to 60 minutes before the first meal of the day, to ensure optimal and consistent absorption. Mixing it with food like oatmeal (Option B) significantly decreases its absorption. Calcium and iron supplements (Option C) bind to levothyroxine and prevent its absorption, so they must be spaced out by at least 4 hours. Levothyroxine treats hypothyroidism, which causes fatigue; the medication actually increases energy, so giving it at bedtime (Option D) may cause insomnia.
</details>

---

### Question 273

A 35-year-old male is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with a severe exacerbation of hyperthyroidism (Graves' disease). The nurse is assisting the patient with his lunch order from the dietary menu. Which meal selection indicates that the nurse has chosen the most appropriate diet for this patient's current condition?

A) A large garden salad with fat-free dressing, celery sticks, and a glass of water.
B) A bowl of broth-based vegetable soup, unsalted crackers, and unsweetened tea.
C) A small portion of steamed broccoli, half a plain baked potato, and black coffee.
D) A large grilled chicken breast, macaroni and cheese, and a high-calorie protein shake.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: D) A large grilled chicken breast, macaroni and cheese, and a high-calorie protein shake.**

**Rationale:** Hyperthyroidism puts the body into a hypermetabolic state, which greatly increases the patient's caloric and nutritional needs. Patients frequently experience severe weight loss and muscle wasting. Therefore, a diet high in calories, protein, and carbohydrates is required to meet these demands. Option D provides the necessary macronutrients. Options A, B, and C are low-calorie and low-protein meals, which are inappropriate for someone burning calories at a highly accelerated rate. Additionally, caffeine (found in tea and coffee in Options B and C) should be avoided as it can worsen the tachycardia and tremors associated with hyperthyroidism.
</details>

---

### Question 274

A clinic nurse is providing discharge instructions to a 50-year-old female recently diagnosed with Cushing's syndrome. The nurse is discussing necessary lifestyle modifications and safety precautions. Which statement by the patient indicates that the teaching has been effective?

A) "I will stop taking my prescribed medications if I start feeling nauseous."
B) "I will wash my hands frequently and avoid visiting crowded indoor places."
C) "I will limit my fluid intake to less than 1 liter per day to prevent swelling."
D) "I will use a heating pad on the highest setting every night to soothe my muscle aches."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: B) "I will wash my hands frequently and avoid visiting crowded indoor places."**

**Rationale:** Cushing's syndrome is characterized by an excess of cortisol, a glucocorticoid that suppresses the body's immune response. Because the immune system is compromised, the patient is at a significantly high risk for contracting infections. Frequent handwashing and avoiding crowds are essential safety precautions. Patients should never abruptly stop endocrine medications without consulting a provider (Option A). While patients may have fluid retention, strictly limiting fluids to under 1 liter (Option C) is not a standard standalone intervention without specific provider orders, as it can cause dehydration. Cushing's syndrome can cause thin, fragile skin; therefore, using a heating pad on high (Option D) puts the patient at high risk for severe burns.
</details>

---

### Question 275

A 22-year-old male with a known history of Addison's disease arrives at the emergency department. He reports extreme fatigue, persistent nausea, and feeling very dizzy whenever he stands up. During the initial physical assessment, which specific action is a priority for the nurse to perform?

A) Auscultate all four quadrants of the abdomen for hyperactive bowel sounds.
B) Check the patient's deep tendon reflexes using a reflex hammer.
C) Measure the patient's blood pressure while lying, sitting, and standing.
D) Inspect the patient's lower extremities for 3+ pitting edema.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: C) Measure the patient's blood pressure while lying, sitting, and standing.**

**Rationale:** Addison's disease involves a deficiency in aldosterone and cortisol. A lack of aldosterone leads to sodium and water loss, resulting in profound hypovolemia (fluid volume deficit). The patient's symptom of dizziness upon standing strongly suggests orthostatic hypotension, which is a classic and potentially dangerous complication of Addison's disease. Measuring orthostatic vital signs (lying, sitting, standing) helps quantify this deficit and guides immediate fluid resuscitation. Auscultating bowel sounds (Option A) or checking reflexes (Option B) are not priority assessments for hypovolemia. Because Addison's causes fluid loss, the nurse would not expect to see pitting edema (Option D), which is a sign of fluid overload.
</details>
### Question 276
An 8-year-old student with type 1 diabetes arrives at the school nurse's office reporting feeling "shaky" and dizzy. The nurse observes that the child is diaphoretic, pale, and having difficulty concentrating. What is the nurse's immediate action?

A) Administer a subcutaneous dose of rapid-acting insulin.
B) Provide the child with 4 ounces of apple juice.
C) Instruct the child to rest quietly on the clinic cot for 15 minutes.
D) Call the parents to immediately take the child to the emergency department.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
Correct Answer: B<br><br>
The child is exhibiting classic signs of hypoglycemia (shakiness, diaphoresis, pallor, and confusion). The priority nursing action for a conscious patient with hypoglycemia is to provide 15 grams of a fast-acting, simple carbohydrate, such as 4 ounces of fruit juice or regular soda. Administering insulin (Option A) would dangerously worsen the hypoglycemia. Resting (Option C) delays necessary treatment. While parents should be notified (Option D), treating the immediate hypoglycemic episode takes clinical priority.
</details>

---

### Question 277
A nurse in a long-term care facility is reviewing the medication administration record for a 72-year-old client with hypothyroidism. The client is prescribed daily oral levothyroxine. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate when administering this medication?

A) Administer the medication with a high-calcium morning snack.
B) Mix the crushed medication into a cup of yogurt during lunch.
C) Give the medication on an empty stomach at least 30 minutes before breakfast.
D) Provide the medication at bedtime with a full glass of warm milk.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
Correct Answer: C<br><br>
Levothyroxine must be taken on an empty stomach, typically 30 to 60 minutes before breakfast, to ensure optimal absorption. Taking it with food (Option B), especially foods or supplements rich in calcium or iron (Options A and D), significantly decreases the absorption of the medication and reduces its effectiveness. 
</details>

---

### Question 278
A home health nurse is observing a 60-year-old client with type 2 diabetes perform routine daily foot care. Which action by the client requires the nurse to intervene and provide further teaching?

A) Soaking the feet in hot water for twenty minutes to soften calluses.
B) Trimming the toenails straight across using clean clippers.
C) Inspecting the bottom of the feet daily using a hand-held mirror.
D) Wearing clean, white cotton socks with well-fitting, closed-toe shoes.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
Correct Answer: A<br><br>
Clients with diabetes often have peripheral neuropathy (decreased sensation) and poor circulation. Soaking feet, especially in hot water, can lead to severe burns that the client may not feel, and it causes skin maceration, increasing the risk for breakdown and infection. Therefore, the nurse must intervene. Options B, C, and D are all correct and highly recommended practices for diabetic foot care.
</details>

---

### Question 279
A nurse in the surgical intensive care unit is caring for a 45-year-old client who is 12 hours post-transsphenoidal hypophysectomy (pituitary tumor removal). The nurse notes the client has excreted 3,000 mL of pale, odorless urine over the last 4 hours. Based on this observation, which assessment finding should the nurse anticipate next?

A) Bounding peripheral pulses
B) Hypertension and severe bradycardia
C) Pitting edema in the lower extremities
D) Dry mucous membranes and excessive thirst

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
Correct Answer: D<br><br>
The client is exhibiting signs of Diabetes Insipidus (DI), a common complication following pituitary surgery. DI is characterized by a deficiency of antidiuretic hormone (ADH), leading to massive diuresis of dilute urine. This rapid fluid loss leads to severe dehydration, which manifests as dry mucous membranes, excessive thirst (polydipsia), tachycardia, and hypotension. Options A, B, and C are signs of fluid volume overload, which is the opposite of what occurs in DI.
</details>

---

### Question 280
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is providing discharge instructions to a 35-year-old client recently diagnosed with Graves' disease. The client is experiencing exophthalmos (bulging eyes). Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching plan to protect the client's vision?

A) "Avoid using artificial tears as they can cause corneal irritation over time."
B) "Apply warm, moist compresses to your eyes every morning to reduce swelling."
C) "Use dark-tinted glasses when you are in brightly lit or windy environments."
D) "Tape your eyelids open at night to prevent your eyelashes from matting."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
Correct Answer: C<br><br>
Clients with exophthalmos due to Graves' disease often experience photophobia (sensitivity to light) and are at high risk for corneal abrasions due to incomplete eyelid closure. Wearing dark-tinted glasses protects the eyes from light, wind, and debris. Artificial tears (Option A) are highly recommended to prevent drying. Cool compresses (not warm, Option B) are used to help reduce periorbital edema. If eyelids cannot close completely during sleep, they should be taped *closed* (not open, Option D) to protect the cornea from drying out.
</details>
### Question 281
A clinic nurse is providing discharge instructions to a 45-year-old client newly prescribed levothyroxine for hypothyroidism. Which statement by the client indicates to the nurse that the teaching was effective?

A) "I should take this medication right before I go to sleep."
B) "I will take this pill every morning on an empty stomach."
C) "I can take this pill with my morning coffee and cereal."
D) "I will take this medication whenever I feel tired or sluggish."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Levothyroxine is a synthetic thyroid hormone used to treat hypothyroidism. It should be taken in the morning on an empty stomach, at least 30 to 60 minutes before eating, to ensure optimal absorption. Taking it with food or at night can decrease its absorption and lead to insomnia. It is a daily maintenance medication, not a medication taken "as needed" for fatigue.
</details>

---

### Question 282
A school nurse is called to the gymnasium for an 8-year-old child with type 1 diabetes who is trembling, sweating, and pale after playing tag. The child's blood glucose is checked and reads 58 mg/dL. What is the nurse's immediate action?

A) Administer a subcutaneous injection of short-acting regular insulin.
B) Encourage the child to drink a large glass of water and lie down to rest.
C) Provide the child with 4 ounces of regular apple juice.
D) Give the child a high-fat snack like peanut butter and cheese.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The child is experiencing a mild to moderate hypoglycemic episode. According to the "Rule of 15" for hypoglycemia management in a conscious client, the nurse should immediately provide 15 grams of fast-acting simple carbohydrates, such as 4 ounces (half a cup) of regular juice or regular soda, to quickly raise the blood glucose level. Giving insulin would worsen the hypoglycemia, and high-fat snacks slow the absorption of sugar.
</details>

---

### Question 283
A nurse on the medical-surgical unit is caring for a 28-year-old client with Graves' disease who has pronounced exophthalmos. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate to include in this client's daily care plan?

A) Provide lubricating eye drops to keep the client's corneas moist.
B) Keep the client's room brightly lit to prevent visual field deficits.
C) Position the client perfectly flat in bed to reduce pressure behind the eyes.
D) Tape the client's eyelids completely closed all day to prevent spasms.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Exophthalmos (bulging of the eyes) is a common clinical manifestation of Graves' disease (hyperthyroidism). Because the eyelids may not close completely, the exposed corneas are at high risk for dryness, irritation, and ulceration. The nurse should administer artificial tears or lubricating eye drops to protect the eyes. Elevating the head of the bed (not laying flat) helps reduce periorbital edema, and taping eyes shut all day is inappropriate and limits vision. 
</details>

---

### Question 284
A nurse in a long-term care facility is assisting a 72-year-old resident with Cushing's syndrome. During morning care, the nurse observes that the client's skin is extremely thin and fragile. Which action should the nurse take to prevent complications?

A) Rub the skin vigorously with a coarse towel to dry after bathing.
B) Apply strong adhesive tape directly to the skin to secure any bandages.
C) Keep the client in the exact same position all day to avoid friction.
D) Use a lift sheet to gently turn and reposition the client in bed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Clients with Cushing's syndrome have an excess of corticosteroids, which causes protein wasting and leads to paper-thin, fragile skin that is highly prone to bruising and tearing. The nurse must handle the client very gently, using a draw sheet or lift sheet to turn and reposition them in order to prevent shearing forces and skin breakdown. Vigorously rubbing the skin or using strong adhesive tape will cause skin tears.
</details>

---

### Question 285
A nurse in the neurosurgical intensive care unit is monitoring a 50-year-old client who just had a pituitary tumor removed. The nurse notes that the client has produced 1,500 mL of very pale, clear urine over the last hour and is complaining of extreme thirst. Which condition should the nurse suspect the client is developing?

A) Diabetes insipidus (DI)
B) Syndrome of inappropriate antidiuretic hormone (SIADH)
C) Diabetic ketoacidosis (DKA)
D) Adrenal crisis

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Surgery on or near the pituitary gland can interfere with the secretion of antidiuretic hormone (ADH), leading to diabetes insipidus. Classic clinical signs of DI include massive polyuria (large volumes of very dilute urine) and polydipsia (excessive thirst). SIADH causes water retention and decreased urine output, which is the opposite of this client's presentation.
</details>
### Question 286
A nurse is observing a 68-year-old client on the medical-surgical unit who has chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The client is practicing pursed-lip breathing. Which observation by the nurse indicates that the client is performing the technique correctly?

A) The client inhales deeply through the mouth and exhales forcefully.
B) The client inhales slowly through the nose and exhales slowly through puckered lips.
C) The client holds their breath for 10 seconds before exhaling through the nose.
