D) The client breathes in and out rapidly through the mouth to catch their breath.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Pursed-lip breathing is used to prolong exhalation, prevent alveolar collapse, and help the client gain control of their breathing. The correct technique involves inhaling slowly through the nose and exhaling slowly and steadily through puckered (pursed) lips, usually taking twice as long to exhale as to inhale. Breathing through the mouth, holding the breath, or breathing rapidly does not facilitate the release of trapped air characteristic of COPD.
</details>

---

### Question 287
A nurse in a pediatric clinic is teaching a 10-year-old client and their parents about using a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) for mild asthma management. The parents ask why a plastic spacer is attached to the inhaler. What is the nurse's best response?

A) "It prevents the child from accidentally swallowing the inhaler mouthpiece."
B) "It sterilizes the medication before it enters the child's airway."
C) "It decreases the amount of time needed to administer the daily dose."
D) "It helps deliver more of the medication directly into the lungs."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
A spacer is a tube attached to the MDI that holds the medication in a small chamber after it is sprayed. This allows the child to inhale the medication slowly and deeply, ensuring more of the drug reaches the lungs rather than landing on the back of the throat or tongue. It does not sterilize the medication, prevent swallowing the mouthpiece, or decrease the administration time.
</details>

---

### Question 288
A 45-year-old client who recently underwent abdominal surgery is given an incentive spirometer to prevent post-operative atelectasis. When assessing the client's use of the device, which action by the client requires the nurse to intervene immediately?

A) Blowing forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston.
B) Sitting in a high-Fowler's position while using the device.
C) Inhaling slowly and deeply to elevate the internal piston.
D) Holding their breath for a few seconds at the end of maximal inspiration.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The nurse must intervene if the client blows into the incentive spirometer. The purpose of the device is to promote deep *inspiration* to expand the alveoli and prevent atelectasis. The client should seal their lips around the mouthpiece and inhale slowly and deeply. Sitting upright (high-Fowler's) and holding the breath at the peak of inspiration are correct techniques that facilitate maximum lung expansion.
</details>

---

### Question 289
A 78-year-old client is admitted to the medical unit with bilateral pneumonia. The nurse notes that the client is visibly short of breath, using accessory muscles to breathe, and has an oxygen saturation of 91% on room air. What is the most appropriate initial nursing action?

A) Administer a PRN dose of an opioid analgesic to calm the client.
B) Encourage the client to lie flat in bed to conserve energy.
C) Elevate the head of the bed to at least 45 degrees.
D) Prepare the client for immediate endotracheal intubation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Elevating the head of the bed (Fowler's or high-Fowler's position) uses gravity to drop the abdominal organs away from the diaphragm, allowing for maximal lung expansion and easing the work of breathing. Lying flat will worsen respiratory distress. Opioids can depress the respiratory drive and are contraindicated as an initial step for shortness of breath. An O2 saturation of 91% requires positioning and likely supplemental oxygen, but does not immediately warrant intubation without further assessment.
</details>

---

### Question 290
A home health nurse is visiting a 28-year-old client who has a newly established tracheostomy. The nurse is observing the client perform routine stoma care. Which action by the client indicates that further teaching is necessary?

A) Washing their hands thoroughly before starting the cleaning procedure.
B) Untying the old tracheostomy ties completely before securing the new ones.
C) Using a pre-packaged, sterile tracheostomy care kit for the procedure.
D) Cleaning the stoma area gently with moistened sterile gauze.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
The nurse must intervene because removing the old tracheostomy ties completely before the new ones are secured places the client at a high risk for accidental decannulation (the tube falling out), especially if the client coughs. The proper technique is to leave the old ties in place until the new ties are securely fastened, or to have a second person hold the tube in place. Handwashing, using sterile supplies, and gentle cleaning are all correct actions.
</details>
### Question 291
A nurse in a long-term care facility is caring for a 72-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The client reports feeling severely short of breath, and the nurse notes the client is slumped down in the bed. Which action should the nurse take *first*?

A) Administer oxygen at 2 L/min via nasal cannula.
B) Elevate the head of the client's bed to a high-Fowler's position.
C) Auscultate the client's lung sounds in all fields.
D) Notify the healthcare provider of the change in condition.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The first action the nurse should take when a client is experiencing shortness of breath is to reposition them to maximize lung expansion. Elevating the head of the bed to a high-Fowler's position allows gravity to drop the abdominal organs away from the diaphragm, making it easier for the lungs to expand. While administering oxygen, auscultating lungs, and notifying the provider are important subsequent steps, airway and breathing management through immediate repositioning is always the priority.
</details>

---

### Question 292
A 10-year-old child comes to the school nurse's office experiencing an acute asthma attack. The nurse observes the child wheezing audibly and using accessory muscles to breathe. The child has a prescribed rescue inhaler on hand. Which action should the nurse take immediately?

A) Administer the prescribed albuterol inhaler.
B) Have the child lie down flat on a cot to rest.
C) Instruct the child to take slow, deep breaths through the nose.
D) Offer the child a glass of cold water to soothe the airway.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Albuterol is a short-acting bronchodilator (rescue inhaler) used to rapidly open the airways during an acute asthma attack. Immediate administration is critical when a child is exhibiting signs of respiratory distress, such as wheezing and accessory muscle use. Lying flat worsens respiratory distress, breathing exercises alone will not stop a severe acute bronchospasm, and cold water does not address the underlying airway constriction.
</details>

---

### Question 293
A nurse is caring for a 45-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with right lower lobe pneumonia. The client is coughing up thick, greenish sputum and states, "It hurts my chest too much to cough." Which intervention should the nurse implement to help the client clear the secretions?

A) Place the client on a strict fluid restriction.
B) Administer a prescribed over-the-counter cough suppressant.
C) Teach the client to splint the chest with a pillow when coughing.
D) Instruct the client to breathe shallowly to avoid triggering pain.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Splinting the chest with a pillow provides support to the chest wall and reduces pain during coughing, which encourages the client to cough more effectively and clear infectious secretions. Fluid restrictions would thicken secretions, making them harder to expectorate. Cough suppressants are generally contraindicated for productive coughs in pneumonia because the client needs to clear the sputum. Shallow breathing promotes atelectasis and worsens respiratory infections.
</details>

---

### Question 294
A 28-year-old client is 12 hours postoperative following an open appendectomy. The nurse is instructing the client on the use of an incentive spirometer to prevent atelectasis. Which observation by the nurse indicates that the client is using the device correctly?

A) The client exhales forcefully into the mouthpiece.
B) The client takes quick, short breaths in and out of the device.
C) The client blows into the spirometer until the piston rises to the top.
D) The client inhales slowly and deeply through the mouthpiece.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The incentive spirometer is designed to mimic natural sighing or yawning to encourage maximal lung expansion and prevent atelectasis. The correct technique involves a slow, deep inhalation through the mouthpiece to raise the piston or ball, holding the breath for a few seconds to allow alveoli to open, and then exhaling normally outside of the device. Exhaling or blowing into the device is incorrect.
</details>

---

### Question 295
A nurse in the pediatric unit is caring for a 6-month-old infant admitted with respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) bronchiolitis. The infant has copious nasal secretions and is becoming fussy while attempting to feed from a bottle. Which action should the nurse take to assist the infant?

A) Administer a prescribed oral corticosteroid immediately.
B) Place the infant in a prone position during the feeding.
C) Suction the infant's nares with a bulb syringe before offering the bottle.
D) Perform chest physiotherapy while the infant is eating.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale and Correct Answer</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C
<strong>Rationale:</strong> Infants are obligate nose breathers, meaning nasal congestion can significantly impair their ability to breathe and eat simultaneously. Suctioning the nares with a bulb syringe clears the upper airway, making it easier for the infant to breathe and coordinate swallowing during the feeding. Prone positioning is a SIDS risk and not recommended. Chest physiotherapy is generally not recommended for RSV and should never be done during feeding due to the severe risk of aspiration.
</details>
### Question 296
A school nurse is evaluating a 9-year-old student who comes to the clinic reporting that their chest feels "tight" after playing outside at recess. The nurse auscultates expiratory wheezes and notes a respiratory rate of 28 breaths per minute. Based on the student's known history of asthma, what is the nurse's *first* action?

A) Administer the prescribed short-acting bronchodilator inhaler.
B) Encourage the child to drink a large glass of ice water.
C) Call the child's parents to immediately take them to the hospital.
D) Have the child lie flat on the examination cot to rest.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Administer the prescribed short-acting bronchodilator inhaler.

**Rationale:** The child is exhibiting classic signs of an acute asthma exacerbation (chest tightness, wheezing, tachypnea). The immediate priority is to open the airway by administering a short-acting bronchodilator (like albuterol), which is the rescue medication of choice. Drinking ice water does not address bronchospasm. Having the child lie flat can actually increase the work of breathing; an upright position is preferred. While parents should be notified, treating the acute respiratory distress takes priority over making a phone call.
</details>

---

### Question 297
A home health nurse is visiting an 81-year-old client with advanced chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The client's spouse states, "I turned up the oxygen to 6 liters per minute because he looked a little tired this morning." Which observation by the nurse requires *immediate* intervention?

A) The client is resting in a recliner with the television on.
B) The client's respiratory rate drops to 8 breaths per minute.
C) The client has a noticeably barrel-shaped chest.
D) The client reports coughing up a small amount of clear sputum.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) The client's respiratory rate drops to 8 breaths per minute.

**Rationale:** Many clients with advanced COPD rely on a hypoxic drive to breathe. Delivering high levels of oxygen (like 6 liters per minute) can suppress this drive, leading to dangerous hypoventilation (bradypnea). A respiratory rate of 8 breaths per minute is abnormally low and requires immediate intervention to adjust the oxygen flow rate and assess oxygen saturation. A barrel chest is a chronic, expected finding in advanced COPD. Resting in a recliner and coughing up small amounts of clear morning sputum are also typical, non-emergent findings.
</details>

---

### Question 298
A 54-year-old client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with right lower lobe bacterial pneumonia. The client is coughing up thick, tenacious, yellow sputum and tells the nurse they feel too exhausted to keep coughing. Which nursing intervention will *best* assist the client in clearing these airway secretions?

A) Administer an over-the-counter cough suppressant to promote rest.
B) Restrict oral fluid intake to 1,000 mL per day to prevent fluid overload.
C) Encourage an oral fluid intake of 2 to 3 liters daily, if not contraindicated.
D) Keep the client on strict bed rest until the fever completely resolves.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Encourage an oral fluid intake of 2 to 3 liters daily, if not contraindicated.

**Rationale:** Adequate hydration is one of the most effective ways to thin respiratory secretions, making them easier to expectorate. Administering a cough suppressant is contraindicated because the client needs to cough up the infected sputum to clear the lungs. Restricting fluids would cause secretions to become thicker and harder to move. Strict bed rest can promote pooling of secretions; early and frequent mobilization is preferred to help clear the lungs.
</details>

---

### Question 299
A 30-year-old client is recovering on the surgical unit 24 hours after an open cholecystectomy. The nurse is teaching the client how to use an incentive spirometer to prevent post-operative atelectasis. Which action by the client during a return demonstration indicates that the teaching has been *effective*?

A) The client forcefully blows air into the device's mouthpiece.
B) The client takes short, rapid, shallow breaths through the device.
C) The client exhales deeply, then holds their breath for 10 seconds before using the device.
D) The client seals their lips around the mouthpiece and inhales deeply and slowly.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) The client seals their lips around the mouthpiece and inhales deeply and slowly.

**Rationale:** An incentive spirometer is designed to mimic natural sighing or yawning to encourage maximal lung expansion and prevent alveoli from collapsing (atelectasis). The correct technique is a slow, deep inhalation with the lips sealed firmly around the mouthpiece, followed by holding the breath for 2 to 3 seconds at the peak of inspiration. Blowing into the device or taking rapid, shallow breaths is incorrect and defeats the purpose of the therapy. 
</details>

---

### Question 300
A nurse at a public health clinic is evaluating a 35-year-old client who recently started taking rifampin and isoniazid for active pulmonary tuberculosis. The client is very anxious and reports that their urine, sweat, and tears have turned a reddish-orange color over the last few days. What is the nurse's *best* action?

A) Instruct the client to go directly to the nearest emergency department.
B) Reassure the client that this is a normal, expected side effect of the medication.
C) Hold the client's next scheduled dose and notify the prescribing healthcare provider.
D) Encourage the client to immediately stop wearing glasses and switch to contact lenses.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Reassure the client that this is a normal, expected side effect of the medication.

**Rationale:** Rifampin is a standard anti-tubercular medication that commonly causes a harmless reddish-orange discoloration of bodily fluids, including urine, sweat, tears, and saliva. The nurse should reassure the client that this is an expected side effect and does not indicate toxicity. Because tears can permanently stain soft contact lenses, clients are actually advised to wear glasses rather than contact lenses (making option D incorrect). There is no need to hold the dose or send the client to the emergency department.
</details>
### Question 301
A nurse is observing a 9-year-old child with asthma use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer. Which action by the child indicates to the nurse that the teaching has been effective?

A) The child exhales rapidly immediately after pressing the canister.
B) The child holds their breath for 10 seconds after inhaling the medication.
C) The child presses the canister multiple times before taking a single breath.
D) The child removes the spacer and places the mouthpiece directly on their teeth.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Holding the breath for about 10 seconds after inhaling the medication allows the aerosolized drug to settle deep within the lungs, maximizing its effectiveness. Exhaling rapidly (Option A) blows the medication out before it can be absorbed. Pressing the canister multiple times for one breath (Option C) delivers an inaccurate dose. Using a spacer (Option D is incorrect because it removes the spacer) is recommended for children to ensure the medication reaches the lungs rather than the back of the throat.
</details>

---

### Question 302
A home health nurse is visiting a 68-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) who reports feeling breathless after walking to the bathroom. The nurse instructs the client to use pursed-lip breathing. Which observation shows the client is performing this technique correctly?

A) The client inhales through the mouth and exhales forcefully through the nose.
B) The client puffs out their cheeks while inhaling quickly through the nose.
C) The client inhales deeply through the nose and exhales slowly through puckered lips.
D) The client breathes in and out rapidly with a wide-open mouth.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Pursed-lip breathing consists of inhaling deeply and slowly through the nose, then exhaling slowly through pursed or puckered lips (as if blowing out a candle). This technique increases airway pressure during exhalation, preventing small airways from collapsing and helping the client expel trapped air. Forceful or rapid breathing (Options A and D) can worsen breathlessness and airway collapse.
</details>

---

### Question 303
A nurse is caring for a 45-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with right lower lobe pneumonia. The client has thick, tenacious secretions and a weak cough. Which initial nursing action is best to help mobilize the client's respiratory secretions?

A) Encourage the client to increase oral fluid intake to 2 to 3 liters per day.
B) Administer high-flow oxygen via a non-rebreather mask.
C) Place the client in a supine position to promote relaxation.
D) Request a prescription for a cough suppressant from the healthcare provider.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Adequate hydration thins respiratory secretions, making them easier to cough up and clear from the airway. Unless contraindicated by conditions like heart failure or kidney disease, increasing fluid intake is a primary nursing intervention for mobilizing secretions. Suppressing the cough (Option D) would trap infected secretions in the lungs. A supine position (Option C) makes breathing harder and increases aspiration risk. High-flow oxygen (Option B) is a medical intervention for hypoxia, not for mobilizing secretions, and dry oxygen can actually thicken secretions if unhumidified.
</details>

---

### Question 304
A nurse in a long-term care facility is preparing to suction the tracheostomy of an 82-year-old resident. Before inserting the suction catheter, which action must the nurse take to prevent a common complication of this procedure?

A) Deflate the tracheostomy cuff completely.
B) Instill 5 mL of normal saline into the tracheostomy tube.
C) Ask the client to swallow forcefully several times.
D) Hyperoxygenate the client with 100% oxygen.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Suctioning removes oxygen from the airway along with the secretions, which can quickly lead to hypoxia and dysrhythmias. The nurse must hyperoxygenate the client (usually with 100% oxygen via a manual resuscitation bag or ventilator setting) prior to suctioning to provide a reserve of oxygen. Instilling saline (Option B) is no longer recommended as it can wash bacteria into the lower airway and cause severe bronchospasm. Deflating the cuff (Option A) or swallowing (Option C) are not related to preventing hypoxia during suctioning.
</details>

---

### Question 305
A 24-year-old client is recovering on the surgical unit following an abdominal appendectomy. The nurse is reinforcing instructions on how to use an incentive spirometer to prevent post-operative atelectasis. Which action by the client requires the nurse to intervene?

A) The client sits upright in a semi-Fowler’s position before using the device.
B) The client blows forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston.
C) The client seals their lips tightly around the mouthpiece.
D) The client takes a slow, deep breath in through the mouthpiece.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
The incentive spirometer is designed to promote sustained maximal inspiration, mimicking a deep sigh or yawn to pop open collapsed alveoli. Therefore, the client must inhale slowly and deeply. Blowing forcefully into the device (Option B) is an incorrect use of the equipment and requires the nurse to intervene and reteach the client. Sitting upright (Option A), sealing the lips (Option C), and taking slow, deep breaths in (Option D) are all correct steps in the procedure.
</details>
### Question 306
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is teaching the parents of a 6-year-old child newly diagnosed with asthma about proper medication administration. Which action by the parent indicates a correct understanding of how to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) attached to a spacer?

A) Pressing the canister multiple times in rapid succession before the child takes a breath.
B) Having the child hold their breath for about 10 seconds after inhaling the medication.
C) Washing the plastic spacer with boiling water after every single use.
D) Instructing the child to exhale quickly and forcefully directly into the spacer.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Having the child hold their breath for about 10 seconds after inhaling the medication.

**Rationale:** Holding the breath for 5 to 10 seconds allows the inhaled medication to settle deeply into the lower airways, maximizing its effectiveness. Option A is incorrect because the inhaler should be pressed only once per breath to ensure the correct dosage is delivered. Option C is incorrect because washing a spacer in boiling water can melt or warp the plastic; it should be washed gently with warm soapy water and air-dried. Option D is incorrect because the child should exhale fully *away* from the spacer before placing the mouthpiece in their mouth, not blow directly into it.
</details>

---

### Question 307
A home health nurse is evaluating a 72-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) who reports feeling severely short of breath and exhausted during meals. Which instruction should the nurse provide to help the client manage this symptom?

A) "Drink a large glass of water right before you start eating to stay hydrated."
B) "Eat three large meals a day to ensure you get adequate caloric intake."
C) "Rest for at least 30 minutes before sitting down to eat your meals."
D) "Perform vigorous chest physiotherapy immediately before you eat."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "Rest for at least 30 minutes before sitting down to eat your meals."

**Rationale:** Clients with COPD exert a significant amount of energy simply breathing, and eating can cause severe fatigue and dyspnea. Resting before meals helps conserve energy. Option A is incorrect because drinking large amounts of fluids right before eating fills the stomach, pressing on the diaphragm and making breathing more difficult, while also reducing appetite. Option B is incorrect; clients with COPD should eat small, frequent, high-calorie meals rather than large ones to reduce fatigue and abdominal distention. Option D is incorrect because chest physiotherapy is tiring and can loosen secretions that may induce coughing or vomiting if done right before a meal.
</details>

---

### Question 308
A nurse is caring for a 45-year-old client recovering from abdominal surgery on a medical-surgical unit. To prevent postoperative pneumonia, the client is prescribed an incentive spirometer. Which observation by the nurse requires immediate intervention?

A) The client exhales quickly and forcefully into the mouthpiece.
B) The client sits in a high-Fowler's position in bed while using the device.
C) The client inhales slowly and deeply to elevate the piston in the chamber.
D) The client holds their breath for 3 to 5 seconds at the peak of inspiration.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) The client exhales quickly and forcefully into the mouthpiece.

**Rationale:** An incentive spirometer is designed to promote deep lung expansion through *inhalation*, mimicking a sigh or yawn. Exhaling forcefully into the device is incorrect technique and requires the nurse to intervene and re-educate the client. Options B, C, and D all describe the correct use of an incentive spirometer: sitting upright allows for maximal lung expansion, inhaling slowly and deeply raises the piston, and holding the breath at the top of the inhalation ensures the alveoli stay open to prevent atelectasis.
</details>

---

### Question 309
A 28-year-old client arrives at the emergency department triage desk reporting a persistent cough for the last four weeks, night sweats, and coughing up blood-tinged sputum. The triage nurse suspects active pulmonary tuberculosis (TB). What is the nurse's priority action?

A) Draw blood to obtain a complete blood count and metabolic panel.
B) Obtain a detailed travel and occupational exposure history from the client.
C) Collect a sputum sample to send to the laboratory for acid-fast bacilli testing.
D) Place a surgical mask on the client and move them to a negative-pressure room.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Place a surgical mask on the client and move them to a negative-pressure room.

**Rationale:** Active tuberculosis is highly contagious and transmitted via airborne droplets. The immediate priority of the nurse is infection control to protect staff, other patients, and visitors. Applying a mask to the client and placing them in an airborne infection isolation room (negative pressure) is the first and most critical step. Options A, B, and C are all appropriate and necessary actions in the assessment and diagnosis of this client, but they are secondary to isolating the client to prevent the spread of the disease.
</details>

---

### Question 310
A nurse in a long-term care facility is rounding on an 85-year-old client who is receiving continuous oxygen at 2 L/min via nasal cannula. The client complains of severe dryness and crusting inside their nose. Which action should the nurse take to alleviate the client's discomfort?

A) Apply a water-soluble lubricant to the inside of the client's nares.
B) Increase the oxygen flow rate to 4 L/min to increase the delivery of humidity.
C) Remove the nasal cannula and independently switch the client to a simple face mask.
D) Coat the inside of the client's nares with a thick layer of petroleum jelly.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Apply a water-soluble lubricant to the inside of the client's nares.

**Rationale:** Continuous oxygen therapy can dry out the mucous membranes in the nasal passages. Applying a water-soluble lubricant helps moisturize the nares safely. Option B is incorrect because increasing the flow rate without a prescription is outside the nurse's scope of practice, and higher flow rates actually increase drying. Option C is incorrect as changing the oxygen delivery device requires a primary care provider's prescription and may not be tolerated by the client. Option D is incorrect and dangerous; petroleum jelly is oil-based, combustible when exposed to oxygen, and if inhaled, can cause lipoid pneumonia.
</details>
### Question 311
An 8-year-old child comes to the school nurse's office complaining of chest tightness and shortness of breath. The nurse notes expiratory wheezing, the use of accessory muscles to breathe, and a respiratory rate of 28 breaths per minute. Which initial action should the nurse take?

A. Instruct the child to lie down flat on a cot to rest the respiratory muscles.
B. Assist the child to sit upright and administer the prescribed albuterol inhaler.
C. Offer the child a glass of warm water to help soothe the airway spasms.
D. Call the parents to pick the child up before taking any further clinical action.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Sitting the child upright allows for maximum lung expansion. Administering a prescribed short-acting bronchodilator, such as albuterol, is the priority clinical action to quickly relieve bronchospasms during an acute asthma attack. Lying flat will worsen the child's respiratory distress. Drinking water does not reverse a bronchospasm, and delaying treatment to call the parents could lead to respiratory failure.
</details>

---

### Question 312
A 45-year-old patient is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with bilateral pneumonia. The nurse observes that the patient has a weak cough and is struggling to bring up thick, tenacious sputum. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate to help this patient clear the respiratory secretions?

A. Administering an over-the-counter cough suppressant every 4 hours.
B. Restricting oral fluid intake to prevent the development of pulmonary edema.
C. Encouraging an oral fluid intake of 2 to 3 liters per day if not medically contraindicated.
D. Keeping the patient on strict bed rest to conserve oxygen and energy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Increasing systemic hydration (2 to 3 liters of fluid per day) helps thin respiratory secretions, making them less tenacious and easier for the patient to cough up and expectorate. Cough suppressants are generally contraindicated because the patient needs to clear the airway. Restricting fluids would make the mucus thicker, and strict bed rest can lead to pooling of secretions in the lungs.
</details>

---

### Question 313
A 22-year-old patient is recovering on the surgical floor on the first day following an open appendectomy. To prevent the development of postoperative atelectasis, the nurse provides an incentive spirometer. Which instruction should the nurse give the patient regarding its proper use?

A. "Inhale slowly and deeply through the mouthpiece to raise the piston."
B. "Exhale forcefully into the incentive spirometer to make the piston rise."
C. "Take shallow, rapid breaths to avoid causing pain at your incision site."
D. "Avoid coughing after using the device until your incision is completely healed."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Incentive spirometry is designed to mimic natural sighing or yawning by encouraging the patient to take long, slow, deep breaths in (inhalations). This promotes optimal lung expansion and prevents alveolar collapse (atelectasis). Patients should not blow into the device. Taking shallow breaths increases the risk of atelectasis, and patients should be encouraged to cough after using the spirometer to clear any loosened secretions (while splinting their incision).
</details>

---

### Question 314
A 35-year-old patient arrives at the emergency department triage desk reporting a persistent cough for three weeks, drenching night sweats, and blood-tinged sputum. Which immediate action should the triage nurse take?

A. Ask the patient to sit in the main waiting room until an examination room is available.
B. Check the patient's blood pressure, heart rate, and temperature manually.
C. Auscultate the patient's lung sounds to assess for the presence of fluid or crackles.
D. Place a surgical mask on the patient and move them to an airborne infection isolation room.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
The patient's symptoms (persistent cough, night sweats, hemoptysis) are classic signs of active pulmonary tuberculosis (TB). The absolute priority is infection control to prevent transmission to staff and other patients. The nurse must immediately place a surgical mask on the patient and isolate them in a negative-pressure room. Vital signs and lung auscultation are important but must be done *after* isolation precautions are initiated.
</details>

---

### Question 315
A 78-year-old resident with severe chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) is experiencing mild dyspnea during a physical therapy session at a rehabilitation facility. The nurse intervenes by teaching the resident the pursed-lip breathing technique. How should the nurse explain this method?

A. "Breathe in deeply through your mouth and exhale as quickly as you can through your nose."
B. "Take a deep breath, hold it for 10 full seconds, and then release it forcefully."
C. "Inhale normally through your nose and exhale slowly through puckered lips."
D. "Pant rapidly through your mouth to get more oxygen into your lungs quickly."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Pursed-lip breathing helps keep the airways open longer during exhalation, preventing airway collapse and reducing the trapping of air common in COPD. The correct technique is to inhale through the nose and exhale slowly through lips puckered like they are blowing out a candle. Exhaling quickly, holding breath for long periods, or panting will increase the work of breathing and worsen dyspnea.
</details>
### Question 316
A nurse is observing an 82-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) demonstrating pursed-lip breathing in the medical-surgical unit. Which action by the client indicates that the teaching was effective?

A) Inhaling deeply through the mouth and exhaling quickly through the nose.  
B) Inhaling slowly through the nose and exhaling slowly through pursed lips.  
C) Holding the breath for 10 seconds before exhaling through the mouth.  
D) Breathing in and out rapidly through the mouth to catch their breath.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Pursed-lip breathing is an easy and highly effective technique for clients with COPD to keep their airways open longer during exhalation, which prevents airway collapse and improves gas exchange. The correct technique involves inhaling slowly through the nose and exhaling slowly through pursed lips, as if blowing out a candle. Exhaling quickly or breathing rapidly defeats the purpose of the exercise and can increase air trapping.
</details>

---

### Question 317
A school nurse is preparing to administer a prescribed albuterol metered-dose inhaler (MDI) to a 9-year-old student experiencing an asthma exacerbation. The nurse attaches a spacer to the inhaler. What is the primary purpose of using the spacer in this scenario?

A) It decreases the total dosage of medication needed to treat the asthma attack.  
B) It sterilizes the mouthpiece before the child inhales the medication.  
C) It ensures more medication is delivered to the lungs rather than the back of the throat.  
D) It measures the exact amount of air the child can forcefully exhale.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
A spacer is a hollow tube attached to the MDI that holds the medication in a chamber, allowing the child to inhale it slowly and deeply. This prevents the medication from simply hitting the back of the throat or mouth, ensuring that a higher concentration of the active drug reaches the lower airways where it is needed. It does not alter the dosage, measure airflow, or sterilize the device.
</details>

---

### Question 318
A 55-year-old client is admitted to the emergency department with suspected bacterial pneumonia. The healthcare provider prescribes intravenous antibiotics and a sputum culture. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Collect the sputum specimen before administering the first dose of antibiotics.  
B) Administer the first dose of antibiotics, then immediately collect the sputum specimen.  
C) Provide the client with a cup of chlorhexidine to rinse their mouth before providing the specimen.  
D) Administer a prescribed cough suppressant so the client can comfortably produce the specimen.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Sputum cultures should always be collected before initiating antibiotic therapy. Administering antibiotics prior to collection can alter the laboratory results by killing the bacteria in the sample, making it difficult to accurately identify the causative organism and tailor ongoing antibiotic therapy. Chlorhexidine should not be used as it is antimicrobial and will affect the culture, and cough suppressants will hinder the client's ability to produce sputum.
</details>

---

### Question 319
A 22-year-old client arrives at the urgent care clinic with an active nosebleed (epistaxis) that started ten minutes ago. What is the most appropriate action for the nurse to take to help control the bleeding?

A) Instruct the client to lie flat on their back and apply a warm compress to the nose.  
B) Ask the client to tilt their head backward and pinch the hard, bony bridge of the nose.  
C) Encourage the client to blow their nose vigorously to clear out any existing blood clots.  
D) Have the client sit upright, lean slightly forward, and pinch the soft part of the nose.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
The correct initial management for epistaxis is to have the client sit upright and lean slightly forward. Leaning forward prevents blood from draining down the back of the throat, which can cause nausea, vomiting, or aspiration. The nurse should instruct the client to apply direct pressure by pinching the soft, lower part of the nose (nostrils) against the facial bones for 10 to 15 minutes. Tilting the head back or lying flat increases the risk of swallowing blood.
</details>

---

### Question 320
A nurse is caring for a 45-year-old client on a surgical unit who is one day postoperative following an abdominal hysterectomy. The nurse is observing the client use an incentive spirometer. Which observation indicates that the client is performing the exercise correctly?

A) The client seals their lips tightly around the mouthpiece and inhales slowly and deeply.  
B) The client takes a deep breath and blows forcefully into the mouthpiece.  
C) The client exhales rapidly into the device to raise the piston as high as possible.  
D) The client uses the device rapidly for 20 continuous breaths to build lung strength.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Incentive spirometry is used postoperatively to promote lung expansion and prevent complications like atelectasis and pneumonia. The proper technique requires the client to sit upright, form a tight seal around the mouthpiece, and inhale (breathe in) as slowly and deeply as possible to raise the indicator. The device is not blown into (exhaled into), and rapid, continuous breathing can cause hyperventilation and dizziness.
</details>
### Question 321
A nurse is observing a 14-year-old patient use an albuterol metered-dose inhaler (MDI) for asthma management during an outpatient clinic visit. Which action by the patient indicates a need for further teaching by the nurse?

A) Shaking the inhaler canister vigorously before administration.  
B) Waiting one full minute between the first and second puff.  
C) Exhaling forcefully directly into the mouthpiece before pressing the canister.  
D) Holding the breath for about 10 seconds after inhaling the medication.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The patient should exhale completely *away* from the mouthpiece before placing the inhaler in their mouth to take a breath. Exhaling directly into the mouthpiece can introduce moisture into the device, potentially clogging it or altering the delivery of the medication. Shaking the canister (A), waiting a minute between puffs of a bronchodilator (B), and holding the breath for 10 seconds to allow the medication to settle in the lungs (D) are all correct actions for using an MDI.
</details>

---

### Question 322
A nurse enters the room of a 72-year-old patient admitted to the medical-surgical unit with a chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) exacerbation. The nurse observes the patient sitting on the edge of the bed, leaning forward with their arms resting on an overbed table. What is the nurse's best initial action?

A) Document the patient's use of the orthopneic position as a helpful breathing strategy.  
B) Assist the patient back into a supine position in bed to promote energy conservation.  
C) Immediately apply a non-rebreather mask at 15 L/min to increase oxygenation.  
D) Prepare to administer a PRN dose of intravenous furosemide for fluid overload.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The patient is demonstrating the orthopneic (or "tripod") position, which is a normal and highly effective compensatory posture for individuals with COPD. This position allows maximum expansion of the chest and utilizes accessory muscles to aid in breathing. The nurse should simply document this as a normal and expected finding. Forcing the patient into a supine position (B) would likely increase their work of breathing and cause distress. High-flow oxygen (C) or diuretics (D) are not indicated based solely on the patient assuming this helpful position.
</details>

---

### Question 323
A 45-year-old patient is recovering on the surgical ward on the first postoperative day following an open cholecystectomy. To prevent atelectasis and pneumonia, the nurse is educating the patient on the correct use of an incentive spirometer. Which instruction should the nurse provide?

A) "Blow into the mouthpiece as hard and fast as you can to move the ball."  
B) "Use the device once a day right before going to sleep at night."  
C) "Take short, shallow pants into the device to keep the piston elevated."  
D) "Inhale slowly and deeply through the mouthpiece to raise the piston."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
An incentive spirometer is designed to mimic natural sighing or yawning by encouraging the patient to take long, slow, deep breaths in (inspiration). This pops open collapsed alveoli and prevents respiratory complications. Blowing into the device (A) is incorrect, as it is not a peak flow meter. The device should be used frequently, typically 10 times an hour while awake, not just once a day (B). Short, shallow breaths (C) do not promote adequate lung expansion.
</details>

---

### Question 324
A 28-year-old patient presents to the emergency department after a motor vehicle collision resulting in multiple rib fractures. While monitoring the patient, the nurse notes the patient is suddenly becoming increasingly restless, agitated, and confused. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Administer a prescribed antianxiety medication to calm the patient down.  
B) Assess the patient's oxygen saturation levels using a pulse oximeter.  
C) Ask the patient's visiting family to step out of the room to reduce stimulation.  
D) Apply soft restraints to prevent the patient from pulling out their intravenous line.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Sudden restlessness, agitation, and confusion are hallmark early signs of hypoxia (inadequate oxygenation), especially in a patient with a compromised airway or chest trauma. The nurse's first action must be to assess the patient's respiratory status and oxygenation. Administering an antianxiety medication (A) could depress the respiratory drive and worsen the hypoxia. Removing family (C) or applying restraints (D) ignores the potentially life-threatening physiological cause of the altered mental status.
</details>

---

### Question 325
A nurse is caring for a 68-year-old patient in the step-down unit who has a closed-chest drainage system in place for a spontaneous pneumothorax. During the morning assessment, the nurse observes gentle rising and falling of the fluid level in the water-seal chamber with the patient's respirations. What is the most appropriate action by the nurse?

A) Document the finding as normal system function and continue to monitor.  
B) Assess the system's tubing immediately for a loose connection or air leak.  
C) Clamp the chest tube near the insertion site and notify the provider.  
D) Increase the wall suction until the fluid in the chamber stops fluctuating.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The gentle rising and falling of fluid in the water-seal chamber is known as "tidaling." This is a normal, expected finding that indicates the chest tube is patent and functioning properly to restore negative pressure in the pleural space. Continuous bubbling (not tidaling) would indicate an air leak requiring assessment (B). Clamping a chest tube (C) is generally contraindicated as it can cause a tension pneumothorax. Increasing suction to stop fluctuation (D) is incorrect and misunderstands the mechanics of the system.
</details>
### Question 326
A triage nurse in the emergency department is assessing a 7-year-old child brought in by their parents for an acute asthma exacerbation. Which observation requires the nurse's immediate intervention?

A) Expiratory wheezing on auscultation
B) Substernal retractions during inspiration
C) Oxygen saturation of 94% on room air
D) A frequent, dry cough

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Substernal retractions during inspiration

**Rationale:** Substernal retractions indicate severe respiratory distress and increased work of breathing, signifying that the child is struggling to move air into the lungs. This requires immediate medical intervention to prevent respiratory failure. While expiratory wheezing and a dry cough are common and expected findings during an asthma attack, retractions are a sign of decompensation. An oxygen saturation of 94% is slightly low but not as immediately life-threatening as the presence of retractions.
</details>

---

### Question 327
A nurse is caring for a 68-year-old client on the surgical unit who is one day postoperative following an abdominal hysterectomy. The client reports significant pain when attempting to take deep breaths. Which nursing action is the most appropriate first step to promote lung expansion and prevent complications?

A) Administer high-flow oxygen via a non-rebreather mask.
B) Instruct the client to take shallow breaths to minimize discomfort.
C) Request a prescription for a cough suppressant from the provider.
D) Teach the client to splint the incision with a pillow while coughing and deep breathing.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Teach the client to splint the incision with a pillow while coughing and deep breathing.

**Rationale:** After abdominal surgery, pain often restricts chest wall movement, putting the client at risk for shallow breathing and atelectasis. Splinting the incision with a pillow provides physical support, reduces pain, and encourages the client to perform necessary deep breathing and coughing exercises. Encouraging shallow breathing or giving a cough suppressant would increase the risk of atelectasis and pneumonia. High-flow oxygen is not indicated for postoperative pain preventing deep breathing.
</details>

---

### Question 328
A 25-year-old client recently diagnosed with active pulmonary tuberculosis (TB) is receiving discharge instructions from the clinic nurse regarding medication management. Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the treatment plan?

A) "I will need to take my prescribed medications for several months."
B) "I can stop taking the antibiotics once my chronic cough goes away."
C) "I should wear a surgical mask whenever I am alone in my bedroom."
D) "I will only need to provide one sputum sample at the end of treatment."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "I will need to take my prescribed medications for several months."

**Rationale:** The treatment for active pulmonary tuberculosis requires a prolonged course of multiple medications, typically lasting 6 to 9 months, to completely eradicate the bacteria and prevent multidrug-resistant TB. Stopping the medication when symptoms resolve is dangerous and leads to resistance. The client does not need to wear a mask when alone in their own room. Multiple sputum cultures are required during the course of treatment to determine if the therapy is effective and if the client is no longer infectious.
</details>

---

### Question 329
A nurse at a long-term care facility is assisting an 82-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) who is experiencing mild shortness of breath while eating breakfast. What is the best initial action by the nurse?

A) Increase the client's supplemental oxygen to 10 liters per minute.
B) Lay the client flat in bed to conserve their energy.
C) Encourage the client to use pursed-lip breathing techniques.
D) Remove the client's breakfast tray and initiate intravenous fluids.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Encourage the client to use pursed-lip breathing techniques.

**Rationale:** Pursed-lip breathing is a simple, effective, and non-invasive technique that helps prolong exhalation, prevents alveolar collapse, and relieves dyspnea in clients with COPD. Laying the client flat would worsen shortness of breath; sitting upright is preferred. Increasing oxygen to 10 liters per minute is contraindicated for COPD clients as it can suppress their hypoxic drive to breathe. Removing the tray and starting IV fluids is an unnecessary overreaction to mild dyspnea associated with exertion in a COPD client.
</details>

---

### Question 330
A home health nurse is visiting a 45-year-old client recovering from bacterial pneumonia. The client asks the nurse how to prevent spreading the infection to family members living in the house. Which instruction should the nurse provide?

A) "Wash your hands frequently, especially after coughing or sneezing."
B) "Make sure everyone in the house shares the same drinking glasses to build immunity."
C) "Avoid opening any windows in the house to keep the air strictly enclosed."
D) "Stop taking your prescribed antibiotics immediately if you feel completely better."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "Wash your hands frequently, especially after coughing or sneezing."

**Rationale:** Frequent and thorough hand washing is the single most effective way to prevent the transmission of respiratory infections like pneumonia. Sharing drinking glasses promotes the spread of pathogens and should be avoided. Poorly ventilated areas increase the risk of transmission; opening windows for fresh air circulation is generally beneficial. Antibiotics must be taken for the full prescribed course to ensure the infection is completely cleared, even if symptoms have resolved.
</details>
### Question 331
An 8-year-old child in an outpatient clinic is demonstrating the use of a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer for asthma management. Which observation by the nurse indicates that the child is using the device correctly?

A. The child exhales rapidly into the spacer before pressing the canister.
B. The child presses the canister multiple times before taking a breath.
C. The child inhales slowly and deeply after pressing the canister once.
D. The child removes the spacer immediately and breathes through the nose.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** To correctly use an MDI with a spacer, the client should press the canister once to release the medication into the chamber, then inhale slowly and deeply through the mouth. This ensures the medication reaches the lower airways rather than hitting the back of the throat. Exhaling into the spacer (Option A) blows the medication out. Pressing the canister multiple times (Option B) delivers an inaccurate dose and wastes medication. Removing the spacer immediately and breathing through the nose (Option D) prevents the medication from entering the lungs properly.
</details>

---

### Question 332
A 55-year-old client arrives at the emergency department triage desk reporting a persistent cough for three weeks, night sweats, and unexplained weight loss. Which action should the nurse take first?

A. Place a surgical mask on the client and move them to an isolation room.
B. Obtain a sputum sample for acid-fast bacilli (AFB) testing.
C. Assess the client's lung sounds and pulse oximetry reading.
D. Start a peripheral intravenous line for fluid administration.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** The client is exhibiting classic signs of active tuberculosis (TB). The nurse's first priority in this clinical scenario is infection control to protect other clients, staff, and visitors. Placing a surgical mask on the client and moving them to a negative pressure isolation room implements airborne precautions. While obtaining a sputum sample (Option B) and assessing lung sounds (Option C) are important subsequent steps, they do not take priority over preventing the potential spread of a highly contagious airborne disease. Starting an IV (Option D) is not the immediate priority for a stable client presenting with these symptoms.
</details>

---

### Question 333
A 45-year-old client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with bilateral pneumonia. The nurse observes that the client is having difficulty coughing up thick, tenacious secretions. Which independent nursing action is most appropriate to assist the client?

A. Administer a prescribed antitussive medication to suppress the cough.
B. Place the client in a supine position to promote airway clearance.
C. Request a prescription for a broad-spectrum intravenous antibiotic.
D. Encourage the client to increase oral fluid intake to 2 to 3 liters per day.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Increasing oral fluid intake to 2 to 3 liters per day (if not contraindicated by conditions like heart failure) is a highly effective, independent nursing action to help thin respiratory secretions, making them easier to expectorate. Administering an antitussive (Option A) is contraindicated because suppressing the cough will cause secretions to pool in the lungs, worsening the pneumonia. Placing the client in a supine position (Option B) hinders lung expansion and makes coughing more difficult; the client should be placed in an upright (Fowler's) position. Requesting antibiotics (Option C) is a collaborative action that treats the infection but does not immediately address the mechanical issue of thick secretions.
</details>

---

### Question 334
A 28-year-old client is recovering on the surgical unit following an abdominal hysterectomy. The nurse is evaluating the client's use of an incentive spirometer to prevent atelectasis. Which action by the client indicates effective teaching?

A. The client blows forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston.
B. The client seals their lips around the mouthpiece and inhales deeply.
C. The client takes quick, shallow breaths through the device every hour.
D. The client uses the device only when experiencing shortness of breath.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Incentive spirometry is designed to promote deep lung expansion and prevent alveolar collapse (atelectasis) postoperatively. The correct technique involves exhaling normally, sealing the lips tightly around the mouthpiece, and inhaling slowly and deeply to elevate the piston or ball. Blowing into the mouthpiece (Option A) is incorrect, as the device is for inhalation, not exhalation. Quick, shallow breaths (Option C) do not promote adequate lung expansion. The device should be used regularly (e.g., 5-10 times an hour while awake) to *prevent* respiratory complications, not just when the client feels short of breath (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 335
A 78-year-old resident in a long-term care facility has a history of severe chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The nurse is checking the client's vital signs and notes an oxygen saturation of 90% on room air. The client appears comfortable and is resting quietly in a chair. Which action should the nurse take?

A. Immediately apply oxygen at 4 liters per minute via nasal cannula.
B. Instruct the client to take rapid, deep breaths to increase oxygen levels.
C. Document the findings as an acceptable baseline for this client.
D. Prepare to administer a short-acting bronchodilator nebulizer treatment.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** In clients with severe COPD, an oxygen saturation level between 88% and 92% is generally considered an acceptable and expected baseline. Because the client is resting quietly and shows no signs of acute respiratory distress, the nurse should simply document the finding. Applying high levels of oxygen (Option A) can be dangerous for some COPD clients, as it may decrease their hypoxic drive to breathe. Instructing the client to take rapid, deep breaths (Option B) can cause hyperventilation and fatigue. There is no indication of an acute bronchospasm requiring a nebulizer treatment (Option D) since the client is comfortable and at their expected baseline.
</details>
### Question 336
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is observing a 68-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) performing breathing exercises. Which observation indicates to the nurse that the client is performing pursed-lip breathing correctly?

A) The client inhales deeply through the mouth and exhales quickly through the nose.
B) The client inhales through the nose and exhales slowly through puckered lips.
C) The client takes short, rapid breaths through the mouth while puffing out the cheeks.
D) The client holds their breath for 10 seconds before exhaling forcefully through the mouth.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Pursed-lip breathing helps keep the airways open longer, allowing the client to expel more trapped air and decreasing the work of breathing. The correct technique involves inhaling normally through the nose and exhaling slowly and gently through puckered (pursed) lips, usually taking twice as long to exhale as to inhale. Options A, C, and D are incorrect techniques that do not promote effective emptying of the lungs or decrease air trapping.
</details>

---

### Question 337
A triage nurse in the emergency department is assessing a 4-year-old child brought in by a parent for an acute asthma exacerbation. Which clinical finding should the nurse recognize as the most reliable indicator of increased respiratory effort?

A) The child frequently clears their throat while answering questions.
B) The child has a respiratory rate of 24 breaths per minute.
C) The child exhibits intercostal retractions during inspiration.
D) The child displays pink mucous membranes and nail beds.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Intercostal retractions (the inward pulling of the muscles between the ribs during inspiration) are a classic sign of respiratory distress and increased work of breathing in children. A respiratory rate of 24 is within normal limits for a 4-year-old. Frequent throat clearing is not a primary sign of respiratory distress, and pink mucous membranes indicate adequate oxygenation, which is a reassuring finding.
</details>

---

### Question 338
A nurse is caring for a 55-year-old client who is recovering from abdominal surgery on the surgical step-down unit. The nurse is instructing the client on the use of an incentive spirometer to prevent pneumonia. Which action by the client demonstrates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A) The client seals their lips tightly around the mouthpiece and inhales deeply and slowly.
B) The client blows forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the indicator piston.
C) The client uses the device while lying completely flat on their back in bed.
D) The client takes quick, shallow breaths into the device every 2 hours.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The correct use of an incentive spirometer requires the client to form a tight seal around the mouthpiece and inhale slowly and deeply to maximize lung expansion and prevent atelectasis. Option B is incorrect because the device requires inspiration, not expiration. Option C is incorrect as the client should be sitting upright (Fowler's or semi-Fowler's position) to allow for optimal lung expansion. Option D is incorrect because breaths should be deep and slow, not quick and shallow. 
</details>

---

### Question 339
A 22-year-old college student presents to the university health clinic reporting a three-week history of a productive cough, night sweats, and unexplained weight loss. What is the priority nursing action?

A) Administer a prescribed broad-spectrum antibiotic.
B) Collect a sputum sample for culture and sensitivity.
C) Place the client on a continuous pulse oximeter to monitor oxygenation.
D) Provide the client with a surgical mask and move them to a private room.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
The client's symptoms (prolonged productive cough, night sweats, weight loss) are highly suspicious for active pulmonary tuberculosis (TB). The priority nursing action is infection control to prevent the potential spread of the airborne disease. The nurse should immediately place a surgical mask on the client and isolate them in a private room (ideally a negative pressure room if available). While sputum collection and oxygen monitoring are important, protecting staff and other patients from exposure is the immediate priority.
</details>

---

### Question 340
A nurse on the pediatric unit is preparing to feed a 6-month-old infant diagnosed with bronchiolitis caused by respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). The infant has copious nasal secretions and is visibly congested. Which initial action by the nurse is most appropriate to promote effective feeding?

A) Administer a prescribed nebulizer treatment immediately before offering the bottle.
B) Warm the infant's formula to help soothe their irritated throat.
C) Suction the infant's nares with a bulb syringe before offering the bottle.
D) Switch the infant to a bottle nipple with a larger hole to decrease sucking effort.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Infants are obligate nose breathers, and nasal congestion severely impacts their ability to breathe while feeding. Suctioning the nares with a bulb syringe (often with a few drops of normal saline) clears the airway, making it easier for the infant to breathe and suck simultaneously. Nebulizer treatments immediately before feeding can agitate the infant and increase the risk of vomiting. Warming the formula or changing the nipple size does not address the underlying issue of the blocked nasal airway.
</details>
### Question 341
A nurse is checking the oxygen saturation of a 72-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with pneumonia. The client's fingers are cold to the touch, and the pulse oximeter is unable to obtain a consistent reading. What is the nurse's best initial action?

A. Document the client's oxygen saturation as unreadable.
B. Apply the sensor probe to the client's earlobe.
C. Request an order for an arterial blood gas (ABG) test.
D. Increase the client's oxygen flow rate immediately.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Apply the sensor probe to the client's earlobe.

**Rationale:** When a client has cold extremities or poor peripheral circulation, a finger pulse oximeter may fail to provide an accurate reading. The nurse's best initial action is to find an alternative, highly vascular site, such as the earlobe or the bridge of the nose, to obtain an accurate oxygen saturation level. Documenting it as unreadable without trying another site is negligent. An ABG is invasive and unnecessary as a first step. Increasing oxygen without knowing the client's actual saturation level is an unsafe clinical practice.
</details>

---

### Question 342
A school nurse is observing a 10-year-old student who is using a short-acting bronchodilator metered-dose inhaler (MDI) for an asthma flare-up. Which action by the child requires the nurse to intervene and provide immediate correction?

A. Shaking the canister vigorously before use.
B. Exhaling completely before placing the mouthpiece in the mouth.
C. Waiting one minute between the first and second puff.
D. Spraying the medication while exhaling rapidly.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Spraying the medication while exhaling rapidly.

**Rationale:** The nurse must intervene if the child exhales while spraying the medication. For an MDI to be effective, the client must inhale slowly and deeply while pressing down on the canister to ensure the medication reaches the lower airways. Shaking the canister, exhaling prior to placing the inhaler in the mouth, and waiting about a minute between puffs are all correct techniques that do not require intervention. 
</details>

---

### Question 343
A nurse is caring for a 24-year-old client who is 12 hours postoperative following an open appendectomy. The nurse is instructing the client on how to use an incentive spirometer to prevent atelectasis. Which observation indicates that the client understands the teaching?

A. The client inhales slowly and deeply through the mouthpiece.
B. The client blows forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston.
C. The client takes quick, shallow breaths into the device.
D. The client breathes in and out of the mouthpiece rapidly.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. The client inhales slowly and deeply through the mouthpiece.

**Rationale:** An incentive spirometer is designed to mimic natural sighing or yawning to encourage lung expansion and prevent atelectasis. The correct technique requires the client to inhale slowly and deeply, elevating the piston or balls inside the device, and then hold their breath for a few seconds before exhaling. Blowing into the device or taking rapid, shallow breaths are incorrect techniques and indicate a need for further teaching.
</details>

---

### Question 344
A nurse in a pulmonary rehabilitation clinic is evaluating a 55-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) who is practicing pursed-lip breathing. Which action by the client demonstrates the correct technique?

A. Inhaling through the mouth and exhaling forcefully through the nose.
B. Holding the breath for 10 seconds before exhaling.
C. Exhaling slowly through pursed lips for twice as long as the inhalation.
D. Taking short, rapid breaths through the nose and mouth simultaneously.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Exhaling slowly through pursed lips for twice as long as the inhalation.

**Rationale:** Pursed-lip breathing helps keep the airways open longer, preventing airway collapse and promoting the release of trapped air in clients with COPD. The correct technique involves inhaling through the nose and exhaling slowly through pursed lips, with the exhalation phase lasting about twice as long as the inhalation phase. Exhaling forcefully, holding the breath for long periods, or taking rapid breaths defeat the purpose of this relaxation and airway-clearance technique.
</details>

---

### Question 345
A home health nurse is visiting an 80-year-old client who needs to provide a sputum specimen for culture. The client asks the nurse when the best time to collect the sample is. How should the nurse respond?

A. "Collect the specimen immediately after eating your dinner."
B. "Wait until right before going to bed tonight to cough it up."
C. "Collect the sample right after using an antibacterial mouthwash."
D. "Provide the sample first thing in the morning upon waking."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. "Provide the sample first thing in the morning upon waking."

**Rationale:** The optimal time to collect a sputum specimen is early in the morning upon waking. Secretions pool in the lower respiratory tract overnight, making it easier for the client to produce a concentrated, high-quality sample. Collecting immediately after eating increases the risk of emesis or food particle contamination. Using antibacterial mouthwash before collection can destroy the microorganisms needed for an accurate culture.
</details>
### Question 346
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is observing a 68-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) perform pursed-lip breathing. Which action by the client indicates to the nurse that the teaching was effective?

A) The client inhales deeply through the mouth and exhales quickly.
B) The client puffs out their cheeks while exhaling forcibly.
C) The client inhales through the nose and exhales slowly through puckered lips.
D) The client holds their breath for ten seconds before exhaling.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Pursed-lip breathing is a technique used to prolong exhalation, prevent airway collapse, and reduce air trapping in clients with COPD. The correct technique involves inhaling through the nose and exhaling slowly and steadily through puckered lips, much like blowing out a candle. Exhaling quickly, mouth-breathing, or puffing the cheeks indicates incorrect technique and requires further teaching.
</details>

---

### Question 347
A school nurse is called to the playground for an 8-year-old child who is experiencing a mild asthma attack. The child has a prescribed albuterol metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer. Which action should the nurse take first when administering the medication?

A) Ask the child to take a deep breath and hold it before attaching the spacer.
B) Shake the inhaler vigorously before attaching it to the spacer.
C) Administer two puffs of the medication into the spacer simultaneously.
D) Have the child lay flat on their back to improve lung expansion.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Before administering a metered-dose inhaler (MDI), the nurse must shake the canister vigorously to properly mix the medication and the propellant. The child should sit upright, not lay flat, to maximize lung expansion. Medication should be administered one puff at a time, allowing the child to inhale and hold their breath after each puff, rather than spraying multiple puffs into the spacer simultaneously.
</details>

---

### Question 348
An urgent care nurse is assessing a 22-year-old client who walks in with an active spontaneous nosebleed (epistaxis). Which initial action should the nurse instruct the client to perform?

A) Blow the nose forcefully to clear any forming blood clots.
B) Lie down flat on an exam table to prevent fainting.
C) Tilt the head back as far as possible to stop the bleeding.
D) Sit upright, lean slightly forward, and pinch the soft part of the nose.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
The correct initial action for epistaxis is to sit upright and lean slightly forward. This position prevents blood from draining down the back of the throat, which can cause nausea, vomiting, or aspiration. The client should apply direct, continuous pressure by pinching the soft lower part of the nose for 5 to 15 minutes. Tilting the head back or lying flat is contraindicated.
</details>

---

### Question 349
A nurse in a long-term care facility is caring for a 75-year-old client diagnosed with bacterial pneumonia. The client has thick, tenacious secretions and a weak cough. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate to help the client clear their airway?

A) Encourage oral fluid intake of at least 2 liters per day, unless contraindicated.
B) Administer a prescribed cough suppressant medication every 4 hours.
C) Place the client in a supine position to promote chest expansion.
D) Restrict the client's physical activity to strictly bed rest.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Adequate hydration is essential for clients with respiratory infections because it helps thin respiratory secretions, making them easier to expectorate. Unless the client has a contraindication (such as heart failure or severe kidney disease), increasing fluid intake is highly effective. Cough suppressants are generally avoided because coughing is necessary to clear the airway. The supine position impairs lung expansion, and the client should be positioned upright (Fowler's or semi-Fowler's).
</details>

---

### Question 350
A nurse is receiving shift report in the intensive care step-down unit for a 50-year-old client who had a new tracheostomy placed 24 hours ago. Upon entering the room for the initial assessment, which equipment is the most critical for the nurse to verify is present at the bedside?

A) An incentive spirometer and a peak flow meter.
B) A sterile dressing change kit and hydrogen peroxide.
C) An obturator and a replacement tracheostomy tube of the same size.
D) A blood pressure cuff and an automated external defibrillator (AED).

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
For a client with a new tracheostomy, accidental decannulation is a life-threatening airway emergency. The nurse must ensure that safety equipment is at the bedside at all times, which includes an obturator, a replacement tracheostomy tube of the same size, and often a tube one size smaller. If the tube becomes dislodged, the obturator is used to guide the replacement tube into the stoma quickly. 
</details>
### Question 351
A nurse is caring for a 68-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The client is receiving continuous oxygen at 2 L/min via nasal cannula. Which observation indicates to the nurse that the current oxygen therapy is effective and safe for this client?

A. The client's respiratory rate increases to 28 breaths per minute.
B. The client's oxygen saturation remains stable between 88% and 92%.
C. The client reports feeling drowsy and is difficult to arouse.
D. The client's lips and nail beds have a faint bluish tinge.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
For clients with COPD, a normal and safe oxygen saturation goal is typically between 88% and 92%. Providing too much oxygen can eliminate their hypoxic drive to breathe, leading to dangerous carbon dioxide retention. An oxygen saturation in this target range demonstrates safe and effective therapy. An increased respiratory rate (A), severe drowsiness (C, a sign of CO2 narcosis), and cyanosis (D) all indicate worsening respiratory status.
</details>

---

### Question 352
A pediatric nurse is assessing a 3-year-old child brought to the emergency department with a harsh, barking cough and suspected croup. While observing the child's breathing pattern, which finding should the nurse immediately identify as an early sign of respiratory distress?

A. A respiratory rate of 24 breaths per minute.
B. The child refusing to lie flat on the examination table.
C. The child’s skin appearing warm and pink.
D. The presence of intercostal retractions during inspiration.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Intercostal retractions (the inward pulling of muscles between the ribs during inspiration) indicate increased work of breathing and are an early, objective clinical sign of respiratory distress in pediatric clients. A respiratory rate of 24 is within the normal range for a toddler (A). Refusing to lie flat is common in toddlers but not a direct physiological sign of distress like retractions (B). Warm, pink skin indicates adequate oxygenation and perfusion (C).
</details>

---

### Question 353
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a 45-year-old client who is recovering from an abdominal hysterectomy. To prevent postoperative atelectasis, the nurse teaches the client how to use an incentive spirometer. Which action by the client demonstrates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. The client forms a tight seal around the mouthpiece and inhales slowly and deeply.
B. The client exhales forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the indicator piston.
