C. The client takes quick, short breaths in and out of the device.
D. The client uses the device only when experiencing active shortness of breath.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The correct technique for using an incentive spirometer is to exhale normally, form a tight seal around the mouthpiece, and inhale slowly and deeply to elevate the piston. This action promotes optimal lung expansion and prevents atelectasis. Exhaling into the device (B) is incorrect. Quick, short breaths (C) do not adequately expand the alveoli. The device should be used regularly as prescribed (e.g., 10 times an hour while awake), not just when the client is short of breath (D).
</details>

---

### Question 354
A nurse in a long-term care facility is caring for an 82-year-old client diagnosed with right lower lobe pneumonia. The client is experiencing mild dyspnea and has a frequent, productive cough. Which initial nursing action is most appropriate to promote optimal oxygenation for this client?

A. Administer a prescribed antitussive medication immediately.
B. Place the client flat in a supine position to rest the respiratory muscles.
C. Elevate the head of the client's bed to a high-Fowler's position.
D. Instruct the client to restrict oral fluid intake to decrease lung secretions.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Elevating the head of the bed to a high-Fowler's position utilizes gravity to pull the diaphragm downward, which allows for maximal chest expansion and improves oxygenation. Placing the client in a supine position (B) restricts lung expansion. Fluid intake should generally be increased, not restricted, to help thin respiratory secretions so they can be coughed up easier (D). Suppressing a productive cough with an antitussive (A) is usually contraindicated in pneumonia, as the client needs to clear the infected secretions from the airway.
</details>

---

### Question 355
An occupational health nurse is called to assist a 24-year-old employee who is having an acute asthma attack. The client is wheezing loudly, visibly anxious, and has personal prescriptions for both an albuterol metered-dose inhaler (MDI) and a fluticasone MDI in their locker. Which action should the nurse take first?

A. Administer the fluticasone inhaler to reduce airway inflammation.
B. Instruct the client to take shallow breaths to conserve energy.
C. Ask the client to drink a glass of warm water to soothe the airway.
D. Administer the albuterol inhaler to provide immediate bronchodilation.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Albuterol is a short-acting beta-agonist (SABA) used as a "rescue" medication to provide rapid bronchodilation during an acute asthma attack. It must be administered first to open the airway. Fluticasone (A) is an inhaled corticosteroid used for long-term maintenance and prevention; it will not relieve an acute bronchospasm. Shallow breaths (B) and drinking water (C) are not appropriate or effective clinical interventions for a client experiencing an acute asthma attack.
</details>
### Question 356
A nurse in a pediatric clinic is teaching the parents of a 6-year-old child how to use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) for acute asthma management. Which action by the parent during a return demonstration indicates that the teaching was effective?

A) The parent instructs the child to inhale quickly and deeply while pressing the canister.
B) The parent attaches a spacer to the MDI before administering the medication.
C) The parent administers two puffs of the medication in rapid succession without pausing.
D) The parent has the child lie flat on their back before administering the medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Using a spacer with a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) is highly recommended, especially for children. It ensures that the medication reaches the lungs rather than depositing on the back of the throat. The child should inhale slowly, not quickly (A), pause for at least 1 minute between puffs (C), and sit upright to allow for maximum lung expansion (D).
</details>

---

### Question 357
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is observing an older adult client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) perform pursed-lip breathing. Which observation by the nurse indicates the client is performing the technique correctly?

A) The client breathes in through the mouth and exhales forcefully through the nose.
B) The client holds their breath for ten seconds after every deep inhalation.
C) The client exhales slowly through puckered lips for twice as long as the inhalation.
D) The client uses their accessory chest muscles to take rapid, shallow breaths.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Pursed-lip breathing helps keep the airways open longer and prolongs exhalation, which allows the client to blow off trapped carbon dioxide. The correct technique involves inhaling through the nose and exhaling slowly through pursed lips, with the exhalation lasting roughly twice as long as the inhalation. Exhaling forcefully (A) or taking rapid, shallow breaths (D) increases respiratory effort and airway collapse. Holding the breath for extended periods (B) is not a component of standard pursed-lip breathing.
</details>

---

### Question 358
A client is admitted to the emergency department with suspected pneumonia. The nurse observes that the client is restless, sweating, and has an oxygen saturation of 88% on room air. What is the nurse's immediate first action?

A) Elevate the head of the client's bed to a semi-Fowler's or high-Fowler's position.
B) Send the client to the radiology department for a stat chest X-ray.
C) Administer a prescribed dose of intravenous antibiotics.
D) Draw blood for a complete blood count and arterial blood gas analysis.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
For a client experiencing respiratory distress and hypoxia, the first and easiest independent nursing action is to elevate the head of the bed. Sitting the client up drops the diaphragm, promotes maximum lung expansion, and immediately improves oxygenation. While a chest X-ray, antibiotics, and lab work are all necessary steps in managing pneumonia, ensuring a patent airway and maximizing breathing capacity must occur first. 
</details>

---

### Question 359
A high school nurse is caring for an adolescent who presents to the office with a sudden, spontaneous nosebleed (epistaxis). Which action should the nurse instruct the student to take first?

A) Lie flat on a cot and place a warm washcloth over the forehead.
B) Tilt the head back as far as possible to stop the blood from dripping.
C) Blow the nose vigorously to clear out any existing blood clots.
D) Sit upright, lean slightly forward, and pinch the soft part of the nose.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
The proper first aid for a nosebleed is to have the client sit upright and lean slightly forward. Leaning forward prevents blood from draining down the back of the throat, which could cause nausea, vomiting, or aspiration. Pinching the soft part of the nose applies direct pressure to the bleeding vessels. Tilting the head back (B) or lying flat (A) increases the risk of swallowing blood. Blowing the nose (C) can dislodge clots and worsen the bleeding.
</details>

---

### Question 360
A home health nurse is visiting a young adult client who requires a sputum specimen collection to test for pulmonary tuberculosis. Which instruction should the nurse provide to ensure an accurate sample is obtained?

A) "Spit the saliva that pools in your mouth directly into the collection cup."
B) "Cough deeply to bring up mucus from your lungs first thing in the morning."
C) "Rinse your mouth with an alcohol-based mouthwash right before collecting the sample."
D) "Collect the specimen immediately after eating your dinner this evening."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Sputum specimens for tuberculosis testing are most accurate when collected early in the morning upon waking, as respiratory secretions pool in the lungs overnight. The sample must be deep sputum from the lungs, not just oral saliva (A). Mouthwashes, especially those with alcohol (C), should be avoided right before collection as they can alter the sample or kill microorganisms. Collecting after a meal (D) increases the risk of contamination with food particles or vomiting during the deep coughing effort.
</details>
### Question 361
A nurse is caring for a 68-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) on a medical-surgical unit. The client reports feeling severely short of breath and is observed using accessory muscles to breathe. Which immediate action should the nurse take to improve the client's breathing?

A) Place the client in a supine position with the knees flexed.
B) Administer a high flow of oxygen via a non-rebreather mask.
C) Assist the client to sit upright and lean forward over an overbed table.
D) Encourage the client to take rapid, shallow breaths.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Assisting the client into the tripod or orthopneic position (sitting upright and leaning forward over a table) drops the diaphragm and allows the accessory muscles of respiration to function more effectively, maximizing chest expansion. Placing the client supine (Option A) would worsen dyspnea. Administering high-flow oxygen (Option B) can be dangerous for some COPD patients whose respiratory drive depends on hypoxia. Rapid, shallow breaths (Option D) are ineffective; pursed-lip breathing is preferred.
</details>

---

### Question 362
An 8-year-old child is brought to the outpatient pediatric clinic by a parent. The nurse observes the child coughing, expiratory wheezing, and demonstrating mild intercostal retractions. Which prescribed medication should the nurse administer first?

A) Albuterol via a metered-dose inhaler with a spacer
B) Fluticasone via a dry powder inhaler
C) Oral prednisone liquid suspension
D) Intravenous methylprednisolone

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Albuterol is a short-acting beta-2 agonist (SABA) and serves as a rapid rescue medication to provide immediate relief of bronchospasm during an acute asthma exacerbation. Fluticasone (Option B) is an inhaled corticosteroid used for long-term maintenance, not acute relief. While systemic corticosteroids like oral prednisone (Option C) and IV methylprednisolone (Option D) are used to reduce inflammation during exacerbations, they take hours to become effective and are not the first-line immediate rescue intervention.
</details>

---

### Question 363
A middle school student comes to the school nurse's office with an active nosebleed (epistaxis) after being accidentally hit in the face with a basketball. Which initial action should the nurse take?

A) Tell the student to blow their nose forcefully to clear the blood clots.
B) Instruct the student to lie flat on their back to prevent dizziness.
C) Have the student tilt their head backward while holding a tissue to the nares.
D) Ask the student to sit up, lean forward, and pinch the soft lower part of the nose.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
The correct first aid management for epistaxis is to have the client sit upright, lean forward, and apply direct pressure by pinching the soft part of the nose for 10 to 15 minutes. Leaning forward prevents blood from draining down the back of the throat, which can lead to swallowing blood and subsequent nausea or vomiting. Tilting the head back (Option C) or lying flat (Option B) encourages swallowing blood. Blowing the nose (Option A) will disrupt clot formation and prolong the bleeding.
</details>

---

### Question 364
A nurse is caring for an 82-year-old resident in a long-term care facility who has recently been diagnosed with bacterial pneumonia. The resident has a weak cough effort and thick, tenacious secretions that are difficult to clear. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate to help the resident expectorate the secretions?

A) Administer a prescribed antitussive medication every 4 hours.
B) Encourage the resident to increase oral fluid intake, if not contraindicated.
C) Place the resident on strict bed rest to conserve their energy.
D) Limit the humidity in the resident's room to keep the airway dry.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Adequate systemic hydration is the most effective way to thin and loosen respiratory secretions, making them easier for the client to cough up and clear from the airway. Administering an antitussive (cough suppressant) (Option A) is contraindicated because the client needs to cough to clear the infection. Strict bedrest (Option C) promotes pooling of secretions; mobility should be encouraged as tolerated. Adding humidity, not limiting it (Option D), helps to moisten the airway and thin secretions.
</details>

---

### Question 365
A nurse is performing routine tracheostomy care for a 45-year-old client recovering from neck surgery in a step-down unit. When it is time to change the soiled tracheostomy ties, which action must the nurse take to ensure the client's airway remains secure?

A) Cut the old ties before measuring and securing the new ties.
B) Ensure there is enough space to fit four fingers between the ties and the neck.
C) Fasten the new ties securely in place before removing the old ties.
D) Instruct the client to hold their breath while the ties are being completely exchanged.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
To prevent accidental decannulation (dislodgement of the tracheostomy tube) if the client coughs or moves, the nurse must always secure the new tracheostomy ties before removing the old ones. Alternatively, a second nurse can hold the flange in place while the ties are changed. Cutting the old ties first (Option A) leaves the airway completely unsecured. The nurse should fit only one or two fingers under the ties (Option B); four fingers would make the ties too loose. Holding the breath (Option D) is unnecessary and does not secure the tube.
</details>
### Question 366
A nurse in a pediatric clinic is observing a 7-year-old child use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) for asthma. Which action by the child indicates to the nurse that the teaching was effective?

A) The child exhales rapidly immediately after pressing the canister.
B) The child takes short, quick breaths through the mouth while using the inhaler.
C) The child attaches a spacer to the MDI before administering the medication.
D) The child pumps the inhaler three times before taking the first breath.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The child attaches a spacer to the MDI before administering the medication.

**Rationale:** Using a spacer with an MDI is highly recommended, especially for children, because it helps ensure that the medication reaches the lungs rather than landing on the back of the throat or tongue. Taking short, quick breaths, pumping multiple times at once, or exhaling too quickly are incorrect techniques that decrease the effectiveness of the medication.
</details>

---

### Question 367
A nurse in a long-term care facility is caring for an 82-year-old resident with an exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The resident is experiencing acute shortness of breath. What is the most appropriate initial nursing action?

A) Assist the resident into a high-Fowler's position leaning forward on an overbed table.
B) Instruct the resident to breathe rapidly and shallowly through the nose.
C) Administer oxygen via a non-rebreather mask at 15 L/min.
D) Place the resident flat in bed with their legs elevated on a pillow.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Assist the resident into a high-Fowler's position leaning forward on an overbed table.

**Rationale:** Placing the patient in a high-Fowler's or orthopneic (tripod) position promotes maximum lung expansion, decreases the work of breathing, and provides immediate relief for shortness of breath. Administering high-flow oxygen (15 L/min) is contraindicated for many COPD patients. Lying flat worsens dyspnea, and rapid, shallow breathing decreases effective gas exchange.
</details>

---

### Question 368
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is teaching a 45-year-old patient with right lower lobe pneumonia how to use an incentive spirometer. Which observation indicates the patient understands the instructions correctly?

A) The patient exhales forcefully into the mouthpiece to move the piston.
B) The patient uses the device once every 4 hours while awake.
C) The patient inhales through the nose and exhales through the mouthpiece.
D) The patient seals their lips tightly around the mouthpiece and takes a slow, deep breath in.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) The patient seals their lips tightly around the mouthpiece and takes a slow, deep breath in.

**Rationale:** To correctly use an incentive spirometer, the patient must form a tight seal around the mouthpiece and inhale slowly and deeply through the mouth. This promotes optimal lung expansion and prevents atelectasis. Exhaling into the device is incorrect, and the device should typically be used 5 to 10 times every hour while awake, not just once every 4 hours.
</details>

---

### Question 369
A nurse in the emergency department is triaging a 10-year-old child who comes in with an active nosebleed (epistaxis). Which action should the nurse instruct the child to perform first?

A) Tilt the head back slightly and pinch the bridge of the nose.
B) Sit upright, lean the head slightly forward, and pinch the soft part of the nose.
C) Lie flat on a stretcher and apply a warm compress to the forehead.
D) Blow the nose forcefully to clear out any existing blood clots.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Sit upright, lean the head slightly forward, and pinch the soft part of the nose.

**Rationale:** Sitting upright and leaning forward prevents blood from draining down the back of the throat, which can cause swallowing of blood, nausea, and vomiting. Pinching the soft, lower part of the nose applies direct pressure to the bleeding vessels. Tilting the head back or lying flat increases the risk of aspiration. Blowing the nose disrupts clot formation and worsens bleeding.
</details>

---

### Question 370
A community health nurse is collecting a sputum specimen from a 25-year-old patient who is being evaluated for suspected pulmonary tuberculosis. Which instruction should the nurse provide to the patient to ensure an optimal sample?

A) "Swish your mouth with an alcohol-based mouthwash right before providing the sample."
B) "Provide the specimen at the end of the day right before going to sleep."
C) "Take a few deep breaths and then cough deeply to bring up mucus from your lungs."
D) "Spit the saliva that naturally collects in your mouth directly into the specimen cup."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "Take a few deep breaths and then cough deeply to bring up mucus from your lungs."

**Rationale:** A proper sputum specimen requires deep secretions from the lungs, not just saliva from the mouth. Taking deep breaths and coughing helps mobilize these secretions. Sputum samples are optimally collected first thing in the morning, not at bedtime. Mouthwash can alter the normal flora and affect the culture results.
</details>
### Question 371
An older adult patient in the outpatient clinic is having their vital signs assessed. The patient has a history of Raynaud's disease, and the nurse notes their hands are cold to the touch. The pulse oximeter reads 85% on the right index finger, but the patient denies shortness of breath and has pink mucous membranes. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Administer supplemental oxygen at 2 L/min via nasal cannula.
B) Call the rapid response team to evaluate the patient.
C) Relocate the pulse oximeter probe to the patient's earlobe.
D) Request a stat arterial blood gas (ABG) draw from the provider.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Pulse oximetry readings can be inaccurate if peripheral perfusion is poor, which is common in older adults with conditions like Raynaud's disease or cold extremities. Because the patient is asymptomatic and has pink mucous membranes, the low reading is likely an artifact of poor peripheral circulation. The nurse's first action should be to relocate the probe to a highly vascular area, such as the earlobe, to obtain a more accurate reading. Administering oxygen, calling a rapid response, or drawing an ABG are unnecessary and premature actions for a patient showing no actual signs of respiratory distress.
</details>

---

### Question 372
A 45-year-old patient is recovering on the surgical unit on the first day following an abdominal cholecystectomy. The nurse observes the patient holding the incentive spirometer and blowing forcefully into the mouthpiece. Which action should the nurse take?

A) Instruct the patient to seal their lips around the mouthpiece and inhale deeply.
B) Document that the patient is correctly utilizing the incentive spirometer.
C) Tell the patient to perform this breathing exercise only once every four hours.
D) Ask the respiratory therapist to change the device to a positive expiratory pressure mask.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
An incentive spirometer is designed to promote lung expansion and prevent atelectasis by encouraging deep, sustained inhalations. The patient is incorrectly exhaling into the device. The nurse should intervene by educating the patient to form a tight seal around the mouthpiece and take slow, deep breaths inward. Documenting this as correct is unsafe. The exercise should be performed 5 to 10 times every hour while awake, not once every four hours. There is no need to switch devices when basic patient education can correct the technique.
</details>

---

### Question 373
The school nurse is evaluating an 8-year-old child who came to the health office directly from the playground. The child is audibly wheezing, coughing, and rubbing their chest. The child's health record indicates a known history of asthma and an active prescription for a rescue medication. Which action should the nurse perform?

A) Offer the child a glass of warm water to soothe the throat.
B) Instruct the child to lie flat on a cot to rest the chest muscles.
C) Auscultate the child's bowel sounds to check for referred pain.
D) Assist the child in using their prescribed albuterol metered-dose inhaler.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Audible wheezing and coughing in a child with a known history of asthma indicate an acute asthma exacerbation, likely triggered by exercise or outdoor allergens. The priority action is to administer a short-acting bronchodilator (rescue inhaler) like albuterol to quickly open the airways. Lying flat is contraindicated as it increases the work of breathing; the child should sit upright. Giving warm water or checking bowel sounds ignores the immediate respiratory distress.
</details>

---

### Question 374
A nurse in a long-term care facility enters the room of a 72-year-old resident with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) states, "The resident was breathing a little fast after walking to the bathroom, so I turned their nasal cannula oxygen up from 2 L/min to 6 L/min." Which action should the nurse take immediately?

A) Praise the UAP for promptly treating the resident's shortness of breath.
B) Reduce the oxygen flow rate back to the prescribed 2 L/min.
C) Switch the resident from a nasal cannula to a non-rebreather mask.
D) Encourage the resident to take rapid, shallow breaths to catch their breath.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
Patients with COPD often rely on a hypoxic drive to breathe, meaning that high levels of supplemental oxygen can suppress their respiratory drive and lead to dangerous carbon dioxide retention. Furthermore, oxygen is a medication and must be administered exactly as prescribed. The nurse must immediately decrease the oxygen back to the prescribed 2 L/min baseline. The UAP should be educated later that oxygen settings cannot be changed without an order. Encouraging rapid, shallow breaths is ineffective; pursed-lip breathing is the correct technique for COPD. 
</details>

---

### Question 375
A 22-year-old patient is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with community-acquired right lower lobe pneumonia. During morning rounds, the nurse observes that the patient is coughing up thick, tenacious green sputum and reports feeling exhausted from the effort. The patient's lung sounds reveal coarse crackles bilaterally. What is the most appropriate nursing intervention?

A) Administer an over-the-counter cough suppressant to stop the coughing.
B) Place the patient in a supine position to promote chest expansion.
C) Encourage the patient to increase oral fluid intake to 2-3 liters per day.
D) Restrict the patient's dietary intake to clear liquids only.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Thick, tenacious secretions are difficult to expectorate and can cause the patient to expend excessive energy. Increasing systemic hydration (2 to 3 liters per day, if not contraindicated) is the easiest and most effective way to thin respiratory secretions, making them easier to cough up and clear from the lungs. Administering a cough suppressant is contraindicated because the patient needs to clear the infected secretions from the lungs. Supine positioning decreases lung expansion; the patient should be elevated (e.g., Fowler's position). There is no indication to restrict the patient's diet to clear liquids.
</details>
### Question 376
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is observing a 68-year-old client with a history of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) who reports mild shortness of breath. Which action by the client indicates they are using an effective breathing technique to manage their symptoms?

A) Breathing rapidly and shallowly through the mouth.  
B) Inhaling deeply through the nose and exhaling slowly through pursed lips.  
C) Using the chest muscles aggressively to pull air into the lungs.  
D) Holding their breath for five seconds after every exhalation.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
Pursed-lip breathing helps prolong exhalation, prevents alveolar collapse, and helps clients with COPD manage shortness of breath by promoting the elimination of carbon dioxide. Rapid breathing, aggressive use of accessory muscles, and holding the breath post-exhalation are ineffective and can worsen respiratory distress.
</details>

---

### Question 377
A triage nurse in the emergency department is assessing a 9-year-old child brought in with an acute asthma exacerbation. The child is wheezing audibly, restless, and has subcostal retractions. Which medication should the nurse anticipate administering first?

A) Intravenous methylprednisolone  
B) Oral montelukast  
C) Inhaled albuterol  
D) Inhaled fluticasone  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Inhaled albuterol is a short-acting beta-agonist (SABA) used as a rescue medication to rapidly relieve bronchospasm and open the airways during an acute asthma attack. Corticosteroids (like methylprednisolone and fluticasone) reduce inflammation but take longer to act, while montelukast is used for daily maintenance, not acute attacks.
</details>

---

### Question 378
A nurse on a surgical unit is caring for a 45-year-old client who is one day postoperative following an abdominal hysterectomy. The nurse is observing the client use an incentive spirometer. Which action by the client requires the nurse to intervene?

A) The client exhales forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston.  
B) The client sits upright on the edge of the bed before starting.  
C) The client seals their lips tightly around the mouthpiece.  
D) The client holds their breath for three seconds at the height of inspiration.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
The nurse must intervene if the client exhales into the device. Incentive spirometry is designed to promote deep, sustained inspiration to expand the alveoli and prevent postoperative atelectasis. The client should inhale slowly and deeply. Sitting upright, sealing the lips, and holding the breath at the end of inspiration are all correct techniques.
</details>

---

### Question 379
A nurse in a long-term care facility is preparing to suction the tracheostomy tube of an 82-year-old client. During the procedure, the nurse notices the client's oxygen saturation drops from 96% to 88%. What is the nurse's priority action?

A) Auscultate the client's lung sounds for crackles.  
B) Document the finding and notify the healthcare provider.  
C) Continue suctioning quickly to clear the airway.  
D) Stop suctioning and hyperoxygenate the client immediately.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
A drop in oxygen saturation below 90% during suctioning indicates hypoxia. The priority nursing action is to stop the procedure immediately and hyperoxygenate the client using a manual resuscitation bag or supplemental oxygen to restore normal saturation levels. Continuing to suction will worsen the hypoxia.
</details>

---

### Question 380
A nurse is performing a morning assessment on a 55-year-old client admitted to a medical-surgical floor with right lower lobe pneumonia. The client is currently lying flat in bed and complains of increased work of breathing. What is the most appropriate initial nursing action?

A) Elevate the head of the bed to a high-Fowler's position.  
B) Instruct the client to cough forcefully.  
C) Call the respiratory therapist for a breathing treatment.  
D) Administer a prescribed dose of pain medication.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
Elevating the head of the bed to a Fowler's or high-Fowler's position uses gravity to drop the diaphragm, which promotes maximum lung expansion and improves oxygenation. This is the fastest, non-invasive initial action for a client experiencing dyspnea while lying flat.
</details>
### Question 381
A pediatric nurse is observing an 8-year-old child use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) for acute asthma symptoms in the emergency department. Which observation indicates to the nurse that the child needs further teaching?

A) The child shakes the inhaler well for several seconds before use.
B) The child exhales completely before pressing down on the canister.
C) The child breathes in rapidly and shallowly while pressing the canister.
D) The child holds their breath for 10 seconds after inhaling the medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The child breathes in rapidly and shallowly while pressing the canister.

**Rationale:** When using a metered-dose inhaler, the client should breathe in slowly and deeply, not rapidly and shallowly, to ensure the medication reaches the lower airways. Rapid inhalation often causes the medication to hit the back of the throat instead of traveling to the lungs. Shaking the inhaler, exhaling completely before use, and holding the breath for about 10 seconds after inhalation are all correct techniques.
</details>

---

### Question 382
A nurse at a long-term care facility is teaching pursed-lip breathing to a 72-year-old resident who is experiencing dyspnea related to chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). Which instruction should the nurse provide?

A) "Inhale deeply through your nose and exhale slowly through puckered lips."
B) "Breathe in through your mouth and exhale quickly through your nose."
C) "Hold your breath for 10 seconds before exhaling forcefully through your mouth."
D) "Pant rapidly through your mouth to help clear the trapped air in your lungs."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "Inhale deeply through your nose and exhale slowly through puckered lips."

**Rationale:** Pursed-lip breathing helps keep the airways open longer, allowing for the removal of trapped air in the lungs, which is a common issue in COPD. The correct technique is to inhale through the nose and exhale slowly and gently through pursed (puckered) lips, with the exhalation taking about twice as long as the inhalation. Panting, forceful exhalation, or mouth-breathing do not promote airway opening and can worsen dyspnea.
</details>

---

### Question 383
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 45-year-old client who is one day postoperative following an abdominal hysterectomy. The nurse is instructing the client on how to properly use an incentive spirometer to prevent atelectasis. What is the correct nursing action?

A) Instruct the client to blow forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston.
B) Advise the client to use the device only when experiencing severe shortness of breath.
C) Tell the client to lie flat on their back while performing the breathing exercises.
D) Encourage the client to inhale slowly and deeply through the mouthpiece.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Encourage the client to inhale slowly and deeply through the mouthpiece.

**Rationale:** The purpose of an incentive spirometer is to promote deep lung expansion and prevent alveolar collapse (atelectasis) after surgery. The correct technique requires the client to inhale slowly and deeply, not exhale (blow) into the device. The client should ideally be sitting upright (Fowler's or semi-Fowler's position) for optimal lung expansion, not lying flat. The device should be used regularly (e.g., 10 times an hour while awake), not just when shortness of breath occurs.
</details>

---

### Question 384
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is obtaining a sputum specimen from a 19-year-old client diagnosed with suspected community-acquired pneumonia. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate for this procedure?

A) Ask the client to spit saliva continuously into the cup until it is half full.
B) Instruct the client to cough deeply from the chest and expectorate into the sterile cup.
C) Provide the client with an alcohol-based mouthwash to cleanse the mouth immediately before collection.
D) Advise the client to collect the specimen in the evening right before going to sleep.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Instruct the client to cough deeply from the chest and expectorate into the sterile cup.

**Rationale:** A proper sputum culture requires mucus originating from the lower respiratory tract, which is obtained by a deep chest cough. Saliva (spit) is not acceptable for a sputum culture as it does not reflect the bacteria in the lungs. Using mouthwash, especially one containing alcohol, before collection is contraindicated as it can kill the bacteria needed for the culture. Sputum specimens are best collected early in the morning upon waking, as secretions pool in the lungs overnight.
</details>

---

### Question 385
A nurse on the pediatric floor is caring for a 6-month-old infant admitted with respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) bronchiolitis. The infant has thick, copious nasal secretions and is becoming fussy and fatigued while attempting to take a bottle. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Administer a prescribed broad-spectrum oral antibiotic mixed with the infant's formula.
B) Elevate the foot of the infant's crib to promote postural drainage of the lungs.
C) Suction the infant's nares with a bulb syringe before offering the bottle.
D) Place a warm, moist towel over the infant's face to loosen the secretions.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Suction the infant's nares with a bulb syringe before offering the bottle.

**Rationale:** Infants are obligate nose breathers. When their nasal passages are obstructed with thick secretions, they have significant difficulty breathing and feeding simultaneously. Suctioning the nares with a bulb syringe clears the airway, allowing the infant to breathe comfortably while eating. RSV is a viral infection, so antibiotics are not indicated unless there is a secondary bacterial infection. Elevating the foot of the bed is contraindicated as it increases the work of breathing; the head of the bed should be elevated instead. Placing a towel over an infant's face poses a suffocation risk.
</details>
### Question 386
A 10-year-old student comes to the school nurse's office wheezing, coughing, and holding their chest after running at recess. The child has a known history of asthma. What is the nurse's priority action?

A. Administer the prescribed short-acting bronchodilator inhaler.
B. Have the child lie flat on an examination table to rest.
C. Call the child's parents to come pick them up from school.
D. Offer a glass of cold water to soothe the child's throat.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The child is exhibiting signs of an acute asthma attack. The priority action is to open the airway by administering a prescribed short-acting bronchodilator (such as albuterol), which acts quickly to relax the smooth muscles of the airways. Lying flat can worsen respiratory distress. Calling the parents is important, but only after the child's airway and breathing are stabilized. Drinking cold water does not address the bronchoconstriction.
</details>

---

### Question 387
The nurse is rounding on a 72-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with an acute exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The nurse finds the client sitting on the edge of the bed, leaning forward with their arms resting on the overbed table. What is the most appropriate action by the nurse?

A. Assist the client into a supine position to promote sleep.
B. Recognize this as a compensatory posture and allow the client to remain in this position.
C. Immediately increase the client's oxygen flow rate to 10 liters per minute via nasal cannula.
D. Instruct the client to take shallow, rapid breaths to conserve energy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
The client is sitting in the orthopneic (or tripod) position. This is a common and effective compensatory posture used by clients with COPD to maximize lung expansion and decrease the work of breathing. The nurse should recognize this as beneficial and allow the client to remain in this position. Placing the client supine would increase respiratory distress. Giving high levels of oxygen to a COPD client can be dangerous if it blunts their hypoxic drive, and shallow, rapid breaths do not promote effective gas exchange.
</details>

---

### Question 388
A 25-year-old client is evaluated in the outpatient clinic and diagnosed with community-acquired pneumonia. The nurse is providing discharge instructions regarding the newly prescribed oral antibiotics. Which statement by the client indicates that the nurse's teaching has been effective?

A. "I should stop taking the medication as soon as my fever goes away."
B. "I will limit my fluid intake to help dry up the fluid in my lungs."
C. "I need to complete the entire course of pills, even if my cough disappears."
D. "I can save the leftover antibiotics for the next time I get a cold."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
To prevent the recurrence of infection and the development of antibiotic-resistant bacteria, clients must be taught to complete the entire prescribed course of antibiotics, even if their symptoms improve or completely resolve. Stopping early or saving pills for later are incorrect and potentially harmful practices. Fluid intake should actually be increased, not limited, to help thin respiratory secretions.
</details>

---

### Question 389
A 45-year-old client is 12 hours postoperative following an open cholecystectomy. The nurse is planning care to prevent postoperative respiratory complications such as atelectasis. Which intervention should the nurse implement?

A. Maintain the client on strict bed rest for the first 48 hours.
B. Administer a prescribed antitussive medication to suppress coughing.
C. Instruct the client to breathe into a paper bag to prevent hyperventilation.
D. Teach and encourage the client to use the incentive spirometer every hour while awake.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Using an incentive spirometer encourages deep breathing, which promotes lung expansion and prevents the collapse of alveoli (atelectasis), a common postoperative complication. Early ambulation, rather than bed rest, is encouraged. Coughing should be encouraged (often with a splint pillow), not suppressed with antitussives, to help clear secretions. Breathing into a paper bag is an intervention for hyperventilation, not atelectasis prevention.
</details>

---

### Question 390
A 35-year-old client approaches the emergency department triage nurse reporting a three-week history of a persistent bloody cough, night sweats, and unintentional weight loss. What is the priority nursing action?

A. Place a surgical mask on the client and move them to an isolation room.
B. Auscultate the client's bowel sounds and palpate the abdomen.
C. Administer a prescribed oral antipyretic for the night sweats.
D. Instruct the client to sit in the general waiting area until their name is called.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The client's symptoms (persistent bloody cough, night sweats, weight loss) are highly suspicious for active pulmonary tuberculosis (TB). The priority action for the nurse is to implement infection control measures immediately by placing a surgical mask on the client and placing them in an airborne infection isolation room (negative-pressure room) to protect other clients and staff. Letting them sit in the waiting room risks exposing others. Auscultating bowel sounds or giving antipyretics are not the immediate priority over infection control.
</details>
### Question 391
A nurse in the emergency department is caring for a 12-year-old patient who arrives with audible wheezing, tachypnea, and use of accessory muscles. Which of the following is the priority nursing action?

A) Obtain a comprehensive family health history.
B) Teach the patient about common asthma triggers.
C) Administer the prescribed albuterol via nebulizer.
D) Encourage the patient to drink warm fluids.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Administer the prescribed albuterol via nebulizer.

**Explanation:** In an acute respiratory distress scenario, such as an asthma exacerbation, the priority is to open the airway. Albuterol is a short-acting bronchodilator that acts quickly to relax smooth muscles and relieve bronchospasms. Obtaining a history, teaching about triggers, and encouraging fluids are important interventions, but they are not the immediate priority when a patient is actively struggling to breathe.
</details>

---

### Question 392
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is observing a 68-year-old client with Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease (COPD) performing pursed-lip breathing. Which observation indicates to the nurse that the client is using the correct technique?

A) The client exhales slowly through puckered lips for twice as long as the inhalation.
B) The client inhales deeply through the mouth rather than the nose.
C) The client holds their breath for ten seconds before exhaling.
D) The client exhales quickly and forcefully to empty the lungs rapidly.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) The client exhales slowly through puckered lips for twice as long as the inhalation.

**Explanation:** Pursed-lip breathing helps keep the airways open longer during exhalation, promoting the release of trapped air in the lungs, which is a common problem in COPD. The correct technique involves inhaling slowly through the nose and exhaling slowly through pursed lips, with the exhalation phase lasting roughly twice as long as the inhalation. Forceful or rapid exhalation can cause airways to collapse. 
</details>

---

### Question 393
A nurse is instructing a 45-year-old client in an outpatient clinic on how to collect a sputum specimen for culture and sensitivity to help diagnose suspected pneumonia. Which instruction should the nurse provide?

A) "Rinse your mouth with an alcohol-based mouthwash right before coughing."
B) "Spit the saliva from your mouth directly into the collection cup."
C) "Eat a full breakfast before producing the sample to increase energy."
D) "Collect the specimen first thing in the morning upon waking up."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) "Collect the specimen first thing in the morning upon waking up."

**Explanation:** Sputum specimens are best collected in the early morning because secretions pool in the lungs overnight, making it easier to obtain a deep, concentrated sample. The client should not use alcohol-based mouthwash, as it can kill the bacteria needed for the culture. Saliva is not an acceptable specimen; the sample must be deep respiratory sputum. Eating before the test increases the risk of vomiting during the deep coughing process.
</details>

---

### Question 394
A nurse in a long-term care facility is caring for a 75-year-old resident who has a history of respiratory issues. The nurse notes the resident has suddenly become restless, agitated, and confused. Which of the following should be the nurse's first action?

A) Administer a prescribed PRN mild sedative.
B) Assess the resident's oxygen saturation with a pulse oximeter.
C) Dim the lights and decrease noise in the room to calm the resident.
D) Request a prescription for soft wrist restraints for safety.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Assess the resident's oxygen saturation with a pulse oximeter.

**Explanation:** Sudden restlessness, agitation, and confusion in an older adult are classic early signs of hypoxia (inadequate oxygenation). The nurse should immediately assess the client's oxygen saturation before assuming the behavior is strictly neurological or psychiatric. Administering a sedative could further depress the resident's respiratory drive and worsen the hypoxia. Restraints and environmental changes ignore the potential underlying physiological emergency.
</details>

---

### Question 395
A nurse is caring for a 50-year-old client on the first postoperative day following abdominal surgery. The nurse is observing the client use an incentive spirometer. Which action by the client indicates proper understanding of the device?

A) The client inhales slowly and deeply, keeping the flow indicator within the targeted range.
B) The client blows forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston as high as possible.
C) The client takes quick, shallow breaths through the mouthpiece to avoid abdominal pain.
D) The client uses the device exactly once every four hours while awake.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) The client inhales slowly and deeply, keeping the flow indicator within the targeted range.

**Explanation:** An incentive spirometer is designed to promote deep lung expansion and prevent postoperative atelectasis. The correct technique requires the patient to form a tight seal around the mouthpiece and inhale slowly and deeply (not exhale/blow into it), holding the breath at the end of the inspiration. Quick, shallow breaths defeat the purpose of the device. The device should be used frequently, typically 5 to 10 times every hour while awake, rather than just once every four hours.
</details>
### Question 396
An 8-year-old child comes to the school nurse's office wheezing and short of breath after recess. The nurse is observing the child use a prescribed albuterol metered-dose inhaler (MDI) attached to a spacer device. Which action by the child indicates correct use of the equipment?

A) Exhaling rapidly into the spacer immediately before pressing the canister.
B) Holding their breath for 10 seconds after inhaling the medication.
C) Pressing the canister multiple times while taking quick, shallow breaths.
D) Removing the spacer to spray the medication directly onto the back of the tongue.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Holding the breath for about 10 seconds (or as long as comfortable) after inhaling allows the medication to settle deeply into the lower airways, maximizing its effectiveness. The child should exhale away from the spacer, not into it. Using a spacer is highly recommended for children to ensure proper delivery, so it should not be removed. Medication should be inhaled slowly and deeply with one puff at a time, rather than using quick, shallow breaths.
</details>

---

### Question 397
A 78-year-old resident in a long-term care facility is recovering from bacterial pneumonia. The nurse is assessing the client and notes thick, tenacious respiratory secretions that are difficult for the client to cough up. Which action should the nurse take to assist with airway clearance?

A) Administer a prescribed over-the-counter cough suppressant.
B) Place the client in a flat, supine position to rest.
C) Restrict oral fluid intake to prevent accidental aspiration.
D) Encourage the client to drink warm oral fluids throughout the day.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Adequate hydration, especially with warm fluids, helps to thin respiratory secretions, making them easier for the client to expectorate. A cough suppressant is generally contraindicated for a client with a productive cough, as the goal is to clear the lungs of secretions. Placing the client in a supine position makes breathing more difficult and increases aspiration risk. Restricting fluids would cause the secretions to become thicker and harder to clear.
</details>

---

### Question 398
A 45-year-old client is on the surgical unit recovering from an abdominal hysterectomy performed earlier in the day. The nurse enters the room to perform a routine check and notes that the client has developed new-onset restlessness and confusion. What is the nurse's priority action?

A) Check the client's oxygen saturation level.
B) Administer the prescribed PRN opioid pain medication.
C) Provide a warm blanket to promote comfort and reduce anxiety.
D) Request a prescription for a mild sedative from the provider.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Restlessness, agitation, and confusion are early clinical signs of hypoxia. The nurse's first action must be to assess the client's oxygenation status using a pulse oximeter. Administering an opioid or a sedative could worsen respiratory depression if the client is already hypoxic. While a warm blanket may provide comfort, it does not address the potential life-threatening issue of inadequate oxygenation.
</details>

---

### Question 399
A 62-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) is experiencing mild shortness of breath while walking down the hospital hallway. The nurse instructs the client to stop and perform pursed-lip breathing. Which observation indicates the client is performing the technique correctly?

A) The client inhales through the mouth and exhales forcefully through the nose.
B) The client puffs out their cheeks while inhaling deeply through the nose.
C) The client inhales through the nose and exhales slowly through puckered lips.
D) The client pants quickly through the mouth to catch their breath.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Pursed-lip breathing involves inhaling normally through the nose and exhaling slowly through pursed or puckered lips (as if blowing out a candle). This technique creates mild resistance during exhalation, which increases airway pressure, keeps the small airways open longer, and helps remove trapped air in the lungs, a common issue in COPD. Panting or forceful exhalation wastes energy and can worsen airway collapse.
</details>

---

### Question 400
A 6-month-old infant is admitted to the pediatric unit with respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) bronchiolitis. The parents report the infant is having difficulty bottle-feeding and keeps pulling away crying. The nurse notes thick, clear secretions in the infant's nares. Which action should the nurse take to improve the infant's ability to feed?

A) Prop the bottle in the crib so the infant can feed at their own pace.
B) Use a bulb syringe to suction the nares immediately before the feeding.
C) Administer a prescribed oral antihistamine to dry up the nasal passages.
D) Switch the infant to pureed solid foods to eliminate the need to suck.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Infants are obligate nose breathers, meaning nasal congestion severely impairs their ability to breathe, especially while sucking on a bottle. Suctioning the nares with a bulb syringe before feedings clears the airway, allowing the infant to breathe and eat simultaneously. Propping a bottle is a severe choking and aspiration hazard. Antihistamines are not typically indicated for RSV and can overly dry secretions, making them harder to clear. A 6-month-old sick infant should not be abruptly transitioned entirely to solid foods for hydration and nutrition.
</details>
### Question 401
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is observing a 72-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) demonstrate pursed-lip breathing. Which observation indicates to the nurse that the client is performing the technique correctly?

A) Inhaling deeply through the mouth and exhaling through the nose.
B) Puffing the cheeks out forcefully while exhaling.
C) Exhaling slowly through puckered lips for twice as long as the inhalation.
D) Holding the breath for 10 seconds before releasing it rapidly.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Exhaling slowly through puckered lips for twice as long as the inhalation.

**Rationale:** Pursed-lip breathing is designed to slow down the respiratory rate and keep the airways open longer during exhalation, which prevents airway collapse and helps remove trapped air in clients with COPD. The correct technique involves inhaling slowly through the nose and exhaling slowly through pursed lips, with the exhalation phase lasting roughly twice as long as the inhalation. Inhaling through the mouth, puffing cheeks out, or holding the breath and releasing it rapidly are incorrect and ineffective techniques.
</details>

---

### Question 402
An 8-year-old child is brought to the emergency department experiencing an acute asthma attack. The nurse auscultates high-pitched wheezing and notes an increased respiratory rate. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Administer the prescribed short-acting bronchodilator.
B) Obtain a detailed family history of respiratory allergies.
C) Teach the child how to use a peak flow meter.
D) Offer the child a warm cup of water to loosen secretions.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Administer the prescribed short-acting bronchodilator.

**Rationale:** In an acute asthma exacerbation, the priority nursing action is to address the airway and breathing. A short-acting bronchodilator (such as albuterol) works quickly to relax the smooth muscles of the airways and relieve bronchospasm. Obtaining a history and teaching about a peak flow meter are important but are not priorities during an acute attack. Offering water is not an immediate life-saving intervention and could pose an aspiration risk if the child is experiencing severe respiratory distress.
</details>

---

### Question 403
A nurse on a surgical unit is caring for a 45-year-old client who had an abdominal hysterectomy yesterday. The nurse is instructing the client on how to use an incentive spirometer to prevent respiratory complications. Which action by the client indicates effective teaching?

A) The client blows forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston.
B) The client takes quick, shallow breaths in and out of the device.
C) The client uses the device once a day before going to sleep.
D) The client seals her lips tightly around the mouthpiece and inhales slowly and deeply.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) The client seals her lips tightly around the mouthpiece and inhales slowly and deeply.

**Rationale:** An incentive spirometer is used to promote maximum lung expansion and prevent atelectasis and pneumonia postoperatively. The correct technique requires the client to form a tight seal around the mouthpiece and inhale slowly and deeply to elevate the piston or ball. Exhaling (blowing) into the device is incorrect. Taking shallow breaths or using it only once a day is ineffective; it should be used at regular intervals (typically 5-10 times every hour while awake).
</details>

---

### Question 404
A 68-year-old client is admitted to the medical-surgical floor with bilateral pneumonia. The client complains of increased shortness of breath and has an oxygen saturation of 89% on room air. What is the most appropriate initial nursing action to improve the client's breathing?

A) Encourage the client to lie flat on their back.
B) Elevate the head of the bed to a High-Fowler's position.
C) Administer a prescribed PRN dose of a narcotic cough suppressant.
D) Instruct the client to breathe rapidly to take in more oxygen.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Elevate the head of the bed to a High-Fowler's position.

**Rationale:** Placing a client in a High-Fowler's position (head of the bed elevated 60 to 90 degrees) uses gravity to drop the diaphragm away from the lungs, allowing for maximum chest expansion and improved oxygenation. Lying flat on the back limits lung expansion and can worsen dyspnea. Administering a narcotic cough suppressant can depress the respiratory drive and prevent the clearing of secretions. Rapid breathing can lead to hyperventilation and does not efficiently improve oxygen saturation.
</details>

---

### Question 405
A 6-month-old infant is hospitalized on the pediatric unit with respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and bronchiolitis. The nurse notes that the infant has copious nasal secretions and is having difficulty feeding from a bottle. Which intervention should the nurse implement?

A) Switch the infant to solid foods to prevent aspiration of the formula.
B) Administer a prescribed oral antihistamine to dry up the secretions.
C) Suction the infant's nares with a bulb syringe before offering the bottle.
D) Prop the bottle in the crib so the infant can eat at their own pace.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Suction the infant's nares with a bulb syringe before offering the bottle.

**Rationale:** Infants are obligate nose breathers, meaning they rely primarily on breathing through their noses. Copious nasal secretions from RSV can severely impair their ability to breathe while simultaneously sucking on a bottle. Suctioning the nares before feeding clears the airway, allowing the infant to feed and breathe safely. Switching a 6-month-old to solid foods solely due to acute secretions is inappropriate. Antihistamines are generally not indicated for viral bronchiolitis and can thicken secretions. Propping a bottle is a severe choking and aspiration risk and should never be done.
</details>
### Question 406
A clinic nurse is observing an 8-year-old child with asthma use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) with a spacer. Which action by the child indicates to the nurse that the teaching was successful?

A) The child removes the spacer to get a stronger dose of the medication.
B) The child takes a slow, deep breath after pressing the canister.
C) The child breathes out rapidly into the mouthpiece before pressing the canister.
D) The child presses the canister three times in a row before inhaling.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Using a spacer with an MDI helps deliver the medication to the lungs rather than leaving it at the back of the throat. Taking a slow, deep breath allows the medication to reach the lower airways effectively. Removing the spacer, blowing directly into it, or pressing it multiple times without breathing in between are all incorrect techniques that reduce the efficacy of the medication.
</details>

---

### Question 407
A nurse at a long-term care facility is caring for a 72-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) who is receiving oxygen via nasal cannula. The client reports that the oxygen tubing is causing dryness and irritation inside their nares. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Discontinue the oxygen therapy immediately to prevent tissue breakdown.
B) Change the nasal cannula to a simple face mask.
C) Apply a petroleum-based jelly to the client's nares.
D) Pad the tubing and apply a water-soluble lubricant to the nares.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Oxygen therapy can cause drying and irritation of the nasal mucosa. Applying a water-soluble lubricant prevents dryness and soothes the nares without the fire hazard associated with petroleum-based products. Padding the tubing reduces mechanical friction. Discontinuing oxygen or changing the delivery method without a provider's order is outside the nurse's scope of practice.
</details>

---

### Question 408
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 45-year-old client recovering from bacterial pneumonia. The provider has ordered the use of an incentive spirometer. What instruction should the nurse provide to the client regarding its proper use?

A) "Exhale completely, place your lips tightly around the mouthpiece, and inhale slowly and deeply."
B) "Blow into the mouthpiece as hard and fast as you can to raise the piston."
C) "Use the device once a day right before you go to sleep."
D) "Take short, rapid breaths in and out through the mouthpiece."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
An incentive spirometer is used to promote lung expansion and prevent atelectasis. The correct technique involves exhaling completely, forming a tight seal around the mouthpiece, and taking a slow, deep inhalation to elevate the piston. Blowing into the device or using short, rapid breaths is incorrect. The device should be used frequently (e.g., 10 times an hour while awake), not just once a day.
</details>

---

### Question 409
A triage nurse in the emergency department assesses a 30-year-old client who presents with a persistent cough, night sweats, and blood-tinged sputum. The nurse suspects active pulmonary tuberculosis. Which immediate action should the nurse take?

A) Place the client in a standard examination room with the door open.
B) Ask the client to provide a sputum sample at the triage desk.
C) Place a surgical mask on the client and move them to an airborne infection isolation room.
D) Don a pair of clean gloves and a face shield before taking vital signs.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Clients with suspected active tuberculosis require airborne precautions to prevent the spread of the disease. The priority action is to place a surgical mask on the client to contain respiratory secretions and immediately place them in a negative-pressure airborne infection isolation room. Standard precautions alone (gloves/face shield) or placing them in a standard room are insufficient and compromise infection control. Sputum samples should be collected in the isolation room, not a public area.
</details>

---

### Question 410
A pediatric nurse is caring for a 4-month-old infant diagnosed with Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) bronchiolitis. The infant is congested, fussy, and having difficulty feeding. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate prior to the infant's feeding?

A) Administer a prescribed oral antihistamine.
B) Suction the infant's nares gently using a bulb syringe.
C) Place the infant in a flat, supine position to clear the airway.
D) Withhold the feeding until the respiratory rate drops below 20 breaths per minute.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Infants are obligate nose breathers, and nasal congestion from RSV can severely impair their ability to breathe and feed. Gently suctioning the nares with a bulb syringe (often preceded by saline drops) prior to feeding clears the airway and makes eating easier. Antihistamines are not typically indicated for RSV. The infant's head should be elevated, not placed supine. A normal infant respiratory rate is 30-60 breaths per minute; waiting for it to drop below 20 would mean waiting for the infant to experience dangerous respiratory depression.
</details>
### Question 411
A pediatric nurse in the emergency department is assessing a 7-year-old child experiencing an acute asthma exacerbation. Which clinical observation should the nurse recognize as the earliest indicator that the child is experiencing worsening hypoxia?

A) Increasing restlessness and anxiety  
B) A heart rate dropping below 60 beats per minute  
C) A bluish discoloration around the lips  
D) Deepening somnolence and lethargy  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** In both pediatric and adult populations, the earliest signs of hypoxia are neurological changes such as restlessness, agitation, and anxiety due to decreased oxygen supply to the brain. Cyanosis, bradycardia, and somnolence are late and ominous signs of severe respiratory failure.
</details>

---

### Question 412
A home health nurse is visiting a 78-year-old client who is managing chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The nurse asks the client to demonstrate pursed-lip breathing. Which action by the client indicates to the nurse that the teaching was effective?

A) The client inhales through the mouth and exhales quickly through the nose.  
B) The client holds their breath for 10 seconds before forcefully exhaling.  
C) The client inhales through the nose and exhales slowly through puckered lips.  
D) The client takes quick, shallow breaths using their accessory chest muscles.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** Pursed-lip breathing helps keep the airways open longer and prolongs exhalation, which decreases the trapping of air in the lungs characteristic of COPD. The correct technique is to inhale through the nose and exhale slowly through puckered (pursed) lips, usually taking twice as long to exhale as to inhale.
</details>

---

### Question 413
A nurse on the medical-surgical unit is caring for a 45-year-old client admitted with right lower lobe bacterial pneumonia. When the nurse auscultates the right lower posterior chest, which adventitious breath sound is most expected based on the client's diagnosis?

A) High-pitched, musical wheezes  
B) Clear, quiet vesicular sounds  
C) Loud, high-pitched stridor  
D) Discontinuous, popping crackles  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Rationale:** Crackles (rales) are typically heard in clients with pneumonia. They are caused by air passing through fluid, pus, or mucus in the alveoli and small airways. Wheezes are associated with narrowed airways (like asthma), stridor indicates upper airway obstruction, and clear vesicular sounds are normal findings, not expected over an area of consolidation.
</details>

---

### Question 414
An intensive care nurse is preparing to perform endotracheal suctioning for a 22-year-old client recovering from facial trauma who has a newly placed tracheostomy. Before inserting the suction catheter, which priority action must the nurse perform?

A) Apply continuous negative pressure while inserting the catheter.  
B) Hyperoxygenate the client with 100% oxygen.  
C) Instill 5 mL of sterile normal saline into the tracheostomy tube.  
D) Completely deflate the tracheostomy tube cuff.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Rationale:** Suctioning removes oxygen from the airway along with secretions, placing the client at high risk for hypoxia. The nurse must hyperoxygenate the client (usually with 100% oxygen) before and after suctioning to prevent hypoxemia. Suction should only be applied upon withdrawal of the catheter, routine saline instillation is no longer recommended, and the cuff should not be deflated for routine suctioning.
</details>

---

### Question 415
A surgical nurse is instructing a 50-year-old client who is 1-day postoperative from a total abdominal hysterectomy on how to use an incentive spirometer to prevent atelectasis. Which observation by the nurse requires immediate correction and further teaching?

A) The client sits in an upright, semi-Fowler's position before using the device.  
B) The client completely exhales before placing the mouthpiece tightly between their lips.  
C) The client blows forcefully into the mouthpiece to raise the piston as high as possible.  
D) The client holds their breath for 3 seconds at the maximum height of inhalation.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** Incentive spirometry is designed to mimic natural sighing or yawning by encouraging slow, deep *inhalation* to expand the alveoli and prevent atelectasis. If the client blows (exhales) forcefully into the mouthpiece, they are using the device incorrectly and require further teaching. Sitting upright, exhaling prior to use, and holding the breath at peak inhalation are all correct steps.
</details>
### Question 416
A nurse in a pediatric clinic is observing an 8-year-old client with asthma use a metered-dose inhaler (MDI) without a spacer. Which action by the child indicates to the nurse that further teaching is needed?

A) Shaking the canister vigorously before use  
B) Inhaling slowly and deeply while pressing the canister  
C) Exhaling forcefully and rapidly immediately after taking a puff  
D) Waiting one full minute before administering a second puff  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
When using a metered-dose inhaler, the client should hold their breath for at least 5 to 10 seconds after inhaling the medication to allow the medicine to settle in the lower airways. Exhaling immediately prevents the medication from being fully absorbed and indicates a need for further teaching. Shaking the inhaler, inhaling slowly, and waiting a minute between puffs are all correct techniques.
</details>

---

### Question 417
A nurse at an outpatient rehabilitation center is teaching a 72-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) how to perform pursed-lip breathing. Which observation indicates the client is performing the technique correctly?

A) The client breathes in through the nose and exhales slowly through puckered lips.  
B) The client takes a quick, shallow breath in through the mouth and forcefully exhales.  
C) The client holds their breath for 10 seconds before exhaling through the nose.  
D) The client breathes in deeply through puckered lips and exhales rapidly through the nose.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Pursed-lip breathing helps keep airways open longer, decreasing the work of breathing and preventing airway collapse. The correct technique involves inhaling normally through the nose and exhaling slowly and gently through puckered (pursed) lips, with the exhalation taking roughly twice as long as the inhalation. 
</details>

---

### Question 418
A 45-year-old client is one day postoperative following an abdominal surgery on the medical-surgical unit. The nurse provides an incentive spirometer to help prevent atelectasis. Which action by the client requires the nurse to intervene immediately?

A) Sitting in an upright, high-Fowler's position before using the device  
B) Inhaling slowly and deeply to elevate the piston in the chamber  
C) Holding their breath for 3 seconds at the peak of inspiration  
D) Blowing forcefully into the mouthpiece to make the indicator rise  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Incentive spirometry is designed to promote lung expansion through sustained maximal *inspiration*. The client must inhale deeply through the mouthpiece, not exhale (blow) into it. Blowing into the device indicates a misunderstanding of how the equipment works, requiring immediate correction by the nurse. Options A, B, and C describe the correct technique.
</details>

---

### Question 419
A 28-year-old client arrives at an urgent care clinic reporting chest discomfort, fatigue, and a fever. The healthcare provider diagnoses the client with bacterial pneumonia. Which clinical observation should the nurse expect to find during the respiratory assessment?

A) A dry, nonproductive cough accompanied by clear, watery nasal discharge  
B) A productive cough yielding thick, yellowish-green sputum  
C) A prolonged expiratory phase with audible, high-pitched inspiratory stridor  
D) Symmetrical chest expansion with a resting respiratory rate of 12 breaths per minute  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Bacterial pneumonia typically presents with a productive cough containing purulent (yellowish-green or rust-colored) sputum, along with fever, chills, and chest pain. A dry cough is more common with viral infections, stridor indicates an upper airway obstruction, and a respiratory rate of 12 is a normal, healthy finding rather than an expected finding for a client with active pneumonia.
</details>

---

### Question 420
A nurse is caring for a 55-year-old client with a newly placed tracheostomy in a progressive care unit. The nurse hears coarse crackles over the airway and determines the client needs to be suctioned. Which action is most appropriate for the nurse to take during this procedure?

A) Applying suction only while withdrawing the catheter from the airway  
B) Instilling sterile normal saline drops into the tracheostomy tube before suctioning  
C) Applying continuous suction while advancing the catheter down the stoma  
D) Suctioning the airway continuously for at least 30 seconds to ensure it is clear  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
When suctioning an artificial airway, the nurse should only apply suction while *withdrawing* the catheter, using a twisting motion, for no longer than 10 to 15 seconds to prevent hypoxia and mucosal trauma. Applying suction during insertion (C) or for up to 30 seconds (D) can cause severe hypoxia and tissue damage. Routine instillation of normal saline (B) is no longer recommended as it can wash bacteria into the lower airways.
</details>
### Question 421
A 9-year-old child comes to the school clinic reporting chest tightness after playing outside during recess. The nurse observes that the child is wheezing audibly and using accessory muscles to breathe. The child has a known history of asthma. Which action should the nurse take first?

A. Administer the prescribed short-acting bronchodilator inhaler.  
B. Offer the child a glass of warm water to soothe the airway.  
C. Instruct the child to take slow, deep breaths into a paper bag.  
D. Notify the parents to pick the child up for a sick day.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
The child is experiencing an acute asthma exacerbation, as evidenced by wheezing, chest tightness, and accessory muscle use. The priority nursing action in this clinical scenario is to administer a short-acting bronchodilator (such as albuterol) to rapidly relax the smooth muscles of the airway and improve breathing. Breathing into a paper bag is for hyperventilation and is contraindicated in asthma. While notifying the parents is important, it is not the immediate priority over stabilizing the child's airway. 
</details>

---

### Question 422
A nurse in a long-term care facility is caring for a 78-year-old client with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). The client reports feeling severely short of breath whenever they try to eat breakfast. Which action should the nurse take to assist the client?

A. Increase the client's supplemental oxygen to 6 L/min via nasal cannula.  
B. Encourage the client to eat three large meals daily instead of snacking.  
C. Teach the client to use pursed-lip breathing while chewing and resting.  
D. Place the client in a supine position immediately after eating.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Clients with COPD often experience shortness of breath during meals due to the effort required to eat and digest, compounded by lung hyperinflation. Teaching pursed-lip breathing helps the client keep airways open longer, allowing trapped air to escape and reducing the work of breathing during activities like eating. Increasing oxygen to 6 L/min can be dangerous for a COPD patient as it may suppress their hypoxic drive to breathe. Clients should eat small, frequent meals rather than large ones, and should sit upright after eating to facilitate lung expansion.
</details>

---

### Question 423
A 45-year-old client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with right lower lobe pneumonia is coughing up thick, greenish sputum. The nurse is planning care to help mobilize and clear the client's respiratory secretions. Assuming there are no contraindications, which intervention should the nurse implement?

A. Limit oral fluid intake to 1,000 mL per day to prevent fluid overload.  
B. Administer an over-the-counter cough suppressant every 4 hours.  
C. Maintain the client on strict bed rest to conserve oxygen.  
D. Encourage the client to drink 2 to 3 liters of oral fluids daily.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
