Restraints are contraindicated as holding a seizing client down can cause muscle tears or bone fractures. Bright, glaring lights can sometimes trigger seizures and disrupt the client's rest; the room should have calm, dim lighting when possible.
</details>

---

### Question 492
A 25-year-old client is brought to the emergency department after a minor fall from a bicycle. The nurse enters the room to conduct a neurological assessment and observes that the client's eyes are closed and they appear to be sleeping. What is the nurse's initial action to assess the client's level of consciousness?

A) Apply a firm sternal rub.
B) Squeeze the client's trapezius muscle.
C) Speak the client's name in a normal tone.
D) Gently shake the client's shoulder.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Speak the client's name in a normal tone.

**Rationale:** When assessing level of consciousness and arousal, the nurse should always begin with the least noxious (least invasive) stimulus. Speaking the client's name in a normal tone of voice is the first appropriate step. If there is no response, the nurse systematically progresses to a loud voice, then light touch (such as gently shaking the shoulder), and finally painful stimuli (such as a trapezius squeeze or sternal rub) only if the client remains unresponsive.
</details>

---

### Question 493
A 68-year-old client on the medical-surgical unit has just returned to their room following a lumbar puncture. The nurse is providing post-procedure instructions. Which directive should the nurse give to the client to minimize the risk of a spinal headache?

A) "Remain lying flat in bed for the time specified by the provider."
B) "Sit upright in a chair to allow the puncture site to drain."
C) "Perform deep breathing and coughing exercises every hour."
D) "Restrict your fluid intake to small sips for the rest of the day."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "Remain lying flat in bed for the time specified by the provider."

**Rationale:** Lying flat for a prescribed period (typically 1 to several hours) after a lumbar puncture is a standard nursing intervention to help prevent cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leakage from the puncture site, significantly reducing the risk of a spinal headache. Fluids should be encouraged, not restricted, to help replenish CSF volume. Sitting upright increases the risk of a spinal headache due to gravity pulling fluid downward. Coughing increases intracranial pressure and should be avoided immediately post-procedure.
</details>

---

### Question 494
The nurse is observing a 72-year-old resident with advanced Parkinson's disease eating lunch in the dining room of a long-term care facility. The client exhibits resting tremors in the hands, causing them to frequently drop food. Which nursing intervention best promotes the client's safety and nutritional independence?

A) Feed the client their entire meal to prevent spilling.
B) Request the dietary department to change the meal to a pureed diet.
C) Provide standard lightweight plastic utensils to reduce fatigue.
D) Supply the client with weighted utensils and a cup with a lid.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Supply the client with weighted utensils and a cup with a lid.

**Rationale:** Weighted utensils help stabilize the hands of a client with Parkinsonian tremors, allowing them to grasp the utensil better and promoting independence with feeding. A cup with a lid prevents hot or cold liquids from spilling. Feeding the client takes away their independence and should only be done if they cannot feed themselves at all. A pureed diet is indicated for swallowing difficulties (dysphagia), not specifically for hand tremors. Lightweight plastic utensils are harder to control and can actually make tremors more disruptive during eating.
</details>

---

### Question 495
A 55-year-old client is on a rehabilitation unit recovering from a right-sided ischemic stroke. The client has left-sided facial weakness and is cleared to eat a mechanical soft diet. Which action by the nurse is correct when assisting this client with their meal?

A) Place the food on the right side of the client's mouth.
B) Position the client in a semi-Fowler's position during the meal.
C) Encourage the client to tilt their head backward when swallowing.
D) Instruct the client to wash down solid foods with large gulps of water.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Place the food on the right side of the client's mouth.

**Rationale:** A right-sided stroke commonly causes left-sided weakness (hemiparesis). Because the client has left-sided facial weakness, food should be placed on the unaffected (right) side of the mouth to facilitate proper chewing and prevent pocketing of food, which is an aspiration risk. The client should be positioned upright in a high Fowler's (90-degree) position, not semi-Fowler's. Tilting the head back increases the risk of food entering the airway; a chin-tuck position is preferred. Washing food down with large gulps of water is unsafe and highly increases the risk of aspiration.
</details>
### Question 496
A clinic nurse is observing a 72-year-old client who was recently diagnosed with Parkinson's disease. Which physical finding should the nurse expect to document during the routine assessment?

A) Spasticity in the lower extremities  
B) A resting tremor in the hands  
C) Flaccid paralysis of the right arm  
D) An intention tremor when reaching for a cup  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Parkinson's disease is a progressive neurological disorder characterized by a classic triad of symptoms: bradykinesia, rigidity, and a resting tremor (often described as "pill-rolling"). Intention tremors are typically associated with cerebellar disorders, not Parkinson's. Spasticity and flaccid paralysis are not expected findings in this disease process.
</details>

---

### Question 497
A pediatric nurse is admitting a 14-year-old client with a known history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures to the medical-surgical unit. Which initial action should the nurse take when preparing the client's room?

A) Ensure functioning suction equipment and oxygen are at the bedside.  
B) Place a padded tongue blade on the bedside table for easy access.  
C) Position the bed in the highest position to allow for rapid interventions.  
D) Keep all four side rails lowered to prevent entrapment during a seizure.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Standard seizure precautions require the nurse to ensure that oxygen and functioning suction equipment are readily available at the bedside to maintain a patent airway if a seizure occurs. A padded tongue blade should never be placed in a client's mouth during a seizure due to the risk of airway obstruction and dental trauma. The bed should be kept in the lowest position, and side rails should be padded and raised according to facility policy to protect the client from falls.
</details>

---

### Question 498
A nurse in the emergency department is caring for a 22-year-old client who just had a lumbar puncture to rule out meningitis. Which intervention should the nurse implement immediately following the procedure?

A) Encourage the client to ambulate in the hallway to prevent stiffness.  
B) Restrict the client's oral fluid intake for the next 4 hours.  
C) Elevate the head of the bed to a high-Fowler's position.  
D) Instruct the client to remain flat in bed for several hours.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Following a lumbar puncture, the client should remain flat in bed (supine) for a prescribed period (usually 4 to 8 hours) to prevent a cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leak from the puncture site, which can cause a severe spinal headache. Elevating the head of the bed or early ambulation increases the risk of a spinal headache. Unless contraindicated, oral fluids should be encouraged, not restricted, to help replace the removed CSF.
</details>

---

### Question 499
A nurse on a rehabilitation unit is assisting a 55-year-old client who is recovering from an ischemic stroke and has mild dysphagia. Which action by the nurse is best to prevent aspiration during mealtime?

A) Mix solid foods and liquids together in the same bite.  
B) Provide a straw to help the client swallow liquids more easily.  
C) Instruct the client to tuck their chin downward when swallowing.  
D) Tilt the client's head backward when they are swallowing.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Instructing the client to tuck their chin downward (the chin-tuck maneuver) helps prevent aspiration by narrowing the airway opening and directing food and liquid toward the esophagus. Mixing solids and liquids or using a straw increases the risk of aspiration, as they require more complex muscle control. Tilting the head backward opens the airway, greatly increasing the risk of aspiration.
</details>

---

### Question 500
A school nurse is called to the playground to assess a 9-year-old student who fell off the swings and bumped their head. The student is awake and crying. Which action should the nurse take first to assess the student's basic neurological function?

A) Assess the child's pupils with a penlight for size and reaction.  
B) Have the child stand on one foot with their eyes closed.  
C) Palpate the child's cervical spine for misalignment.  
D) Check the child's deep tendon reflexes using a reflex hammer.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Assessing the pupils for size, equality, and reaction to light (PERRLA) is a quick, primary neurological assessment to evaluate cranial nerve function and check for signs of increased intracranial pressure after a head injury. Having the child perform complex balance tests (like standing on one foot) immediately after a head injury is unsafe. Palpating the cervical spine evaluates musculoskeletal trauma, not baseline neurological function, and reflex testing is not the immediate priority in acute head trauma.
</details>
### Question 501
A 19-year-old male on a medical-surgical unit suddenly begins experiencing a generalized tonic-clonic seizure while resting in bed. The nurse enters the room and observes the patient's limbs jerking rapidly. Which of the following is the priority nursing action?

A) Insert a padded tongue blade into the patient's mouth to protect the teeth.
B) Firmly hold the patient's arms and legs to prevent him from thrashing.
C) Turn the patient onto his side and clear the immediate area of hard objects.
D) Quickly obtain a full set of vital signs, including a blood pressure reading.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
During a seizure, the nurse's priority is patient safety and airway maintenance. Turning the patient onto their side helps prevent aspiration of secretions, and clearing the area of hard objects prevents physical injury. The nurse should never insert anything into the mouth of a seizing patient (A) or physically restrain their limbs (B), as this can cause severe injury. Obtaining vital signs (D) is not appropriate or feasible during the active tonic-clonic phase and should be done after the seizure subsides.
</details>

---

### Question 502
A 45-year-old female patient has just returned to the outpatient recovery area following a lumbar puncture to rule out meningitis. When instructing the patient on post-procedure care, which position should the nurse encourage the patient to maintain?

A) Lying flat in a supine position for several hours.
B) Sitting in a high-Fowler's position to promote fluid drainage.
C) Ambulating in the hallway to prevent deep vein thrombosis.
D) Resting on the right side with the knees pulled tightly to the chest.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Following a lumbar puncture, the patient is typically instructed to lie flat (supine) for several hours. This positioning helps prevent cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leakage from the puncture site and minimizes the risk of developing a severe post-lumbar puncture headache. Sitting up (B) or ambulating (C) too soon increases the likelihood of a spinal headache. The fetal position (D) is used during the procedure, not for post-procedure recovery.
</details>

---

### Question 503
The nurse is assisting a 72-year-old male patient with right-sided hemiplegia and mild dysphagia with his lunch in the rehabilitation unit. To prevent aspiration while the patient is eating, which action is safest for the nurse to take?

A) Provide all beverages using a standard drinking straw.
B) Place small bites of food into the right side of the patient's mouth.
C) Encourage the patient to tilt his head backward just before swallowing.
D) Instruct the patient to slightly tuck his chin toward his chest when swallowing.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Instructing the patient to use the chin-tuck method when swallowing narrows the airway opening and pushes the epiglottis backward, significantly reducing the risk of aspiration. Using a straw (A) can propel liquids too quickly to the back of the throat. Food should be placed on the unaffected (left) side of the mouth, not the weak right side (B). Tilting the head backward (C) opens the airway and increases the risk of choking and aspiration.
</details>

---

### Question 504
An 81-year-old female resident with Parkinson's disease is walking to the dining room in a long-term care facility. The nurse notices the resident's feet suddenly "freeze" in place, even though her upper body continues to lean forward. What is the most appropriate action by the nurse to help the resident resume walking?

A) Gently but firmly pull the resident forward by her dominant arm.
B) Instruct the resident to imagine stepping over a line on the floor.
C) Tell the resident to look up at the ceiling, take a deep breath, and walk.
D) Instruct the resident to pivot rapidly on one foot to break the freezing spell.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Patients with Parkinson's disease often experience "freezing" of gait. Providing a visual cue, such as telling the patient to imagine stepping over a line or crack on the floor, helps bypass the basal ganglia defect and triggers the brain to initiate movement. Pulling the patient (A) or asking them to pivot rapidly (D) can cause a loss of balance and result in a fall. Looking at the ceiling (C) alters the center of gravity and is unsafe for an elderly patient with mobility issues.
</details>

---

### Question 505
A 30-year-old male patient is admitted to the Intensive Care Unit following a severe traumatic brain injury. The nurse is adjusting the patient's bed to help minimize the risk of increased intracranial pressure (ICP). Which bed position is the safest and most effective for this patient?

A) Supine with the bed completely flat and no pillows.
B) Trendelenburg position to promote adequate cerebral perfusion.
C) Head of the bed elevated 30 degrees with the neck in a neutral midline position.
D) Side-lying position with the neck and hips sharply flexed.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Elevating the head of the bed to 15-30 degrees and keeping the head and neck in a neutral, midline position promotes optimal venous drainage from the brain via the jugular veins, which effectively helps decrease increased intracranial pressure (ICP). Lying flat (A) or in the Trendelenburg position (B) prevents venous drainage and increases ICP. Extreme flexion of the neck or hips (D) increases intra-abdominal and intrathoracic pressure, which in turn elevates ICP.
</details>
### Question 506
An 8-year-old child sitting in the waiting room of a pediatric clinic suddenly loses consciousness and begins to experience rhythmic jerking of the arms and legs. Which action should the clinic nurse take first?

A) Insert a padded tongue blade into the child's mouth.  
B) Gently ease the child to the floor and place a jacket under their head.  
C) Hold the child's arms and legs firmly to prevent physical injury.  
D) Go immediately to the supply room to retrieve a bag-valve mask.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Patient safety is the absolute priority during a seizure. Easing the patient to the floor and protecting the head from striking hard surfaces prevents traumatic injury. Inserting objects into the mouth, restraining the patient's movements, or leaving the patient unattended are contraindicated and can cause severe harm.
</details>

---

### Question 507
A 78-year-old resident in a long-term care facility is recovering from an ischemic stroke and has been diagnosed with right-sided weakness and mild dysphagia. When assisting the resident with lunch, which intervention should the nurse implement?

A) Mix all solid foods into liquids to make swallowing easier.  
B) Encourage the resident to tilt their head backward when swallowing.  
C) Place the food on the right side of the resident's mouth.  
D) Instruct the resident to tuck their chin toward their chest when swallowing.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Tucking the chin downward toward the chest when swallowing helps close the trachea and prevents aspiration in patients with dysphagia. Food should be placed on the unaffected (left) side. Tilting the head back increases the risk of aspiration, and mixing solids into liquids can actually make swallowing more difficult and dangerous for some patients with dysphagia.
</details>

---

### Question 508
A 35-year-old client on a medical-surgical unit has just returned from having a diagnostic lumbar puncture. Which instruction should the nurse provide to the client regarding post-procedure care?

A) "You need to remain lying flat on your back for the next several hours."  
B) "You should sit upright in a chair to help the puncture site drain."  
C) "Avoid drinking any fluids for the rest of the day to prevent nausea."  
D) "Perform deep breathing and coughing exercises every fifteen minutes."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
After a lumbar puncture, the client is typically instructed to remain flat (supine) for 4 to 8 hours. This helps prevent cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leakage from the puncture site and greatly reduces the risk of developing a severe spinal headache. Fluid intake should actually be encouraged, not restricted, to help replenish CSF.
</details>

---

### Question 509
A 22-year-old patient is brought to the emergency department after a motorcycle accident. While performing a rapid neurological assessment, the nurse observes that the patient's eyes remain closed until the nurse loudly calls the patient's name, at which point the patient looks up. How should the nurse interpret this specific observation?

A) Spontaneous eye opening  
B) Eye opening to pain  
C) Eye opening to speech  
D) No eye opening response  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
According to the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS), opening the eyes in response to being called by name or spoken to is classified as "eye opening to speech" or "sound" (a score of 3 in the eye-opening category). Spontaneous opening means the eyes are already open without prompting, and opening to pain requires a physical painful stimulus.
</details>

---

### Question 510
A home health nurse is evaluating the mobility of a 65-year-old client with Parkinson's disease. As the client is walking toward the kitchen, they suddenly stop and state, "My feet feel like they are glued to the floor." Which action should the nurse take to assist the client?

A) Provide a wheelchair and tell the client they should no longer walk unassisted.  
B) Instruct the client to imagine a line on the floor and step over it.  
C) Gently push the client forward from the shoulders to initiate momentum.  
D) Tell the client to pivot rapidly on one foot to break the freezing episode.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
"Freezing" of gait is a common symptom in Parkinson's disease. Using visual or cognitive cues, such as imagining a line on the floor or stepping over a laser pointer line, helps bypass the basal ganglia and allows the motor cortex to initiate movement. Pushing the client or rapid pivoting will likely cause a fall due to postural instability.
</details>
### Question 511
A nurse is admitting a 24-year-old client with a history of severe tonic-clonic seizures to the medical-surgical unit. Which action should the nurse take first when preparing the client's room?

A) Ensure the side rails of the bed are padded.
B) Place a wooden tongue blade on the bedside table.
C) Attach soft wrist restraints to the bed frame.
D) Position the bed at the highest level to allow for rapid assessment.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Ensure the side rails of the bed are padded.

**Rationale:** When setting up a room for a client on seizure precautions, the nurse's priority is injury prevention. Padding the side rails helps protect the client from striking their head or limbs against hard surfaces during a seizure. Option B is incorrect because placing objects (like tongue blades) in a client's mouth during a seizure is strictly contraindicated as it can cause broken teeth or airway obstruction. Option C is incorrect; restraining a client during a seizure can cause musculoskeletal injuries. Option D is incorrect; the bed should be kept in the lowest possible position to minimize the risk of injury if the client falls out of bed.
</details>

---

### Question 512
A nurse is observing an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) feed a 65-year-old client who recently had a left hemispheric stroke and has right-sided facial weakness. Which action by the UAP requires immediate intervention by the nurse?

A) Reminding the client to tuck their chin toward their chest when swallowing.
B) Placing a spoonful of mashed potatoes on the right side of the client's mouth.
C) Keeping the head of the bed elevated for 30 minutes after the meal is finished.
D) Asking the client to swallow twice after each bite of food to clear the mouth.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Placing a spoonful of mashed potatoes on the right side of the client's mouth.

**Rationale:** A client with right-sided facial weakness has decreased muscle control and sensation on that side, increasing the risk of pocketing food and aspiration. Food should always be placed on the unaffected side (in this case, the left side) of the mouth. The nurse must intervene immediately to correct this unsafe practice. Options A, C, and D are all safe and appropriate interventions to prevent aspiration in a client with dysphagia following a stroke.
</details>

---

### Question 513
A 60-year-old client in the diagnostic unit is preparing for a lumbar puncture to rule out meningitis. How should the nurse position the client for this procedure?

A) Prone with a flat pillow placed firmly under the pelvis.
B) Supine with the head of the bed elevated to exactly 45 degrees.
C) Sitting on the edge of the bed with the back straight and feet flat on the floor.
D) Lateral recumbent with the knees drawn up to the abdomen and chin tucked.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Lateral recumbent with the knees drawn up to the abdomen and chin tucked.

**Rationale:** For a lumbar puncture, the client must be positioned to maximize the space between the lumbar vertebrae, allowing the provider to safely insert the spinal needle. The lateral recumbent (fetal) position, with knees pulled to the chest and chin tucked, arches the back and spreads the vertebrae appropriately. Alternatively, leaning forward over a bedside table while sitting can also be used, but option C describes sitting with a straight back, which is incorrect. Prone or supine positions (Options A and B) do not open the vertebral spaces.
</details>

---

### Question 514
A nurse in a long-term care facility is walking in the hallway with a 78-year-old client who has Parkinson's disease. The client experiences a "freezing" episode and suddenly stops walking, feeling as though their feet are glued to the floor. Which action should the nurse take first to help the client resume walking?

A) Gently pull the client forward by their gait belt to provide momentum.
B) Provide a wheelchair and immediately transport the client back to their room.
C) Instruct the client to imagine stepping over a specific line on the floor.
D) Return the client to bed and administer a PRN dose of carbidopa-levodopa.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Instruct the client to imagine stepping over a specific line on the floor.

**Rationale:** "Freezing" of gait is a common symptom in Parkinson's disease. Nurses can help clients overcome this by using visual or auditory cues. Asking the client to imagine stepping over a line on the floor (or placing a real object/laser line in front of them) provides a visual target that bypasses the blocked motor pathways and helps initiate movement. Option A is dangerous because pulling the client can easily cause a loss of balance and a fall. Option B does not promote independence or address the temporary freezing episode. Option D is not an immediate behavioral intervention for a freezing episode during ambulation.
</details>

---

### Question 515
A nurse in the emergency department is assessing a 45-year-old client who sustained a head injury in a motor vehicle collision. Which finding during the neurological assessment should the nurse report to the healthcare provider immediately?

A) The client's right pupil is dilated to 7 mm and does not constrict to light.
B) The client repeatedly asks the nurse where they are and what happened.
C) The client complains of a continuous, dull headache and generalized fatigue.
D) The client opens their eyes spontaneously whenever the nurse enters the room.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) The client's right pupil is dilated to 7 mm and does not constrict to light.

**Rationale:** A unilateral fixed and dilated pupil is a neurologic emergency. It is a classic sign of increased intracranial pressure (ICP) and possible brain herniation compressing the oculomotor nerve (Cranial Nerve III). This requires immediate medical intervention. Options B and C (amnesia, confusion, and dull headache) are common, expected findings in a client who has sustained a mild concussion or traumatic brain injury, though they should be monitored. Option D is a normal, positive finding indicating alertness.
</details>
### Question 516
A nurse is admitting a 7-year-old child who has a history of frequent generalized tonic-clonic seizures to the pediatric unit. When setting up the child's hospital room, which action should the nurse take first to ensure the child's safety?

A) Place a padded tongue blade on the bedside table in case of an airway obstruction.
B) Pad the side rails of the hospital bed to prevent injury.
C) Request a prescription for soft wrist restraints to use during a seizure episode.
D) Assign the child to a quiet private room at the far end of the hallway.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Padding the side rails is a standard seizure precaution to protect the child from striking their head or limbs against hard surfaces during the motor activity of a seizure. A padded tongue blade (Option A) is contraindicated, as nothing should ever be placed in the mouth of a client actively seizing due to the risk of broken teeth or choking. Restraints (Option C) are also contraindicated during a seizure because restricting movement can lead to musculoskeletal injuries. The child should ideally be placed in a room close to the nurse's station for frequent observation, not at the far end of the hallway (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 517
A 22-year-old client in the emergency department has just undergone a lumbar puncture to rule out meningitis. The nurse is providing post-procedure instructions. Which statement by the nurse is the most appropriate regarding the client's positioning?

A) "You should sit upright in a chair for the next 4 hours to help the puncture site drain."
B) "You need to walk around the unit every hour to prevent blood clots in your legs."
C) "Keep your head elevated on at least two pillows while resting in bed."
D) "Lie flat on your back for several hours as prescribed by the provider."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
After a lumbar puncture, the client should remain flat on their back (supine) for several hours to prevent a cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leak from the puncture site, which is the primary cause of a post-lumbar puncture "spinal" headache. Sitting upright (Option A), walking (Option B), or elevating the head on pillows (Option C) can increase the leakage of CSF and significantly raise the risk of a severe headache.
</details>

---

### Question 518
A nurse is evaluating a 68-year-old client admitted to the neurological unit with a traumatic brain injury following a fall. While assessing the client's pupils with a penlight, the nurse notes that the right pupil is dilated and does not constrict when light is shined into it. Which action should the nurse take next?

A) Notify the healthcare provider immediately.
B) Document the finding as an expected age-related change.
C) Dim the room lights and recheck the pupil in one hour.
D) Ask the client to read a newspaper to test visual acuity.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
A newly fixed and dilated pupil in a client with a traumatic brain injury is a major neurological warning sign indicating increased intracranial pressure (ICP) and possible brain herniation (cranial nerve III compression). This is a medical emergency, and the nurse must notify the healthcare provider immediately. It is not a normal age-related change (Option B). Waiting an hour (Option C) or testing visual acuity (Option D) delays critical, life-saving interventions.
</details>

---

### Question 519
A home health nurse is visiting a 55-year-old client recently diagnosed with Parkinson's disease. The client is experiencing a shuffling gait and expresses fear of falling at home. Which recommendation should the nurse make to improve the client's mobility and safety?

A) "Keep your eyes focused on your feet while you are walking."
B) "Walk on the balls of your feet to increase your walking speed."
C) "Consciously think about stepping over imaginary lines on the floor."
D) "Wear slippers with a smooth leather sole so you can slide across the floor easier."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Clients with Parkinson's disease often experience a shuffling gait and "freezing" episodes. Instructing the client to consciously think about stepping over imaginary lines (or using visual cues like actual tape on the floor) helps them pick up their feet and lengthen their stride, reducing the risk of falls. Looking at the feet (Option A) alters the center of gravity and increases fall risk. Walking on the balls of the feet (Option B) creates instability. Clients should wear sturdy, non-slip shoes, not smooth-soled slippers that increase sliding and falling (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 520
A nurse in a rehabilitation center is assisting an 82-year-old client with breakfast. The client has right-sided weakness and facial drooping following an ischemic stroke. What is the best action for the nurse to take to prevent aspiration while the client eats?

A) Place the food on the right side of the client's mouth.
B) Encourage the client to tuck their chin toward the chest when swallowing.
C) Provide a straw for all thin liquids served on the meal tray.
D) Position the client in a semi-Fowler's position at 30 degrees during the meal.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Having the client tuck their chin to their chest while swallowing helps close the trachea and open the esophagus, significantly reducing the risk of aspiration. Food should be placed on the unaffected (left) side of the mouth, not the weak right side (Option A). Straws (Option C) are contraindicated because they deliver liquids to the back of the throat too quickly, increasing aspiration risk. The client should be seated in a high-Fowler's position (90 degrees) during meals, not semi-Fowler's (Option D), to utilize gravity for safe swallowing.
</details>
### Question 521
A nurse is admitting a 45-year-old client with a known history of tonic-clonic seizures to the medical-surgical unit. Which of the following actions is the highest priority for the nurse to complete during the admission process?

A) Tape a padded tongue blade to the wall above the bed.
B) Ensure that functioning suction equipment and oxygen are at the bedside.
C) Request a prescription for soft wrist restraints to prevent injury.
D) Keep the bed in the highest position to facilitate rapid assessment.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Ensure that functioning suction equipment and oxygen are at the bedside.

**Rationale:** The priority for a client with a history of seizures is maintaining airway patency and safety (seizure precautions). Having functioning suction and oxygen at the bedside allows the nurse to immediately clear the airway of secretions and provide oxygen if a seizure occurs. Padded tongue blades (Option A) are contraindicated as forcing objects into the mouth during a seizure can cause broken teeth or airway obstruction. Restraints (Option C) can cause injury during the active motor movements of a seizure. The bed should be kept in the lowest, not highest, position (Option D) to reduce the risk of injury from falls.
</details>

---

### Question 522
A nurse in the emergency department observation unit is caring for a 16-year-old client who sustained a mild concussion during a sporting event. Which observation requires the nurse to immediately notify the healthcare provider?

A) The client complains of a persistent, mild headache.
B) The client exhibits equal and bilaterally reactive pupils.
C) The client becomes increasingly difficult to arouse from sleep.
D) The client repeatedly asks what happened during the game.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) The client becomes increasingly difficult to arouse from sleep.

**Rationale:** A decreasing level of consciousness (LOC) or becoming difficult to arouse is a classic early sign of increasing intracranial pressure (ICP) and neurological deterioration. This requires immediate medical intervention. A mild headache (Option A) and repeating questions (amnesia regarding the event, Option D) are common, expected findings following a mild concussion. Equal and reactive pupils (Option B) are a normal neurological finding.
</details>

---

### Question 523
A 22-year-old client has just returned to their room following a lumbar puncture to rule out meningitis. Which intervention should the nurse implement to minimize the client's risk of developing a post-procedure complication?

A) Instruct the client to remain lying flat in bed for several hours.
B) Encourage immediate ambulation in the hallway to prevent venous stasis.
C) Maintain the client on strict fluid restriction for the next 12 hours.
D) Elevate the head of the bed to a 45-degree angle right away.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Instruct the client to remain lying flat in bed for several hours.

**Rationale:** Following a lumbar puncture, clients are at risk for a "spinal headache" caused by the leakage of cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) from the puncture site. Keeping the client lying flat (supine) for a prescribed amount of time (usually 4 to 8 hours) helps prevent this complication. Elevating the head of the bed (Option D) or immediate ambulation (Option B) increases the risk of a spinal headache. Fluids should be encouraged, not restricted (Option C), to help replenish CSF volume.
</details>

---

### Question 524
A nurse in a long-term care facility is assisting a 78-year-old client with Parkinson's disease who frequently experiences "freezing" episodes while walking. Which instruction should the nurse provide to promote safe mobility?

A) "Try to look down directly at your feet while you are walking."
B) "Shuffle your feet smoothly across the floor instead of lifting them."
C) "Use a wheelchair at all times to eliminate your risk of falling."
D) "Imagine a line on the floor in front of you and step over it."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) "Imagine a line on the floor in front of you and step over it."

**Rationale:** Clients with Parkinson's disease often experience bradykinesia and "freezing" of gait, where they feel stuck to the floor. Teaching the client to consciously lift their toes and step over an imaginary line helps bypass the basal ganglia defect by utilizing the frontal cortex to initiate purposeful movement. Looking down at the feet (Option A) alters their center of gravity and increases fall risk. Shuffling (Option B) is a symptom of Parkinson's that increases the risk of tripping, so clients should be taught to pick up their feet. Wheelchairs (Option C) should not be used exclusively unless absolutely necessary, as maintaining independent mobility and strength is crucial.
</details>

---

### Question 525
A nurse on a rehabilitation unit is preparing to feed a 62-year-old client who has right-sided weakness and dysphagia following an ischemic stroke. Which action should the nurse take to prevent aspiration?

A) Place the food onto the right side of the client's mouth.
B) Offer thin liquids through a straw between bites of solid food.
C) Position the client in an upright, seated position at 90 degrees.
D) Ask the client to tilt their head backward when swallowing.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Position the client in an upright, seated position at 90 degrees.

**Rationale:** To prevent aspiration in a client with dysphagia, the nurse should ensure the client is sitting in a high-Fowler's position (90 degrees) during meals and for at least 30 minutes afterward. Food should be placed on the unaffected (left) side of the mouth, not the weak side (Option A). Straws (Option B) deliver fluids too quickly to the back of the throat and increase the risk of choking, especially with thin liquids which are hardest to swallow. The client should be taught to tuck their chin to their chest when swallowing, rather than tilting the head backward (Option D), because a chin-tuck protects the airway.
</details>
### Question 526
A nurse in the emergency department is evaluating a 22-year-old patient who fell from a ladder. When the nurse calls the patient's name loudly, the patient does not open their eyes. The patient only opens their eyes when the nurse applies a sternal rub. How should the nurse document this specific finding on the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) assessment?

A) Spontaneous eye opening  
B) Eye opening to speech  
C) Eye opening to pain  
D) No eye opening  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Under the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS), eye opening is scored based on the stimulus required. Since the patient did not open their eyes to a verbal command but did respond to a painful stimulus (sternal rub), this is documented as "eye opening to pain" (a score of 2 in the eye-opening category). Spontaneous eye opening is a score of 4, to speech is 3, and no response is 1.
</details>

---

### Question 527
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is preparing a room for a 45-year-old patient being admitted with a known history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures. Which action is the highest priority for the nurse to complete before the patient arrives?

A) Ensure suction equipment and an oxygen setup are functioning at the bedside.  
B) Place padded tongue blades on the bedside table for immediate use during a seizure.  
C) Request an order for soft wrist restraints to prevent injury if the patient seizes.  
D) Keep all four side rails lowered to prevent the patient from feeling trapped.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The highest priority for a patient on seizure precautions is maintaining a patent airway. The nurse must ensure that functioning suction equipment and oxygen are readily available at the bedside. Inserting anything into the mouth (like padded tongue blades) during a seizure is strictly contraindicated due to the risk of choking and jaw injury. Restraints are also contraindicated as they can cause severe injury or fractures during convulsions. Bed rails should be padded, and typically the bed is kept in the lowest position, but lowering all four rails is not a standard seizure precaution.
</details>

---

### Question 528
A nurse in a rehabilitation facility is assisting an 80-year-old patient with right-sided weakness following a recent left hemispheric stroke. During breakfast, the nurse observes the patient coughing repeatedly after taking a sip of water. Which immediate action should the nurse take?

A) Provide a straw to make swallowing the water easier.  
B) Encourage the patient to tilt their head backward before swallowing.  
C) Offer the patient a piece of dry toast to help clear the airway.  
D) Keep the patient NPO (nothing by mouth) and notify the healthcare provider.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Coughing after drinking water is a primary sign of dysphagia and indicates that the patient may be aspirating fluid into their airway. The nurse must immediately stop the feeding (keep the patient NPO) and notify the provider so a formal swallowing evaluation (often by speech therapy) can be conducted. Using a straw or tilting the head backward actually increases the risk of aspiration. Giving dry toast is dangerous and could cause choking.
</details>

---

### Question 529
A 16-year-old patient has just undergone a lumbar puncture in an outpatient diagnostic clinic to rule out a central nervous system infection. Which intervention should the nurse implement immediately following the procedure?

A) Ambulate the patient around the clinic to promote spinal fluid circulation.  
B) Maintain the patient in a flat, supine position for several hours.  
C) Apply a warm heating pad directly over the puncture site to reduce pain.  
D) Encourage the patient to sit in a high-Fowler's position to prevent a headache.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Following a lumbar puncture, the patient should be kept in a flat, supine position for several hours (usually 4 to 8 hours depending on facility policy) to prevent cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leakage and minimize the risk of a post-lumbar puncture headache. Sitting up in a high-Fowler's position or ambulating immediately increases the risk of a severe headache. A heating pad is not a standard post-procedure intervention and could increase bleeding risk at the site.
</details>

---

### Question 530
A nurse in a long-term care facility is walking in the hallway with a 74-year-old resident who has Parkinson's disease. The resident suddenly stops and states, "My feet feel like they are glued to the floor." Which instruction should the nurse provide to help the resident resume walking?

A) "Try to spin around quickly and walk in the other direction."  
B) "Lean your upper body as far forward as you can."  
C) "Pretend you are stepping over an imaginary line on the floor."  
D) "Look up at the ceiling and close your eyes for a moment."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Patients with Parkinson's disease often experience "freezing" of gait, where they feel stuck to the floor. Having the patient imagine stepping over a line, stepping over a laser pointer on the floor, or marching in place can help bypass the basal ganglia and initiate movement. Spinning, closing the eyes, or leaning far forward are highly dangerous and significantly increase the risk of falls due to the postural instability associated with Parkinson's disease.
</details>
### Question 531
A nurse is admitting an 8-year-old child with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures to the pediatric unit. Which of the following actions should the nurse take first when setting up the client's room?

A) Place a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.
B) Secure soft wrist restraints to the bed frame.
C) Pad the side rails of the bed.
D) Keep the overhead room lights brightly lit at all times.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Pad the side rails of the bed.

**Rationale:** Padding the side rails is an essential, easy-to-implement seizure precaution that helps protect the child from physical injury if a seizure occurs. Placing anything in the mouth (such as a tongue blade) during a seizure is strictly contraindicated due to the risk of airway obstruction and dental damage. Restraints should never be used during a seizure as they can cause musculoskeletal injury. A calm, dimly lit environment is usually preferred to reduce sensory triggers, rather than bright lights.
</details>

---

### Question 532
A 25-year-old client has just returned to the medical-surgical unit after a lumbar puncture. Which instruction should the nurse provide to the client to prevent post-procedure complications?

A) "Remain flat in bed for the next several hours."
B) "Ambulate in the hallway immediately to prevent blood clots."
C) "Restrict your fluid intake for the rest of the day."
D) "You should cough and deep breathe forcefully every hour."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "Remain flat in bed for the next several hours."

**Rationale:** After a lumbar puncture, the client should remain flat in bed (supine) for several hours to prevent a post-lumbar puncture headache, which can occur due to the leakage of cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) from the puncture site. Ambulating immediately increases the risk of a severe headache. Fluids should be encouraged, not restricted, to help replace the removed CSF. Forceful coughing can increase intracranial pressure and exacerbate CSF leakage.
</details>

---

### Question 533
A nurse in a rehabilitation facility is assisting a 72-year-old client who recently suffered a right-sided ischemic stroke. The client has left-sided weakness and mild dysphagia. Which action by the nurse is most appropriate during mealtime?

A) Provide a straw for the client to drink all thin liquids.
B) Place the client in a 30-degree semi-Fowler's position.
C) Place the food on the left side of the client's mouth.
D) Instruct the client to tilt their chin down while swallowing.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Instruct the client to tilt their chin down while swallowing.

**Rationale:** The chin-tuck maneuver narrows the airway opening and widens the vallecular space, significantly reducing the risk of aspiration in clients with dysphagia. Using a straw can propel liquids too quickly to the back of the throat, increasing aspiration risk. The client should be seated perfectly upright (High-Fowler's, 90 degrees), not at 30 degrees. Food should always be placed on the strong, unaffected side of the mouth (the right side, in this case).
</details>

---

### Question 534
A home health nurse is visiting a 68-year-old client diagnosed with Parkinson's disease. The client experiences frequent "freezing" of gait while walking through the house. Which strategy should the nurse teach the client to use when this occurs?

A) Pivot quickly on one foot to change direction.
B) Imagine stepping over a specific line on the floor.
C) Close both eyes and wait for the freezing to stop.
D) Look up at the ceiling and take a quick step backward.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Imagine stepping over a specific line on the floor.

**Rationale:** "Freezing" is a common symptom of Parkinson's disease where the client suddenly feels their feet are glued to the floor. Using visual cues, such as imagining a line to step over or placing a laser pointer line on the floor, bypasses the basal ganglia and uses the motor cortex to initiate movement. Pivoting quickly, closing the eyes, or looking up while stepping backward all drastically increase the client's risk of falling due to existing postural instability.
</details>

---

### Question 535
A 45-year-old client is brought to the emergency department after a motor vehicle crash. The nurse assesses the client's level of consciousness and notes that the client keeps their eyes closed but opens them when the nurse calls their name loudly. How should the nurse document this specific finding on the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS)?

A) Opens eyes spontaneously.
B) Opens eyes to pain.
C) Opens eyes to speech.
D) Unresponsive to external stimuli.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Opens eyes to speech.

**Rationale:** The Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS) evaluates eye-opening, verbal, and motor responses. Opening eyes when the nurse speaks or calls the client's name corresponds to "opens eyes to speech" (or verbal command), scoring a 3 in the eye-opening category. Opening eyes without prompt is "spontaneous" (score 4), opening only to physical discomfort is "to pain" (score 2), and keeping them closed despite all stimuli is "unresponsive" (score 1).
</details>
### Question 536
A nurse is caring for an 8-year-old child in the pediatric unit who suddenly begins having a generalized tonic-clonic seizure while lying in bed. What is the nurse's priority action?

A) Insert a padded tongue blade into the child's mouth.  
B) Turn the child to a side-lying position.  
C) Firmly hold the child's arms to prevent injury.  
D) Leave the room briefly to call for emergency help.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
Turning the child to a side-lying position is the priority action because it helps maintain a patent airway and allows saliva or vomitus to drain, preventing aspiration. The nurse should never insert anything into the mouth of a seizing patient (Option A), as this can cause injury or airway obstruction. Restraining the patient (Option C) can lead to musculoskeletal injuries. The nurse should stay with the patient and call for help from the bedside (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 537
A 45-year-old client has just returned to the day surgery observation room following a diagnostic lumbar puncture. Which action should the nurse implement next?

A) Encourage the client to remain flat in bed for the prescribed time.  
B) Ambulate the client to the bathroom to void immediately.  
C) Elevate the head of the bed to a 45-degree angle to promote drainage.  
D) Assess the client's gag reflex before offering oral fluids.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
After a lumbar puncture, the client is typically instructed to remain flat in bed for a specified amount of time (usually 1 to several hours) to prevent the development of a spinal headache caused by cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leakage. Ambulating the client (Option B) or elevating the head of the bed (Option C) increases the risk of a spinal headache. A lumbar puncture does not affect the gag reflex (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 538
The nurse is observing a 72-year-old resident with Parkinson's disease eating lunch in the dining room of a long-term care facility. Which observation requires immediate intervention by the nurse?

A) The resident's hands are visibly shaking while holding the soup spoon.  
B) The resident is taking very small, slow bites of food.  
C) The resident clears their throat continuously after taking a sip of water.  
D) The resident shuffles their feet when transferring from the chair to a walker.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Continuous throat clearing after drinking is a key sign of dysphagia (difficulty swallowing) and a potential indicator of aspiration, which is a major complication of Parkinson's disease. This requires immediate intervention to protect the airway. Resting tremors (Option A), bradykinesia/slow movements (Option B), and a shuffling gait (Option D) are expected, classic symptoms of Parkinson's disease and do not require immediate emergency intervention.
</details>

---

### Question 539
A nurse is assisting a 60-year-old client in a rehabilitation unit who recently had a left-hemisphere stroke, resulting in right-sided hemiplegia and expressive aphasia. How should the nurse best communicate with this client when assessing their morning pain level?

A) Speak loudly and clearly into the client's right ear.  
B) Provide a blank notebook and ask the client to write a detailed description.  
C) Wait until a family member arrives to translate the client's needs.  
D) Provide a picture board and ask the client to point to a pain face.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Clients with expressive aphasia understand what is being said but have difficulty speaking or forming words. Providing an alternative communication method, such as a picture board, allows the client to effectively express their needs. Speaking loudly (Option A) is unnecessary as the client is not hard of hearing. Asking the client to write (Option B) is inappropriate because expressive aphasia often affects writing, and the client also has right-sided paralysis. The nurse should not delay pain assessment by waiting for a family member (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 540
A 22-year-old client is admitted to the neurosurgical unit for observation following a mild concussion sustained during a sporting event. During the nurse's hourly assessment, which finding should be reported to the healthcare provider immediately?

A) The client complains of a mild, dull headache.  
B) The client has a small, superficial abrasion on their forehead.  
C) The client becomes increasingly difficult to awaken.  
D) The client states they are very tired and want to sleep.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
A decrease in the level of consciousness (LOC), such as becoming increasingly difficult to awaken, is an early and critical sign of increased intracranial pressure (ICP) and deteriorating neurological status. This requires immediate notification of the healthcare provider. A mild headache (Option A), a superficial abrasion (Option B), and feeling tired (Option D) are expected findings after a mild head injury and do not indicate immediate neurological decline on their own.
</details>
### Question 541

A nurse in an acute rehabilitation unit is caring for a 68-year-old male patient who recently suffered an ischemic stroke. The patient has right-sided weakness and exhibits expressive (Broca’s) aphasia. Which action should the nurse take to facilitate effective communication with this patient?

A. Speak loudly and clearly directly into the patient's left ear.
B. Ask open-ended questions to encourage the patient to practice speaking.
C. Provide a picture board and allow extra time for the patient to respond.
D. Finish the patient's sentences for him to prevent anxiety and frustration.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Provide a picture board and allow extra time for the patient to respond.

**Rationale:** Expressive (Broca's) aphasia means the patient understands language but has severe difficulty forming words and speaking. Providing alternative communication methods, such as a picture board, and allowing ample time for the patient to formulate a response promotes independence and reduces frustration. Speaking loudly (Option A) is unnecessary as aphasia is a neurological language deficit, not a hearing impairment. Open-ended questions (Option B) are too difficult for a patient with expressive aphasia; yes/no questions are preferred. Finishing the patient's sentences (Option D) can cause further frustration and undermines their rehabilitation.
</details>

---

### Question 542

A 22-year-old female patient on a medical-surgical floor suddenly loses consciousness and begins to experience a generalized tonic-clonic seizure while lying in bed. What is the nurse's *initial* priority action?

A. Turn the patient onto their side and clear the immediate area of hazards.
B. Insert a padded tongue blade into the patient's mouth to protect the airway.
C. Restrain the patient's arms and legs to prevent musculoskeletal injury.
D. Administer oral antiseizure medication immediately to stop the tremors.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. Turn the patient onto their side and clear the immediate area of hazards.

**Rationale:** The priority during an active seizure is safety and airway protection. Turning the patient on their side allows oral secretions to drain, preventing aspiration. Clearing the area prevents physical injury from striking bed rails or other objects. A nurse should never insert anything into the mouth of a seizing patient (Option B) due to the risk of breaking teeth or causing airway obstruction. Restraining the patient (Option C) can cause fractures or muscle tears. Oral medications (Option D) cannot be safely given to an unconscious, seizing patient due to aspiration risk.
</details>

---

### Question 543

A 45-year-old male patient in an outpatient neurology clinic has just undergone a lumbar puncture to rule out meningitis. Which action should the nurse implement immediately following the procedure?

A. Ambulate the patient in the hallway to prevent deep vein thrombosis.
B. Elevate the head of the bed to 45 degrees to decrease intracranial pressure.
C. Restrict oral fluid intake for the next 4 hours to prevent nausea.
D. Keep the patient in a flat, supine position for several hours.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Keep the patient in a flat, supine position for several hours.

**Rationale:** After a lumbar puncture, the patient should be kept in a flat, supine position for a prescribed period (usually 4 to 8 hours). This prevents the leakage of cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) from the puncture site, which is the primary cause of a severe post-lumbar puncture headache. Elevating the head (Option B) or ambulating immediately (Option A) increases the risk of CSF leakage and a subsequent spinal headache. Encouraging oral fluids is actually recommended to help replace CSF volume, making fluid restriction (Option C) incorrect.
</details>

---

### Question 544

A nurse in a long-term care facility is walking down the hallway with a 74-year-old female patient diagnosed with Parkinson's disease. The patient suddenly experiences a "freezing" episode and is unable to take another step. Which intervention should the nurse use to help the patient resume walking?

A. Gently push the patient forward from the shoulders to initiate movement.
B. Instruct the patient to pretend she is stepping over an imaginary line on the floor.
C. Provide a wheelchair immediately and discourage any further walking.
D. Tell the patient to look up at the ceiling and take a large step backward.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Instruct the patient to pretend she is stepping over an imaginary line on the floor.

**Rationale:** "Freezing" of gait is a common symptom of Parkinson's disease. Using visual or cognitive cues, such as imagining a line to step over, dropping a tissue to step over, or counting steps out loud, helps bypass the basal ganglia deficits and initiates motor movement. Pushing the patient (Option A) increases the risk of a fall due to the patient's altered center of gravity and rigidity. Providing a wheelchair (Option C) removes the opportunity for necessary mobility and independence. Looking at the ceiling or stepping backward (Option D) creates an immense fall risk.
</details>

---

### Question 545

A nurse in a memory care unit is assisting an 81-year-old female patient with Alzheimer’s disease who has developed mild neurological dysphagia (difficulty swallowing). During mealtime, which action by the nurse is most appropriate to prevent aspiration?

A. Provide a plastic straw to make swallowing liquids easier for the patient.
B. Position the patient in a semi-Fowler's position (30 degrees) during the meal.
C. Encourage the patient to tilt her head slightly forward (chin-tuck) while swallowing.
D. Mix all solid foods and thin liquids together into a single bowl to save time.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Encourage the patient to tilt her head slightly forward (chin-tuck) while swallowing.

**Rationale:** The chin-tuck maneuver narrows the airway opening, widens the valleculae to catch unswallowed food, and pushes the epiglottis backward to protect the trachea, significantly reducing the risk of aspiration in patients with dysphagia. Using a straw (Option A) is contraindicated for patients with dysphagia as it propels liquids to the back of the throat too quickly, increasing aspiration risk. The patient should be sitting completely upright at 90 degrees (high-Fowler's), not semi-Fowler's (Option B). Mixing solids and thin liquids (Option D) creates a mixed consistency, which is generally the most difficult type of food for a dysphagia patient to manage safely.
</details>
### Question 546
A 32-year-old client with a history of epilepsy suddenly loses consciousness and begins to experience tonic-clonic movements while sitting in a chair in the facility's dayroom. Which action should the nurse take first?

A. Insert a padded tongue blade into the client's mouth to protect the airway.
B. Restrain the client's arms and legs to prevent musculoskeletal injury.
C. Gently ease the client to the floor and place a cushion under their head.
D. Administer a PRN dose of oral lorazepam to stop the seizure activity.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
During an active seizure, the nurse's priority is the client's physical safety. Easing a seated client to the floor and protecting their head prevents traumatic injury from falling or striking hard surfaces. The nurse should never insert anything into the mouth of a seizing client (Option A), as this can cause dental damage or airway obstruction. Restraining the client (Option B) can result in fractures or soft tissue injuries. Oral medications (Option D) are contraindicated during a seizure due to the high risk of aspiration; if medication is needed, it should be administered via IV, IM, or rectal routes.
</details>

---

### Question 547
The nurse is caring for a 75-year-old client in a rehabilitation facility who has right-sided weakness and dysphagia following an ischemic stroke. When preparing to assist the client with breakfast, which action is the priority?

A. Ensure the client is sitting upright in a chair or at a 90-degree angle in bed.
B. Provide a straw for all liquids to make swallowing easier and cleaner.
C. Ask the client to tilt their head backward when swallowing their food.
D. Mix solid foods with thin liquids to create a smoother, softer texture.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Clients with dysphagia are at a high risk for aspiration. Positioning the client upright at a 90-degree angle utilizes gravity to facilitate proper swallowing and prevents food from entering the airway. Straws (Option B) are generally contraindicated for clients with dysphagia because they deliver liquids to the back of the throat too quickly, increasing aspiration risk. The client should be taught to use a "chin-tuck" maneuver, not a backward head tilt (Option C), to protect the airway. Thin liquids often increase the risk of aspiration, so mixing them with solids (Option D) is unsafe; liquids usually need to be thickened according to speech therapy recommendations.
</details>

---

### Question 548
A home health nurse is evaluating the living environment of a 68-year-old client newly diagnosed with Parkinson's disease. The client experiences a shuffling gait and frequent freezing episodes. Which observation by the nurse requires immediate intervention?

A. The client's bed is maintained in the lowest possible position.
B. The client wears firm, enclosed, rubber-soled shoes while walking indoors.
C. The bathroom is equipped with newly installed grab bars near the toilet.
D. Several decorative throw rugs are placed along the main hallway.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Clients with Parkinson's disease often exhibit a propulsive or shuffling gait, meaning they do not lift their feet high off the ground when walking. Throw rugs are a major tripping hazard and should be removed from the environment immediately to prevent falls. A low bed (Option A), wearing firm supportive shoes (Option B), and installing grab bars (Option C) are all excellent, safe interventions that promote independence and reduce the risk of injury for a client with Parkinson's disease.
</details>

---

### Question 549
A 21-year-old client is being discharged from an urgent care clinic after sustaining a mild concussion during a recreational soccer game. The nurse is providing discharge instructions to the client and their roommate. Which statement by the roommate indicates a correct understanding of the teaching?

A. "I need to wake them up every hour tonight to shine a light in their eyes."
B. "I will bring them back to the clinic or emergency room if they start vomiting repeatedly."
C. "I should give them ibuprofen immediately if they complain of a mild headache."
D. "They can go back to playing soccer tomorrow as long as they feel okay."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Repeated vomiting is a red flag following a head injury, as it may indicate increasing intracranial pressure (ICP), and warrants immediate medical evaluation. Routine waking every hour (Option A) is no longer standard practice for a mild concussion unless specifically ordered, as uninterrupted cognitive and physical rest is needed for healing. Acetaminophen is the preferred analgesic for a headache post-concussion; NSAIDs like ibuprofen (Option C) should be avoided initially due to the potential risk of intracranial bleeding. Return to contact sports (Option D) requires a gradual, medically supervised return-to-play protocol, not just a subjective feeling of wellness.
</details>

---

### Question 550
A nurse on a pediatric unit is caring for an 8-year-old child who has just returned to the room following a lumbar puncture to rule out bacterial meningitis. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate at this time?

A. Encourage the child to sit upright in a chair to eat their favorite snack.
B. Apply a warm compress to the child's lower back to prevent muscle spasms.
C. Keep the child lying flat in bed for the prescribed amount of time.
D. Instruct the child to perform active range-of-motion exercises with their legs.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Following a lumbar puncture, the client should remain lying flat in bed (supine) for the time specified by the provider (typically 1 to several hours). This helps prevent a cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leak from the puncture site, which can cause a severe spinal headache. Having the child sit upright immediately (Option A) increases the risk of a spinal headache. Warm compresses (Option B) and active range-of-motion exercises (Option D) are not standard immediate post-procedure interventions and could interfere with necessary rest and healing of the puncture site.
</details>
### Question 551
A 68-year-old patient in a rehabilitation facility is recovering from an ischemic stroke that caused right-sided hemiplegia. The nurse is preparing to help the patient transfer from the bed to a wheelchair. Which action should the nurse take to ensure a safe transfer?

A. Place the wheelchair at a 45-degree angle on the patient's right side.  
B. Place the wheelchair at a 45-degree angle on the patient's left side.  
C. Instruct the patient to bear most of their weight on their right leg.  
D. Stand directly behind the patient and lift them by the shoulders.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
When transferring a patient with unilateral weakness (hemiplegia), the nurse should always position the wheelchair on the patient's unaffected (strong) side. Since this patient has right-sided weakness, the left side is the strong side. Placing the chair on the left allows the patient to use their functional arm and leg to pivot and assist safely. Bearing weight on the weak leg (Option C) increases the risk of falls. Lifting by the shoulders (Option D) is unsafe and can cause injury to both the patient and the nurse.
</details>

---

### Question 552
A 7-year-old child with a known history of epilepsy is admitted to the pediatric unit for observation after a viral illness. While setting up the room, which action should the nurse prioritize to implement standard seizure precautions?

A. Ensure that working suction equipment and oxygen are at the bedside.  
B. Keep a padded tongue blade on the bedside table for easy access.  
C. Place the patient's bed in the highest position to allow for quick assessment.  
D. Request an order for soft limb restraints to prevent injury during a seizure.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Standard seizure precautions include having working suction and oxygen equipment readily available at the bedside to maintain airway patency and oxygenation if a seizure occurs. Nothing should ever be placed in the mouth of a seizing patient, making a padded tongue blade (Option B) contraindicated. The bed should be kept in the lowest position, not the highest (Option C), to prevent injury from falls. Restraints (Option D) are contraindicated during a seizure as they can cause severe musculoskeletal injury.
</details>

---

### Question 553
A 45-year-old patient returns to the medical-surgical unit after undergoing a diagnostic lumbar puncture. The nurse is providing instructions to the patient regarding post-procedure care. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate?

A. Elevate the head of the patient's bed to at least 45 degrees.  
B. Ambulate the patient to the bathroom immediately to empty their bladder.  
C. Maintain the patient in a flat, supine position for several hours.  
D. Encourage the patient to cough and deep breathe vigorously every hour.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
After a lumbar puncture, the patient should remain flat in a supine position for several hours (typically 4 to 8 hours, depending on facility policy) to prevent cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leakage and minimize the risk of a post-lumbar puncture spinal headache. Elevating the head of the bed (Option A) or ambulating immediately (Option B) increases the risk of a spinal headache. Vigorous coughing (Option D) increases intracranial and intraspinal pressure, which can exacerbate a CSF leak.
</details>

---

### Question 554
A 72-year-old resident in a long-term care facility has late-stage Parkinson's disease and experiences frequent tremors and moderate dysphagia. The nurse is assisting the resident during lunch. Which intervention is most important for the nurse to implement?

A. Provide thin liquids to make swallowing easier and prevent dehydration.  
B. Rush the patient through the meal to prevent chewing fatigue.  
C. Instruct the patient to tilt their head backward when swallowing food.  
D. Ensure the patient is sitting in a strict upright, 90-degree position.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Patients with Parkinson's disease often experience dysphagia (difficulty swallowing) and are at a high risk for aspiration. The nurse must ensure the patient is sitting upright at a 90-degree angle (High Fowler's) during meals and for at least 30 minutes afterward to prevent aspiration. Thin liquids (Option A) are actually more difficult to control and increase aspiration risk; thickened liquids are often preferred. Rushing the patient (Option B) increases the risk of choking. Tilting the head backward (Option C) opens the airway and increases aspiration risk; a "chin-tuck" position is generally recommended instead.
</details>

---

### Question 555
A 22-year-old patient is brought to the Emergency Department after sustaining a mild head bump during a soccer game. The patient is awake and alert. As part of a baseline neurological assessment, the nurse wants to evaluate the patient's pupillary response. Which action should the nurse perform?

A. Ask the patient to smile, frown, and puff out their cheeks.  
B. Shine a penlight into the patient's eyes to observe for pupil constriction.  
C. Whisper a word in the patient's ear and ask them to repeat it.  
D. Have the patient read letters from a Snellen chart at a distance of 20 feet.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Evaluating pupillary response is a basic and crucial component of a neurological assessment, testing the Oculomotor nerve (Cranial Nerve III). The nurse accomplishes this by shining a penlight into the eyes to check for direct and consensual pupil constriction (PERRLA). Smiling and puffing cheeks (Option A) tests the Facial nerve (CN VII). Whispering (Option C) tests the Vestibulocochlear nerve (CN VIII). Reading a Snellen chart (Option D) tests visual acuity via the Optic nerve (CN II).
</details>
### Question 556

A nurse is admitting a 22-year-old patient with a history of generalized tonic-clonic seizures to the medical-surgical unit. As the nurse prepares the patient's room, which action is the priority to ensure the patient's safety?

A) Placing a padded tongue blade on the bedside table for easy access.  
B) Keeping all four side rails in the down position to prevent entrapment.  
C) Ensuring that functioning suction equipment and oxygen are at the bedside.  
D) Assigning the patient to a room at the very end of the hallway for a quiet environment.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Ensuring that functioning suction equipment and oxygen are at the bedside.

**Rationale:** The priority in seizure precautions is maintaining airway patency and safety during and after a seizure. Having suction equipment and oxygen readily available at the bedside allows the nurse to quickly clear secretions and provide supplemental oxygen if needed. Placing a tongue blade in a patient's mouth during a seizure is strictly contraindicated as it can cause dental damage and airway obstruction. Keeping all four rails down is unsafe; standard seizure precautions involve padding the side rails and keeping two or three up. A patient with a high seizure risk should ideally be placed close to the nurse's station for close observation, not at the end of the hall.
</details>

---

### Question 557

A nurse is assisting a 74-year-old patient who recently had a right-sided stroke with their first meal. The patient exhibits mild left-sided facial weakness. Which action should the nurse take to promote safe swallowing and prevent aspiration?

A) Instruct the patient to tuck their chin to their chest when swallowing.  
B) Position the patient in a 30-degree semi-Fowler's position during the meal.  
C) Provide a completely liquid diet using a straw for all beverages.  
D) Place the food onto the weaker (left) side of the patient's mouth.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Instruct the patient to tuck their chin to their chest when swallowing.

**Rationale:** Tucking the chin to the chest helps close the trachea and open the esophagus, significantly reducing the risk of aspiration in patients with dysphagia following a stroke. The patient should be positioned upright in a high-Fowler's position (90 degrees), not a 30-degree angle. Food should always be placed on the stronger (unaffected) side of the mouth to aid in chewing and swallowing. Using a straw can increase the speed of liquid entering the mouth, which increases the risk of aspiration, especially with thin liquids.
</details>

---

### Question 558

An emergency department nurse is evaluating a 19-year-old patient brought in after a skateboarding accident. When the nurse calls the patient's name loudly, the patient does not open their eyes. However, when the nurse applies a mild sternal rub, the patient's eyes open. How should the nurse document this specific eye-opening response using the Glasgow Coma Scale?

A) Spontaneous eye opening.  
B) Eye opening to speech.  
C) No eye opening response.  
D) Eye opening to pain.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Eye opening to pain.

**Rationale:** On the Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS), eye opening is scored based on the stimulus required to elicit a response. Because the patient did not open their eyes to a verbal command (speech) but did open them in response to a physical, painful stimulus (sternal rub), it is documented as "eye opening to pain." Spontaneous eye opening occurs without any external stimulus. 
</details>

---

### Question 559

A home health nurse is observing a 68-year-old patient with Parkinson's disease walking from the living room to the kitchen. The patient suddenly experiences a "freezing" episode, stopping completely in the doorway and stating they cannot move their feet. What is the most appropriate initial action by the nurse?

A) Grasp the patient firmly by the waist and gently pull them forward into the room.  
B) Instruct the patient to imagine stepping over a line drawn on the floor.  
C) Tell the patient to look directly up at the ceiling and take a quick, large step.  
D) Have the patient stand completely still with their eyes closed for five minutes before trying again.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
