**Correct Answer:** B) Instruct the patient to imagine stepping over a line drawn on the floor.

**Rationale:** Patients with Parkinson's disease often experience "freezing" of gait. Using visual or cognitive cues, such as imagining a line on the floor, stepping over an imaginary laser, or focusing on a specific spot to step on, can help bypass the basal ganglia deficits and initiate movement. Pulling the patient alters their center of gravity and severely increases the risk of a fall. Looking up at the ceiling impairs balance and vision of the pathway. Standing with eyes closed for five minutes does not resolve freezing and may increase dizziness or unsteadiness.
</details>

---

### Question 560

A 45-year-old outpatient is recovering in the clinic immediately after undergoing a lumbar puncture to rule out an infection. Which action should the nurse take to prevent a common post-procedure complication?

A) Instruct the patient to remain flat in bed for several hours as prescribed.  
B) Ambulate the patient around the room immediately to assess motor function.  
C) Restrict the patient's oral fluid intake for the first 12 hours.  
D) Elevate the head of the patient's bed to a 90-degree angle.  

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Instruct the patient to remain flat in bed for several hours as prescribed.

**Rationale:** Following a lumbar puncture, patients are at a high risk for developing a post-lumbar puncture headache, which is caused by the leakage of cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) from the puncture site. Keeping the patient in a flat, supine position for an extended period (usually 1 to several hours, per facility protocol) helps prevent this headache. The nurse should encourage oral fluid intake, not restrict it, as hydration helps replenish CSF volume. Ambulating immediately or elevating the head to 90 degrees dramatically increases the likelihood of a severe spinal headache.
</details>
### Question 561
A nurse is admitting a 7-year-old child who has a history of frequent tonic-clonic seizures to the pediatric floor. Which action should the nurse take first when preparing the child's room for safety?

A) Place a padded tongue blade on the bedside table.  
B) Ensure the side rails of the bed are padded.  
C) Keep the overhead room lights on brightly at all times.  
D) Secure soft restraints to the bed frame just in case.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
Padding the side rails is a standard and essential seizure precaution to protect the child from traumatic injury if a seizure occurs while in bed. A padded tongue blade (Option A) should never be placed in a client's mouth during a seizure due to the risk of airway obstruction and dental damage. Bright lights (Option C) can sometimes trigger seizures and interfere with rest. Restraints (Option D) are strictly contraindicated during a seizure as they can cause musculoskeletal injuries.
</details>

---

### Question 562
A nurse is assisting a 78-year-old client with right-sided weakness and dysphagia following an ischemic stroke. The client is seated in the rehabilitation unit's dining room for lunch. Which nursing action is most appropriate to prevent aspiration?

A) Encourage the client to tilt their head backward when swallowing.  
B) Offer a glass of plain, thin water after every bite of solid food.  
C) Instruct the client to tuck their chin to their chest while swallowing.  
D) Mix all the client's solid foods and liquids together into a single puree.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
Instructing the client to use the "chin-tuck" method helps prevent aspiration by narrowing the airway opening and directing food toward the esophagus. Tilting the head backward (Option A) opens the airway and drastically increases the risk of aspiration. Thin liquids like plain water (Option B) are typically the most difficult for dysphagia clients to swallow and often require thickening. Mixing all food and liquids together (Option D) is unappetizing, unnecessary if the client can safely eat a prescribed diet, and does not address the mechanics of safe swallowing.
</details>

---

### Question 563
A 45-year-old client has just returned to the medical-surgical unit after a diagnostic lumbar puncture. The client complains of a mild, throbbing headache. Which instruction should the nurse provide to the client?

A) "Sit upright in a chair for an hour to relieve the pressure in your head."  
B) "I will dim the lights and limit your fluid intake for the next 8 hours."  
C) "Ambulate around the room slowly to promote spinal fluid circulation."  
D) "Lie flat in bed for the next several hours and drink plenty of fluids."  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Following a lumbar puncture, the client is at risk for a "spinal headache" due to cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leakage at the puncture site. Lying flat helps prevent and relieve this type of headache, and increased fluid intake helps replace the withdrawn CSF. Sitting upright (Option A) or ambulating (Option C) increases the pull of gravity on the CSF, which typically worsens a spinal headache. Fluid intake should be encouraged, not limited (Option B).
</details>

---

### Question 564
A nurse at an outpatient neurology clinic is observing a 68-year-old client with Parkinson's disease walking down the hallway. The client suddenly freezes and appears unable to take another step. What is the best action for the nurse to take?

A) Tell the client to imagine stepping over a line on the floor.  
B) Grasp the client's arm and gently pull them forward.  
C) Instruct the client to pivot quickly on one foot to break the freeze.  
D) Immediately bring a wheelchair to transport the client the rest of the way.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
Clients with Parkinson's disease often experience "freezing" of gait. Using visual cues, such as imagining a line on the floor or dropping a tissue and asking the client to step over it, can help bypass the basal ganglia block and initiate movement. Pulling the client (Option B) or asking them to pivot quickly (Option C) significantly increases the risk of a fall due to their postural instability. Providing a wheelchair immediately (Option D) promotes dependence rather than encouraging mobility strategies.
</details>

---

### Question 565
A 22-year-old client is being monitored in the intensive care unit following a severe concussion from a snowboarding accident. Which observation by the nurse should be reported immediately to the healthcare provider as the earliest sign of increasing intracranial pressure (ICP)?

A) The client demonstrates decorticate posturing when stimulated.  
B) The client becomes unusually restless and confused about their location.  
C) The client develops a widened pulse pressure and severe bradycardia.  
D) The client's pupils become bilaterally dilated and fixed to light.  

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
A change in the level of consciousness (LOC), such as restlessness, confusion, or lethargy, is the earliest and most sensitive indicator of increasing intracranial pressure. Decorticate posturing (Option A), Cushing's triad (widened pulse pressure, bradycardia, altered respirations) (Option C), and fixed, dilated pupils (Option D) are all late and ominous signs of dangerously high ICP and impending brain herniation.
</details>
### Question 566
A nurse is assisting an 82-year-old client with right-sided hemiparesis to eat lunch. The client recently had a left-hemisphere ischemic stroke. Which action should the nurse take to promote safe swallowing?

A) Place the food on the left side of the client's mouth.
B) Position the client in a 30-degree semi-Fowler's position.
C) Encourage the client to tilt their head backward when swallowing.
D) Offer thin liquids to help wash down solid food.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Place the food on the left side of the client's mouth.

**Explanation:** In a client with right-sided weakness (hemiparesis) from a stroke, placing food on the unaffected side (the left side) allows for better chewing control, decreases the risk of pocketing food, and significantly reduces the risk of aspiration. The client should be positioned upright at 90 degrees, not 30 degrees (Option B). The client should be encouraged to tuck their chin downward, rather than tilt the head backward, to protect the airway (Option C). Thin liquids are typically harder to control and increase the risk of aspiration for stroke clients, so thickened liquids are generally preferred (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 567
A 24-year-old client with a history of epilepsy suddenly loses consciousness and begins experiencing generalized tonic-clonic movements in the outpatient waiting room. What is the nurse's immediate priority action?

A) Insert a tongue depressor into the client's mouth to protect the airway.
B) Restrain the client's arms and legs to prevent joint dislocation.
C) Protect the client's head and ease them safely to the floor.
D) Administer the client's prescribed oral antiepileptic medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Protect the client's head and ease them safely to the floor.

**Explanation:** During an active seizure, the nurse's priority is client safety and preventing physical injury. Easing the client to the floor, protecting the head with a pad or pillow, and clearing away hazardous objects are the most appropriate immediate actions. The nurse should never insert anything into the mouth of a seizing client, as this can break teeth or block the airway (Option A). Restraining the client can cause fractures or muscle tears (Option B). Administering oral medications during an active seizure is contraindicated due to the high risk of aspiration (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 568
A nurse in a long-term care facility is walking with a 75-year-old client who has Parkinson's disease. The client suddenly stops and reports feeling like their feet are "glued to the floor." Which instruction should the nurse provide to assist the client?

A) "Look up at the ceiling and take a deep breath."
B) "Imagine stepping over a line on the floor."
C) "Pivot quickly on your heels to change direction."
D) "Lean backward slightly to shift your body weight."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "Imagine stepping over a line on the floor."

**Explanation:** "Freezing of gait" is a common symptom in Parkinson's disease where the client feels stuck. Providing a visual or cognitive cue, such as telling the client to imagine stepping over a line or an object, helps bypass the basal ganglia deficits and initiates movement. Looking at the ceiling alters balance and increases fall risk (Option A). Pivoting quickly or leaning backward are highly dangerous for a client with Parkinson's due to postural instability and high risk for falls (Options C and D).
</details>

---

### Question 569
A 16-year-old client has just returned to the medical-surgical unit following a lumbar puncture to rule out meningitis. Which action should the nurse include in the client's immediate plan of care?

A) Ambulate the client in the hallway to prevent deep vein thrombosis.
B) Elevate the head of the bed to a high-Fowler's position.
C) Restrict oral fluid intake for the next 4 to 6 hours.
D) Maintain the client in a flat, supine position.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Maintain the client in a flat, supine position.

**Explanation:** Following a lumbar puncture, the client should remain flat in a supine position (usually for 4 to 8 hours depending on facility protocol) to prevent cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leakage from the puncture site, which can cause a severe spinal headache. Ambulating or elevating the head of the bed prematurely increases the risk of a spinal headache (Options A and B). Unless contraindicated, oral fluids should actually be encouraged, not restricted, to help replace the removed CSF and maintain hydration (Option C).
</details>

---

### Question 570
A nurse in the emergency department is assessing a 45-year-old client who sustained a mild concussion in a minor motor vehicle collision. The client is currently resting with their eyes closed. What is the first action the nurse should take to assess the client's level of consciousness?

A) Apply firm pressure to the client's nail bed.
B) Call the client's name in a normal tone of voice.
C) Perform a sternal rub.
D) Shine a penlight into the client's eyes.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Call the client's name in a normal tone of voice.

**Explanation:** When assessing a client's level of consciousness, the nurse should always proceed from the least noxious (mildest) stimulus to the most noxious. The first step is to call the client's name in a normal voice. If they do not respond, the nurse would escalate to a loud voice, then gentle touch, and finally painful stimuli like a nail bed pressure or sternal rub (Options A and C) only if the client remains unresponsive. Shining a penlight into the eyes assesses cranial nerve function (pupillary response), but does not assess initial arousal or overall level of consciousness (Option D).
</details>
### Question 571
A nurse in a rehabilitation center is caring for an 82-year-old client who has expressive aphasia following a left-hemisphere stroke. Which action should the nurse take when communicating with this client?

A) Speak loudly and clearly into the client's right ear.
B) Ask open-ended questions to encourage the client to practice speaking.
C) Provide a picture board for the client to point to daily needs.
D) Finish the client's sentences to reduce their frustration.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C

**Rationale:** Expressive aphasia (Broca's aphasia) impairs the client's ability to produce spoken language, even though their comprehension is often intact. Using alternative forms of communication, such as a picture board, allows the client to effectively express their needs. Speaking loudly is unnecessary unless the client has a hearing impairment. Open-ended questions can cause severe frustration; instead, nurses should use yes/no questions or simple choices. The nurse should allow the client ample time to respond and avoid finishing their sentences for them, as this can feel demeaning and increase frustration.
</details>

---

### Question 572
A school nurse is observing a 12-year-old student who suddenly falls to the floor and begins experiencing a generalized tonic-clonic seizure. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Clear the immediate area of any hard or sharp objects.
B) Insert a padded tongue blade into the student's mouth.
C) Restrain the student's arms to prevent joint dislocation.
D) Administer oral antiepileptic medication immediately.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** The primary goal during an active seizure is to protect the client from injury. Clearing the area of hard or sharp objects ensures the client does not strike themselves against something dangerous during the uncontrolled, convulsive movements. Inserting anything into the mouth during a seizure is strictly contraindicated as it can cause broken teeth or airway obstruction. Restraining the client can lead to muscle tears or bone fractures. Oral medications should never be administered to an actively seizing patient due to a high risk of aspiration.
</details>

---

### Question 573
A nurse at a long-term care facility is observing a 74-year-old client with Parkinson's disease walking down the hallway. The client suddenly experiences "freezing" of gait and stops moving. Which intervention should the nurse implement?

A) Push the client gently from behind to help them regain momentum.
B) Have the client look up at the ceiling and take a deep breath.
C) Offer the client a wheelchair and instruct them not to walk unassisted.
D) Instruct the client to imagine stepping over a line on the floor.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D

**Rationale:** Clients with Parkinson's disease frequently experience "freezing" episodes, where they feel as though their feet are glued to the floor. Instructing the client to imagine stepping over a line, focusing on a target on the ground, or dropping a visual cue (like a tissue) helps bypass the basal ganglia block in the brain and re-initiate movement. Pushing the client significantly increases the risk of a fall. Looking at the ceiling alters the center of gravity and impairs balance. Confining the client to a wheelchair prematurely discourages independence and promotes physical decline.
</details>

---

### Question 574
A nurse in the emergency department is discharging a 22-year-old client who sustained a mild concussion while playing soccer. The nurse is providing discharge instructions to the client's roommate. Which observation should the nurse instruct the roommate to report to the healthcare provider immediately?

A) The client complains of a mild, dull headache.
B) The client is difficult to awaken from sleep.
C) The client states they feel fatigued and want to rest.
D) The client has a small, superficial abrasion on their forehead.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B

**Rationale:** Difficulty awakening or a significant decrease in the level of consciousness is a critical warning sign of increasing intracranial pressure and potential intracranial bleeding. This is a medical emergency and must be reported immediately. A mild headache, general fatigue, and superficial abrasions are expected findings following a mild concussion and can be managed with rest and non-narcotic analgesics as prescribed.
</details>

---

### Question 575
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is receiving a 45-year-old client who just returned from having a lumbar puncture to rule out meningitis. Which nursing action is most appropriate for this client immediately following the procedure?

A) Maintain the client in a flat, supine position for several hours.
B) Encourage the client to ambulate in the hallway to prevent deep vein thrombosis.
C) Restrict the client's fluid intake to reduce cerebrospinal fluid production.
D) Elevate the head of the bed to 45 degrees to promote comfort.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A

**Rationale:** After a lumbar puncture, the client should remain flat (supine) for 4 to 8 hours, depending on facility protocol and provider orders. This minimizes the risk of cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) leaking from the puncture site, which is the primary cause of severe post-lumbar puncture spinal headaches. Ambulating or elevating the head of the bed too soon significantly increases the risk of developing a spinal headache. Fluids should be encouraged, not restricted, to help replenish the removed CSF.
</details>
### Question 576
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is instructing a 25-year-old female client on how to collect a urine specimen for a suspected urinary tract infection (UTI). Which instruction is most important for the nurse to provide to ensure an accurate sample?

A) "Collect the first urine you pass immediately upon waking up."
B) "Begin urinating into the toilet, then pass the cup into the urine stream."
C) "Fill the specimen cup to the very top to ensure an adequate sample."
D) "Wipe the perineal area from back to front with the provided cleansing wipe."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: B**

**Rationale:** To collect a clean-catch midstream urine specimen, the client should start urinating into the toilet to flush out any contaminants from the urethra, and then catch the "midstream" urine in the cup. This provides the most accurate sample for diagnosing a UTI. 
*Option A* is incorrect because while a first-morning void is highly concentrated and useful for certain tests (like pregnancy tests), a clean-catch midstream sample can be collected at any time for a UTI culture. 
*Option C* is incorrect because the cup only needs to be about half full to provide an adequate sample for the lab; overfilling increases the risk of spilling and contamination. 
*Option D* is incorrect because women should always wipe from front to back to avoid introducing bacteria from the rectal area into the urinary meatus.
</details>

---

### Question 577
An emergency department nurse is caring for a 55-year-old male client with end-stage renal disease who has a newly created arteriovenous (AV) fistula in his left arm. During the initial assessment, which action should the nurse take to verify the patency of the fistula?

A) Obtain the client's blood pressure using the left arm.
B) Check the capillary refill on the fingers of the right hand.
C) Palpate gently over the fistula site for a buzzing sensation.
D) Draw a blood sample directly from the fistula.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: C**

**Rationale:** To assess the patency of an AV fistula, the nurse should palpate over the site to feel a "thrill" (a buzzing or vibrating sensation) and auscultate over the site with a stethoscope to hear a "bruit" (a swooshing sound). This indicates proper, turbulent blood flow through the fistula. 
*Option A* is incorrect because blood pressure should never be taken on an arm with an AV fistula, as the pressure from the cuff can damage the graft or cause clotting. 
*Option B* is incorrect because checking capillary refill on the *right* hand does not assess the blood flow in the left arm where the fistula is located. 
*Option D* is incorrect because routine blood draws should not be taken from an AV fistula by a bedside nurse; it is reserved exclusively for dialysis access.
</details>

---

### Question 578
A 42-year-old male client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with severe right flank pain and nausea. The provider suspects right-sided renal calculi (kidney stones). Which nursing intervention is the highest priority when assisting this client with urination?

A) Straining all of the client's urine through a gauze or filter.
B) Restricting oral fluid intake to reduce workload on the kidneys.
C) Inserting a straight catheter to empty the bladder completely.
D) Measuring the specific gravity of every voided sample.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: A**

**Rationale:** The highest priority intervention for a client with suspected kidney stones is to strain all urine. This allows the nurse to catch any small stones or gravel that the client passes, which can then be sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine their composition (e.g., calcium oxalate, uric acid) and guide future dietary and medical treatment. 
*Option B* is incorrect because fluids should be encouraged (unless contraindicated) to help flush the stone out of the urinary tract. 
*Option C* is incorrect because inserting a catheter is unnecessary and introduces an infection risk; the client can void normally unless there is a complete obstruction causing retention. 
*Option D* is incorrect because measuring specific gravity does not aid in capturing the stone for analysis, which is the priority in this scenario.
</details>

---

### Question 579
A nurse is assessing a 68-year-old male client in a primary care clinic. The client reports frequent urination, a weak stream, and the feeling that his bladder is not completely empty after voiding. The nurse suspects benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Based on this clinical picture, which physical assessment finding would the nurse most likely observe?

A) Costovertebral angle (CVA) tenderness upon gentle percussion
B) Severe pitting edema in both lower extremities
C) Hyperactive, high-pitched bowel sounds in the lower quadrants
D) A palpable, distended mass above the symphysis pubis

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: D**

**Rationale:** BPH causes an enlarged prostate gland that obstructs the outflow of urine from the bladder, leading to urinary retention. If the bladder is retaining a significant amount of urine, the nurse would likely be able to palpate a distended bladder, which presents as a firm, rounded mass just above the symphysis pubis. 
*Option A* is incorrect because CVA tenderness is a classic sign of pyelonephritis (kidney infection), not BPH. 
*Option B* is incorrect because bilateral lower extremity edema is typically associated with heart failure or systemic fluid volume overload, not localized urinary retention from BPH. 
*Option C* is incorrect because bowel sounds assess gastrointestinal function and are unrelated to the genitourinary symptoms of BPH.
</details>

---

### Question 580
A nurse in a long-term care facility is observing an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) provide perineal care to an 81-year-old female client who experiences chronic urinary incontinence. Which action by the UAP requires immediate intervention by the nurse?

A) Washing the perineal area starting from the back toward the front.
B) Drying the perineum thoroughly by gently patting the skin with a towel.
C) Using warm water and a mild, pH-balanced cleanser on the area.
D) Applying a designated moisture barrier cream to the intact perineal skin.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: A**

**Rationale:** The nurse must intervene immediately because washing the perineal area from back to front can drag bacteria (like *E. coli*) from the anal region to the urethra, significantly increasing the risk of a urinary tract infection (UTI). Female clients should always be wiped and washed from front to back. 
*Option B* is a correct action; patting the skin dry prevents friction damage to fragile older adult skin. 
*Option C* is a correct action; using warm water and a mild, pH-balanced cleanser prevents skin irritation and breakdown. 
*Option D* is a correct action; applying a moisture barrier cream protects intact skin from the macerating effects of urinary incontinence.
</details>
### Question 581
A nurse in a community clinic is providing discharge teaching to a 24-year-old female client recently treated for an uncomplicated urinary tract infection (UTI). Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the nurse's instructions to prevent future infections?

A) "I should limit my water intake so I don't have to use public restrooms."  
B) "I will wipe from back to front after having a bowel movement."  
C) "I should empty my bladder completely as soon as I feel the urge to void."  
D) "Taking bubble baths instead of showers will help keep the area clean."  

<details>
<summary>Correct Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "I should empty my bladder completely as soon as I feel the urge to void."

**Rationale:** Emptying the bladder fully and regularly prevents urinary stasis, which is a major risk factor for bacterial growth and UTIs. The client should wipe from front to back to prevent introducing rectal bacteria into the urethra. Fluid intake should be increased, not limited, to help flush out bacteria. Showers are preferred over bubble baths, as bubble baths can irritate the urethra and introduce bacteria.
</details>

---

### Question 582
An 82-year-old male resident in a long-term care facility has a history of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). The nurse observes the resident pacing the hallway and rubbing his lower abdomen, stating he has not urinated since the early morning. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Palpate the resident's suprapubic area for distention.  
B) Administer the prescribed PRN pain medication for abdominal cramping.  
C) Provide the resident with a large glass of cranberry juice.  
D) Prepare to insert a routine indwelling Foley catheter.  

<details>
<summary>Correct Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Palpate the resident's suprapubic area for distention.

**Rationale:** The client is exhibiting signs of acute urinary retention, a common complication of BPH. The nurse should first assess the bladder (via palpation or bladder scanner) to confirm urinary retention before taking further action. Administering pain medication or fluids does not resolve the immediate physical blockage, and inserting a catheter is an invasive procedure that requires a specific provider order after an assessment confirms retention.
</details>

---

### Question 583
A 45-year-old male arrives at the emergency department complaining of severe right flank pain that radiates to his groin. The healthcare provider suspects a renal calculus (kidney stone). Which intervention should the nurse implement while caring for this client?

A) Restrict oral fluid intake to reduce pressure on the kidneys.  
B) Place the client in a strict supine position with legs elevated.  
C) Administer antibiotics immediately before collecting a urine sample.  
D) Strain all of the client's voided urine through a mesh filter.  

<details>
<summary>Correct Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Strain all of the client's voided urine through a mesh filter.

**Rationale:** When a client is suspected of having a kidney stone, the nurse must strain all urine to catch any stones or fragments that are passed. These fragments are sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine their composition, which guides future dietary and medical treatment. Fluids should be encouraged, not restricted, to help flush the stone out. The client does not need to be kept strictly supine; ambulation can actually help pass the stone. Antibiotics are not the priority unless an active infection is confirmed, and cultures should always be drawn before initiating antibiotics.
</details>

---

### Question 584
A 68-year-old female client with stage 4 chronic kidney disease (CKD) is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with shortness of breath and dependent edema. The nurse is assigning tasks to the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP). Which instruction is the most important for the nurse to give the UAP regarding this client's daily care?

A) "Encourage the client to drink at least three liters of water today."  
B) "Weigh the client every morning using the same scale before breakfast."  
C) "Provide the client with extra packets of salt for her meals to prevent hyponatremia."  
D) "Assist the client to ambulate in the hallway every hour to reduce leg swelling."  

<details>
<summary>Correct Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "Weigh the client every morning using the same scale before breakfast."

**Rationale:** Daily weights are the most reliable indicator of fluid volume status in clients with chronic kidney disease and fluid retention. The UAP should weigh the client at the same time each day, using the same scale, and in similar clothing. Clients with CKD who have edema typically require fluid and sodium restrictions, so encouraging large amounts of water or extra salt would worsen their condition.
</details>

---

### Question 585
A 50-year-old client is given an empty collection jug by the nurse and instructed on how to complete a 24-hour urine collection at home to assess kidney function. Which action described by the client indicates that the teaching was successful?

A) "I will discard the first morning urine, note the time, and then save all urine for the next 24 hours."  
B) "I will collect my first morning urine to start the test and keep the jug at room temperature."  
C) "If I accidentally flush one void, I can just add an extra hour to the end of the collection time."  
D) "I should collect only the midstream portion of my urine each time I use the restroom."  

<details>
<summary>Correct Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) "I will discard the first morning urine, note the time, and then save all urine for the next 24 hours."

**Rationale:** A 24-hour urine collection begins by discarding the first voided specimen so that the bladder is completely empty at the start time. The exact time is noted, and all subsequent urine for the next 24 hours is saved. The collection container should typically be kept on ice or refrigerated, not left at room temperature. If any urine is accidentally discarded during the 24-hour period, the entire test is invalid and must be restarted. Midstream collection is a technique used for a clean-catch urine culture, not a 24-hour volume test.
</details>
### Question 586
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is teaching a 28-year-old female client who experiences frequent urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which action by the client indicates a correct understanding of the preventive measures taught by the nurse?

A) Taking a daily bubble bath to maintain perineal hygiene.  
B) Holding urine for as long as possible to strengthen the bladder.  
C) Wiping from front to back after using the toilet.  
D) Decreasing fluid intake to reduce the frequency of urination.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Wiping from front to back prevents the introduction of gastrointestinal bacteria, such as E. coli, from the anal area into the urethra, which is the most common cause of UTIs in women. Bubble baths (Option A) should be avoided as they can irritate the urethra and promote infection. Holding urine (Option B) allows bacteria to multiply in the bladder. Fluid intake should be increased, not decreased (Option D), to help flush bacteria out of the urinary tract.
</details>

---

### Question 587
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 55-year-old client who has an indwelling urinary catheter placed following pelvic surgery. As the nurse assists the client from the bed into a chair, which action is most important to ensure proper catheter function and prevent complications?

A) Clamping the catheter tubing while the client is sitting in the chair.  
B) Disconnecting the drainage bag temporarily to untangle the tubing.  
C) Placing the urinary drainage bag on the floor next to the chair.  
D) Securing the drainage bag to the chair frame below the level of the client's bladder.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
To prevent catheter-associated urinary tract infections (CAUTIs), the urinary drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder to prevent the backflow of contaminated urine. The bag should never touch the floor (Option C) due to the risk of bacterial contamination. The tubing should never be routinely clamped (Option A) or disconnected (Option B), as this introduces bacteria into the closed system and can cause urinary retention or infection.
</details>

---

### Question 588
A nurse in a long-term care facility is admitting a 78-year-old male client with a history of untreated benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). During the initial assessment, which observation by the nurse directly correlates with this condition?

A) The client takes several minutes to initiate a stream of urine.  
B) The client voids large amounts of clear, odorless urine.  
C) The client reports severe flank pain radiating to the groin.  
D) The client exhibits swelling in the face and hands in the morning.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) is an enlargement of the prostate gland that compresses the urethra. This commonly results in urinary hesitancy (difficulty starting a stream), a weak stream, dribbling, and incomplete bladder emptying. Severe flank pain (Option C) is indicative of kidney stones. Facial swelling (Option D) is associated with kidney failure or nephrotic syndrome, and voiding large amounts of urine (Option B) is not a symptom of the obstructive nature of BPH.
</details>

---

### Question 589
A 35-year-old client in an outpatient testing center is given instructions by the nurse on how to perform a 24-hour urine collection for creatinine clearance. Which statement by the client indicates that the nurse's teaching has been effective?

A) "I should collect my first morning urine and then start the 24-hour timer."  
B) "I will discard my first voided urine in the morning and then collect all urine for the next 24 hours."  
C) "I will keep the urine collection container at room temperature on my bathroom counter."  
D) "If I accidentally flush one void, I can just add an extra hour to the collection time."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
A 24-hour urine collection must begin with an empty bladder. The client should discard the first void, note the exact time, and then collect all subsequent urine for exactly 24 hours. The collection container should be kept on ice or in the refrigerator, not at room temperature (Option C). If any urine is accidentally discarded or flushed (Option D), the entire test is invalidated and must be restarted from the beginning.
</details>

---

### Question 590
A nurse is caring for a 68-year-old client with stage 4 chronic kidney disease (CKD) who is on a strict fluid restriction. When evaluating the client's fluid status during morning rounds, which assessment finding should the nurse prioritize as a sign of worsening fluid overload?

A) A reported weight loss of 1 pound since yesterday.  
B) Dry mucous membranes and increased thirst.  
C) The client requesting an extra cup of ice chips.  
D) A weight gain of 3 pounds over the past 24 hours.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Daily weights are the most reliable indicator of fluid status in clients with chronic kidney disease. A rapid weight gain of 3 pounds in 24 hours indicates significant fluid retention (1 kg of weight equals roughly 1 liter of fluid), which can lead to life-threatening complications like pulmonary edema. Weight loss (Option A) and dry mucous membranes (Option B) indicate fluid loss or mild dehydration, not fluid overload. Requesting ice chips (Option C) is common for clients on fluid restrictions but does not objectively indicate overload.
</details>
### Question 591
A nurse in a long-term care facility is caring for an 84-year-old client who has suddenly become confused, agitated, and is experiencing new-onset urinary incontinence. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Administer a prescribed PRN sedative to calm the client.
B) Obtain a urine sample for a prescribed urinalysis and culture.
C) Restrict the client's oral fluid intake to prevent further incontinence.
D) Apply a continuous electronic bed alarm to prevent wandering.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
In elderly clients, a sudden change in mental status, such as new-onset confusion or agitation, is a classic and early clinical manifestation of a urinary tract infection (UTI). Obtaining a urine sample is the priority nursing action to help the healthcare provider identify the underlying physiological cause before masking symptoms with a sedative. 
</details>

---

### Question 592
A 38-year-old male client is admitted to the emergency department with sharp, severe right-sided flank pain that radiates to the groin. The healthcare provider suspects nephrolithiasis. Which intervention should the nurse include in the client's immediate plan of care?

A) Maintain the client on strict bed rest until the pain subsides.
B) Limit the client's oral fluid intake to 1,000 mL per day.
C) Strain all of the client's voided urine through a gauze or filter.
D) Apply a cold compress to the client's right flank area.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
For a client with suspected kidney stones (nephrolithiasis), it is essential to strain all voided urine. This allows the nurse to catch any passed stones or fragments, which are then sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine the stone's composition and guide future dietary or medical treatment. 
</details>

---

### Question 593
A nurse is assisting a 52-year-old female client who is one day postoperative following an abdominal hysterectomy. The client has an indwelling urinary catheter in place and is preparing to ambulate in the hallway for the first time. How should the nurse manage the catheter drainage bag during ambulation?

A) Carry the drainage bag below the level of the client's bladder.
B) Clamp the catheter tubing to prevent urine leakage while walking.
C) Instruct the client to hold the drainage bag securely at waist height.
D) Disconnect the drainage bag and place a sterile cap on the tubing.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
To prevent the backflow of urine into the bladder and reduce the risk of a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI), gravity must be maintained. The drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the client's bladder, including during ambulation and transfers. Tubing should never be routinely clamped or disconnected.
</details>

---

### Question 594
A 68-year-old male client visits the urology clinic reporting a weak urinary stream, difficulty starting urination, and waking up several times at night to void. Which instruction should the nurse provide to help manage these symptoms?

A) "You should decrease your intake of high-fiber foods to reduce pelvic pressure."
B) "Try to drink a large glass of water right before going to bed."
C) "Ignore the urge to void for as long as possible to help stretch your bladder."
D) "Avoid drinking caffeine and alcohol, especially in the evening."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
This client is exhibiting classic symptoms of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Caffeine and alcohol act as mild diuretics and bladder irritants, which can worsen urinary frequency and urgency. Avoiding them, particularly in the evening, helps decrease nocturia and improves sleep quality.
</details>

---

### Question 595
A 45-year-old female client tells the clinic nurse that she frequently leaks small amounts of urine whenever she sneezes, coughs, or laughs heavily. Which initial intervention should the nurse teach the client to help manage this condition?

A) Performing intermittent self-catheterization twice a day.
B) Performing pelvic floor muscle exercises daily.
C) Taking an over-the-counter diuretic every morning.
D) Limiting total daily fluid intake to 800 mL.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Leaking urine during physical exertion, coughing, or sneezing is the definition of stress incontinence. The first-line, non-invasive intervention for stress incontinence is performing pelvic floor muscle (Kegel) exercises to strengthen the muscles that control and support the urethra and bladder.
</details>
### Question 596
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for an 80-year-old female patient who has an indwelling urinary catheter. When repositioning the patient in bed, which action by the nurse is essential to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A. Routine changing of the catheter every 48 hours.
B. Ensuring the drainage bag remains below the level of the patient's bladder.
C. Resting the drainage bag on the floor to prevent tension on the tubing.
D. Flushing the catheter daily with sterile saline.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
Keeping the drainage bag below the level of the bladder prevents the backflow of stagnant urine into the bladder, which is a major risk factor for developing a CAUTI. Placing the bag directly on the floor increases the risk of bacterial contamination. Routine changing of the catheter and daily flushing break the sterile seal and increase the risk of infection; these are not evidence-based practices for CAUTI prevention.
</details>

---

### Question 597
A 68-year-old male patient arrives at the primary care clinic reporting recent changes in his urinary habits. Which observation or statement by the patient should the nurse recognize as a classic early sign of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH)?

A. "My urine has been very cloudy and smells foul."
B. "I have severe, stabbing pain in my lower back."
C. "I have to push really hard to get my urine stream to start."
D. "I am leaking a large amount of urine when I sneeze."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Difficulty initiating a urine stream (hesitancy), a weak stream, and incomplete bladder emptying are classic signs of BPH due to the enlarged prostate compressing the urethra. Foul-smelling and cloudy urine suggests a urinary tract infection (UTI). Severe back pain is more indicative of pyelonephritis or renal calculi. Leaking urine when sneezing is a symptom of stress incontinence, which is less common in uncomplicated BPH.
</details>

---

### Question 598
A 42-year-old female patient is admitted to the emergency department with sharp, severe right flank pain and hematuria. The healthcare provider suspects a kidney stone. Which nursing action should the nurse implement immediately?

A. Filter all of the patient's urine through a strainer.
B. Restrict the patient's oral fluid intake to 500 mL per day.
C. Prepare the patient for an emergency hemodialysis session.
D. Insert a three-way urinary catheter for continuous bladder irrigation.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
For a patient suspected of having a kidney stone (urolithiasis), the nurse must strain all urine to catch any passed stones or fragments. These fragments are sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine their composition, which guides future treatment and dietary teaching. Fluids should be encouraged to help flush the stone, not restricted. Dialysis and continuous bladder irrigation are not indicated interventions for a standard kidney stone passage.
</details>

---

### Question 599
A home health nurse is instructing a 55-year-old male patient on how to perform a 24-hour urine collection to assess creatinine clearance. Which instruction must the nurse include to ensure accurate results?

A. "Keep the collection container in a warm, dry place during the test."
B. "If you accidentally forget to collect a void, just add an extra hour to the test."
C. "Save your first morning urine and use it to start the 24-hour collection period."
D. "Discard your very first morning urine, and then collect all urine for the next 24 hours."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
To begin a 24-hour urine collection, the patient should void and discard that first urine so the bladder is completely empty at the precise start time. All subsequent urine is then collected for exactly 24 hours. The container must typically be kept on ice or refrigerated to prevent bacterial growth and degradation of the sample. If a void is accidentally flushed or missed, the entire test is invalidated and must be restarted.
</details>

---

### Question 600
A nurse in an outpatient dialysis center is reviewing the 24-hour dietary log of a 62-year-old female patient with end-stage renal disease (ESRD). Which meal choice by the patient indicates to the nurse that further dietary teaching is needed?

A. Grilled chicken breast with white rice and green beans.
B. Scrambled eggs with a slice of white toast and apple juice.
C. Baked potato loaded with cheese and a side of sliced bananas.
D. Pasta salad with cucumbers, bell peppers, and an olive oil dressing.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Patients with ESRD must restrict their intake of potassium, phosphorus, and sodium, as their kidneys can no longer effectively filter these out of the blood. Potatoes, bananas, and dairy products (like cheese) are extremely high in potassium (and cheese is also high in phosphorus and sodium). Choosing this meal indicates a need for further teaching. The other options are lower in potassium and phosphorus and represent more appropriate choices for a renal diet.
</details>
### Question 601
A 25-year-old female is on post-operative day one following an open appendectomy and currently has an indwelling urinary catheter in place. The nurse is assisting the patient out of bed to a chair for the first time. Which action by the nurse is appropriate regarding the catheter?

A) Place the drainage bag on the bed next to the patient to prevent tension.
B) Keep the drainage bag below the level of the patient's bladder at all times.
C) Clamp the catheter tubing while the patient is ambulating to the chair.
D) Empty the drainage bag only when it is completely full to maintain a closed system.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> B) Keep the drainage bag below the level of the patient's bladder at all times.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> To prevent catheter-associated urinary tract infections (CAUTIs) and the backflow of urine into the bladder, the drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the patient's bladder. Placing the bag on the bed (above the bladder) or clamping the tubing can cause urine reflux, increasing the risk of infection. The bag should be emptied regularly (typically when half to two-thirds full), not just when it is completely full.
</details>

---

### Question 602
A 42-year-old male is admitted to the Emergency Department complaining of severe, colicky left flank pain radiating to his groin. The healthcare provider suspects renal calculi (kidney stones). Which specific action should the nurse implement when managing this patient's elimination needs?

A) Insert a straight urinary catheter immediately to empty the bladder.
B) Restrict the patient's oral fluid intake to decrease urine production.
C) Strain all of the patient's urine through a gauze or a specialized strainer.
D) Measure the specific gravity of every void using a dipstick.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C) Strain all of the patient's urine through a gauze or a specialized strainer.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> When a patient is suspected of having kidney stones, the nurse must strain all urine. This ensures that any passed stones or gravel are caught and can be sent to the laboratory for analysis, which guides future treatment and dietary recommendations. Fluid intake should generally be encouraged, not restricted, to help flush the stone out. Catheterization is not indicated merely for passing a stone, and measuring specific gravity for every void is unnecessary for this specific condition.
</details>

---

### Question 603
A 50-year-old female in an outpatient clinic is given supplies to conduct a 24-hour urine collection at home to assess creatinine clearance. The nurse is teaching the patient how to properly perform the collection. Which instruction by the nurse is correct?

A) "Discard your very first morning urine and begin the 24-hour timing from that moment."
B) "Store the urine collection container at room temperature on your bathroom counter."
C) "Start the 24-hour timing by saving your very first morning void in the large container."
D) "Stop the collection and bring in what you have if you accidentally flush one void."

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> A) "Discard your very first morning urine and begin the 24-hour timing from that moment."<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A 24-hour urine collection begins with an empty bladder. The patient should be instructed to void, discard that first specimen, and note the time. All subsequent urine for the next 24 hours is saved. The collection container should be kept cool (usually on ice or refrigerated). If a void is accidentally missed or flushed, the entire test is invalid and must be restarted.
</details>

---

### Question 604
A 78-year-old female resident in an assisted living facility is exhibiting new-onset confusion and foul-smelling urine. The healthcare provider orders a clean-catch midstream urine specimen to test for a urinary tract infection. Which action by the nurse ensures proper technique for this procedure?

A) The nurse collects the very first drops of the patient's urine into the sterile cup.
B) The nurse touches the inside of the specimen cup with a sterile swab to remove debris.
C) The nurse obtains the specimen from the bottom of a previously used, clean bedpan.
D) The nurse instructs the patient to clean the perineum from front to back before voiding.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> D) The nurse instructs the patient to clean the perineum from front to back before voiding.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> A clean-catch midstream specimen requires cleaning the urethral meatus beforehand to reduce contamination by normal flora. In females, cleansing must be done from front to back to prevent introducing intestinal bacteria (like E. coli) into the specimen or urethra. The patient should initiate the stream, stop, and then void into the cup (midstream), rather than collecting the first drops. The inside of the cup must remain completely sterile, and urine cannot be collected from a bedpan for a clean-catch sterile culture.
</details>

---

### Question 605
A 68-year-old male patient on a medical-surgical unit has a history of Benign Prostatic Hyperplasia (BPH). He reports feeling a strong urge to urinate but is only passing small dribbles of urine. The nurse observes that he appears restless and complains of fullness in his lower abdomen. Which initial action should the nurse take?

A) Administer a PRN oral diuretic to increase the patient's urine production.
B) Encourage the patient to bear down forcefully while attempting to urinate.
C) Palpate the suprapubic area and perform an ultrasound bladder scan.
D) Prepare the patient for an immediate emergency hemodialysis session.

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer:</strong> C) Palpate the suprapubic area and perform an ultrasound bladder scan.<br><br>
<strong>Rationale:</strong> The patient is exhibiting classic signs of acute urinary retention (strong urge to void, dribbling, lower abdominal fullness, restlessness), which is a common complication of BPH. The nurse should first assess the bladder by palpating the suprapubic area for distention and using a non-invasive bladder scanner to measure the volume of retained urine. Administering a diuretic would worsen the retention and pain. Bearing down forcefully can cause injury and will not bypass the prostatic obstruction. Hemodialysis is a treatment for renal failure, not urinary retention.
</details>
### Question 606
A nurse in a long-term care facility is observing an 82-year-old client who has a history of Alzheimer's disease. Which new clinical finding should prompt the nurse to suspect that the client is developing a urinary tract infection (UTI)?

A) A sudden, acute increase in confusion and agitation
B) A new onset of a dry, hacking non-productive cough
C) A resting heart rate that drops to 55 beats per minute
D) A complaint of severe, radiating upper quadrant pain

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) A sudden, acute increase in confusion and agitation

**Rationale:** In older adults, especially those with underlying cognitive deficits like dementia or Alzheimer's disease, the classic symptoms of a UTI (such as dysuria or frequency) are often absent. Instead, an acute change in mental status, increased confusion, agitation, or sudden lethargy is frequently the first and most prominent sign of a UTI. A cough is related to respiratory issues, bradycardia is not a typical UTI sign (tachycardia would be more common with infection), and upper quadrant pain is more associated with gallbladder or liver issues.
</details>

---

### Question 607
A nurse is providing care for a 54-year-old client who has an indwelling urinary catheter following abdominal surgery. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A) Emptying the drainage bag only when it is completely full to the top
B) Securing the catheter tubing to the client's upper thigh with a stat-lock device
C) Irrigating the catheter tubing daily with 30 mL of sterile normal saline
D) Resting the urinary drainage bag on the client's bed during transport to radiology

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) Securing the catheter tubing to the client's upper thigh with a stat-lock device

**Rationale:** Securing the catheter to the client's leg prevents pulling, friction, and movement of the catheter, which can irritate the meatus and introduce bacteria into the urinary tract. The drainage bag should be emptied when it is half to two-thirds full, not completely full, to prevent tension and reflux. Routine irrigation is not recommended as it breaks the closed sterile system and increases infection risk. The drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder to prevent the backflow of urine; placing it on the bed violates this rule.
</details>

---

### Question 608
An outpatient clinic nurse is instructing a 40-year-old client on how to perform a 24-hour urine collection to evaluate kidney function. Which instruction must the nurse include in the teaching?

A) "Collect your first morning void in the container and then start the 24-hour timer."
B) "Keep the urine collection container at room temperature during the entire test."
C) "Discard your first morning void, note the exact time, and save all urine after that."
D) "If you accidentally miss catching one void, just extend the collection time by an hour."

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "Discard your first morning void, note the exact time, and save all urine after that."

**Rationale:** To perform a 24-hour urine collection accurately, the bladder must begin empty. The client is instructed to void, flush that first specimen down the toilet, and record that exact time as the start of the 24-hour period. All subsequent urine must be collected. The container must be kept cool (on ice or in a refrigerator) to prevent bacterial overgrowth and degradation of the specimen. If a single void is missed, the entire test is invalidated and must be restarted from the beginning.
</details>

---

### Question 609
A nurse is caring for a 71-year-old client in the post-anesthesia care unit (PACU) who is receiving continuous bladder irrigation (CBI) following a transurethral resection of the prostate (TURP). The nurse observes that the drainage in the catheter bag has just changed to bright red with several large, thick clots. What is the nurse's immediate action?

A) Stop the bladder irrigation completely and notify the provider.
B) Instruct the client to perform Kegel exercises to clear the clots.
C) Administer the client's prescribed PRN antispasmodic medication.
D) Increase the flow rate of the continuous irrigation solution.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Increase the flow rate of the continuous irrigation solution.

**Rationale:** The primary purpose of continuous bladder irrigation (CBI) following a TURP is to prevent the formation of blood clots that can obstruct the urethra. The nurse should titrate the rate of the irrigation fluid based on the color of the drainage. If the drainage becomes bright red or contains clots, the nurse should immediately increase the flow rate until the drainage returns to a light pink or clear color. Stopping the irrigation would allow more clots to form and block the catheter.
</details>

---

### Question 610
A pediatric nurse is assessing a 7-year-old child who recently recovered from a streptococcal throat infection. The parent reports that the child's face looks puffy and their urine looks dark, "like cola." Based on these observations, which clinical assessment should the nurse prioritize to check for acute glomerulonephritis?

A) Check the child's bilateral pedal pulses.
B) Assess the child's pupillary light reflex.
C) Measure the child's blood pressure.
D) Palpate the right lower quadrant for rebound tenderness.

<details>
<summary><strong>Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Measure the child's blood pressure.

**Rationale:** The child is exhibiting classic signs of acute post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis (APSGN), which include periorbital edema (puffy face) and hematuria (cola-colored urine). Because the kidneys are inflamed and retaining fluid and sodium, hypertension is a very common and potentially dangerous complication of this condition. Therefore, measuring the child's blood pressure is a critical priority assessment. Pedal pulses, pupillary reflexes, and right lower quadrant tenderness do not directly assess for the complications of glomerulonephritis.
</details>
### Question 611
A nurse is caring for an 80-year-old client in a long-term care facility. The unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) reports that the client, who is usually alert and cooperative, has become suddenly agitated and confused this morning. Which additional clinical finding should the nurse look for during the assessment to identify the most likely cause of this acute change?

A) A significant drop in resting heart rate
B) Dark, cloudy, or foul-smelling urine
C) A sudden elevation in blood glucose levels
D) A new-onset productive cough with clear sputum

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** In elderly clients, atypical presentations of urinary tract infections (UTIs) are extremely common. A sudden change in mental status, such as new-onset confusion, agitation, or lethargy, is often the first and sometimes only clinical sign of a UTI in this age group. The nurse should immediately assess the client's urine characteristics (such as foul odor or cloudiness) and vital signs. A drop in heart rate, elevated blood glucose, or clear sputum are not the primary expected findings linked to sudden confusion caused by a typical geriatric UTI.
</details>

---

### Question 612
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is preparing to transport a 45-year-old client with an indwelling urinary catheter to the radiology department for an x-ray. Which action by the nurse is correct to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A) Placing the urine drainage bag on the client's abdomen during transport
B) Clamping the catheter tubing securely for the duration of the procedure
C) Securing the drainage bag below the level of the client's bladder
D) Deflating the catheter balloon slightly before moving the client to the wheelchair

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** To prevent a CAUTI and urinary reflux, the drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the client's bladder, including during transport. Placing the bag on the abdomen or raising it above the bladder allows contaminated urine to flow back into the urinary tract. Clamping the tubing can cause urinary stasis and bladder distension, increasing the risk of infection. Deflating the balloon would cause the catheter to become dislodged or fall out entirely.
</details>

---

### Question 613
A 28-year-old client is admitted to the emergency department with severe right flank pain, nausea, and suspected nephrolithiasis (kidney stones). Which immediate nursing action should be implemented regarding the client's urinary output?

A) Strain all of the client's voided urine through a filter or gauze.
B) Restrict the client's oral fluid intake to 500 mL per day.
C) Insert an indwelling urinary catheter to monitor output hourly.
D) Collect a sterile midstream urine specimen for a 24-hour culture.

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** For a client with suspected nephrolithiasis, it is a priority nursing action to strain all urine to catch any passed stones or gravel. These captured fragments are sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine their composition, which guides long-term prevention and dietary treatment. Fluid restriction is contraindicated; clients are actually encouraged to drink plenty of fluids to flush the stone out. An indwelling catheter is not routinely indicated for kidney stones and unnecessarily increases infection risk. A 24-hour culture is not a standard immediate action for uncomplicated nephrolithiasis.
</details>

---

### Question 614
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a 35-year-old female client who needs to perform a 24-hour urine collection at home. Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching was successful?

A) "I will keep the urine collection container at room temperature on my bathroom counter."
B) "I will save the very first urine I pass in the morning to start the 24-hour test."
C) "If I accidentally flush one void, I can just extend the test by a few hours."
D) "I will flush my first morning urine and then save all other urine for the next 24 hours."

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** A 24-hour urine collection always begins after the first morning void is discarded (flushed). The start time is noted, and all subsequent urine produced for the next 24 hours must be saved in the designated container. The collection container should typically be kept on ice or in a refrigerator to prevent bacterial overgrowth, not left at room temperature. If any urine is accidentally discarded during the 24-hour collection window, the test becomes invalid and must be completely restarted from the beginning.
</details>

---

### Question 615
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is interviewing a 68-year-old male client who reports waking up multiple times during the night to urinate. The nurse suspects benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Which additional question is most important for the nurse to ask to assess for this specific condition?

A) "Do you have difficulty starting your urine stream?"
B) "Have you noticed any new swelling in your ankles or feet?"
C) "Does your urine look bright red or cloudy?"
D) "Are you experiencing a sharp, stabbing pain in your lower back?"

<details>
<summary><strong>View Answer and Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) involves an age-related enlargement of the prostate gland, which gradually compresses the urethra. This commonly leads to lower urinary tract symptoms such as difficulty initiating a urinary stream (hesitancy), a weak stream, post-void dribbling, urinary frequency, and nocturia. Ankle swelling is generally associated with fluid retention, venous insufficiency, or heart failure. Gross hematuria (bright red urine) or sharp flank/back pain are more indicative of urinary tract infections, kidney stones, or malignancies rather than uncomplicated BPH.
</details>
### Question 616
A nurse in a long-term care facility is caring for an 82-year-old resident who is typically alert and oriented. This morning, the nurse observes that the patient is highly agitated, acutely confused, and repeatedly asking for their mother. Which action should the nurse take first based on these new clinical findings?

A) Administer a PRN dose of an antipsychotic medication to calm the patient.
B) Assess the patient's vital signs and prepare to collect a urine sample. 
C) Apply soft wrist restraints to prevent the patient from falling or wandering.
D) Explain to the patient that they are safe and living in a nursing home.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: B**

**Rationale:** In older adults, a sudden onset of confusion, agitation, or altered mental status is a classic, early presentation of a urinary tract infection (UTI). The nurse's priority should be to assess the patient's physiological status by checking vital signs (including temperature) and obtaining a urinalysis to rule out infection before assuming a psychiatric cause or using restraints.
</details>

---

### Question 617
An outpatient clinic nurse is providing instructions to a 16-year-old patient and their parent regarding a 24-hour urine collection for creatinine clearance. Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching plan to ensure accurate results?

A) "You should keep the large urine collection container at room temperature."
B) "Begin the test by putting the very first time you urinate tomorrow morning into the jug."
C) "Flush your first morning urine down the toilet, and then start the 24-hour timer."
D) "If you forget to collect one void, just add an extra hour to the end of the test."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: C**

**Rationale:** The correct procedure for a 24-hour urine collection requires the patient to empty their bladder entirely and discard that first void. The 24-hour clock begins at the exact time of that discarded void. All subsequent urine over the next 24 hours must be saved. The container must be kept on ice or refrigerated, and if a void is missed, the entire 24-hour collection must be restarted.
</details>

---

### Question 618
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is assessing a 55-year-old patient who has chronic kidney disease and a newly created arteriovenous (AV) fistula in the left arm. Which observation during the nurse's assessment indicates that the AV fistula is patent and functioning properly?

A) Palpating a gentle buzzing sensation over the surgical site.
B) Noting a strong, bounding radial pulse in the patient's right arm.
C) Hearing a high-pitched wheeze when listening to the patient's lung bases.
D) Observing warmth, redness, and swelling at the fistula insertion site.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: A**

**Rationale:** An AV fistula connects an artery directly to a vein, creating a high-flow blood system. To confirm patency, the nurse should easily palpate a "thrill" (a buzzing or vibrating sensation) and auscultate a "bruit" (a swooshing sound) directly over the fistula site. Redness and swelling are signs of infection, not normal patency.
</details>

---

### Question 619
A nurse is caring for a 70-year-old male patient who is 24 hours post-operative from a transurethral resection of the prostate (TURP). The patient is receiving continuous bladder irrigation (CBI). During rounds, the nurse observes that the fluid in the urinary drainage bag is light pink with no visible clots. What is the most appropriate action for the nurse to take?

A) Increase the flow rate of the irrigation fluid immediately.
B) Manually irrigate the catheter with a syringe to clear out potential blockages.
C) Stop the continuous bladder irrigation and prepare to remove the catheter.
D) Document the color of the drainage and continue monitoring the patient.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: D**

**Rationale:** Light pink urine without clots is a normal and expected finding 24 hours after a TURP procedure with continuous bladder irrigation. The nurse does not need to intervene by altering the irrigation rate or manually irrigating; simply documenting the finding and continuing routine observation is the correct action.
</details>

---

### Question 620
A 35-year-old female patient at a women's health clinic reports experiencing small leaks of urine whenever she coughs, sneezes, or laughs heavily. She asks the nurse how she can manage this issue at home without medication. Which intervention should the nurse recommend?

A) Limit total daily fluid intake to less than one liter.
B) Wear a highly absorbent adult brief at all times to prevent skin breakdown.
C) Perform daily pelvic floor muscle exercises.
D) Request a prescription to learn self-catheterization techniques.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer: C**

**Rationale:** The patient is describing symptoms of stress incontinence, which is caused by a weakening of the pelvic floor muscles. The most effective, non-pharmacological first-line intervention is teaching the patient to perform daily pelvic floor muscle exercises (Kegel exercises) to strengthen the muscles that control the flow of urine. Severely restricting fluids can lead to concentrated urine, which irritates the bladder and can worsen incontinence or cause UTIs.
</details>
### Question 621
A nurse is caring for an 82-year-old bedbound patient on a medical-surgical unit who has an indwelling urinary catheter. The nurse is assisting the patient with repositioning in bed. Which action by the nurse is the priority to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A. Clamping the catheter tubing before turning the patient
B. Ensuring the drainage bag remains below the level of the patient's bladder
C. Disconnecting the drainage bag to empty it before repositioning
D. Resting the drainage bag on the bed next to the patient's legs

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** B. Ensuring the drainage bag remains below the level of the patient's bladder

**Rationale:** Keeping the urinary drainage bag below the level of the bladder relies on gravity to keep urine flowing properly and prevents the backflow of stagnant urine into the bladder, which is a primary risk factor for a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). The system should never be disconnected routinely, and the bag should never be placed on the bed or raised above the bladder. Clamping the tube is unnecessary for simple repositioning and could lead to urinary retention if forgotten.
</details>

---

### Question 622
A nurse at a long-term care facility is admitting a 74-year-old male client with a history of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). During the initial genitourinary assessment, which symptom should the nurse expect the client to report?

A. Painful, burning urination
B. Red-tinged urine with large clots
C. Total inability to produce any urine
D. Difficulty starting the flow of urine

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** D. Difficulty starting the flow of urine

**Rationale:** Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) causes an enlargement of the prostate gland, which compresses the urethra. This commonly leads to lower urinary tract symptoms such as hesitancy (difficulty starting the urine stream), a weak stream, dribbling, and frequent urination. Painful urination is more indicative of a urinary tract infection, while a total inability to produce urine is a medical emergency (acute urinary retention or renal failure) and not merely an expected daily symptom of stable BPH. 
</details>

---

### Question 623
A 45-year-old male arrives at the emergency department complaining of severe flank pain radiating to his groin. The healthcare provider suspects urolithiasis (kidney stones). Which nursing intervention is most important for this specific condition when the patient voids?

A. Straining all of the patient's urine through a gauze or filter
B. Collecting a clean-catch midstream urine sample every hour
C. Limiting the patient's oral fluid intake to prevent bladder spasms
D. Measuring the specific gravity of every voided output

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** A. Straining all of the patient's urine through a gauze or filter

**Rationale:** When a patient has suspected kidney stones, it is crucial for the nurse to strain all voided urine. This allows the nurse to catch any passed stones or fragments, which are then sent to the laboratory for analysis. Determining the chemical composition of the stone helps the healthcare provider prescribe the correct dietary and medical interventions to prevent future stones. Patients should be encouraged to increase fluids, not limit them.
</details>

---

### Question 624
A nurse in a community health clinic is providing discharge teaching to a 22-year-old female client who was newly diagnosed with an uncomplicated urinary tract infection (UTI). Which instruction should the nurse include in the teaching plan?

A. "Take tub baths instead of showers until the infection clears."
B. "Restrict your daily water intake to reduce the urge to void."
C. "Wipe your perineal area from front to back after using the toilet."
D. "Stop taking the prescribed antibiotics as soon as your symptoms disappear."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** C. "Wipe your perineal area from front to back after using the toilet."

**Rationale:** Wiping from front to back (urethra to rectum) prevents the introduction of gastrointestinal bacteria, such as E. coli, into the urinary meatus. This is a vital preventative measure for women due to their shorter urethra. The patient should be taught to take showers instead of tub baths to avoid bacteria entering the urethra, increase (not restrict) fluid intake to flush out the urinary tract, and always finish the entire course of antibiotics even if symptoms subside.
</details>

---

### Question 625
A 35-year-old postpartum client visits the women's health clinic and tells the nurse, "I leak a little bit of urine every time I sneeze, laugh, or cough." Which nursing action is the most appropriate initial response?

A. Prepare the client for immediate insertion of an indwelling catheter
B. Teach the client how to perform pelvic floor muscle exercises
C. Advise the client to avoid drinking any liquids after 5:00 PM
D. Inform the client that this indicates a serious neurological injury

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** B. Teach the client how to perform pelvic floor muscle exercises

**Rationale:** The client is describing stress incontinence, which is very common in postpartum women due to the weakening of the pelvic floor muscles during pregnancy and childbirth. Teaching pelvic floor muscle exercises (Kegel exercises) is a highly effective, non-invasive first-line intervention to strengthen the muscles that control urine flow. An indwelling catheter is inappropriate and poses an infection risk. Fluid restriction is not the primary treatment for stress incontinence, and this symptom alone does not indicate a severe neurological injury.
</details>
### Question 626
A nurse is assisting a 45-year-old postoperative patient who has an indwelling urinary catheter to get out of bed for the first time. Which action should the nurse take regarding the catheter during ambulation?

A) Clamp the catheter tubing to prevent urine leakage.
B) Raise the drainage bag above the patient's waist level.
C) Keep the drainage bag below the level of the patient's bladder.
