D) Disconnect the drainage bag from the catheter tubing temporarily.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Keeping the drainage bag below the level of the bladder is a critical, fundamental nursing action that prevents the backflow of contaminated urine into the bladder. This significantly reduces the patient's risk of developing a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). Clamping the tube, raising the bag, or breaking the closed system by disconnecting the tube are all unsafe practices that increase the risk of infection and injury.
</details>

---

### Question 627
A 28-year-old male patient is admitted to the emergency department with severe right flank pain, and the provider suspects a kidney stone (nephrolithiasis). Which initial nursing action is most important when the patient needs to void?

A) Collect a midstream clean-catch urine sample.
B) Strain all of the patient's urine through a gauze or strainer.
C) Restrict the patient's oral fluid intake until the stone passes.
D) Insert a straight catheter to accurately measure output.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
When a patient is suspected of having a kidney stone, the nurse must strain all of the patient's urine. This action ensures that any passed stones or gravel fragments are caught and can be sent to the laboratory for analysis. Determining the stone's composition is vital for guiding future medical treatment and dietary modifications. Restricting fluids is contraindicated (patients should increase fluid intake), and catheterization is invasive and unnecessary for this purpose.
</details>

---

### Question 628
A nurse is reviewing the breakfast tray for a 68-year-old patient with end-stage chronic kidney disease (CKD). Which item on the tray should the nurse remove before serving the patient?

A) A slice of white toast
B) A small bowl of applesauce
C) A pat of unsalted butter
D) A glass of orange juice

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Patients with end-stage chronic kidney disease typically require a potassium-restricted diet because their kidneys can no longer excrete potassium effectively, placing them at high risk for life-threatening hyperkalemia. Orange juice is very high in potassium and must be removed from the tray. White toast, applesauce, and unsalted butter are low-potassium options and are generally acceptable on a renal diet.
</details>

---

### Question 629
A nurse is providing discharge instructions to a 17-year-old female patient treated for an uncomplicated urinary tract infection (UTI) at a community health clinic. Which statement by the patient indicates that the nurse's teaching has been effective?

A) "I should wipe from front to back after using the toilet."
B) "I will limit my daily fluid intake to avoid using the bathroom."
C) "It is safe to use perfumed bubble baths once the infection is gone."
D) "I should wear tight-fitting synthetic underwear to keep the area dry."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Wiping from front to back is a crucial hygiene practice that prevents the introduction of bacteria (such as E. coli) from the anal area into the urethra. To prevent future UTIs, the patient should also be taught to increase (not limit) fluid intake, avoid irritating substances like perfumed bubble baths, and wear loose-fitting cotton (not synthetic) underwear to allow air circulation and prevent moisture buildup.
</details>

---

### Question 630
A nurse is assessing an 8-year-old child recently admitted to the pediatric unit with acute post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis. When emptying the child's urinal, which observation should the nurse expect to find?

A) Clear, pale yellow urine
B) Urine that smells strongly of ammonia
C) Cola-colored, brownish urine
D) Bright red blood with large clots

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Gross hematuria is a classic clinical manifestation of acute post-streptococcal glomerulonephritis. Because the red blood cells are processed and broken down by the kidneys before excretion, the urine typically takes on a dark, smoky, tea, or cola-colored appearance. Bright red blood with large clots is not characteristic of this condition and would indicate lower urinary tract bleeding.
</details>
### Question 631
A nurse is reinforcing discharge teaching for a 78-year-old female client in a long-term care facility who recently completed treatment for an uncomplicated urinary tract infection (UTI). Which statement by the client indicates a correct understanding of the preventative teaching?

A) "I should take a bubble bath instead of a shower."
B) "I will wipe from front to back after using the toilet."
C) "I should limit my fluid intake to avoid using the bathroom."
D) "I will wear nylon underwear to stay dry."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) "I will wipe from front to back after using the toilet."

**Rationale:** Wiping from front to back prevents the introduction of intestinal bacteria (such as *E. coli*) from the anal area into the urethra, which is a leading cause of UTIs in women. Bubble baths can irritate the urethra and increase infection risk. Fluid intake should be increased, not limited, to help flush out bacteria. Cotton underwear is recommended over nylon because it is more breathable and prevents moisture buildup that encourages bacterial growth.
</details>

---

### Question 632
A 45-year-old male client arrives at the emergency department reporting severe flank pain and nausea. The healthcare provider suspects renal calculi (kidney stones). Which of the following is the priority nursing action?

A) Strain all of the client's urine through a gauze or special filter.
B) Restrict the client's oral fluid intake until the pain subsides.
C) Apply a cold compress directly over the client's flank area.
D) Insert a large-bore indwelling urinary catheter immediately.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Strain all of the client's urine through a gauze or special filter.

**Rationale:** Straining all urine is essential for a client with suspected renal calculi to catch any passed stones. The stones are then sent to the laboratory for composition analysis, which guides future treatment and dietary teaching. Fluid intake should be encouraged, not restricted, to help flush the stone. Warm compresses are typically more effective than cold for flank pain caused by spasms. Inserting an indwelling catheter is invasive, increases infection risk, and is not indicated for a standard kidney stone unless there is severe retention or an obstruction requiring drainage.
</details>

---

### Question 633
A nurse is caring for a 60-year-old client who has an indwelling urinary catheter in place following abdominal surgery. While making rounds, the nurse observes the catheter drainage bag hooked onto the movable side rail of the bed. What is the most appropriate action for the nurse to take?

A) Empty the drainage bag immediately to reduce weight on the rail.
B) Loop the tubing tightly over the side rail to prevent tangling.
C) Document the correct placement of the drainage bag in the chart.
D) Move the drainage bag to the bed frame below the level of the client's bladder.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Move the drainage bag to the bed frame below the level of the client's bladder.

**Rationale:** The catheter drainage bag should always be hung on an immovable part of the bed frame and kept below the level of the bladder. Hanging it on a movable side rail can cause the bag to be raised above the bladder when the rail is lifted, leading to urine backflow and increasing the risk of a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). Tubing should not be looped tightly, as this can obstruct urine flow. 
</details>

---

### Question 634
A 68-year-old male client comes to the outpatient clinic reporting difficulty starting his urine stream and a feeling that his bladder is never completely empty. Which initial assessment technique should the nurse perform to check for urinary retention?

A) Percuss the costovertebral angle for tenderness.
B) Auscultate the lower abdomen for bowel sounds.
C) Palpate the suprapubic area for distention.
D) Assess the bilateral flank area for swelling.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) Palpate the suprapubic area for distention.

**Rationale:** Palpating the suprapubic area allows the nurse to assess for a distended bladder, which is a classic sign of urinary retention often seen in older men with conditions like benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Percussing the costovertebral angle checks for kidney inflammation (like pyelonephritis), not a full bladder. Auscultating bowel sounds assesses the gastrointestinal tract. Checking the flanks for swelling does not assess bladder volume. 
</details>

---

### Question 635
A home health nurse is developing a plan of care for an 82-year-old client who experiences frequent urge incontinence. Which nursing intervention is most appropriate to include in the client's bladder retraining program?

A) Establish a strict schedule for the client to use the toilet every 2 hours.
B) Instruct the client to restrict all fluid intake after 12:00 PM.
C) Encourage the client to hold their urine for at least 8 hours during the day.
D) Advise the client to use adult diapers as the primary management strategy.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Establish a strict schedule for the client to use the toilet every 2 hours.

**Rationale:** Bladder retraining involves scheduled toileting (e.g., every 2 to 3 hours) to help the client empty the bladder before an incontinence episode occurs, gradually increasing bladder capacity over time. Restricting fluids early in the day can lead to dehydration and concentrated urine, which irritates the bladder and worsens urge incontinence. Holding urine for 8 hours is unrealistic and potentially harmful. While adult briefs can manage leaks, they are a coping mechanism, not a retraining intervention.
</details>
### Question 636
A nurse is evaluating a 78-year-old female patient brought to the emergency department by her daughter. The daughter reports her mother has been highly agitated and confused over the past 24 hours, which is unusual for her. The patient's vital signs are stable, but the nurse notes she is incontinent of cloudy, foul-smelling urine. Which condition should the nurse primarily suspect based on these clinical observations?

A) Chronic kidney disease
B) Renal calculi
C) Urinary tract infection
D) Acute kidney injury

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
In older adult patients, a urinary tract infection (UTI) frequently presents with atypical symptoms, most notably a sudden onset of confusion, agitation, or changes in mental status. This, combined with the observation of foul-smelling and cloudy urine, strongly indicates a UTI rather than kidney disease, injury, or stones.
</details>

---

### Question 637
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is obtaining a health history from a 68-year-old male patient who is being evaluated for benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Which statement by the patient indicates a common early clinical manifestation of this condition?

A) "I have to wait several seconds before my urine starts to flow."
B) "My urine has been looking pink and cloudy recently."
C) "I feel a sharp, stabbing pain in my side when I pee."
D) "I am producing much larger amounts of urine now than I used to."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
Hesitancy, or difficulty starting the urinary stream, is a classic and common early symptom of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). This occurs because the enlarged prostate gland compresses the urethra, making it difficult for urine to pass out of the bladder. The other options describe symptoms of infection, kidney stones, or polyuria, which are not primary early signs of BPH.
</details>

---

### Question 638
A 25-year-old male patient is admitted to the medical-surgical unit with severe right flank pain and suspected nephrolithiasis (kidney stones). The healthcare provider orders strict monitoring of the patient's output. Which action is the most important for the nurse to implement regarding the patient's urination?

A) Restrict the patient's oral fluid intake to decrease renal pressure and pain.
B) Insert an indwelling urinary catheter immediately to monitor hourly output.
C) Administer a prescribed diuretic to rapidly flush the stone out of the urinary tract.
D) Strain all of the patient's urine through a specialized filter or gauze.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Straining all urine is an essential nursing action for a patient with suspected kidney stones. This ensures that any passed stones or fragments are caught and can be sent to the laboratory for composition analysis, which guides future treatment and dietary teaching. Fluid intake should be encouraged, not restricted, and catheterization is not routinely indicated unless there is a severe obstruction or urinary retention. 
</details>

---

### Question 639
A nurse is caring for a 45-year-old female patient on bed rest who has an indwelling urinary catheter attached to a continuous drainage system. While assisting the patient with a bed bath, the nurse observes the placement of the collection equipment. Which observation requires immediate intervention by the nurse to prevent a complication?

A) The drainage tubing is coiled loosely on the bed without dependent loops.
B) The collection bag is resting on the mattress next to the patient's waist.
C) The collection bag is hung securely on the immovable portion of the bed frame.
D) The catheter tubing is secured to the patient's upper thigh with an anchoring device.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
The urinary collection bag must always be kept below the level of the patient's bladder. Resting the bag on the mattress next to the patient's waist allows urine to flow backward (reflux) into the bladder, which significantly increases the risk of a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). All other options represent correct nursing practices for indwelling catheter care.
</details>

---

### Question 640
A nurse is providing instructions to a 32-year-old pregnant patient who has been ordered to complete a 24-hour urine collection to assess for preeclampsia. Which instruction should the nurse provide to ensure the accuracy of the test?

A) "Keep the collection container at room temperature next to your toilet."
B) "Include the very first urine you void tomorrow morning to start the test."
C) "Discard your first morning urine, note the time, and collect all urine for the next 24 hours."
D) "Stop the collection immediately if you accidentally flush one of your voids."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
For a 24-hour urine collection, the test begins exactly after the first morning void is discarded. The exact time of this discarded void marks the start of the 24-hour period, and all subsequent urine voided over the next 24 hours must be saved. The container should be kept on ice or in a refrigerator, not at room temperature. If a void is accidentally flushed, the 24-hour collection typically must be completely restarted, not just stopped.
</details>
### Question 641
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is instructing a 24-year-old female client on how to collect a clean-catch midstream urine specimen for a suspected urinary tract infection. Which action by the client indicates that the teaching was successful?

A) The client collects the first drops of urine that come out into the cup.
B) The client cleans the perineal area from back to front before voiding.
C) The client touches the inside of the specimen cup with her fingers to ensure it is open.
D) The client begins to void into the toilet, stops, and then voids into the sterile cup.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
To collect a clean-catch midstream specimen, the client should start voiding into the toilet to clear the urethra of external contaminants, and then catch the midstream urine in the sterile cup. The perineal area should be cleaned front to back to avoid introducing fecal bacteria, and the inside of the cup must remain sterile (never touched).
</details>

---

### Question 642
A nurse on a surgical unit is caring for a 72-year-old male client who just returned from a transurethral resection of the prostate (TURP) and has continuous bladder irrigation (CBI) running. During the assessment, the nurse notes that the fluid in the drainage bag is bright red with several thick clots. What is the nurse's immediate action?

A) Increase the flow rate of the continuous irrigation solution.
B) Stop the irrigation and notify the healthcare provider immediately.
C) Apply a warm compress to the client's lower abdomen.
D) Deflate the catheter balloon and reposition the urinary catheter.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
The primary goal of continuous bladder irrigation is to prevent clot formation and maintain catheter patency after prostate surgery. If bright red blood or clots are noted, the nurse should immediately increase the irrigation rate until the drainage turns light pink or clear. Stopping the irrigation could allow a large clot to form and block the catheter. 
</details>

---

### Question 643
A nurse in a women's health clinic is evaluating a 55-year-old female client who recently started a pelvic floor muscle (Kegel) exercise program for stress urinary incontinence. Which statement by the client demonstrates a correct understanding of how to perform the exercises?

A) "I practice doing these exercises while I am actively emptying my bladder on the toilet."
B) "I tighten my abdominal and thigh muscles as hard as I can for five minutes straight."
C) "I squeeze the muscles I would use to stop passing gas and hold the contraction for a few seconds."
D) "I only need to do these exercises once a week to see improvement in my symptoms."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Kegel exercises isolate and contract the pelvic floor muscles, which are the same muscles used to stop passing gas or stop the flow of urine. The client should not tighten the abdomen or thighs. Additionally, Kegel exercises should not be routinely practiced while actively voiding, as this can lead to urinary retention and incomplete bladder emptying. They must be done multiple times daily, not just once a week.
</details>

---

### Question 644
A nurse at a long-term care facility is observing an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) provide morning care to an 85-year-old male resident who has an indwelling urinary catheter. Which action by the UAP requires immediate intervention by the nurse?

A) The UAP cleans the client's perineal area with mild soap and warm water.
B) The UAP hooks the urinary drainage bag to the movable side rail of the bed.
C) The UAP secures the catheter tubing to the resident's upper thigh with a stabilization device.
D) The UAP empties the drainage bag into a graduated cylinder using the spout at the bottom.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
The urinary drainage bag must be attached to an immovable part of the bed frame and always kept below the level of the bladder. Hooking it to the side rail is incorrect because raising or lowering the side rail could pull on the catheter, causing trauma, or elevate the bag above the bladder, causing a backflow of urine that increases the risk of infection.
</details>

---

### Question 645
A 40-year-old male client is admitted to the emergency department complaining of severe, sharp pain in his right flank that radiates to his groin. The healthcare provider suspects renal calculi (kidney stones). Which initial nursing intervention is the most appropriate for this client regarding his urinary output?

A) Strain all of the client's urine through a paper filter or fine gauze.
B) Restrict the client's oral fluid intake to 500 mL per day.
C) Insert a straight catheter every 4 hours to measure residual urine.
D) Administer a loop diuretic to rapidly flush the stone out of the kidneys.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
For a client with suspected renal calculi, the nurse must strain all urine to catch any stones or fragments that are passed. These stones are then sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine their composition, which guides further treatment and dietary teaching. Fluid intake should be highly encouraged, not restricted, to help flush the stone out naturally.
</details>
### Question 646
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is instructing a 24-year-old female client on how to collect a clean-catch midstream urine specimen for a suspected urinary tract infection (UTI). Which action by the client indicates to the nurse that the teaching was successful?

A) The client collects the very first drops of urine into the sterile specimen cup.
B) The client cleanses the perineal area from front to back before urinating.
C) The client touches the inside of the specimen cup lid to keep it secure.
D) The client fills the cup completely to the brim before securing the lid.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Cleansing the perineum from front to back is essential to prevent introducing gastrointestinal bacteria into the urethra and contaminating the specimen. For a clean-catch midstream specimen, the client should void a small amount into the toilet first to flush the urethra (making A incorrect), avoid touching the inside of the cup or lid to maintain sterility (making C incorrect), and fill the cup only about halfway to prevent spilling and contamination (making D incorrect).
</details>

---

### Question 647
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 45-year-old female client who has an indwelling urinary catheter in place following abdominal surgery. During routine care, the nurse observes the placement of the urine drainage bag. Which observation requires an immediate intervention by the nurse?

A) The drainage bag is hanging on the bed frame below the level of the client's bladder.
B) The catheter tubing is secured to the client's inner thigh with a stabilization device.
C) The drainage bag is resting directly on the floor beneath the client's bed.
D) The tubing is coiled neatly on the bed and is free of kinks.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** The urinary drainage bag should never rest on the floor, as this significantly increases the risk of bacterial contamination and catheter-associated urinary tract infections (CAUTIs). The bag must be kept below the level of the bladder to allow gravity drainage and prevent backflow (A is an expected, correct action). Securing the tubing to the thigh prevents pulling and trauma to the urethra (B is correct). Keeping tubing free of kinks and dependent loops ensures proper urine flow (D is correct).
</details>

---

### Question 648
A 38-year-old male client is admitted to the emergency department with severe right flank pain and is diagnosed with suspected renal calculi (kidney stones). Which nursing action is the highest priority when assisting this client with urination?

A) Pouring all of the client's voided urine through a paper strainer.
B) Restricting the client's oral fluid intake to prevent bladder spasms.
C) Measuring the exact specific gravity of each individual void.
D) Sending a urine sample for a 24-hour creatinine clearance test.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Straining all urine is an essential nursing intervention for a client with suspected renal calculi. This allows the nurse to catch any passed stones or gravel, which can then be sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine their composition and guide future treatment. Oral fluids should be encouraged (unless contraindicated) to help flush the stone out, not restricted. While measuring specific gravity and collecting 24-hour urines may be done in some renal conditions, catching the stone via straining is the immediate priority for this acute presentation.
</details>

---

### Question 649
A nurse is performing an admission assessment on a 72-year-old male client in a long-term care facility. The client has a documented history of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Which clinical manifestation should the nurse anticipate observing when interviewing the client about his urinary habits?

A) Painless hematuria with large blood clots
B) Complete inability to produce any urine
C) Passing large volumes of highly dilute urine
D) Difficulty initiating a steady stream of urine

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) causes the prostate gland to enlarge, which compresses the urethra. Expected symptoms include difficulty initiating urination (hesitancy), a weak stream, dribbling at the end of voiding, and urinary frequency. Painless hematuria is a classic sign of bladder cancer, not BPH. Complete inability to produce urine (anuria) is a sign of acute kidney injury or end-stage renal disease. Passing large volumes of dilute urine (polyuria) is associated with conditions like diabetes mellitus or diabetes insipidus.
</details>

---

### Question 650
A dialysis nurse is evaluating a 65-year-old female client with end-stage chronic kidney disease (CKD) who is on a prescribed fluid restriction. The nurse determines that the client understands how to manage her daily fluid intake when the client makes which statement?

A) "I will suck on hard sugar-free mints to help relieve my dry mouth."
B) "I can eat as much gelatin and soup as I want since they are considered foods."
C) "I will replace my daily water intake with caffeinated coffee to reduce my thirst."
D) "I only need to count the fluids I drink during my three main meals."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** Sucking on hard candy, mints, or chewing gum can help stimulate saliva production and relieve dry mouth, which is a common complaint for clients on strict fluid restrictions. Gelatin, soup, ice cream, and ice chips melt into liquid at body temperature and must be strictly counted as part of the daily fluid allowance (making B incorrect). Caffeinated beverages can act as mild diuretics and often worsen thirst (making C incorrect). All fluids consumed throughout the entire day, not just with meals, must be tracked and counted (making D incorrect).
</details>
### Question 651
A clinic nurse is evaluating the understanding of a 22-year-old female client who recently recovered from an uncomplicated urinary tract infection (UTI). Which statement by the client indicates to the nurse that further teaching is needed?

A) "I will wipe from front to back after using the toilet."  
B) "I should drink plenty of water throughout the day."  
C) "I will take a bubble bath every night to keep the area clean."  
D) "I plan to urinate immediately after having sexual intercourse."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
Taking bubble baths can irritate the urethra and alter the normal flora of the genitourinary tract, increasing the risk of recurrent UTIs. The nurse should intervene and educate the client to take showers instead. Wiping from front to back, increasing fluid intake, and voiding after intercourse are all appropriate, evidence-based practices to prevent UTIs.
</details>

---

### Question 652
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is admitting a 75-year-old male client with a history of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). During the initial nursing assessment, which urinary elimination pattern should the nurse expect the client to report?

A) Difficulty starting the flow of urine  
B) Painful urination accompanied by a sudden fever  
C) Complete inability to produce urine in the morning  
D) Urine that is consistently bright red in color  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) causes an enlargement of the prostate gland, which compresses the urethra. This results in classic symptoms such as urinary hesitancy (difficulty starting the stream), weak urine flow, and frequent urination. Fever and dysuria suggest an infection, bright red urine suggests active bleeding, and complete anuria is a medical emergency requiring immediate intervention, not an expected baseline finding.
</details>

---

### Question 653
A nurse in a long-term care facility is providing morning care for a 50-year-old client who has an indwelling urinary catheter. Which action by the nurse is most appropriate to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A) Changing the catheter and drainage system every 48 hours to maintain sterility  
B) Elevating the drainage bag above the level of the client's bladder while they sit in a chair  
C) Cleansing the urethral meatus with a strong antiseptic solution daily  
D) Ensuring the drainage tubing has no kinks and urine flows freely into the bag  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Maintaining a closed system with unobstructed, free-flowing urine is a primary intervention to prevent CAUTIs. Kinks in the tubing can cause urine to pool and flow backward, increasing infection risk. Routine changing of catheters is not recommended unless clinically indicated. The drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder. Cleansing should be done with mild soap and water, as strong antiseptics are irritating and not recommended for routine catheter care.
</details>

---

### Question 654
A 30-year-old male client presents to the emergency department with severe, colicky flank pain and is suspected of having nephrolithiasis (kidney stones). After administering prescribed intravenous pain medication, what is the next priority action for the nurse to take?

A) Limit the client's oral fluid intake to decrease kidney workload  
B) Strain all of the client's voided urine through a filter  
C) Insert an indwelling urinary catheter to monitor strict output  
D) Place the client in a prone position to relieve renal pressure  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
When a client is suspected of having kidney stones, the nurse must strain all voided urine to catch any passed stones or fragments. These stones can then be sent to the laboratory for analysis, which guides future treatment and dietary teaching. The client should be encouraged to increase fluids, not restrict them. Inserting a catheter is invasive and unnecessary unless there is an obstruction causing urinary retention. Position changes are based on patient comfort, and the prone position is not a specific requirement.
</details>

---

### Question 655
A nurse at a women's health clinic is caring for a 28-year-old postpartum client who reports leaking small amounts of urine whenever she sneezes, coughs, or laughs. Which intervention should the nurse recommend first to address this issue?

A) Preparing the client for the insertion of a vaginal pessary  
B) Suggesting the use of an over-the-counter mild diuretic  
C) Teaching the client how to perform pelvic floor muscle exercises  
D) Instructing the client to restrict her daily fluid intake  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The client is describing stress incontinence, which is common in postpartum women due to weakened pelvic floor muscles. The first-line, least invasive nursing intervention is to teach the client how to perform Kegel (pelvic floor muscle) exercises to strengthen the area. Restricting fluid can lead to concentrated urine, which irritates the bladder. Diuretics would increase urine volume, and a pessary is a more advanced medical intervention, not an initial nursing recommendation.
</details>
### Question 656
A nurse in a long-term care facility is caring for a 78-year-old client who suddenly becomes confused, combative, and refuses to eat. The client has no prior history of dementia or cognitive impairment. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Administer a prescribed PRN sedative to calm the client.
B) Obtain a urine sample to check for a urinary tract infection.
C) Restrict the client's fluid intake to prevent urinary incontinence.
D) Request a prescription for a daily antipsychotic medication.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
In older adults, sudden onset of confusion or changes in mental status are classic, early indicators of a urinary tract infection (UTI). The nurse should first assess for underlying physiological causes, such as a UTI, before administering sedatives or antipsychotics (Options A and D), which only mask the symptoms and act as chemical restraints. Restricting fluid intake (Option C) is contraindicated as it can worsen a potential UTI and cause dehydration. 
</details>

---

### Question 657
A nurse on a surgical unit is assisting a 45-year-old client who is recovering from an appendectomy and has an indwelling urinary (Foley) catheter. The client is preparing to ambulate in the hallway for the first time since surgery. Which action by the nurse is correct regarding the catheter drainage bag?

A) Clamp the catheter tubing to prevent urine backflow during the walk.
B) Disconnect the drainage bag and place a sterile cap on the catheter tubing.
C) Have the client hold the drainage bag at chest level while walking.
D) Ensure the drainage bag is carried below the level of the client's bladder.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
To prevent the backflow of urine into the bladder and reduce the risk of a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI), the drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder, allowing gravity to facilitate drainage. Clamping the tubing (Option A) can cause urinary retention and is unnecessary. Disconnecting the bag (Option B) breaks the sterile closed system, greatly increasing the risk of infection. Holding the bag at chest level (Option C) would cause urine to flow backward into the bladder.
</details>

---

### Question 658
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is teaching a 52-year-old client how to collect a 24-hour urine specimen to evaluate kidney function. Which instruction should the nurse provide to the client to initiate the collection properly?

A) "Discard your first morning urine and start the 24-hour clock from that moment."
B) "Save your first morning urine in the container and start the clock immediately."
C) "Keep the urine collection container at room temperature for the entire 24 hours."
D) "Stop the collection exactly 23 hours after you collect the first specimen."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
To properly begin a 24-hour urine collection, the client should empty their bladder, discard that first urine, and note the time. This ensures the collection starts with an empty bladder. All subsequent urine over the next 24 hours must be saved. Saving the first urine (Option B) would include urine produced before the 24-hour period started, skewing the results. The container should be kept on ice or refrigerated, not at room temperature (Option C). The collection must last a full 24 hours, not 23 (Option D), and the client should void one last time at the 24-hour mark and add it to the container.
</details>

---

### Question 659
A nurse on the postpartum unit is caring for a 28-year-old client who recently delivered a healthy newborn vaginally. The client reports leaking small amounts of urine when coughing or laughing. Which intervention should the nurse include in the client's teaching plan?

A) Limit daily fluid intake to less than 1 liter to reduce bladder filling.
B) Schedule the temporary insertion of an indwelling urinary catheter.
C) Teach the client how to perform pelvic floor muscle (Kegel) exercises.
D) Advise the client to bear down forcefully when urinating to empty the bladder.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
The client is experiencing stress incontinence, which is common after vaginal delivery due to weakened pelvic floor muscles. Pelvic floor muscle (Kegel) exercises help strengthen these muscles and improve urinary control. Limiting fluid intake (Option A) can lead to dehydration and concentrated urine, which irritates the bladder. Inserting a catheter (Option B) is an invasive procedure that introduces a high risk of infection and is inappropriate for stress incontinence. Bearing down forcefully (Option D) can further damage the pelvic floor and is not recommended.
</details>

---

### Question 660
A nurse in a hemodialysis clinic is assessing a 34-year-old client who has a newly created arteriovenous (AV) fistula in the left arm. Which assessment finding indicates to the nurse that the AV fistula is patent and functioning correctly?

A) A bounding pulse palpated in the client's left radial artery.
B) A palpable thrill over the fistula site.
C) The absence of a bruit when auscultating over the fistula.
D) Paleness and coolness of the fingers on the client's left hand.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
An AV fistula is a surgical connection between an artery and a vein used for hemodialysis. A patent and functioning AV fistula will have a palpable thrill (a vibrating sensation) and an auscultated bruit (a swooshing sound) due to the turbulent blood flow. Therefore, feeling a thrill is an expected and positive finding. A bounding radial pulse (Option A) assesses distal arterial flow but does not directly confirm fistula patency. The absence of a bruit (Option C) indicates the fistula may be clotted or blocked. Paleness and coolness (Option D) are signs of arterial steal syndrome, a complication indicating inadequate blood flow to the extremity.
</details>
### Question 661
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is providing discharge teaching to a 24-year-old female client recently diagnosed with a lower urinary tract infection (UTI). Which statement by the client indicates that the teaching was effective?

A) "I should limit my daily fluid intake so I don't have to urinate as often."
B) "I will wipe from front to back after using the toilet."
C) "I should take tub baths every night instead of showering."
D) "I will hold my urine until my bladder feels completely full."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** Wiping from front to back prevents the introduction of bacteria from the anal area into the urethra, which is a primary cause of UTIs in women. Clients with UTIs should be encouraged to increase fluid intake to flush the urinary tract (making A incorrect). Showers are preferred over tub baths to prevent bacteria from entering the urethra (making C incorrect). Clients should be taught to void regularly and avoid holding their urine for long periods to prevent urinary stasis and bacterial growth (making D incorrect).
</details>

---

### Question 662
An 82-year-old female client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit and has an indwelling urinary catheter in place. The nurse observes an unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) assisting the client. Which action by the UAP requires immediate intervention by the nurse?

A) Using a dedicated measuring container to empty the catheter drainage bag.
B) Securing the catheter tubing to the client's upper thigh with a stat-lock.
C) Washing the client's perineal area with mild soap and warm water.
D) Placing the urinary drainage bag on the mattress next to the client.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** The urinary drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder to prevent the backflow of urine, which can lead to a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). Placing the bag on the bed next to the client raises it above or level with the bladder, requiring immediate intervention. Using a dedicated container, securing the tubing to the thigh to prevent pulling, and providing routine perineal care are all correct and appropriate actions.
</details>

---

### Question 663
A 55-year-old male client with end-stage renal disease comes to the dialysis clinic for his scheduled treatment. The nurse is assessing the newly created arteriovenous (AV) fistula in his left arm. Which finding indicates to the nurse that the fistula is patent and functioning properly?

A) Palpating a vibrating thrill over the fistula site.
B) Observing bounding radial pulses in the left wrist.
C) Noting that the left hand is cold and pale.
D) Auscultating a muffled heartbeat near the antecubital space.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** To assess the patency of an AV fistula, the nurse should palpate for a "thrill" (a vibrating sensation) and auscultate for a "bruit" (a swooshing sound) over the site. The presence of a thrill indicates adequate, turbulent blood flow through the fistula. A cold, pale hand could indicate "steal syndrome," a complication where blood supply to the extremity is compromised (making C incorrect). Bounding pulses or muffled heartbeats are not the standard indicators of a functional AV fistula (making B and D incorrect).
</details>

---

### Question 664
A nurse is completing an admission assessment on a 68-year-old male client in a long-term care facility. The client has a documented history of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Which clinical manifestation should the nurse expect to observe or hear reported by the client?

A) Complete absence of urine output for the last 24 hours.
B) Painless, bright red blood in the urine.
C) Difficulty initiating a stream of urine.
D) Uncontrollable, continuous leaking of urine.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) involves the enlargement of the prostate gland, which surrounds the urethra. As the prostate enlarges, it compresses the urethra, leading to lower urinary tract symptoms such as hesitancy (difficulty starting the stream), a weak stream, frequent urination, and nocturia. Complete absence of urine (anuria) is a medical emergency indicating total obstruction or renal failure, not an expected baseline finding (making A incorrect). Painless hematuria is often associated with bladder cancer (making B incorrect). Continuous leaking is more typical of severe incontinence or a fistula, whereas BPH typically causes retention and overflow or urgency, not a continuous leak (making D incorrect).
</details>

---

### Question 665
A 35-year-old male client arrives at the emergency department reporting severe, sharp flank pain that radiates to his groin. The healthcare provider suspects renal calculi (kidney stones). Which initial nursing action is the most appropriate for this client?

A) Restricting oral fluid intake to 500 mL per day.
B) Preparing the client for an emergency hemodialysis session.
C) Administering a loop diuretic to force a massive urine output.
D) Straining all of the client's voided urine through a filter.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** When a client is suspected of having renal calculi, the nurse must strain all voided urine through a filter or gauze to catch any stones or fragments that are passed. These stones are then sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine their composition, which guides future treatment and dietary teaching. Clients with kidney stones should be encouraged to drink plenty of fluids to help flush the stone out, not restricted (making A incorrect). Hemodialysis is used for renal failure, not kidney stones (making B incorrect). Administering strong diuretics is not the standard first-line treatment for a kidney stone (making C incorrect).
</details>
### Question 666
A 6-year-old female is brought to the pediatric clinic after experiencing her third urinary tract infection (UTI) this year. Which statement by the mother indicates to the nurse that teaching about UTI prevention has been effective?

A) "I will have her take a bubble bath every night before bed."
B) "I should dress her in tight-fitting nylon underwear."
C) "I will teach her to wipe from front to back after using the toilet."
D) "I should limit her water intake so she doesn't have to hold her urine at school."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Wiping from front to back prevents the introduction of bacteria from the anal area into the urethra, which is a common cause of UTIs in young females. Bubble baths (A) can irritate the urethra and should be avoided. Cotton underwear is preferred over nylon (B) because it is breathable and prevents moisture buildup. Fluid intake should be encouraged, not limited (D), to help flush bacteria from the urinary tract.
</details>

### Question 667
A 72-year-old male arrives at the primary care clinic reporting waking up four times a night to urinate. The nurse suspects benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Which additional finding should the nurse expect to observe when taking the patient's history?

A) Difficulty initiating a urine stream
B) Pain radiating to the right shoulder
C) Urine that is bright red and contains large clots
D) Increased force of the urinary stream

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A
**Rationale:** An enlarged prostate in BPH compresses the urethra, leading to lower urinary tract symptoms such as difficulty starting a urine stream (hesitancy), a weak stream, and frequent nighttime urination (nocturia). Pain radiating to the shoulder (B) is unrelated to BPH. Gross hematuria with large clots (C) is a concerning sign that may indicate bladder cancer or severe trauma, not typical unmedicated BPH. The force of the urinary stream typically decreases, not increases (D), in a patient with BPH.
</details>

---

### Question 668
The nurse is caring for a 45-year-old patient who has an indwelling urinary catheter following abdominal surgery. Which action by the nurse is essential to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A) Disconnecting the catheter from the drainage tubing to flush it daily.
B) Resting the urinary drainage bag on the patient's bed during transport.
C) Cleaning the urinary meatus vigorously with alcohol swabs every shift.
D) Ensuring the drainage bag remains positioned below the level of the patient's bladder.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D
**Rationale:** Keeping the drainage bag below the level of the bladder ensures that urine flows by gravity and prevents the backflow of contaminated urine into the bladder, which significantly reduces the risk of infection. Disconnecting the catheter (A) breaks the closed sterile drainage system and increases infection risk. Placing the bag on the bed (B) removes the gravity gradient and promotes backflow. Routine perineal care with soap and water is recommended; vigorous cleaning with alcohol (C) can cause irritation and tissue damage.
</details>

### Question 669
A 38-year-old male is admitted to the emergency department reporting severe flank pain and nausea. The healthcare provider suspects urolithiasis (kidney stones). Which nursing action is the priority for this patient's urinary care?

A) Restricting oral fluid intake to reduce kidney workload.
B) Straining all voided urine through a gauze or filter.
C) Inserting an indwelling urinary catheter immediately.
D) Collecting a 24-hour urine specimen on ice.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B
**Rationale:** The priority action for a patient with suspected kidney stones is to strain all urine. This allows the nurse to catch any passed stones or fragments, which can then be sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine the stone's composition and guide future preventative treatment. Fluids should generally be encouraged, not restricted (A), to help flush out the stone. Catheterization (C) is invasive and not routinely indicated simply for kidney stones unless there is an obstruction causing retention. A 24-hour urine collection (D) may be ordered later for metabolic workup, but is not the immediate priority over straining for the acute stone passage.
</details>
