### Question 670
A 68-year-old female with chronic kidney disease (CKD) is admitted to the medical-surgical unit. The nurse is monitoring the patient for fluid volume overload. Which daily nursing action provides the most accurate data regarding the patient's fluid status?

A) Measuring the patient's abdominal girth.
B) Assessing the patient's oral mucosa for moisture.
C) Weighing the patient at the same time every morning using the same scale.
D) Checking the patient's skin turgor on the back of the hand.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C
**Rationale:** Daily weights are the most reliable indicator of fluid volume status in patients with kidney disease or heart failure. A gain of 1 kilogram (2.2 lbs) equates to roughly 1 liter of retained fluid. Abdominal girth (A) is more specific to ascites. Oral mucosa moisture (B) and skin turgor (D) are better indicators of dehydration rather than fluid overload, and skin turgor on the back of the hand is especially unreliable in older adults due to age-related loss of skin elasticity.
</details>
### Question 671
A nurse in a long-term care facility is teaching an 82-year-old female client about preventing recurrent urinary tract infections (UTIs). Which action by the client indicates that the teaching has been effective?

A) The client wipes from back to front after using the toilet.
B) The client drinks at least 2 liters of water daily.
C) The client takes a bubble bath every evening instead of showering.
D) The client holds her urine for as long as possible to stretch her bladder.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: B</b><br>
Drinking adequate fluids (at least 2 to 3 liters daily, unless contraindicated) helps flush bacteria from the urinary tract, preventing UTIs. Wiping from back to front introduces bacteria from the anal area to the urethra. Bubble baths can irritate the urethra and increase UTI risk. Holding urine allows bacteria to multiply in the bladder.
</details>

---

### Question 672
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is assessing a 45-year-old male client who has an indwelling urinary (Foley) catheter. Which observation by the nurse requires an immediate intervention?

A) The catheter tubing is secured to the client's upper thigh.
B) The drainage bag is hanging on the bed frame below the level of the bladder.
C) The drainage bag is resting directly on the floor.
D) The urine in the tubing appears pale yellow and clear.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: C</b><br>
The urinary drainage bag should never rest directly on the floor, as this heavily increases the risk of bacterial contamination and subsequent catheter-associated urinary tract infections (CAUTIs). Securing the tubing to the thigh, hanging the bag below the bladder on the bed frame, and having pale, clear urine are all normal and correct findings.
</details>

---

### Question 673
A nurse in the emergency department is caring for a 28-year-old male client admitted with suspected renal calculi (kidney stones). The client is crying, holding his side, and reporting severe flank pain rated 10 on a 0-to-10 scale. Which nursing action is the priority?

A) Administering the prescribed intravenous pain medication.
B) Straining all the client's urine through a gauze or strainer.
C) Educating the client on a low-purine and low-calcium diet.
D) Encouraging the client to ambulate in the hallway to help pass the stone.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
According to Maslow's hierarchy of needs, addressing severe, acute pain is the immediate priority for a client experiencing renal colic. While straining the urine to catch the stone is an important nursing intervention for this condition, it is secondary to providing pain relief. Education and ambulation are also secondary interventions once the client's pain is managed.
</details>

---

### Question 674
A nurse at a primary care clinic is interviewing a 70-year-old male client who is being evaluated for benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Based on this diagnosis, which statement by the client should the nurse expect to hear?

A) "My urine is consistently bright red and cloudy."
B) "I have severe pain in my lower back every time I urinate."
C) "I produce huge amounts of urine every time I go to the bathroom."
D) "I have to get up several times during the night to urinate."

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: D</b><br>
Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) causes enlargement of the prostate, which compresses the urethra. This leads to urinary retention, difficulty starting a stream, a weak urinary stream, and frequent nighttime urination (nocturia). Large amounts of urine are not typical; instead, clients often void in frequent, small amounts. Gross hematuria (bright red urine) and severe back pain are not expected symptoms of simple BPH and warrant evaluation for other conditions, such as infection or cancer.
</details>

---

### Question 675
A nurse in a women's health clinic is providing discharge instructions to a 32-year-old postpartum client who reports leaking small amounts of urine whenever she sneezes, laughs, or coughs. Which action should the nurse instruct the client to perform?

A) Perform pelvic floor muscle (Kegel) exercises daily.
B) Limit daily fluid intake to 1,000 mL to keep the bladder empty.
C) Void only once every 8 hours to train the bladder to hold more urine.
D) Use an intermittent straight catheter whenever a cough is anticipated.

<details>
<summary>Rationale</summary>
<b>Correct Answer: A</b><br>
The client is experiencing stress incontinence, which is common postpartum due to the stretching and weakening of the pelvic floor muscles during pregnancy and childbirth. Kegel exercises help strengthen these muscles and reduce incontinence. Restricting fluids can lead to dehydration and concentrated urine, which irritates the bladder. Delaying voiding for 8 hours increases the risk of overdistention and UTIs. Straight catheterization is invasive and inappropriate for stress incontinence.
</details>
### Question 676
A nurse in a pediatric clinic is providing discharge instructions to the parents of a 6-year-old girl recently diagnosed with a urinary tract infection (UTI). Which statement by the parent indicates a correct understanding of the nurse's teaching on UTI prevention?

A. "I will have her wipe from back to front after using the toilet."
B. "I will encourage her to take frequent bubble baths to stay clean."
C. "I will make sure she wears cotton underwear instead of nylon."
D. "I will limit her water intake in the evening so she doesn't urinate at night."

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. "I will make sure she wears cotton underwear instead of nylon."

**Rationale:** Cotton underwear is breathable and helps prevent the accumulation of moisture, which inhibits bacterial growth and reduces the risk of UTIs. Wiping should always be done from front to back to prevent introducing stool bacteria to the urethra. Bubble baths can irritate the urethra and increase the risk of infection, so they should be avoided. Restricting water intake is incorrect, as adequate fluid intake flushes bacteria out of the urinary tract.
</details>

---

### Question 677
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is assessing a 72-year-old male patient with a history of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). When observing the patient's voiding patterns, which clinical manifestation should the nurse expect as a direct result of this diagnosis?

A. Difficulty initiating a urine stream
B. Dark, amber-colored urine with a strong odor
C. Large amounts of dilute urine output
D. Painful spasms in the lower back radiating to the groin

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A. Difficulty initiating a urine stream

**Rationale:** Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) involves the enlargement of the prostate gland, which surrounds the urethra. This enlargement compresses the urethra, leading to obstructive urinary symptoms such as hesitancy (difficulty starting a stream), a weak stream, and incomplete bladder emptying. Dark, foul-smelling urine suggests dehydration or a UTI. Polyuria (large amounts of dilute urine) is more indicative of diabetes or diuretic use. Painful flank spasms radiating to the groin are characteristic of renal calculi (kidney stones).
</details>

---

### Question 678
A nurse is caring for a 45-year-old patient who had an open appendectomy and currently has an indwelling urinary (Foley) catheter in place. Which action by the nurse is the most appropriate to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A. Disconnecting the catheter tubing to flush it with normal saline every shift
B. Keeping the urinary drainage bag below the level of the patient's bladder
C. Emptying the urinary drainage bag only when it is completely full
D. Applying a broad-spectrum antibiotic ointment to the urethral meatus daily

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B. Keeping the urinary drainage bag below the level of the patient's bladder

**Rationale:** Keeping the drainage bag below the level of the bladder relies on gravity to maintain a continuous flow of urine and prevents the backflow (reflux) of potentially contaminated urine into the bladder, which is a key step in CAUTI prevention. The drainage system should remain closed; routinely disconnecting it for irrigation increases infection risk. The bag should be emptied when it is one-half to two-thirds full to prevent tension on the catheter and reflux. Routine application of antibiotic ointments to the meatus is not recommended and can lead to resistant organisms.
</details>

---

### Question 679
A nurse in an outpatient hemodialysis clinic is assessing a 58-year-old patient with end-stage renal disease. The patient has a newly placed arteriovenous (AV) fistula in the left arm. Which action should the nurse take to verify that the fistula is patent and functioning properly?

A. Obtain a blood pressure reading on the left arm using a manual cuff
B. Flush the fistula with a heparinized saline solution
C. Draw a small blood sample from the fistula to check for brisk blood return
D. Palpate gently over the fistula site to feel for a continuous thrill

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D. Palpate gently over the fistula site to feel for a continuous thrill

**Rationale:** To assess the patency of an AV fistula, the nurse should palpate over the site for a "thrill" (a buzzing or vibrating sensation) and auscultate with a stethoscope for a "bruit" (a swishing sound). These indicate normal, turbulent blood flow through the fistula. Blood pressure cuffs, venipunctures, and IV insertions are strictly contraindicated on the arm with an AV fistula to prevent compression, clotting, and damage to the site.
</details>

---

### Question 680
A 29-year-old male is admitted to the emergency department reporting severe, colicky flank pain and nausea. The healthcare provider suspects renal calculi (kidney stones). Which nursing intervention is the most appropriate to implement for this patient's urinary care?

A. Restrict the patient's oral fluid intake to decrease kidney workload
B. Insert a straight urinary catheter to empty the bladder completely
C. Strain all of the patient's voided urine to collect any passed stones
D. Instruct the patient to remain on strict bed rest until the pain subsides

<details>
<summary><strong>Correct Answer & Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C. Strain all of the patient's voided urine to collect any passed stones

**Rationale:** For a patient with suspected kidney stones, all urine must be strained using a gauze or a fine mesh strainer. If a stone or gravel is passed, it should be sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine its composition, which will guide future treatment and dietary changes. Fluids should be encouraged, not restricted, to help flush the stone out. Routine catheterization is unnecessary and increases the risk of infection. Ambulation is actually encouraged, if tolerated, to help facilitate the passage of the stone.
</details>
### Question 681
A nurse in a college health clinic is assessing a 22-year-old female client who reports a sudden onset of frequent, painful urination. Which additional finding should the nurse identify as most consistent with a lower urinary tract infection (UTI)?

A. Hematuria and suprapubic discomfort
B. Flank pain and a high-grade fever
C. Urinary retention and a weak urine stream
D. Copious dilute urine and extreme thirst

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A. Hematuria and suprapubic discomfort</strong><br>
Lower urinary tract infections (cystitis) commonly present with dysuria, frequency, urgency, suprapubic discomfort, and sometimes hematuria (blood in the urine). Flank pain and high fever (Option B) indicate pyelonephritis (an upper UTI). Urinary retention and a weak stream (Option C) are associated with obstructions like benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) in males. Copious dilute urine and thirst (Option D) are signs of diabetes insipidus or diabetes mellitus, not a UTI.
</details>

---

### Question 682
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 65-year-old male client with an indwelling urinary catheter. Which action by the nurse is best to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A. Raising the drainage bag above the level of the bladder when transferring the client to a chair
B. Cleaning the urinary meatus with a chlorhexidine solution every 4 hours
C. Ensuring the drainage tubing is free of kinks and the bag is kept below the bladder level
D. Disconnecting the catheter from the drainage tubing to flush it with sterile water daily

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C. Ensuring the drainage tubing is free of kinks and the bag is kept below the bladder level</strong><br>
Keeping the bag below the bladder and the tubing free of kinks promotes gravity drainage and prevents the backflow of urine into the bladder, which is a major risk factor for CAUTI. The drainage bag should never be raised above the bladder (Option A). Routine meatal care should be done with mild soap and water, not chlorhexidine, which can be irritating to mucous membranes (Option B). Disconnecting the catheter from the tubing breaks the closed drainage system and introduces bacteria, increasing infection risk (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 683
A nurse is initiating a 24-hour urine collection for a 50-year-old female client admitted to the telemetry unit. What should the nurse instruct the client to do with the very first voided urine specimen?

A. Save it in the designated large collection container to begin the test
B. Send it to the laboratory immediately for a baseline urinalysis
C. Place it in a separate sterile cup and refrigerate it until the next day
D. Discard it and note the exact time as the start of the 24-hour collection period

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D. Discard it and note the exact time as the start of the 24-hour collection period</strong><br>
When starting a 24-hour urine collection, the bladder must be empty at the beginning of the time period. Therefore, the first void is discarded, and the exact time is recorded as the official start time. All subsequent urine over the next 24 hours is saved in the collection container (Option A, B, and C are incorrect procedures for this test).
</details>

---

### Question 684
A nurse at an outpatient hemodialysis center is reviewing dietary choices with a 72-year-old male client who has end-stage renal disease (ESRD). The client brings in a list of his favorite snacks. Which snack choice indicates a need for further teaching by the nurse?

A. Fresh apple slices
B. Half of a fresh banana
C. A cup of unsalted popcorn
D. Two pieces of white toast with butter

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B. Half of a fresh banana</strong><br>
Clients with end-stage renal disease on hemodialysis must adhere to a renal diet, which strictly limits dietary potassium, phosphorus, and sodium due to the kidneys' inability to excrete them. Bananas are very high in potassium and can lead to dangerous hyperkalemia. Apple slices, unsalted popcorn, and white toast (which is lower in phosphorus than whole wheat) are generally acceptable, low-potassium, low-sodium snack options (Options A, C, and D).
</details>

---

### Question 685
A nurse at a long-term care facility is observing an 80-year-old male resident use the bathroom. The client has a history of benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Which observation by the nurse is an expected finding associated with this condition?

A. The client involuntarily loses urine when coughing or sneezing
B. The client reports severe burning and visible blood clots during urination
C. The client strains to initiate the urine stream and has dribbling at the end of voiding
D. The client voids a large volume of clear, pale urine in less than a minute

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C. The client strains to initiate the urine stream and has dribbling at the end of voiding</strong><br>
Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) involves the enlargement of the prostate gland, which compresses the urethra. This commonly leads to hesitancy (straining to start the stream), a weak stream, and post-void dribbling. Involuntary loss of urine with coughing is stress incontinence, typical of weakened pelvic floor muscles, not BPH (Option A). Severe burning and blood clots suggest an acute issue like a UTI, bladder stones, or cancer, not simple BPH (Option B). Voiding quickly and clearly without issue demonstrates normal urinary function, which is impaired in BPH (Option D).
</details>
### Question 686
A nurse in a long-term care facility is assessing an 82-year-old male resident who has a history of benign prostatic hyperplasia. The unlicensed assistive personnel reports that the patient has been acting differently since this morning. Which finding should the nurse recognize as an early and common indication of a urinary tract infection (UTI) in this older adult patient?

A) A high-grade fever with shaking chills
B) A sudden onset of new confusion and agitation
C) Reports of severe, colicky flank pain
D) The presence of bright red blood in the urine

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) A sudden onset of new confusion and agitation

**Rationale:** In older adults, typical signs of a urinary tract infection (such as fever, dysuria, and flank pain) are often absent. Instead, an acute change in mental status, sudden confusion, or unexpected agitation is frequently the earliest and sometimes the only sign of a UTI. High-grade fever (A), severe flank pain (C), and copious hematuria (D) are not the most common early, atypical presentations for older adults, though they can occur with severe infections or other renal issues like kidney stones.
</details>

---

### Question 687
A triage nurse in the emergency department is caring for a 35-year-old female patient who presents with sudden, severe pain radiating from her right flank to her groin. The healthcare provider suspects nephrolithiasis (kidney stones). Which nursing action is the priority when assisting this patient with elimination?

A) Restrict the patient's oral fluid intake to decrease kidney workload
B) Encourage the patient to maintain strict bed rest to prevent stone movement
C) Insert an indwelling urinary catheter to accurately measure output
D) Pour all voided urine through a mesh filter or strainer

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** D) Pour all voided urine through a mesh filter or strainer

**Rationale:** When a patient is suspected of having kidney stones, it is critical for the nurse to strain all of the patient's urine. This allows the nurse to catch any passed stones or gravel, which can then be sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine the stone's composition and guide future treatment. Fluid intake should generally be encouraged, not restricted (A), to help flush the stone. Ambulation is usually encouraged, not bed rest (B), as movement can help the stone pass. Inserting a catheter (C) is invasive, increases infection risk, and is not indicated simply for passing a kidney stone.
</details>

---

### Question 688
A nurse on a postpartum unit is caring for a 28-year-old female who had a cesarean section earlier in the day and currently has an indwelling Foley catheter in place. To prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI), which action should the nurse perform?

A) Ensure the urinary drainage bag is kept below the level of the patient's bladder
B) Briefly disconnect the catheter from the drainage tubing when emptying the bag
C) Clean the patient's perineal area with a hydrogen peroxide solution twice daily
D) Tape the drainage tubing firmly to the bed rails to prevent accidental dislodgment

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** A) Ensure the urinary drainage bag is kept below the level of the patient's bladder

**Rationale:** Keeping the drainage bag below the level of the bladder prevents the backflow (reflux) of contaminated urine from the bag into the bladder, which is a major risk factor for a CAUTI. The closed drainage system should never be routinely disconnected (B), as this introduces bacteria. Routine perineal care should be done with mild soap and water, not harsh chemicals like hydrogen peroxide (C). The drainage tubing should be secured to the patient's leg, not the bed rails (D), to prevent tension and trauma if the patient or bed moves.
</details>

---

### Question 689
A nurse in an outpatient urology clinic is gathering an intake history from a 65-year-old male patient who is being evaluated for benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). Which patient statement would the nurse most likely expect to hear during the assessment?

A) "I urinate large amounts every time I go to the bathroom."
B) "I have sharp pain in my lower back every time I try to urinate."
C) "I have to strain and it takes a while to get the urine stream started."
D) "My urine looks very cloudy and has a strong, foul odor."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** C) "I have to strain and it takes a while to get the urine stream started."

**Rationale:** Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) causes an enlargement of the prostate gland, which compresses the urethra. This commonly leads to urinary hesitancy (difficulty starting the stream), straining, a weak urine stream, and frequent voiding of small amounts. Patients with BPH typically do not void large amounts at once due to incomplete bladder emptying (A). Sharp lower back pain with urination (B) and cloudy, foul-smelling urine (D) are indicative of an infection (like pyelonephritis or a UTI), not simple BPH.
</details>

---

### Question 690
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is assessing a 50-year-old female patient with Stage 4 Chronic Kidney Disease (CKD). The nurse is monitoring the patient for complications related to decreased renal function. Which assessment finding indicates to the nurse that the patient is experiencing fluid volume overload?

A) Poor skin turgor and dry mucous membranes
B) 2+ pitting edema in the bilateral lower extremities
C) Completely flat neck veins when lying in a supine position
D) A heart rate of 115 beats per minute with a weak, thready pulse

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>

**Correct Answer:** B) 2+ pitting edema in the bilateral lower extremities

**Rationale:** In Chronic Kidney Disease, the kidneys lose their ability to excrete excess fluid and sodium, frequently leading to fluid volume overload. Classic signs of fluid volume overload include peripheral edema (such as 2+ pitting edema), bounding pulses, hypertension, jugular vein distention (JVD), and crackles in the lungs. Poor skin turgor (A), flat neck veins when supine (C), and a weak, thready tachycardia (D) are all signs of fluid volume deficit (dehydration), not overload.
</details>
### Question 691
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is providing discharge teaching to a 22-year-old female client recently diagnosed with an uncomplicated urinary tract infection (UTI). Which statement by the client indicates an understanding of the preventive teaching?

A) "I will wipe from back to front after using the toilet."  
B) "I should take tub baths instead of showers to keep the area clean."  
C) "I will urinate immediately after having sexual intercourse."  
D) "I can stop taking the antibiotics once my burning sensation goes away."  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** Urinating immediately after sexual intercourse helps to flush bacteria out of the urethra, reducing the risk of a UTI. The client should wipe from front to back (not back to front) to prevent introducing fecal bacteria to the urethra. Showers are preferred over tub baths, as bathwater can introduce bacteria into the urinary tract. The client must finish the entire course of antibiotics prescribed, even if symptoms like burning (dysuria) subside, to prevent recurrence and antibiotic resistance.
</details>

---

### Question 692
The nurse is initiating a 24-hour urine collection for a 68-year-old male client admitted with suspected impaired renal function. Which action is the priority for the nurse to take when starting this test?

A) Ask the client to void, discard the first specimen, and note the start time.  
B) Have the client void and save the first specimen in the collection container.  
C) Insert an indwelling urinary catheter to ensure no urine is missed during the 24 hours.  
D) Keep the collection container at room temperature at the client's bedside.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** A  
**Rationale:** To properly initiate a 24-hour urine collection, the client must first empty their bladder. This initial urine is discarded because it has been sitting in the bladder before the test began. The start time of the 24-hour collection is noted at the exact time of this discarded void. All subsequent urine for the next 24 hours is saved. The specimen container should generally be kept on ice or refrigerated to prevent bacterial growth and degradation of urine components. Routine insertion of an indwelling catheter is invasive and increases the risk of infection; it is not standard protocol for a 24-hour collection unless the client is incontinent or critically ill.
</details>

---

### Question 693
A 45-year-old male client returns to the ambulatory care unit after undergoing a cystoscopy. When assessing the client's first void after the procedure, which observation should the nurse expect as a normal finding?

A) Bright red bleeding with large blood clots  
B) Pink-tinged urine with a slight burning sensation  
C) Cloudy, foul-smelling urine  
D) Complete inability to urinate  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** B  
**Rationale:** A cystoscopy involves passing a scope through the urethra into the bladder, which causes minor mucosal trauma. Therefore, pink-tinged urine and a mild burning sensation on urination are expected findings for the first few voids following the procedure. Bright red blood with large clots is an abnormal finding indicating hemorrhage and should be reported to the provider immediately. Cloudy, foul-smelling urine suggests a urinary tract infection, which is a complication. An inability to urinate indicates urinary retention, which is abnormal and requires immediate intervention.
</details>

---

### Question 694
A nurse in a long-term care facility is assisting an 82-year-old female resident who has an indwelling urinary catheter to transfer from her bed to a wheelchair. To maintain proper infection control and safety, where should the nurse secure the urinary drainage bag?

A) On the client's lap to prevent pulling on the tubing.  
B) Attached to the movable armrest of the wheelchair.  
C) Hooked onto the back handles of the wheelchair above the waist.  
D) Attached to the lower, non-movable frame of the wheelchair.  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** D  
**Rationale:** The urinary drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the client's bladder to prevent the backflow of urine into the bladder, which can cause a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). It must also be attached to a non-movable part of the wheelchair (like the lower frame) to prevent accidental pulling or dislodgement of the catheter. Placing it on the lap or back handles puts the bag above the bladder. Attaching it to a movable armrest poses a risk of pulling the catheter out when the armrest is adjusted.
</details>

---

### Question 695
A nurse on the pediatric unit is serving a lunch tray to an 8-year-old child admitted with acute poststreptococcal glomerulonephritis. The child is currently exhibiting peripheral edema and elevated blood pressure. Which food item on the tray should the nurse remove?

A) Sliced fresh apples  
B) A small portion of grilled chicken  
C) Canned chicken noodle soup  
D) A bowl of plain white rice  

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer:** C  
**Rationale:** A child with acute glomerulonephritis experiencing edema and hypertension requires a low-sodium diet to help manage fluid retention and blood pressure. Canned soups are notoriously high in sodium and should be avoided or removed from the meal tray. Fresh fruits (like apples), plain grilled meats, and plain grains (like white rice) are naturally low in sodium and are appropriate choices for this client.
</details>
### Question 696
A clinic nurse is evaluating a 28-year-old female client who frequently presents with uncomplicated urinary tract infections (UTIs). The nurse is providing education on prevention strategies. Which statement by the client indicates effective teaching?

A) "I will limit my fluid intake to avoid using the bathroom too often."
B) "I should take a bubble bath every night to keep the perineal area clean."
C) "I will wipe from front to back after using the toilet."
D) "I will wear tight-fitting nylon underwear to prevent moisture buildup."

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** C) "I will wipe from front to back after using the toilet."

**Rationale:** Wiping from front to back (from the urethra toward the rectum) prevents the introduction of gastrointestinal bacteria, such as *E. coli*, into the urinary tract. Limiting fluids, taking bubble baths, and wearing non-breathable nylon underwear all increase the risk of developing a UTI and indicate a need for further teaching.
</details>

### Question 697
A nurse is caring for a 72-year-old male client admitted to the medical-surgical unit with benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). The client reports feeling a strong, painful urge to void but states he is unable to pass any urine. Which action should the nurse take first?

A) Palpate the client's suprapubic area.
B) Administer a prescribed oral loop diuretic.
C) Insert an indwelling urinary catheter immediately.
D) Encourage the client to drink two large glasses of water.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** A) Palpate the client's suprapubic area.

**Rationale:** The client is exhibiting signs of acute urinary retention, a common complication of BPH. The nurse's first action should be a non-invasive assessment of the bladder by palpating the suprapubic area to check for distention (or using a bladder scanner). Administering a diuretic or giving fluids would worsen the painful retention. Catheterization requires a provider's order and should follow the initial assessment.
</details>

### Question 698
A nurse is assisting a 55-year-old female client who is two days postoperative and has an indwelling urinary catheter in place. As the client is transferred from the bed to a wheelchair, which action by the nurse is essential to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A) Disconnecting the catheter from the drainage tubing briefly to untangle the lines.
B) Resting the drainage bag on the client's lap during the transfer.
C) Clamping the catheter tubing securely for the duration of the time in the wheelchair.
D) Securing the drainage bag to the wheelchair below the level of the client's bladder.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** D) Securing the drainage bag to the wheelchair below the level of the client's bladder.

**Rationale:** To prevent CAUTI, the drainage bag must always remain below the level of the bladder to facilitate gravity drainage and prevent the backward flow of stagnant urine into the bladder. Disconnecting the tubing breaks the sterile seal, resting the bag on the lap raises it above the bladder, and clamping the tube can cause urinary stasis—all of which increase infection risk.
</details>

### Question 699
An emergency department nurse is caring for a 40-year-old male client complaining of severe flank pain and nausea. The healthcare provider suspects renal calculi (kidney stones). Which specific nursing action must be implemented when the client asks to use the restroom?

A) Instruct the client to restrict fluids until the pain subsides.
B) Provide a urine strainer and instruct the client to void through it.
C) Obtain a bladder scanner reading immediately after the client voids.
D) Collect a clean-catch midstream urine sample for a 24-hour protein test.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** B) Provide a urine strainer and instruct the client to void through it.

**Rationale:** When a client is suspected of having renal calculi, all urine must be strained. This allows the nurse to catch any passed stones or gravel so they can be sent to the laboratory for analysis, which determines the stone's composition and guides future treatment. Fluids should be encouraged, not restricted, to help pass the stone.
</details>

### Question 700
A nurse in an outpatient dialysis center is assessing a 65-year-old female client with end-stage chronic kidney disease. The client has a newly placed arteriovenous (AV) fistula in her left arm. Which observation requires an immediate intervention by the nurse?

A) The nurse feels a buzzing sensation when gently touching the fistula site.
B) The nurse hears a swooshing sound when listening over the fistula with a stethoscope.
C) A patient care technician is placing a blood pressure cuff on the client's left arm.
D) The client is repeatedly squeezing a soft rubber ball with her left hand.

<details>
<summary>Answer & Rationale</summary>
**Correct Answer:** C) A patient care technician is placing a blood pressure cuff on the client's left arm.

**Rationale:** Limb precautions must be strictly enforced for a client with an AV fistula; no blood pressure readings, venipunctures, or IV insertions should be performed on the affected arm to prevent damaging the fistula or causing thrombosis. A palpable thrill (buzzing) and an auscultated bruit (swooshing) are expected, normal findings that indicate a patent fistula. Squeezing a rubber ball is an encouraged exercise to help the fistula mature.
</details>
### Question 701
A nurse is caring for an 82-year-old female resident in a long-term care facility who has an indwelling urinary catheter. When entering the room to assist with morning care, the nurse observes that the unlicensed assistive personnel (UAP) has placed the catheter drainage bag resting on the patient's abdomen while preparing to turn her. What is the most appropriate initial action by the nurse?

A) Document the volume of urine in the bag before the UAP begins repositioning.
B) Immediately move the drainage bag below the level of the client's bladder.
C) Clamp the catheter tubing to prevent leakage during the turning process.
D) Empty the drainage bag completely before continuing the repositioning.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B) Immediately move the drainage bag below the level of the client's bladder.</strong><br><br>
The urinary drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder to prevent the backflow of urine into the urinary tract, which significantly increases the risk of a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). The nurse should intervene immediately to correct the placement of the bag. Clamping the tube is unnecessary for turning, and while emptying the bag is good practice before major mobilization, preventing backflow takes immediate priority. 
</details>

### Question 702
A 45-year-old male arrives at the emergency department reporting severe, sharp right flank pain that radiates down to his groin, along with nausea. The healthcare provider suspects renal calculi (kidney stones). When the client states he feels the urge to void, which action should the nurse take?

A) Ask the client to hold the urine until an abdominal ultrasound is performed.
B) Provide a sterile specimen cup for a clean-catch urine culture.
C) Strain all of the client's urine through a gauze or a specialized urine strainer.
D) Insert a straight catheter to accurately measure the client's urine output.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C) Strain all of the client's urine through a gauze or a specialized urine strainer.</strong><br><br>
When a client is suspected of having renal calculi, it is standard nursing practice to strain all urine. This allows the nurse to catch any stones or gravel that the client passes so they can be sent to the laboratory for analysis. Knowing the composition of the stone (e.g., calcium oxalate, uric acid) dictates future dietary and medical management. Catheterization is invasive and unnecessary, and holding urine can exacerbate the severe pain.
</details>

### Question 703
The nurse is preparing a 68-year-old male client for his scheduled hemodialysis treatment at an outpatient clinic. The client has a mature arteriovenous (AV) fistula in his left forearm. Which assessment technique should the nurse perform to verify the patency of the fistula?

A) Palpate for a thrill and auscultate for a bruit directly over the fistula site.
B) Obtain a baseline blood pressure reading on the client's left arm.
C) Flush the fistula site with 10 mL of sterile normal saline.
D) Assess the capillary refill and skin turgor in the fingers of the right hand.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A) Palpate for a thrill and auscultate for a bruit directly over the fistula site.</strong><br><br>
A functioning AV fistula has a palpable pulsation or vibration called a "thrill" and a swishing sound called a "bruit" upon auscultation. These indicate adequate blood flow and patency. Blood pressure should never be taken on an arm with an AV fistula, as the compression can damage the graft or fistula. Flushing the fistula is an advanced skill specifically for dialysis access and is not an initial assessment technique for patency. Assessing the right hand does not check the left arm's circulation.
</details>

---

### Question 704
A 24-year-old female client calls the primary care clinic reporting a strong urge to urinate frequently, a burning sensation during urination, and cloudy urine that began the previous evening. Which instruction should the clinic nurse provide first?

A) "Limit your fluid intake to reduce the frequency of your trips to the bathroom."
B) "Begin taking the leftover antibiotics you have from your last infection."
C) "Apply a heating pad to your lower back on the highest setting for comfort."
D) "Come into the clinic so we can collect a midstream urine sample."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D) "Come into the clinic so we can collect a midstream urine sample."</strong><br><br>
The client is exhibiting classic signs of acute cystitis (urinary tract infection). A urinalysis and a urine culture and sensitivity are required to confirm the infection and identify the causative organism before beginning treatment. Clients should never be advised to take leftover antibiotics. Fluid intake should be increased, not limited, to help flush out bacteria. 
</details>

### Question 705
A 72-year-old male client is returning to the medical-surgical unit after a transurethral resection of the prostate (TURP). He has a 3-way indwelling catheter with continuous bladder irrigation (CBI) running. During hourly rounds, the nurse observes that the urinary drainage in the bag has turned bright red with multiple large blood clots. Which action should the nurse take immediately?

A) Stop the irrigation fluid and notify the healthcare provider.
B) Administer the prescribed PRN pain medication for bladder spasms.
C) Increase the flow rate of the continuous bladder irrigation.
D) Deflate the catheter balloon to relieve pressure on the prostate fossa.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C) Increase the flow rate of the continuous bladder irrigation.</strong><br><br>
The primary purpose of continuous bladder irrigation (CBI) following a TURP is to prevent the formation of blood clots that can obstruct the catheter. If the drainage becomes bright red or contains clots, the nurse should immediately increase the flow rate of the irrigation fluid until the drainage becomes light pink and clear of clots. Stopping the irrigation would allow the clots to quickly obstruct the catheter. The catheter balloon must remain inflated to maintain placement and apply hemostatic pressure.
</details>
### Question 706
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is instructing a 28-year-old female client on how to collect a clean-catch midstream urine specimen for a suspected urinary tract infection. Which action by the client indicates that the teaching was successful?

A) The client collects the very first drops of urine into the specimen cup.
B) The client initiates the urine stream, stops, and then collects the specimen.
C) The client wipes the perineal area from back to front before urinating.
D) The client completely fills the specimen cup to the very top.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
A clean-catch midstream urine collection requires the client to first cleanse the perineal area from front to back to avoid moving rectal bacteria to the urethra. The client should then initiate the urine stream into the toilet to flush out superficial urethral contaminants, pause the stream, and then collect the midstream portion of the urine in the sterile cup. Collecting the first drops (Option A) introduces urethral bacteria into the sample. Wiping back to front (Option C) increases the risk of contamination. The cup does not need to be filled to the top (Option D); a standard sample size is sufficient.
</details>

---

### Question 707
A nurse on a surgical unit is caring for a 72-year-old male client who just returned from a transurethral resection of the prostate (TURP) and has continuous bladder irrigation (CBI) running. Upon assessing the drainage bag, the nurse notes that the fluid is light pink without clots. What is the nurse's best action?

A) Increase the flow rate of the irrigation solution immediately.
B) Clamp the catheter tubing to prevent further blood loss.
C) Notify the healthcare provider of active hemorrhage.
D) Document the finding and maintain the current irrigation rate.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: D</strong><br>
Following a TURP procedure, a light pink urinary drainage without clots is an expected and normal finding when continuous bladder irrigation is running at an appropriate rate. The nurse should document the finding and continue to monitor the client. Increasing the rate (Option A) is unnecessary if the urine is already light pink and clot-free. Clamping the tube (Option B) is contraindicated as it can cause bladder distention and spasms. Light pink drainage does not indicate active, severe hemorrhage (Option C), which would present as bright red, thick, or ketchup-like output.
</details>

---

### Question 708
A nurse in a dialysis center is preparing to initiate hemodialysis for a 55-year-old client with end-stage renal disease. The client has a newly mature arteriovenous (AV) fistula in the left arm. Which assessment finding indicates to the nurse that the AV fistula is patent and functioning properly?

A) Palpation of a continuous thrill over the fistula site.
B) The absence of a bruit when listening with a stethoscope.
C) The left arm is significantly cooler than the right arm.
D) Capillary refill in the left hand is greater than 5 seconds.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: A</strong><br>
A functioning AV fistula should have a palpable thrill (a continuous buzzing or vibrating sensation) and an audible bruit (a whooshing sound heard with a stethoscope). These findings indicate strong, turbulent blood flow through the anastamosis. The absence of a bruit (Option B) indicates a potential occlusion. A cooler arm (Option C) or delayed capillary refill (Option D) suggests inadequate arterial perfusion to the extremity distal to the fistula, which is an abnormal finding known as "steal syndrome."
</details>

---

### Question 709
A 34-year-old male client arrives at the emergency department complaining of severe right flank pain that radiates to his groin. The healthcare provider suspects renal calculi. Which intervention should the nurse implement first when the client needs to void?

A) Insert an indwelling urinary catheter to accurately measure output.
B) Administer prescribed intravenous fluids to forcefully flush out the stone.
C) Provide a urine strainer and instruct the client to use it for every void.
D) Instruct the client to restrict oral fluid intake to reduce kidney pressure.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: C</strong><br>
For a client with suspected renal calculi (kidney stones), all urine must be strained to catch any stones or fragments that pass. These fragments are then sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine the stone's composition, which guides future treatment and dietary restrictions. Inserting a catheter (Option A) is invasive, increases infection risk, and is not a first-line intervention for simple renal calculi. While IV fluids may be ordered, catching the stone takes priority when the client is voiding (Option B). Restricting oral fluids (Option D) is contraindicated; fluids are encouraged to help flush the stone out.
</details>

---

### Question 710
A nurse is making rounds in a long-term care facility and observes a nursing assistant helping an 82-year-old female client out of bed into a wheelchair. The client has an indwelling Foley catheter. Which action by the nursing assistant requires immediate intervention by the nurse?

A) Securing the catheter tubing to the client's inner thigh.
B) Placing the urinary drainage bag on the armrest of the wheelchair.
C) Emptying the drainage bag before transferring the client to the chair.
D) Ensuring there are no dependent loops in the catheter tubing.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>
<strong>Correct Answer: B</strong><br>
The urinary drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the client's bladder to prevent the retrograde flow of urine, which can lead to a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI). Placing the bag on the armrest of the wheelchair positions it above the bladder, requiring immediate intervention. Securing the tubing to the thigh (Option A) prevents trauma and pulling. Emptying the bag before a transfer (Option C) and preventing dependent loops (Option D) are both correct, evidence-based practices for Foley catheter management.
</details>
### Question 711
A nurse in an outpatient clinic is caring for a 22-year-old female client who reports painful urination, lower abdominal discomfort, and the constant urge to urinate. The nurse collects a clean-catch urine sample for urinalysis. Based on the client's symptoms, which of the following visual findings should the nurse most likely expect to observe?

A. Pale yellow, completely clear urine
B. Dark amber urine with a sweet, fruity odor
C. Cloudy urine with a strong, foul odor
D. Bright orange urine with no noticeable odor

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: C**

**Rationale:** The client is presenting with classic symptoms of a lower urinary tract infection (UTI), including dysuria, suprapubic discomfort, and urinary frequency. The nurse would expect to see cloudy, foul-smelling urine, which indicates the presence of bacteria, white blood cells, and cellular debris. Pale yellow, clear urine (Option A) is a normal finding. Sweet, fruity-smelling urine (Option B) is associated with diabetic ketoacidosis. Bright orange urine (Option D) is typically an expected side effect of the urinary analgesic phenazopyridine, not a primary symptom of the infection itself.
</details>

---

### Question 712
A nurse on a medical-surgical unit is caring for a 65-year-old male client who has an indwelling urinary (Foley) catheter in place following abdominal surgery. Which of the following actions should the nurse take to prevent a catheter-associated urinary tract infection (CAUTI)?

A. Keep the urinary drainage bag resting directly on the floor at all times.
B. Disconnect the drainage tubing from the catheter daily to flush the system.
C. Elevate the drainage bag above the client's bladder when transferring him to a chair.
D. Ensure the urinary drainage bag is maintained below the level of the client's bladder.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: D**

**Rationale:** To prevent catheter-associated urinary tract infections (CAUTIs), the drainage bag must always be kept below the level of the bladder to prevent the backflow of stagnant, potentially contaminated urine into the bladder. The drainage bag should never rest on the floor (Option A) as this increases the risk of bacterial contamination. Routine disconnection of the closed drainage system (Option B) breaks the sterile seal and introduces bacteria, greatly increasing infection risk. Elevating the bag above the bladder (Option C) allows urine to flow back into the bladder, promoting infection.
</details>

---

### Question 713
A 40-year-old male client arrives at the emergency department reporting severe, colicky left flank pain that radiates down to his groin. The healthcare provider suspects urolithiasis (kidney stones). Which of the following nursing actions is most important for identifying the cause of the client's condition?

A. Straining all of the client's urine through a gauze or paper filter.
B. Restricting the client's oral fluid intake until the severe pain subsides.
C. Inserting an indwelling urinary catheter to accurately measure hourly output.
D. Administering a strong intravenous diuretic to help flush out the stone.

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: A**

**Rationale:** For a client with suspected kidney stones, the nurse should strain all urine. This allows the nurse to catch any passed stones or gravel, which can then be sent to the laboratory for analysis to determine their composition (e.g., calcium oxalate, uric acid). This analysis dictates future medical and dietary management. Fluids should be encouraged, not restricted (Option B), to help flush the stone out. An indwelling catheter is not routinely indicated for kidney stones and increases infection risk (Option C). Diuretics are not typically the primary treatment for acute kidney stones; rather, hydration and pain management are prioritized (Option D).
</details>

---

### Question 714
A nurse in an assisted living facility is assessing a 75-year-old male client recently diagnosed with benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH). When asking the client about his urinary habits, which of the following statements by the client should the nurse expect?

A. "I produce very large amounts of urine every time I go."
B. "It takes me a long time to start my urine stream."
C. "I feel intense burning and pain at the end of urination."
D. "I lose control of my bladder completely when I sneeze."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: B**

**Rationale:** Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) involves the enlargement of the prostate gland, which surrounds and compresses the urethra. This causes lower urinary tract symptoms such as hesitancy (difficulty starting a stream), weak stream, dribbling at the end of urination, and urinary retention. Producing large amounts of urine (Option A) is polyuria, seen in conditions like diabetes, whereas BPH typically causes frequent, small voids. Intense burning (Option C) indicates a urinary tract infection. Losing control of the bladder when sneezing (Option D) is stress incontinence, which is more common in women due to weakened pelvic floor muscles, not BPH.
</details>

---

### Question 715
A nurse is providing discharge teaching in a women's health clinic to a 32-year-old client who is 6 weeks postpartum. The client reports occasionally leaking a small amount of urine when she laughs, coughs, or lifts her baby. Which of the following recommendations should the nurse provide first?

A. "You should limit your fluid intake to less than 1 liter per day."
B. "You will need a referral for a surgical bladder sling."
C. "Practice performing pelvic floor muscle exercises daily."
D. "Insert an absorbent tampon to help block the flow of urine."

<details>
<summary><strong>Rationale</strong></summary>

**Correct Answer: C**

**Rationale:** The client is experiencing stress incontinence, which is common after childbirth due to the weakening of the pelvic floor muscles. The most appropriate initial nursing intervention is to teach the client to perform Kegel (pelvic floor muscle) exercises to strengthen these muscles and improve urinary control. Limiting fluid intake (Option A) can lead to concentrated urine, which irritates the bladder and increases incontinence, and can lead to dehydration. Surgery (Option B) is an invasive, last-resort option, not a first-line recommendation. Tampons (Option D) are for menstrual flow and do not block the urethra; using them for this purpose is ineffective and increases the risk of infection.
</details>
